# Anvil

Anvil is a parametric solid modeler that runs entirely in the browser. It has a SolidWorks-style workspace (CommandManager ribbon, FeatureManager tree, PropertyManager, task pane, heads-up view toolbar) on top of a small CSG kernel, and every model is also a script: you can build parts by clicking, by typing a script in the console, or by driving `window.anvil` from automation.

There is no build step and everything runs offline: three.js r128 is in `vendor/three.min.js`. With the optional OpenCascade engine installed (`vendor/get-occt.ps1`, 50 MB, not in git) every part and assembly is built by a real B-rep kernel as well (see *OpenCascade kernel*). Open `index.html` over HTTP, or double-click the *Anvil* Desktop shortcut (see *Running* below).

Since 2026-10-02 the work has been refinement rather than new feature areas: eight rounds aimed at making Anvil feel like SolidWorks — no sketch grid, inference while drawing, gliding views, a viewport that only redraws when something changed, on-model handles in the PropertyManagers, SmartMates and pop-up toolbars in assemblies, exact linework in drawings. *Sketching*, *Viewport and PropertyManagers* and *Performance* below describe the current behaviour.

## Files

| File | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `index.html` | Page skeleton. Loads the scripts in dependency order. |
| `csg.js` | BSP-tree constructive solid geometry (union, subtract, intersect, transform, signed volume). Global `CSG`. |
| `kernel.js` | Modeling kernel, root file: creates the global `Kernel` (one object, extended by the eight `kernel-*.js` files below and never reassigned) and its internal namespace `Kernel._` (non-enumerable; the private helpers and module state the kernel files share), the vector helpers, planes, face references, triangulation, primitive tool bodies. The nine files load in the order listed here, right after `csg.js`. |
| `kernel-sketch.js` | Sketch entities to polylines (tessellation settings), profiles with nesting, 2D geometry utilities, 2D entity editing (mirror, pattern, trim), sketch dimensions. |
| `kernel-curves.js` | True curves: the analytic surface records curved facets carry, the extrude / revolve / transform tool builders, exact volumes and mass properties from the records, analytic edges (true circles and arcs). |
| `kernel-equations.js` | Equations (global variables, expression grammar) and configurations. |
| `kernel-mesh.js` | Meshing (`meshFromBody`), mesh quality, face outlines, body edges and the chamfer / fillet edge features. |
| `kernel-rebuild.js` | The feature tree: `newModel`, feature names, `buildTool`, `rebuild` and its cache, multi-body parts, build serialization for Web Worker rebuilds (`packBuild` / `unpackBuild`). |
| `kernel-features.js` | Sweep, loft and shell. |
| `kernel-sheetmetal.js` | Sheet metal: edge flange, hem, jog, sketched bend, normal cut, bend allowance, flat pattern. |
| `kernel-script.js` | STL / DXF export (sketch, face, flat pattern), the script `Builder` / `SketchBuilder`, `runScript`, `generateScript`. |
| `sketch-solver.js` | Sketch relations and driving dimensions: the numeric constraint solver (Levenberg-Marquardt, minimum-norm steps), DOF / Under-Fully-Over Defined analysis, inference, dragging, and the `.rel()` script hook. Global `SketchSolver`, no DOM. See *Sketch relations*. |
| `assembly.js` | Assembly documents: components, mates (Standard / Advanced / Mechanical), the mate solver, component patterns, exploded views, sub-assemblies (rigid or Flexible), live links to saved documents (`Assembly.Documents`), BOM / mass properties / interference across levels, Pack and Go, assembly scripts (`a.insert` / `a.mate`). Global `Assembly`. See *Assemblies*. |
| `materials.js` | Material library (classes of materials with density, elastic modulus, Poisson's ratio, tensile and yield strength, appearance colour), part and per-body materials, mass properties with per-body densities, custom / configuration-specific properties with `"SW-…"` links, and the `p.material` / `p.bodyMaterial` / `p.property` script round trip. Global `Materials`, no DOM. See *Materials and custom properties*. |
| `exchange.js` | Neutral CAD formats: STEP AP214, 3MF and OBJ export; STEP (planar / faceted B-reps), STL, OBJ and 3MF import; the *Imported* feature and the hook that builds it inside `Kernel.rebuild`. Global `Exchange`, no DOM. See *Import and export of neutral formats*. |
| `evaluate.js` | The Evaluate tab's analyses as plain functions over kernel meshes and CSG bodies: Check (open / non-manifold edges, zero-area faces, self-intersections), smooth normals for Zebra Stripes, Section Properties of a planar face or sketch, Draft Analysis, Clearance Verification (exact minimum distance, closest points) and Interference Detection over selected bodies. Global `Evaluate`, no DOM. See *Evaluate tools*. |
| `holewizard.js` | Hole Wizard: the ANSI Inch tables (#2–1 in: SolidWorks 2022's Hole Wizard clearance fits and counterbores (lane FH2), ASME B18.6.3 82° countersinks, UNC / UNF tap drills, number / letter / fractional drills) and the ISO M2–M20 table (ISO 273 clearance fits, ISO 4762 counterbores, ISO 10642 countersinks, tap drills), the `hole` feature (registered with `Kernel.registerFeature`), `p.holeWizard` and cosmetic threads (`HoleWizard.threads`). Global `HoleWizard`, no DOM. See *Hole Wizard*. |
| `ui-holewizard.js` | The Hole Wizard PropertyManager (Type / Positions tabs) and the viewport's dashed cosmetic threads. |
| `nest.js` | Geometry Nesting engine (no DOM, global `Nest`): true-shape nesting of flat parts (outer loop + holes of exact lines and arcs) on sheets by conservative raster bottom-left fill; exact overlap check; per-sheet sketch entities and DXF. See *Geometry Nesting*. |
| `ui-nesting.js` | Transform ▸ Geometry Nesting PropertyManager (`UI.cmdTNesting`): parts from the flat pattern, a sketch or a DXF, sheet presets, live Nest with a preview, Save DXF per sheet, ✓ makes the Nest document. |
| `worker.js` | Web Worker entry: `importScripts('csg.js', 'kernel.js', the eight 'kernel-*.js' files, 'holewizard.js', 'exchange.js')`, rebuilds a model off the main thread and posts the packed build back with its edges, curve info, smooth normals and volume; also builds the PropertyManagers' live previews (`{ type: 'preview' }`, newest request wins) (see *Background rebuilds*). |
| `drawing-worker.js` | Drawing views off the main thread (`importScripts('csg.js', 'kernel.js', the eight 'kernel-*.js' files, 'drawing.js', …)` with the six `drawing-*.js` engine files, not the UI ones): gets the part / assembly mesh once, then works out each view's hidden-line projection (`Drawing.projectView`) and each section view's cut, projection and cut-face loops (`Drawing.sectionJob`), and posts them back, so opening a drawing or placing a view shows the sheet at once and the views drop in. |
| `occt-kernel.js` | The OpenCascade kernel: builds an Anvil model's feature tree with OpenCascade (exact B-rep), reads / writes STEP and IGES, turns the result into an Anvil build (`OcctKernel.build`, `importFile`, `toBuild`, `mapFaces`). Global `OcctKernel`, no DOM. See *OpenCascade kernel*. |
| `occt-worker.js` | The OpenCascade module worker (loaded only while *Tools ▸ OpenCascade kernel* is on): checks the engine's SHA-256 before it runs (plain or gzipped `.wasm`, or a file the user picked), builds, per-part rebuild caches, packs the Anvil bodies / edges / normals off the main thread, STEP / IGES import. |
| `ui-occt.js` | *Tools ▸ OpenCascade kernel*: runs every rebuild through OpenCascade too, shows its solid when it agrees with (or goes beyond) Anvil's, swaps it in without flicker (selection restored by component and B-rep face; *Show the quick result first* cross-fades, *Wait for the exact solid* holds the frame), the status chip, *Tools ▸ OpenCascade kernel ▸ Engine file…*, `anvil.occt()`. |
| `ui-rebuild.js` | Rebuild feedback: the status bar's rebuild chip, error / warning badges and tooltips in the FeatureManager, the Dangling tag, the *What's Wrong* dialog. |
| `vendor/` | `three.min.js` (three.js r128, MIT); `get-occt.ps1` fetches opencascade.js 2.0.0-beta.b5ff984 (LGPL-2.1, licence in `vendor/occt/`) into `vendor/occt/` and checks the SHA-256 of the loader and the binary. |
| `icons.js` | Inline SVG icon set on a 16 grid in a SolidWorks-2024-like style (grey solids with yellow feature faces, dark sketch strokes with grey points, no blue). Global `Icons(name, size)`, `Icons.has(name)`; each icon is re-weighed and pixel-hinted for the size and devicePixelRatio it is drawn at (use 16, 24, 32 or 48 px). |
| `style.css` | The whole UI stylesheet. |
| `ui-core.js` | App state, three.js scene, camera (view animation, eased wheel zoom, rotation about the model centre), render-on-demand frame loop, picking, highlights, rebuild and worker-preview plumbing, undo. Creates the shared `UI` namespace. |
| `ui-sketch.js` | Viewport mouse handling (SolidWorks mouse mapping), sketch mode, the 2D sketch tools, inference while drawing (no grid), typed values while drawing, the sketch dimension overlay and its Modify box, the confirmation corner. |
| `ui-commands.js` | PropertyManager rendering (on-model handles, selection lists, spin steps) and every feature command, incl. the sketch PMs (Offset, Fillet, Trim, Extend, Mirror, Pattern). |
| `ui-panels.js` | Ribbon, menu bar, feature tree, heads-up toolbar, status bar, modals (patched in place, no flicker). |
| `ui-app.js` | Task pane, script console, files and exports, shortcuts, `window.anvil`, boot. |
| `ui-materials.js` | Materials and properties in the UI: the Edit Material dialog, the FeatureManager *Material* row and body *Material ▸* menus, per-body viewport colours, *File ▸ Properties* (Summary / Custom / Configuration Specific), `anvil.material` / `anvil.properties`. |
| `ui-assembly.js` | Assembly commands beyond Insert / Mate: component drag by pose (placements-only solve per frame), SmartMates, the Mate pop-up and component context toolbars, Tab / Shift+Tab hide / show, Change Transparency, Isolate, component patterns, Exploded View and the assembly ConfigurationManager, sub-assembly rows in the FeatureManager, Open Part / Edit Part / Return to assembly, Solve as Rigid / Flexible, Make Linked Document, Pack and Go, the Bill of Materials dialog, and the saved-files backing of the document store. |
| `ui-evaluate.js` | The Evaluate tab in the UI: Check, Zebra Stripes (stripe shader overlay), Section Properties, Draft Analysis (face colours), Clearance Verification and Interference Detection limited to selected components; `UI.evaluate` and `anvil.check / sectionProperties / draftAnalysis / clearance / interferenceOf / zebra`. |
| `ui-select.js` | Selection: the Selection Filter toolbar (F5 / F6), box selection (window / crossing) in parts, sketches and assemblies, multi-selection with Ctrl / Shift, FeatureManager ↔ viewport sync, Select Other, the orange pre-highlight, Instant3D drag handles on extrude / revolve faces and sketch dimensions, and the drawing of the PropertyManagers' on-model handles. `BoxSelect` (screen-space geometry, no DOM) is usable on its own. See *Selection and Instant3D*. |
| `ui-relations.js` | Sketch relations in the UI: relation and inference icons (placed clear of labels), distance / angle Smart Dimensions, Add Relations and Display/Delete Relations PropertyManagers, dragging sketch geometry, the (-) / (+) prefix of sketches in the FeatureManager. |
| `drawing.js`, `drawing-curves.js`, `drawing-hlr.js`, `drawing-views.js`, `drawing-sheet.js`, `drawing-annotations.js`, `drawing-render.js` | The drawing engine (global `Drawing`, usable without the UI; loaded in this order by `index.html`, the test pages and `drawing-worker.js`): `drawing.js` makes the `Drawing` object (and `Drawing._`, the engine's internal namespace shared by the files that follow), view orientations and model edges; then true curves from the kernel's analytic records, projection with exact hidden-line removal and tangent edge modes, section and detail views with hatch per material and dimensions that follow the part, sheets and the drawing document (title block, model dimensions, Bill of Materials, balloons), annotations (centre marks, centrelines, hole callouts, ordinate / baseline / chamfer dimensions, tolerances, driving dimensions), and the sheet primitives with the SVG / DXF / PDF writers (see *Drawings*). |
| `drawing-ui.js`, `drawing-ui-tools.js`, `drawing-ui-panels.js`, `drawing-ui-sketch.js` (the Sketch tab, Line Format, Hide/Show Edges, Relative View), `drawing.css` | The drawing document UI (only when `UI` exists; the three share `UI.drawingInternal`): state, the sheet in the viewport with pan / zoom and view dragging, open / close, rendering with the views worked out in `drawing-worker.js`, hit testing and the mouse; the commands, the Projected / Section / Detail View and annotation tools and their PropertyManagers, title block fields edited in place, dimension dragging; the drawing FeatureManager, ribbon tabs, menus, keys and `anvil.drawing` (see *Drawings*). |
| `examples/examples.js` | Sample parts, and assemblies marked `kind: "assembly"`, as scripts (`AnvilExamples`). Picked up by the Design Library when loaded. |
| `library.js` | The Design Library's own content, no DOM (`AnvilLibrary`): inch hardware families as part scripts (hex bolts, hex nuts, USS / SAE flat washers, PEM S nuts, FH studs, SO standoffs), curved parts (sweeps with big radii, a helix spring, a rolled cover, a filleted plate, a revolved knob, a lofted flare) and library features (`featureEntities`, `featureArea`, `addFeature`). See *Design Library*. |
| `tests/kernel-tests.html` | Headless kernel test page (no three.js needed). |
| `tests/bench.html`, `tests/BENCHMARKS.md` | Rebuild timing page (median of N runs per example plus stress parts) and the recorded baselines (latest: 2026-10-02, sum of medians 381 → 165 ms). |
| `tests/ui-smoke.html` | Loads the app in a frame and drives it through `window.anvil` and the `UI.cmd*` functions: examples, PMs, undo/redo, exports, modals, sheet metal, multi-body parts, equations, configurations, assemblies. `?watch` lays it out for screen recordings; downloads are captured, never saved. |
| `tests/occt-tests.html` | Every part example and feature model built by both kernels: OpenCascade's solid valid, volume / bounding box / body count matching Anvil's, exact volumes where known, STEP / IGES round trips, the rebuild cache. Needs `vendor/occt`. |
| `experiments/` | `occt-lab.html` and `OCCT.md`: the experiment that led to the OpenCascade kernel. |
| `serve.ps1` | Tiny PowerShell static server for local use (`-Port`, default 8765; serves `.wasm` and `.gz` with their types). |
| `launch-anvil.ps1` | Opens Anvil like a desktop app: starts `serve.ps1` hidden on port 8812 if nothing answers there, then opens <http://localhost:8812/> in an Edge or Chrome app window (`--app`, 1600 × 1000; the default browser otherwise). The Desktop shortcut *Anvil* runs it. |
| `ROADMAP.md`, `SESSION_LOG.md` | Plan and per-session history. |

## Running

The scripts are loaded with relative paths, so the folder has to be served over HTTP (a `file://` page cannot load the modules in every browser). No node or python is assumed:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\serve.ps1 -Port 8812
```

Then open <http://localhost:8812/>. The default port is 8765, but on some Windows machines 8765 falls in an excluded port range and cannot be bound, so the launcher and the tests use 8812. The kernel tests are at <http://localhost:8812/tests/kernel-tests.html>; the page prints a pass/fail table and exposes `window.__anvilTests` for automation. `tests/ui-smoke.html` and `tests/occt-tests.html` work the same way (see *Tests*). Besides files, `serve.ps1` answers one API, `HEAD` / `POST /api/dxf2dwg`, which turns DXF text into a DWG with the ODA File Converter or LibreDWG for *DXF/DWG Output* in the app (see *Exports ▸ DWG*).

**As a desktop app.** `launch-anvil.ps1` does both steps: when nothing answers on port 8812 it starts `serve.ps1 -Port 8812` in a hidden PowerShell window and waits for it (up to 10 s), then opens Anvil in its own window — Edge (or Chrome) in app mode, no tabs or address bar, 1600 × 1000 — or in the default browser when neither is installed. The server keeps running in the background (it uses almost nothing) and the next launch reuses it. The Desktop shortcut *Anvil* runs this script, so Anvil starts with a double-click:

```powershell
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\launch-anvil.ps1
```

This shortcut is the browser way: files go through the browser's file picker and downloads. The Windows app (`desktop/`, see `desktop/README.md`) uses Windows' own Open and Save boxes instead: File ▸ Open remembers the file, Ctrl+S saves straight back to it, Save As and every export ask where with Windows' Save box (smoke group `rfHZ`).

**Drag and drop.** Drop any file File ▸ Open takes (an Anvil part, assembly, drawing or script, STEP, IGES, STL, OBJ, 3MF, DXF, DWG) anywhere on the Anvil window and it opens exactly as File ▸ Open would: "Save changes?" first when the open document has unsaved changes, then the file. An overlay says what the drop will do while you drag; a type Anvil cannot open is refused with a message listing the types it can. Anvil holds one document at a time, so of several files dropped together the first one it can open opens. The Open dialog's own drop zone still takes a file dropped on it. In the Windows app a dropped part keeps its path: the title names it and Ctrl+S saves back to it (smoke group `rfID`, `node desktop/check.js drop`).

**Recent documents and the title.** The File menu ends with the last 10 documents opened or saved, newest first (SolidWorks' recent list), and **File ▸ Browse Recent Documents…** shows them with their folders. In the Windows app every entry is a file path: a click opens it ("Save changes?" first) and Ctrl+S then saves back to it; a file that was moved or deleted is greyed and can be removed. In a browser a page cannot reopen a file by its path, so the list is honest about it: documents saved in this browser (File ▸ Save) reopen at once, and a file opened from disk is listed by name and its click shows the Open dialog asking for it again. In the Windows app an imported STEP, IGES, DXF, DWG or STL opens as a new part whose first Save starts in the import's folder with its name, and a drawing opened from a `.drawing` file is written back to it by Ctrl+S. The window and browser-tab title is the document's file name (else its name), the drawing's name and sheet while a drawing shows, with ` *` while unsaved. API: `UI.recentDocuments` `{ list(fresh), cached(), open(entry), remove(entry), clear(), browse() }`, `UI.windowTitle()`; smoke group `rfIK`, `node desktop/check.js recent`.

## Headless (Node)

`headless/anvil.js` runs the engine under Node.js (24 or later; plain built-ins, no npm packages) without a browser or server, so a script or Claude can build parts, check volumes, write exports and make drawings from a shell. `headless/load.js` loads the same DOM-free script set that `tests/kernel-tests.html` lists (it parses that page's `<script src>` tags, so a split of `kernel.js` or `drawing.js` is picked up as soon as the test page lists the new files) into the global scope with `vm.runInThisContext`, as `occt-worker.js` does; `headless/occt.js` loads the OpenCascade engine from `vendor/occt` after checking both files against the pinned SHA-256s. Every command prints a JSON summary to stdout (messages go to stderr), resolves its own paths (run it from any directory) and exits 0 when all is well, 1 on feature errors / failed checks / failed tests, 2 on a usage error or a missing engine. `--help` lists the commands.

```powershell
node anvil/headless/anvil.js build part.anvil.js --stl part.stl --step part.step --json part.anvil.json --script part2.js
node anvil/headless/anvil.js build bracket.js --occt --step bracket.step        # also built by OpenCascade; exact STEP
node anvil/headless/anvil.js build tray.js --dxf-flat tray-flat.dxf --dxf-face 3 top.dxf
node anvil/headless/anvil.js example "Sheet-metal U-bracket" --dxf-flat out.dxf --dxf-version r12 --no-bends   # laser DXF, read back and checked
node anvil/headless/anvil.js build bracket.js --dxf-flat flat.dxf --dxf-version 2000 --dxf-style entities      # R2000, LINE / ARC / CIRCLE
node anvil/headless/anvil.js build tray.js --dwg-flat tray-flat.dwg --dwg-face 3 top.dwg   # DWG through a converter (see Exports ▸ DWG)
node anvil/headless/anvil.js drawing part.anvil.js --svg part.svg --dxf part.dxf --pdf part.pdf --views Front,Top,Right,Isometric --scale 1:2
node anvil/headless/anvil.js drawing part.anvil.js --dwg part.dwg
node anvil/headless/anvil.js example "Sheet-metal U-bracket" --dwg bracket.dwg        # a DWG of the example's drawing
node anvil/headless/anvil.js example "L-bracket" --occt --stl out.stl           # a bundled example (name prefix or index)
node anvil/headless/anvil.js example --all                                      # every example against its documented volume
node anvil/headless/anvil.js examples                                           # the list with documented volumes
node anvil/headless/anvil.js test                                               # tests/kernel-tests.html under Node
node anvil/headless/anvil.js test --only rfGM,rfGN                              # those group files, with every helper of the full page
node anvil/headless/occt-tests.js [--native [dir]] [--json out]                  # tests/occt-tests.html under Node (its worker run in-process)
node anvil/headless/anvil.js example --all --occt --native                       # OpenCascade 7.6.3 native for Windows (desktop/native) instead of the wasm
```

`test` runs each group file as a job in its own process, at most half the machine's threads at a time (`--jobs N` changes that, `--serial` runs the page in one process in order), longest first by `headless/test-timings.json`, and prints the same rows in the same order as a serial run. A group file longer than a third of the even share (all files' seconds / jobs) is split by its rows into several jobs, each a run of consecutive tests at most that long, chosen from the recorded time of each test (every k-th test when none is on record); every part loads every group file, as a whole-file job does, and the runner checks the parts agree on the file's test count and ran each test exactly once (else a FAIL row `… split across n jobs: every row once`). `--no-split` gives one job per file. `--timings` adds seconds per group file, the slowest tests and the largest process; `--save-timings` merges the seconds per file, and each test's time in files of 8 s or more, into the timings file (an `--only` run keeps the other files' records); `--json out` writes `{ passed, total, seconds, failures }`.

`verify` (`headless/verify.js`, `npm run verify`) is the head's whole check in one command: the kernel tests, `example --all`, `example --all --occt`, the full ui-smoke in `--shards` headless Chromes (default 2) and `tests/occt-tests.html` in headless Chrome, all at once in one pool of `--slots` busy processes (default half the threads; a Chrome counts 2). The Chromes and the example runs start first and the kernel jobs fill every slot left; it prints one table (result and seconds per suite, the whole run) and every FAIL row, keeps each suite's full output in a temp folder, and exits 0 / 1 (a failure) / 2 (something could not run). It uses the server at `--url` (default `http://localhost:8812`) and starts `serve.ps1` there for the run when nothing answers; `--port` is the first Chrome debugging port (default 9312), `--slowest [n]` adds the slowest kernel tests and smoke steps, `--skip kernel,examples,ex-occt,smoke,occt` leaves suites out. Without `vendor/occt` the two OpenCascade suites show as skipped.

- **`build <file>`** takes a part or assembly script (the `Builder` API of *Scripting*; an assembly script is recognised by its `a.insert` / `a.mate` calls) or a saved model JSON, rebuilds it and prints `name`, `kind`, `features`, `bodies`, `volume`, `area`, `centroid`, `bbox`, `massKg` (per-body materials, as the Mass Properties dialog), `faces`, `triangles`, `errors` / `warnings` (feature id, name, message) and `rebuildMs`; an assembly adds its `components` and `mates`. `--stl`, `--step` (`exchange.js`'s STEP AP214; OpenCascade's exact STEP with `--occt`), `--dxf-flat` (the sheet-metal flat pattern; an error on a part without a Base Flange), `--dxf-face <index> <out>`, `--json` (the model) and `--script` (`Kernel.generateScript`) write files. The DXF options (see *Exports*, *Laser-ready DXF*): `--dxf-version r12 | 2000` (default R12), `--dxf-style polyline | entities` (one closed polyline per loop, the default, or LINE / ARC entities), `--no-bends`, `--no-notes`, `--no-origin` (the flat keeps its own frame instead of its lower-left corner at 0,0) and `--origin` (a face moved to 0,0; it keeps its frame by default); for the flat also `--dxf-boundary`, `--dxf-bend-layers`, `--dxf-relief` and `--dxf-hidden` (the DXF/DWG Output entities below). Every DXF written is read back by `headless/dxf-check.js` and its result is in the summary as `check: { ok, version, insunits, loops, closed, circles, radii, counts, extents }` (a failed check is a summary error, exit 1). `--step` names the file's length unit (`units: 'in' | 'mm'`, an error when it is not the part's); with `--occt` the exact STEP is also read back by OpenCascade and its size compared with the B-rep's (`readBack: { sizeMm, expectedMm, ok }`). `--dwg-flat <out>` and `--dwg-face <index> <out>` write the same DXF (as R2000, which LibreDWG reads with every entity in model space; from R12 it loses them) through a DXF to DWG converter (`headless/dwg.js`: the ODA File Converter when installed, else the vendored LibreDWG, see *Exports ▸ DWG*) and add `converter: 'oda' | 'libredwg'` to the summary; without a converter they exit 2 and say how to get one. `--occt` also builds the part with OpenCascade and adds `occt: { volume, valid, faces, bodies, unsupported, ms, loadMs, tolerance, volumeDiff, agrees }`; without `vendor/occt` it exits 2 and says how to fetch the engine.
- **`drawing <file>`** builds the part and makes a drawing with the `Drawing` engine half (`newDrawing`, `standard3View`, `compute`, then `toSVG` / `toDXF` / `toPDF`, see *Drawings*): `--views` picks the views (one of Front/Back, one of Top/Bottom, one of Right/Left and one of Isometric/Dimetric/Trimetric; the default is the standard three plus Isometric), `--scale n:m` fixes the sheet scale (automatic otherwise); `--dxf-version r12|2000` picks the sheet DXF's version (R12 by default; the file is read back by `dxf-check.js` and the summary's `outputs.dxf.check` has its version, units and entity counts); `--dwg <out>` is the drawing's R2000 DXF through the converter, as above.
- **`example <name | index>`** does the same for a bundled example (`examples/examples.js`, name case-insensitive, prefix allowed; add `--svg` / `--dxf` / `--dwg` / `--pdf` for a drawing of it); `example --all` builds every example, checks each `volume` against the documented one with the example's `tolerance` (2 % when it has none, as the test page does; `--occt` compares each part with OpenCascade too) and prints a pass / fail table; `examples` lists them.
- **`test`** runs `tests/kernel-tests.html` as it is: the page's inline script under a few-line DOM shim (the result table's rows), `window`, a synchronous `XMLHttpRequest` that reads `tests/fixtures` and the page's test group files (`tests/kernel/*.js`, see *Tests ▸ Test group files*) from disk, `DOMParser` from `headless/xmldom.js` (a small strict XML parser with the selectors the SVG checks use) and `Worker` on `worker_threads` (`importScripts`, `postMessage`, `onmessage` bridged, transfer lists ignored), waits for `window.__anvilTests` and prints `passed / total` with every failure's detail. `npm test` and `npm run examples` (root `package.json`) run `test` and `example --all`.

- **`headless/ui-smoke.js`** (`npm run ui-smoke`) runs `tests/ui-smoke.html` in headless Chrome (or Edge) over the DevTools protocol with Node's built-in WebSocket: the smoke test drives the real app with pointer events and `requestAnimationFrame`, so it needs a visible page at least 880 px wide that nobody resizes, which a browser pane or a background tab is not; headless Chrome reports its page visible, never throttles, and keeps the 1600 × 1000 window it is given, so the count is repeatable (1118 / 1118 on GitHub Actions in four parts). The server must be running; the page URL is the first argument (default `http://localhost:8812/tests/ui-smoke.html`, e.g. `node anvil/headless/ui-smoke.js http://localhost:8815/tests/ui-smoke.html` for a server on 8815); `--port <n>` is Chrome's debugging port (default 9333; use another when two runs share a machine), `--chrome <path>`, `--timeout <s>`, `--keep` (leave Chrome up), `--slowest [n]` (the slowest steps and step groups, and the steps that spend most in `wait()`), `--shards <n>` (n Chromes at once, ports `--port` + 0 … n − 1, each running a consecutive block of the `GROUPS` files through `ui-smoke.html?files=a-b`, the blocks balanced by `headless/smoke-timings.json`; every Chrome loads every group file, the page counts every step it declares, and the driver checks the blocks ran each step exactly once and merges the rows in page order), `--save-timings` (seconds per group file into `smoke-timings.json`), `--json <out>`. The same driver runs `tests/occt-tests.html` (its PASS / FAIL rows; it stops when the page sets `occtTestsDone`).

- **`headless/dxf-check.js`** reads a DXF back the way a laser / CAM import would (`DxfCheck.parse(text)`, `DxfCheck.check(text, { units, layers, tol })`): LINE / ARC entities on the cut layers (`OUTLINE`, `HOLES`) must chain end to end into closed loops (each end meeting exactly one other), polylines must be closed and free of zero-length segments, circles need a radius, no duplicate entities, `$INSUNITS` present (and the part's, given `units`), no `LWPOLYLINE`, `SPLINE` or `ELLIPSE` in an R12 file; an R2000 `SPLINE` (de Boor, its knots and control points must agree) or `ELLIPSE` (ratio in (0, 1]) on a cut layer is a loop of its own when closed (a full ellipse, a closed spline), else its two ends chain with the LINE / ARC entities; it returns the loops, the circles and the extents (arcs and bulges included). (rfAZ) An `INSERT` must name a `BLOCK` of the BLOCKS section that holds something (else an error); its block's geometry is checked where the INSERT puts it (base point, rotation, scale, an X / Y mirror, nested INSERTs), as a CAM import explodes it, so its LINE / ARC ends chain with the rest and its circles count; `check` returns `inserts: [{ layer, block, at, scale, rotation }]`. The kernel tests load it with a `<script>` tag, the runner with `require`. Exit 1 on any failed step, 2 without Chrome or a server.

Rendering (PNG) needs WebGL and stays in the browser; `tests/occt-tests.html` runs in headless Chrome through `headless/ui-smoke.js` (or `verify`).

## The workspace

The layout deliberately mirrors SolidWorks so muscle memory carries over.

- **Menu bar**: File (new, open, save, exports, screenshot, *Properties…*: custom properties), Edit (undo, redo, delete, rebuild), then the quick-access toolbar as in SolidWorks 2024: New, Open, Save, Print | Undo, Redo (greyed when their stack is empty) | Select | Rebuild, File Properties, Options (each runs the menu command of the same name; Print opens the browser's print dialog), View (standard views, display styles, planes, origins, section view, *Sketch Grid*, *Theme ▸ Light / Dark*), Insert (boss, cut, sweep, loft, hole wizard, fillet, chamfer, shell, patterns, mirror, sketch, reference plane, base flange), Tools (script console, measure, mass properties, units, *Sketch Settings ▸ Enable on-screen numeric input on entity creation*, *OpenCascade kernel ▸ Use OpenCascade / Show the quick result first / Wait for the exact solid / Engine file…*), Window, Help.
- **CommandManager ribbon** with Features (boss, cut, revolve, hole wizard, sweep, loft, patterns, mirror, chamfer, fillet, shell, reference plane), Sketch (Smart Dimension, lines, rectangles, circles, arcs, polygon, slots, point, spline, ellipses, text, sketch fillet / chamfer, trim, convert, offset, mirror, sketch patterns, move / copy / rotate / scale / stretch, relations, Fully Define Sketch, levels, DXF in / out, Check / Repair Sketch; see *Sketch tab*), Evaluate (measure, mass properties, section view, face to DXF) and Sheet Metal (base flange, edge flange, miter flange, hem, jog, sketched bend, flatten, flat to DXF) tabs. Related commands share a **flyout button** as in SolidWorks: Fillet / Chamfer, Linear Pattern / Circular Pattern / Mirror, Reference Geometry (Plane; Axis, Coordinate System and Point greyed), and on the Sketch tab Smart Dimension (Horizontal / Vertical Dimension, Horizontal / Vertical Ordinate), Line (Centerline, Midpoint Line, Parallel, Tangent and Perpendicular Line, Bisector), the five rectangles, Circle (Perimeter, Tangent Circle), Arc (Centerpoint, Tangent, 3 Point), the four slots, Ellipse / Partial Ellipse, Sketch Fillet / Chamfer, Trim / Extend / Split / Join Entities, Convert Entities / Intersection Curve, Mirror Entities / Dynamic Mirror, Linear / Circular Sketch Pattern and Move / Copy / Rotate / Scale / Stretch Entities. The button runs the command last picked from its ▾ list (remembered in `localStorage` `anvil.fly.<group>`, `UI.ribbonFlyFace(group, i)`); while one of a group's sketch tools is on, the button shows it. Every ribbon button has a SolidWorks-style tooltip: the name in bold with its shortcut, a one-line description under it, and why a greyed button cannot run (*Open a sketch first*, *Not available in this build yet*); the heads-up view toolbar, Task Pane strip, FeatureManager tabs and the PropertyManager ✓ / ✗ show the same tooltip. In a narrow window the ribbon fits instead of scrolling, as SolidWorks does: the stacked small buttons lose their labels first, then up to four buttons at the right end move into a » menu (with more to move, all large buttons lose their labels and the rest go into the menu); a flyout in the » menu lists its whole group. The CommandManager can be **enlarged by dragging its bottom edge down** (rfIA): buttons, labels, small stacks, the ▾ / » and the tab strip grow together up to 2.5× (the default size is the minimum; double-click the edge for the default), the icons are drawn again at the new whole-pixel size with their lines thickened in proportion (no bitmap scaling), the graphics area gives up the height, the narrow-window fit uses the enlarged sizes, and the size is kept in `localStorage` `anvil.ribbonScale` (`UI.ribbonScale()`, `UI.setRibbonScale(s)`); drawing documents' ribbons follow it. The flyout and » menus keep their size.
- **FeatureManager tree** on the left. Click selects a feature, double-click edits it, F2 renames, Delete removes it with its dependents. Sketches consumed by a feature are hidden unless *View ▸ All Sketches* is on. Drag its right edge to resize it (double-click the edge: default 296 px); the width is kept in `localStorage` (`anvil.fmWidth`) and gives way when the window is too narrow, so the graphics area keeps at least 320 px (or 35 % of the window). Nothing in the workspace scrolls sideways at any window width.
- **PropertyManager** replaces the tree while a command is open; it slides in and puts the cursor in its first value box. Enter accepts (✓), Esc cancels (✗). See *Viewport and PropertyManagers* for its on-model handles, selection lists and spin steps. It is drawn as in SolidWorks 2024: the command's icon and name with a round Help (?) button at the right (a note on the command: `spec.help`, else the current prompt), the green ✓ and red ✗ under it with SolidWorks' pressed Keep Visible pin on a PropertyManager that stays open after ✓ (`spec.keepVisible`; the Mate PM), the yellow Message box, group boxes 8 px apart with a grey title band and the round collapse chevron, pale blue selection boxes with the active one (where graphics-area picks go) pink with a magenta frame. The FeatureManager, Task Pane strip, status bar, menus, dialogs and toasts share its grey chrome and Windows 11 hover / selection blue (`style.css` *rfBG*, colours `--sw-*`, redefined for the dark theme); the tree draws SolidWorks' blue rollback bar under the last feature (above the first rolled-back one during Edit Feature; not draggable) and puts a sketch's (-) / (+) / (?) prefix in its own span (`.ti-pfx.under / .over / .conflict`).
- **Units**: new parts and assemblies are in **inches (IPS)**; *Tools ▸ Default units for new documents* switches the default to millimeters (remembered per browser), and *Tools ▸ Units* changes the open document. Scripts keep their own `.units()` (the bundled examples are metric), saved documents keep theirs, and the headless runner builds a script without `.units()` in mm. An inch document is built at Image Quality 0.002 in (the 0.05 mm default scaled), PropertyManager defaults come in shop fractions (extrude 0.375 in, fillet 0.0625 in) and inch sketch dimensions read as a shop print does (ANSI: three places, no leading zero: `.500`, `6.000`, `Ø.281`, `R.250`). Every typed value takes **shop input** (`UI.parseLength(text, { kind })`, read by `SketchSolver.parseValue`): decimals (`.375`), fractions (`3/8`), mixed numbers (`1-1/2` or `1 1/2`; with spaces round the hyphen, `3 - 1/2` subtracts), unit suffixes converted to the document's units (`25mm` in an inch part is 0.984252; `in`, `"`, `ft`, `'`, `mm`, `cm`, `m`; feet and inches side by side add: `1' 6-1/2"` = 18.5), angles (`45`, `45deg`, `45°`) and arithmetic (`2*3/8`, `1.5+1/16`, parentheses). Text starting with `=` is an equation, evaluated exactly as before; a name that is no unit is tried as an equation variable. It applies to the numeric input while drawing, the Modify box, Instant3D boxes and PropertyManager number fields (the first `/`, letter, space or operator turns the number box into a text box; the hint shows the value read, `= 0.375`; a bad entry shows the reason, Enter is refused and leaving the box puts the old value back).
- **Viewport**: orthographic, with the SolidWorks mouse: middle-drag rotates, Ctrl + middle-drag pans, Shift + middle-drag zooms about the press point, a middle double-click fits the model; the wheel (or a trackpad / pinch) zooms smoothly about the cursor. Left-drag on the model rotates too (Alt + left-drag anywhere), right-drag pans, a left-drag on empty space draws a selection box. The view turns about the model's centre while that centre is on screen. Hovering a face pre-highlights it with a translucent orange tint; clicking selects it (a blue tint; selected edges and vertices are blue too); right-click ▸ *Select Other* reaches the faces behind. A scale bar at the bottom right (a 100 px bar labelled with its length in model units, 2.0038 in say) gives the eye a size reference and follows the zoom; it hides under a drawing document. The triad in the corner shows the current orientation and takes the mouse: hovering an axis lights it up, a click views along it (X = Right, Y = Top, Z = Front), a second click looks from the opposite side, Shift + click turns the view 90° about it (Ctrl + Shift: the other way); `UI.triadClick(axis, { shiftKey, ctrlKey })`. **View Selector** (Space or the heads-up *View Orientation* button): a cube in the middle of the graphics area, turned like the model, next to the orientation menu; a click on a face, an edge strip or a corner orients the view there (standard, edge or isometric view) and zooms to fit; Esc, Space again or a click elsewhere closes it (`UI.showViewSelector()`, `UI.viewSelectorPick("1,1,1")`). Standard views, Normal To, Zoom to Fit and Previous View glide there in 320 ms. Lighting is a camera-mounted studio set (sky / ground ambient, key, fill and rim lights) on a glossy grey with slightly darker faces towards their silhouettes. *View ▸ Background* (also the heads-up *Apply Scene*): *Gradient* (SolidWorks' light blue-grey to white, the default), *Plain* or *Dark*, remembered per browser (`anvil.bg`, `UI.setBackground`); Hidden Lines Removed / Visible show the background through the model.
- **Smart Dimension** (Sketch tab, or `D` in a sketch): click an entity, then click where the dimension should sit, exactly as in SolidWorks — a line gives its length, or its horizontal / vertical distance when you place the dimension above or beside a slanted line; a rectangle edge gives that side; circles give Ø, arcs and polygons R, slots length and width; two entities or points give a distance (horizontal, vertical or aligned by where the cursor is) or, for two lines, an angle (or its supplement, by the cursor's quadrant). A line's midpoint is a dimension end too: rest the cursor at the middle of any line (or straight model edge, or drawing-view edge) until the midpoint marker shows, then click; or right-click the line ▸ Select Midpoint. A small label by the cursor names what the click takes (Midpoint, Endpoint, Center, Line …). The label follows the cursor and stays where it is dropped. The Modify box opens right after placement: type a number (or `=expression`) and press Enter to drive the entity. Placed dimensions are stored on the sketch, drawn with extension lines and arrows, and come back in the generated script. They look as SolidWorks draws them with the ANSI standard (as on drawing sheets, scaled for the screen): Century Gothic, the text horizontal in a break of the dimension line (vertical and aligned ones too), slim arrowheads (outside, with the text beyond them, when the dimension is short), radius and diameter leaders ending in a short shoulder with the text after it; driving dimensions are black, driven ones grey in brackets, and the sketch dimensions Instant3D shows in the part are laid out the same way (blue). Relation icons are small green-bordered squares with the symbol in dark green, crisp at 100 / 125 / 150 / 200 % display scaling. Click a dimension to select it, double-click to change its value, drag to move it; Delete removes a selected one. *View ▸ Sketch Dimensions* (off by default since round 12: SolidWorks shows only the dimensions you add) also shows the driven values of every entity that has no placed dimension. See *Sketching* for the rest of the sketcher.
- **Heads-up View toolbar** centred along the top of the graphics area, in SolidWorks 2024's order on a transparent strip (small icons, each drop-down's ▾ beside its icon, no separators; `UI.HUD_ORDER`, each button's `data-hud`): Zoom to Fit, Zoom to Area, Previous View, Section View, Dynamic Annotation Views (greyed: no annotation views yet), View Orientation ▾ (with the View Selector), Display Style ▾ (Shaded With Edges, Shaded, Hidden Lines Removed, *Hidden Lines Visible*: hidden edges dashed grey, Wireframe), Hide/Show Items ▾, Edit Appearance ▾, Apply Scene ▾ (Gradient, Plain, Dark), View Settings ▾ (Options, Light / Dark theme).
- **Graphics area look (SolidWorks 2024)**: the default scene is a soft light blue at the top fading to white; the part is light grey under a key light from the upper left, a fill from the lower right and sky / ground ambient, all aimed in view space (a face square to the view reads light grey, the top of an isometric view lighter than its sides); shaded-with-edges edges dark grey, the pre-highlight orange, selections blue (faces and edges blue, selected sketch entities and dimensions light blue `#1e9cf0`). Sketch entities are blue under defined, black fully defined, red over defined, construction dashed; dimension text is black with no halo (driven grey); inference lines are dotted, blue for a guide that adds no relation and orange where the click adds one (a direction taken with its relation glyph, such as Tangent); colours are the `--sk-sel`, `--sk-infer`, `--sk-infer-rel` variables on `#viewport` in `style.css`, retuned for the dark theme.
- **Line widths** (`ui-core.js`, "thick lines"): WebGL lines are 1 device pixel on Windows, so every line set whose material has `userData.lineWidth` is drawn by screen-space quads with round joins, in CSS pixels (they follow `devicePixelRatio`, re-read when the window moves to another screen): model edges and silhouettes 1.5, sketch entities 2 (inactive sketches 1.5, selected 2.5), selected / pre-highlighted edges 3, planes and grid 1 (`UI.LINE_W`). The line objects themselves stay in the scene for picking and box selection; `UI.thickLines(false)` draws the plain 1-pixel lines.
- **Task pane** on the right: Home, Design Library (see *Design Library* below), Files (named saves in localStorage), Views, Appearance, Properties (name, units, material, sheet-metal parameters), and the **Script** console. Its left edge resizes it like the FeatureManager (`anvil.tpWidth`, default 340 px); in a window too narrow to dock it, it slides out over the graphics area like SolidWorks' unpinned Task Pane.
- **Status bar** shows the last message, cursor coordinates in sketch mode, and the rebuild time.
- **Rebuild errors and warnings** (as in SolidWorks). The status bar says *Fully rebuilt*, *2 rebuild errors* (red) or *1 rebuild warning* (amber); clicking a red or amber chip opens *What's Wrong*, as does right-clicking the part or a failed feature ▸ *What's Wrong?*. The dialog lists every error and warning (type, feature, description); *Show errors* / *Show warnings* filter it; *Go To* selects the feature in the FeatureManager (opening its folder), *Edit* / *Edit Sketch* opens it. OpenCascade's own problems (a feature it could not build, a volume that differs) are listed as warnings, since Anvil's result is then shown. In the FeatureManager a failed feature keeps its own icon with a small red ✗ badge (amber ⚠ for a warning), its name in red / amber and the message in its tooltip; a feature whose sketch failed and the part itself are badged too. A *Dangling* sketch (the face it was drawn on is gone) gets an olive *Dangling* tag, is drawn in olive in the graphics area, and its tooltip points to *Edit Sketch Plane*. `UI.whatsWrong(id?)`, `UI.rebuildIssues()`.
- **A failed feature never blanks the part** (lane FN). The bodies before a feature that fails are kept exactly as they were and every later feature still builds; a feature built from one that failed (a pattern or mirror of it, a loft or sweep through its sketch) fails with *The parent feature … failed to rebuild*. Refused as SolidWorks refuses them, each with a plain message: zero-thickness results (a pattern of tangent holes or tangent bosses, a sketch whose own outlines touch at a corner; not checked on sheet-metal parts; pieces joined only along an edge or at a point are separate bodies instead, and a pattern or mirror copy joined that way, or landing on empty space, is not created, with a warning), a cut that would remove every body, a revolve of 0°, a loft between two profiles in one plane, a sweep path that doubles back or turns tighter than its profile at a corner. An island inside a hole is extruded / revolved as its own solid, and outlines that share an edge make one solid. A cut that removes nothing is still only a warning (the script's default cut direction is +normal, so many saved parts have forward cuts that miss). `Kernel.nonManifoldCount(csg)`; `Kernel.zeroThicknessCheck.on` switches the check.
- **Theme**: *View ▸ Theme ▸ Dark* switches the whole workspace, viewport included, to a dark scheme; *Light* switches back. The choice is kept in `localStorage` under `anvil.theme` and applied at boot. `UI.setTheme("dark" | "light")` and `UI.theme()` do the same from code; `UI.viewportTheme` reports what the 3D view is currently using.

Keyboard: Ctrl+1 … Ctrl+7 standard views, Ctrl+8 normal-to, F fit, Space the View Orientation menu at the cursor with the View Selector cube (Space again closes them), S the **Shortcut Bar** at the cursor (SolidWorks: a small toolbar of the common commands for the part, the sketch, the assembly or the drawing being edited; a click runs one and closes it, Esc or a click elsewhere closes it, the arrows and Enter pick one, any other key closes it and does its own job; `UI.shortcutBar`), S, S start or exit a sketch (Sketch / Exit Sketch is the bar's first button), L/R/C/A/P/T sketch tools, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y undo and redo, Ctrl+B rebuild, Ctrl+S save, F2 rename (also a second, unhurried click on the name of the selected feature), Esc clears the selection (or only closes an open menu or ribbon flyout, keeping the whole multi-selection). In an assembly, Tab hides the component under the pointer and Shift+Tab shows the hidden one there. *Help ▸ Keyboard shortcuts* lists everything.

### Design Library

The Task Pane's Design Library is laid out as SolidWorks': a toolbar (**Back**, **Up**, a search box that filters every item by name and description; Esc clears it), the folder tree, the path of the open folder and its contents as icon tiles (a folder's sub-folders as folder tiles).

- **Examples**: the sample parts and assemblies (`AnvilExamples` and the built-in ones), unchanged: a click opens one as a new document.
- **Hardware** (`library.js`): one tile per family, its size and length chosen in a small dialog. Hex bolts 1/4-20, 5/16-18, 3/8-16, 1/2-13 × 1/2 to 4 (ASME B18.2.1 hex cap screw, 1/32 point chamfer), hex nuts (ASME B18.2.2), flat washers USS and SAE (ASME B18.22.1 Type A), PEM self-clinching nuts S-440 … S-0420 (shank code 1 / 2), flush-head studs FH-440 … FH-0420 × 1/4 to 1 and thru-threaded standoffs SO-440 … SO-0420. Threads are cosmetic (plain cylinders at the nominal diameter); PEM sizes are the catalogue's nominal values, check the current PEM bulletin before release. In a part, **Open** makes a new inch part; in an assembly, **Insert** puts it in as a component (`{ script }` source).
- **Parts**: curved parts (D-handle and handrail tube swept along lines and big tangent arcs, a helix compression spring, a rolled 16 ga cover, a plate with R1 corners and R1/8 rounds, a revolved domed knob, a three-section lofted flare). Opened or inserted like hardware.
- **Features**: library features cut into a planar face — obround slot, keyhole, D-hole, double-D hole, rectangular cutout with corner radius, inch clearance-hole pattern (ASME B18.2.8 close / normal fit), PEM mounting hole. Select a face and click the item, or click it and pick the face: a PropertyManager takes the size and the location (X, Y from the face sketch's origin; a click on the face puts it there; Angle turns it) with a yellow outline preview, and ✓ adds an ordinary sketch on the face plus a Cut-Extrude Through All (one undo step; editable like any other).
- **Forming Tools**: the kernel's forming tools (`Kernel.FORMING_TOOLS`), each opening its PropertyManager (`UI.cmdFormingTool(id)`); the folder says when none are installed.

Automation: `anvil.library` (= `AnvilLibrary`: `part(family, size, length)` → `{ name, code, volume }`, `allParts()`, `shape(name)`, `featureDefaults(id, scale)`, `featureEntities(id, values, scale)`, `featureArea`, `addFeature(model, plane, id, values)`), `anvil.libraryPart(familyOrShape, size?, length?)` (opens or inserts; resolves to the summary), `anvil.libraryFeature(id, { face, point?, x?, y?, …size })`; `UI.designLibrary.open(folder) / search(text) / state() / reset()`.

## Viewport and PropertyManagers

**Viewport** (`ui-core.js`, `ui-select.js`).

- **View transitions.** Standard views, Normal To, Zoom to Fit, Zoom to Selection and Previous View glide to the new view (320 ms, ease in-out) instead of jumping. Only views a person just asked for glide (a click, key or wheel within the last second): a script, a test or an API call (`anvil.view`, `UI.setView`) lands at once. The animation is visual only — the logical camera, picking and `UI.worldToScreen` are at the new view the moment the call returns — and screen-space overlays (sketch dimensions, Instant3D handles) hide while the view moves. A rotate, pan or zoom during the transition starts from the pose on screen. `UI.viewAnimation = false` (or the system's reduced-motion setting) turns it off; `UI.finishViewAnimation()` lands it at once.
- **Zoom.** Each wheel notch (or trackpad scroll / pinch, in proportion) adds to a zoom that the frame loop eases out over about 50 ms, always about the cursor; one notch is still a factor of 1.12.
- **Render on demand.** The frame loop draws only when something changed — a camera move, a transition or eased zoom in progress, an explicit `UI.render()` / `UI.requestRender()`, or any change in the scene graph (a cheap signature over the scene's objects) — plus a 1 s safety redraw. An idle viewport costs no GPU time (60 → 0 draws/s) and every interactive change is drawn on the next frame. Silhouette edges of curved faces are drawn on the GPU (no per-frame CPU work) and the depth range is fitted to the model.
- **Rebuilds without flicker.** PropertyManager previews are built in the worker (`UI.previewInWorker`; the newest request wins, `UI.previewIdle()` resolves when it is shown): typing into the Funnel's Shell thickness costs 0.1 ms on the main thread instead of 99. The status bar says *Rebuilding…* only after 250 ms. The OpenCascade swap waits for a 160 ms pause and the mouse button up, keeps the selection, and, after an edit of a part it drives, holds the last frame (the previous exact solid, or the preview ✓ was pressed on) with a small *Rebuilding…* cue at the foot of the graphics area for as long as OpenCascade is expected to take (`UI.holdFrame`; the estimate sums the last measured time of each feature from the first changed one, `r.ms.features`, plus tessellation; held at most 1.5 s, never for another part, ended by any camera move or input), so Anvil's faceted part no longer flashes for 0.3–0.8 s before the exact solid (Tray, Spur gear, Hex nut, Handlebar).
- **Edit Feature rolls back.** As in SolidWorks, editing a feature (double-click, *Edit Feature*) shows the part as it was just before that feature, with its later features, sketches and planes hidden, and previews the feature on it in yellow / red; picks (fillet edges, removed shell faces) land on that part. *Edit Sketch* rolls back to just before the sketch the same way (the later features are greyed in the FeatureManager; a build that arrives while the sketch is open waits) and exiting the sketch shows the whole part again. The rolled-back part comes from the worker's preview cache together with the first preview, in one message, so it appears in the same frame (about 20–35 ms after the double-click on the Funnel, Tray and Spur gear). ✗ rolls forward to the part as it was with no rebuild; ✓ keeps the rolled-back part and the preview until the rebuilt part is applied. An OpenCascade solid that arrives meanwhile waits for the roll forward. While OpenCascade drives the part, the rolled-back part is OpenCascade's exact solid too: it is asked for when the edit opens (`occt-worker.js` `upto: n`, built from its rebuild cache's snapshot after feature n, which it leaves untouched) and swapped in (`UI.rollbackSwap`) after the same checks as the whole part, 20–80 ms after the edit opens; it cross-fades over Anvil's rolled-back part (*Show the quick result first*), or the frame is held meanwhile (at most 0.4 s, *Wait for the exact solid*) so Anvil's facets do not show in between. `App.rollback` is the number of features shown (null when not rolled back); `UI.rollbackTo(n)` / `UI.rollForward()`. Without the worker, edit previews overlay the whole part as before.
- **The part never looks broken.** Pinholes where facets meet (T-junctions from the booleans) are filled by drawing the body's triangle edges as 1-pixel lines in the part's own shading; a smoothed normal that is missing or turned away from its facet falls back to the facet's, so no triangle shades black; *Section View* shows the cut faces solid in the part colour with their outline (stencil caps, Shaded styles); a body the mesher or the edge finder cannot read leaves the last good part (or the part without edges) on screen and says so on the status bar. A failed feature is skipped, its row turns red with an error badge, the part row is badged and the status chip opens *What's Wrong* (lane FO, smoke `rfFO`).
- **Pre-highlight and Select Other.** The face or plane under the pointer is pre-highlighted in orange. Right-click on the model ▸ *Select Other* lists every face under the pointer, nearest first, hidden ones included; hovering a row shows its face (through the faces in front), a click selects it (Ctrl / Shift add; an open PropertyManager's selection box takes it). A right-drag pans and shows no menu. `UI.selectOtherAt(x, y)` returns the list.

**PropertyManagers** (`ui-commands.js`, drawn by `ui-select.js`).

- **On-model handles.** While a PropertyManager is open its values have drag handles in the graphics area, each with its value shown: Boss / Cut-Extrude depth D1 and D2, Revolve angle, fillet radius / chamfer distance, Shell thickness, Linear Pattern spacing (both directions), Circular Pattern angle, Plane and Mirror offset. A drag sets the PropertyManager value (the command previews in the worker; nothing is rebuilt or put on the undo stack until ✓). A handle drags freely at display precision and snaps only when the pointer is over its ruler (1-2-5 steps); negative plane offsets get a ruler too. A faded arrow behind a handle flips its direction (the PM's *Reverse* check box); callouts next to other picks show a value and focus its field when clicked. `UI.pmHandles()` lists the handles, `UI.pmSetValue(key, v)` / `UI.pmToggle(key)` / `UI.pmFocusField(key)` drive the fields.
- **Selection lists.** The edge / face boxes of Fillet, Chamfer, Shell, Edge Flange, Hem and Jog, the Mate PM's *Entities to Mate* and the Hole Wizard's positions are lists: hovering a row highlights its item in the graphics area (`UI.pmHoverItem`), Delete / Backspace or right-click ▸ Delete removes it, right-click ▸ *Clear Selections* empties the box. In the Mate PM and the Hole Wizard, clicking a listed face (position) in the graphics area removes it again.
- **Spin steps.** A focused number box steps with ↑ / ↓ and the mouse wheel (an unfocused one leaves the wheel to the panel): 1 mm (0.1 in for inch parts) or the field's own step, ×10 with Shift (`UI.pmSpinStep(field)`). Boxes holding an `=` equation never spin.
- **Instant3D** drags preview only the dragged feature (`UI.featurePreview`); when the drag rests 180 ms on a value the whole part is rebuilt in the background, so patterns, mirrors and other dependents follow before the button is released. One rebuild and one undo step on release.
- The PropertyManager re-renders without losing its scroll position, folded groups or focus; the ribbon, heads-up toolbar and status bar are patched in place, so tooltips do not flicker.

## Coordinate system and sketch planes

World axes are right-handed with +Y up. The three standard sketch planes use the SolidWorks frames, where a sketch point `(u, v)` maps to world space as `origin + u·x + v·y` and extrusions go along `n`:

| Plane | sketch x | sketch y | normal n |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Front | +X | +Y | +Z |
| Top | +X | **−Z** | +Y |
| Right | **−Z** | +Y | +X |

Two consequences worth memorising. On the Top plane a positive sketch *v* is world −Z, so a point you want at world `z = 10` is sketched at `v = −10`. And a plane sitting on top of a part (say `p.sketch("Top", 8)` on an 8 mm plate) has its normal pointing *away* from the part, so a cut into the part needs `reverse: true`. `p.hole(...)` defaults to reversed for exactly this reason, and the Cut-Extrude PropertyManager presets *Reverse direction* for a new cut from `Kernel.cutDirectionHint` (see *Kernel API*): a sketch on the top face of a part opens with the box checked, a sketch on the bottom face with it clear, so the default cut goes into the material either way. Scripts are not affected; `cut(...)` still goes along +n unless `reverse: true`.

A reference plane feature is a standard plane plus an offset along its normal. Sketches can also be placed on a custom frame (`{ custom: true, origin, x, y, n }`), which is what *Sketch on a face* produces via `Kernel.planeFromFace`.

**Sketches on faces follow the face** (as in SolidWorks). A sketch made on a model face, and the positions sketch of a Hole Wizard placed on one, store the frame plus a face reference, `face: { feature, role }` (the feature that made the face). Every rebuild resolves it in both kernels: `role: "cap"` (an extrude's end, or a cut's floor) is computed from that extrude, so it follows depth, direction and upstream edits exactly; `"geom"` (any other planar face) takes the planar face of the body before the sketch with the same outward normal nearest the stored plane. The frame keeps its x / y / n (the sketch's coordinates and orientation stay put) and only moves along n, so thickening a base carries the sketch, its boss and its holes up with the top face; the resolved frame is written back onto the sketch (`build.facePlanes` for worker builds). When the face's feature is deleted or the face cannot be found, the sketch stays at its last position and gets a *Dangling sketch* warning in the FeatureManager. Older models with frozen frames (no `face`) are left as they are. Stored frames are cleaned of float noise (`Kernel.tidyPlane`: 9.999999999999998 → 10). In scripts: `p.sketch({ face: boss, normal: [0, 1, 0] })` (add `role: "geom"` and `offset` or `at` for a face that is not an extrude end); `generateScript` writes the full frame with the face's feature as a variable.

A cap reference also names its cap, `cap: "far"` (where Direction 1 ends: a boss's top, a cut's floor) or `"near"` (the sketch-plane end, or Direction 2's end), so **reversing the extrude keeps the sketch on the cap it was drawn on**: the frame's normal flips and its x with it (the sketch's coordinates stay, read from the other side, as SolidWorks does); a round-10 reference gets its `cap` at the first rebuild. A face of a **pattern or mirror copy** of an extrude is a cap of that LPattern / CirPattern / Mirror feature, `{ feature: pattern, role: "cap", cap, inst, lo, lx, ln }` (`inst` the copy's index among the pattern's own copies, `lo` / `lx` / `ln` the sketch frame carried back to the seed): the sketch moves rigidly with its copy when the seed's depth or direction, the spacing, the angle or the mirror plane change, and is Dangling when the copy is gone. **Edit Sketch Plane** (right-click a sketch in the FeatureManager) rolls the part back to just before the sketch, takes a plane or a planar face made before it and moves the sketch there, its 2D geometry kept, with a new face reference — one undo step; it is also how a Dangling sketch is reattached. **Deleting a feature** lists the sketches drawn on its faces and what is built on them (extrudes, Hole Wizard holes, patterns, sketches on their faces) in the confirmation, with *Also delete these child features* (off by default, as in SolidWorks): checked, they go too; unchecked, they stay where they are, marked Dangling.

## Scripting

Every model is a feature list, and the script API is a builder over that list. *Script from model* in the console (or `Kernel.generateScript`) turns any model back into code, so the two stay interchangeable.

Scripts run with `p` (alias `part`) bound to a `Kernel.Builder`. A short part:

```js
p.name("Flange").units("mm");
p.cylinder(60, 12);                          // disc, axis +Y
p.cylinder(35, 4, { at: [0, 12, 0] });       // raised face
p.sketch("Top", 16).circle(0, 0, 25).cut("through", { reverse: true });
p.hole("Top", 47.5, 0, 11, { offset: 16 });  // one bolt hole …
p.circularPattern({ axis: "y", count: 6 });  // … patterned around Y
```

### Part methods

| Method | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| `name(s)`, `units("mm" \| "in")`, `density(kg_per_m3)`, `color(css)` | Document properties. Chainable. A density that is not the material's unlinks the material (custom density). |
| `material(name \| null)`, `bodyMaterial(featureId, name \| null)`, `property(name, value \| null, { type, config })` | Material (sets density and colour; names or aliases such as `"6061"`), a solid body's own material (the body named by the feature that created it), and a *File ▸ Properties* row. See *Materials and custom properties*. Chainable. |
| `plane(ref, offset, name?) → id` | Reference plane. `ref` is `"Front"`, `"Top"` or `"Right"`. |
| `axis(kind, refs, { name }) → id` | Reference **Axis** (SolidWorks' Axis PM): `kind` `"line"` (one line / edge / axis / cylindrical face), `"planes"` (two planes or planar faces: their intersection), `"points"` (two points / vertices), `"cyl"` (a cylindrical or conical face: its axis), `"pointFace"` (a point and a plane / face: the normal through the point). Pass the id as `axisRef` to `circularPattern` or `revolve` (the axis must lie in the revolve's sketch plane). |
| `point(kind, refs, { distance, percent, reverse, name }) → id` | Reference **Point**: `"arc"` (arc centre of a circular edge / sketch arc), `"face"` (centre of face, area centroid; exact on a planar face, true hole circles and arcs), `"intersect"` (two edges / lines / axes), `"project"` (a point onto a plane / face), `"on"` (a vertex / sketch point / point), `"along"` (`distance` along an edge or sketch line / arc from its start; `percent: true` reads it in %, `reverse` from the other end). |
| `csys({ origin, x, y, z, flipX, flipY, flipZ, name }) → id` | **Coordinate System**: `origin` a point ref (default the part origin); any two of `x` / `y` / `z` (edges, axes, faces, planes) fix the frame (the third follows, right-handed), one fixes that axis; flips reverse an axis. Its XY plane works as a sketch plane (`p.sketch(id, 0)`); Mass Properties can report in it. |

**Reference refs** (`axis`, `point`, `csys`): `{ plane: "Front" | id }`, `{ axis: "x" | "y" | "z" | id }`, `{ point: "origin" | id }`, `{ face: [x, y, z] }`, `{ edge: [x, y, z] }`, `{ vertex: [x, y, z] }` (picked points on the body before the feature, re-resolved at every rebuild, so they follow the model), `{ sketch: id, entity: i, end?: "p1" | "p2" | "c", seg?: k, pt?: k }` (a sketch line, point, line end, arc centre, or the k-th side / corner of a rectangle, polygon or polyline). Shorthands: `"Front"`, `"x"`, `"origin"`, `[x, y, z]` (a vertex). The resolved geometry is in `build.planes[id]`: `{ refgeo: "axis", o, d, a, b }`, `{ refgeo: "point", p }`, `{ refgeo: "csys", origin, x, y, z }`. OpenCascade skips the three (no body); a pattern or revolve about a reference axis is built by Anvil's kernel. |
| `sketch(spec, offset?, name?) → SketchBuilder` | `spec` is a plane name, a plane feature id, `{ ref, offset }`, or a custom frame. |
| `extrude(depth, opts)` | `opts`: `cut`, `reverse`, `midplane`, `throughAll`, `depth2` (second direction), `both` (mirror the depth), `sketch` (id, defaults to the last sketch), `draft` (angle in degrees: the section shrinks by depth·tan(angle) away from the sketch plane, in both directions for mid-plane; holes widen), `draftOutward` (grow instead), `normalCut` (sheet-metal parts only: the cut is made square to the sheet, see *Normal cut* below), `linkT` (cuts on sheet-metal parts: *Link to thickness*, a blind cut whose depth is the sheet thickness and follows it), `merge` (`false` keeps a boss as a separate solid body), `bodies` (`[i, …]`: the bodies a cut acts on; see *Multi-body parts*), `contours` (`[[u, v], …]`, SolidWorks' *Selected Contours*: one point inside each region of the sketch to use, sketch-plane coordinates; the profile is the union of those regions, see *Selected Contours* below), `name`. |
| `cut(depth \| "through", opts)` | `extrude` with `cut: true`. **Direction (rfFS, as SolidWorks):** a cut given no `reverse` / `dir` (one direction, blind or Through All) goes into the material: the rebuild tries +n (the sketch normal) and, when that removes nothing and −n would, cuts along −n; the direction it took is written onto the feature (`reverse: true` / `false`), so the feature JSON, `generateScript` and the Cut-Extrude PropertyManager show it from then on. `reverse: false` (or `dir: 1`) forces +n, `reverse: true` −n; mid-plane and two-direction cuts are left alone. A cut that removes no material (both directions tried when it had none) is a rebuild error ("The cut does not intersect the model …") and the part before it is kept. `box(…, { cut: true })` and `cylinder(…, { cut: true })` stay where `at` puts them (+n). `p.cut("through", { reverse: true, normalCut: true })` is the usual sheet-metal window; `p.cut("through", { bodies: [1] })` cuts body 1 only. |

**Selected Contours.** A sketch with several regions (a rectangle split by a line, a circle inside a rectangle, two overlapping circles) can drive an extrude, cut or revolve from only some of them, as SolidWorks' Selected Contours: `p.extrude(0.5, { contours: [[0.5, -1]] })` takes the region holding (0.5, −1). `Kernel.sketchRegions(sketch)` lists the regions (the faces of the sketch's planar arrangement: entities split where they cross or where an end lands on another entity; dangling pieces bound nothing) as `{ index, loops (outer first, then holes), area, p (a point inside) }`, `Kernel.regionAt(sketch, [u, v])` finds the one holding a point. A pick that no longer falls inside any region (the sketch was edited) errors the feature instead of extruding the whole sketch. Chords are split at the true circle crossings, so volumes stay exact. In the Boss-Extrude / Cut-Extrude / Revolve PropertyManager the *Selected Contours* box (bottom) starts active when the sketch is ambiguous for a plain extrude (`Kernel.regionsAmbiguous`); hovering lights the region under the pointer, a click toggles it (rows `Sketch1-Region<2>`), Delete on a row removes it.
| `revolve(angleDeg, opts)` | `opts.axis`: `"y"` (default), `"x"`, the index of a sketch line, or `{ x1, y1, x2, y2 }`; plus `reverse`, `cut`, `sketch`, `merge`, `bodies` (cuts), `name`. |
| `revolveCut(angleDeg, opts)` | `revolve` with `cut: true`. |
| `linearPattern(opts)` | `feature` (defaults to the last solid feature), `dir` `"x"|"y"|"z"`, `reverse`, `spacing`, `count`, and optionally `dir2`, `reverse2`, `spacing2`, `count2`; `merge: false` keeps the copies as a new body. Patterns of cuts stay cuts. |
| `circularPattern(opts)` | `feature`, `axis`, `center` (`[x, y, z]`), `count`, `angle` (default 360), `equal`, `merge`, `axisRef` (a reference Axis id: turn about it instead of `axis` / `center`). |
| `mirror(plane, opts)` | Mirror the last (or `opts.feature`) feature across a standard plane, or the whole part with `body: true`. `offset` shifts the plane; `merge: false` keeps the mirrored copy as its own body. A Mirror (or LPattern / CirPattern) of a Fillet or Chamfer applies that round / chamfer again to the mirrored edges, as SolidWorks does; mirrored edges that are not on the part are refused in a sentence. |
| `chamfer(d, edges, opts)` | Break the edges nearest to the given points `[[x, y, z], …]` by distance `d`. Convex edges are cut, concave edges are filled; chained neighbours join with mitred caps. `opts.chain: false` stops tangent-chain propagation (default on: edges sharing an endpoint within 28° and of the same convexity are picked up, so one point selects a whole cylinder rim). |
| `fillet(r, edges, opts)` | Same selection as `chamfer`, with a tangent arc of radius `r` (tessellated by the chord tolerance, ≥ 4 segments; between two planar faces the round is an exact cylinder, see *True curves*). On a circular edge where a face square to the axis meets a cylinder or cone (a rim, a hole edge, a boss root, a drafted boss) the round is a torus patch and a chamfer a cone patch, as SolidWorks makes them: exact volume, smooth shading, TOROIDAL / CONICAL_SURFACE in STEP; a chained pick takes the whole circle. Three perpendicular convex edges filleted at one corner get a spherical corner (`sph`, SPHERICAL_SURFACE in STEP). A round larger than a corner round its chain runs round goes straight on past it, the two straight rounds meeting at the corner; a chamfer stops there and its plane runs on through the corner round (a whole circle too small for the round stays refused). Where a plane, cylinder or cone meets a revolved torus / sphere on the same axis (a dish rim, a dome on a shaft) the round is a torus and a chamfer a cone (its distance on the curved face is the chord, as OpenCascade measures it). A round whose setback crosses a hole or pocket in its face is built, trimmed by the hole, and one that runs under a boss standing on its face leaves the boss whole. As SolidWorks: a round on a circular edge with no plane left under its ball rests on the hole's rim, the plate's edge or the boss's root; on a wall shorter than the round it rests on the wall's far rim; on a rim narrower than its setback it cuts through the wall. Where two curved faces meet in a curve (a pipe tee's saddle, cylinders, cones, spheres, tori) the whole edge gets a ball swept along the true curve, and a chamfer the straight line between that ball's contacts. A radius that runs a straight edge off its face or breaks through a thin wall is refused with SolidWorks' message (`Kernel.fitProbe.where` says which: `off`, `curved`, `face`). |
| `sweep({ profile, path, cut, merge, bodies, name })` | Sweep a closed sketch (`profile`, defaults to the last sketch) along `path`: either the id of a sketch of connected lines/arcs (open by design, no warning) or an array of 3D points `[[x, y, z], …]`. The profile is turned perpendicular to the first segment (pivoting about the path start, which is the end nearest the profile); corners are mitred, so the volume is exactly area × path length for a centred section. Disconnected path entities are ignored with a warning. A sketch path of lines and arcs joined tangentially (not closed) is swept exactly: each line a prism and each arc a revolve of the section, so the ends lie in the path's end planes and the volume is area × true path length (lane FH2, as SolidWorks). |
| `loft(sketchIds, { cut, merge, bodies, name })` | Loft through two or more sketches, each holding exactly one closed profile, in the given order; profiles out of order along the loft are refused (SolidWorks: self-intersecting geometry). **Three or more sections** follow SolidWorks (lane lofts, measured on S29, G18, L1 in SolidWorks 2022): a natural cubic spline through corresponding points of the sections, on both engines (OpenCascade: bicubic faces, `Kernel._.loftPatches`). The outlines share one list of pieces, cut at the vertices of the section with the most; a section with fewer vertices takes each corner to the nearest vertex and splits its sides in the same proportions, a circle is cut at the directions of those vertices. Straight sides run uniformly, arcs as one equal-ripple cubic per 90°; the spline's knots are chord lengths from the points at u = 0 and 0.5 of each piece. Volumes: S29 7.672962 (SolidWorks 7.672999), G18 12.474774 (12.475227), L1 23.828663 (23.828711); every measured section within 5e-4. Rows between the sections sit 2/3 of a chord's sag off the surface so that the facets average onto it. **Two sections** are ruled: when both are convex and the sketch planes are parallel, the side is the convex hull of consecutive sections (each edge of one section joined to the other's farthest vertex along its outward normal, quads where edges are parallel), so every side face is planar and each section keeps its own polygon; otherwise outlines are resampled to the same point count and matched by rotation, so a square can loft into a star. Two sections, one a polygon and the other a full circle, loft as SolidWorks does (lane FH2, measured in SolidWorks 2022): straight rulings from each side to the arc between its corners' directions, the arc parametrized as SolidWorks' rational quadratic (a 2 × 2 square to a r 1 circle 2 above: 7.15035 vs SolidWorks 7.15071; the convex hull was 3.8 % bigger). |
| `boundary(dir1, { dir2, cut, tangency1, tangency2, close, mergeTangent, thin, merge, bodies, name })` | Boundary Boss/Base (`boundaryCut(dir1, opts)` or `cut: true`: Boundary Cut; also `boundary({ dir1, … })`). `dir1`: two or more profile sketches (one closed profile each), in order. `dir2`: curves that must touch every profile — a sketch id (each connected open chain in it is a curve), `{ sketch, entity }` (the chain through that entity) or `{ pts: [[x, y, z], …] }` (a 3D polyline). Each patch between two profiles and two neighbouring curves is a Coons patch (bilinear blend of its four sides): straight sides give planar faces and exact volumes. Without curves it is the Loft. `close: true` (3+ profiles, no curves) joins the last profile back to the first; `thin: { t, mid, reverse }` makes walls of thickness t parallel to the sides, open at the end profiles. `tangency1/2: { start, end }` (`'none'`, `'direction'`, `'normal'`) is stored but not applied yet (a warning says so). OpenCascade: ThruSections without curves, Coons BSpline faces with them; Close and a mid-plane Thin Feature leave Anvil's solid driving. |
| `shell(t, faces, opts)` | Hollow the body leaving walls of thickness `t`. `faces` is a list of points `[[x, y, z], …]` on the faces to remove (open); an empty list gives a closed hollow part. Faces are re-resolved against the body at rebuild time. On a multi-body part the body nearest the first point is shelled (body 0 with no faces). Fails with a clear message when the part is thinner than 2·t everywhere, and (as SolidWorks) when `t` is larger than the radius of a convex round or boss face it would offset. Walls behind cylinders, cones, spheres and tori are the exact offset surfaces (Mass Properties exact). |
| `draft(angle, neutral, faces, opts)` | **Draft** (SolidWorks' Neutral Plane draft, Features ▸ *Draft*). `neutral` is a point `[x, y, z]` on a planar face, `"Front"` / `"Top"` / `"Right"`, a plane id, or a ref (`{ face }`, `{ plane, offset }`); `faces` are points on the faces to draft. Each face turns by `angle`° about its line on the neutral plane so the part tapers away from it: the pull is the neutral's normal, pointing to the side the faces lie on (into the part from a neutral face); `opts.reverse` flips it, `opts.propagate: "tangent"` adds tangent faces. Planar faces are exact on both kernels; a cylinder along the pull becomes an exact cone on both (Anvil's own kernel drafts no other curved face). Faces parallel to the neutral plane are refused, and so is a face tangent to a round that is not drafted with it (as SolidWorks: use `propagate: "tangent"`). `{ name }`. |
| `rib(t, { sketch, side, direction, flip, extension, name })` | **Rib** as SolidWorks makes it: the sketch's open lines (or a chain) thickened by `t` and grown until they meet the part. `direction: "parallel"` (default, Parallel to Sketch) grows in the sketch plane toward the material, the thickness across the plane; `"normal"` grows along the sketch normal, the thickness across the line. `side`: `"both"` (default), `"one"` (First Side: +normal for parallel, the line's left for normal), `"other"`. `flip` grows away from the side the part was found on. Free ends extend linearly to the part; a Normal to Sketch rib whose extension reaches no wall is refused (*The rib is not bounded properly*, as SolidWorks). Exact on planar parts (an L-bracket gusset adds triangle area × t), built by both kernels; `Kernel.ribLayout` gives the pieces. UI: Features ▸ Rib (`UI.cmdRib`). |
