| `intersect({ bodies, planes, regions, exclude, merge, mode, name })` | **Intersect**: the selected solids (`bodies`, default all) and `planes` (`"Front"` or `p.plane(...)` ids) split into regions: every in / out pattern of the bodies (A only, B only, A∩B, …), split again by each plane, each lump separate, in that order (`Kernel.intersectRegions(bodies, f, model, planes)` lists them with `volume` / `centroid`). `regions: [keep flags]` (or `exclude: [indices]`) leaves regions out; `merge: false` keeps every region its own body; `mode: "internal"` (Create internal regions) adds the closed voids between the bodies as regions. The kept regions replace the selected bodies; with `merge` kept regions that would touch only along an edge or at a point stay separate bodies, and a region that touches itself so is refused. UI: Features ▸ Intersect (`UI.cmdIntersect`). |
| `deleteBody(points, { keep, name })` | **Delete/Keep Body** (tree name Body-Delete/Keep1): `points` is a point on or inside each body (`[x, y, z]` or a list of them; a bare number is a body index, `indices: true` when you pass exactly three). The listed bodies are deleted, or with `keep: true` kept and the rest deleted. Each point is resolved again on every rebuild (`Kernel.resolveBodyRefs`: the body that holds the point, else the nearest surface within the pick tolerance), so it follows its body when earlier features change; a point that finds no body is a rebuild warning. Deleting every body leaves an empty part. UI: select a body (double-click it, or its Solid Bodies row) and press Delete, or Insert ▸ Features ▸ Delete/Keep Body (`UI.cmdDeleteBody`). |
| `sheet(T, R?, K?, opts?)` | Sheet-metal parameters: thickness, inside bend radius (default `T`) and K-factor (default 0.5). `opts`: `bendAllowanceType` (`"kfactor"` default, `"allowance"`, `"deduction"`, `"table"`), `bendAllowance`, `bendDeduction`, `bendTable`, `gaugeTable` + `gauge` (T, R, K left `null` come from the gauge row), `relief` (`"rect"` \| `"obround"`: the sheet's *Auto Relief*, the relief of every flange without its own) + `reliefRatio` (default 0.5: the relief is ratio·T wide and runs ratio·T past the bend). See *Bend allowance and bend deduction* and *Bend reliefs*. |
| `baseFlange(T?, opts)` | Extrude the last sketch by the sheet thickness and mark it as the base flange. Sets `sheet(T)` if not yet set; after `sheet(...)` a given `T` changes only the thickness (radius, K and method stay). |
| `flange(L, edges, opts)` | Edge flange on straight edges of the flat sheet face: a bend of the sheet radius plus a straight wall of length `L` measured after the bend, placed beyond the edge ("bend outside"). `opts`: `angle` (default 90), `reverse` (fold the other way), `radius` (override), `position` (`"bendOutside"` | `"outside"` | `"inside"` | `"virtualSharp"`), `lengthRef` (`"outer"` | `"inner"` | `"tangent"` | `"wall"`: where `L` is measured from, see *Flange length reference*; absent = `"wall"`), `miter` (close the corners of flanges of this feature that share one: see *Mitred corners*; rfJU: a placement that moves the walls in off the edges, Material Inside / Outside, Bend from Virtual Sharp, Tangent to Bend, mitres its shared corners without it, as SolidWorks always does; only Bend Outside unmitred keeps an open corner), `miterGap` (the gap at those corners; none = SolidWorks' default, 0.01 in / 0.25 mm), `relief` (`"none"` | `"rect"` | `"obround"`: cut a bend relief slot where a flange end runs into more sheet; absent = the sheet's Auto Relief, none on a sheet without one), `reliefWidth` (slot width along the edge, default T), `reliefDepth` (how far the slot reaches inside the edge, default T/2), `name`. |
| `miterFlange(edges, opts)` | **Miter Flange** as SolidWorks makes it (also `p.sketch(...).line(...).miterFlange(edges, {...})`): the flange section comes from a profile sketch (`opts.sketch`, default the last sketch): an open chain of lines, with tangent arcs between them setting that bend's radius, on a plane normal to an edge of the sheet and starting on it (the Right Plane at a corner of a Top sketch, or the plane Sheet Metal ▸ Miter Flange opens on a selected edge). The first line is the flange length to the inner virtual sharp (at 90° the height above the face the edge lies on); each further line adds a bend, its length between the lines' virtual sharps on the profile's side of the metal. Runs along `edges` (`[x, y, z]` points; the one the profile starts on is added when missing), mitred where two meet. `opts`: `position` (`"inside"` Material Inside, the default \| `"outside"` \| `"bendOutside"`), `radius`, `miterGap`, `startOffset` / `endOffset` (the chain's free ends move in; start = the free end of the profile's edge), `relief`, `bendAllowance`, `name` (default `Miter Flange1`). Feature JSON: a `flange` with `miterFlange: true`, `sketch`; an older edge-only `flange` with `miter: true` still builds and edits as before. |
| `hem(L, edges, { gap, reverse, name, hemType, hemPosition, hemRadius, hemAngle })` | A 180° flange folded back over the sheet: bend radius = `gap` / 2 (default T/2, an open hem), length `L` overall from the fold's outer face to the free end (SolidWorks; the straight return is L − (R + T)). Same edge picking and placement as `flange`; unfolds with BA = π·(R + K·T). `hemType`: `"open"` (the default), `"closed"` (flat on the sheet, 0.1 mm between the leaves as SolidWorks), `"teardrop"` / `"rolled"` (a curl of `hemRadius`, default the sheet radius, swept `hemAngle`, default 270°; `L` is not used). `hemPosition`: `"outside"` (Bend Outside, the default) or `"inside"` (Material Inside: the hem stays within the flange's height). See *Hems*. |
| `flange(L, edges, opts)` | Edge flange on straight edges of the flat sheet face: a bend of the sheet radius plus a straight wall of length `L` measured after the bend, placed beyond the edge ("bend outside"). `opts`: `angle` (default 90), `reverse` (fold the other way), `radius` (override), `position` (`"bendOutside"` | `"outside"` | `"inside"` | `"virtualSharp"`), `lengthRef` (`"outer"` | `"inner"` | `"tangent"` | `"wall"`: where `L` is measured from, see *Flange length reference*; absent = `"wall"`), `miter` (join flanges of this feature that share a corner at the corner's bisector, 45° for a square corner), `miterGap` (opening between mitred walls, split evenly, default 0), `relief` (`"none"` | `"rect"` | `"obround"`: cut a bend relief slot where a flange end runs into more sheet; absent = the sheet's Auto Relief, none on a sheet without one), `reliefWidth` (slot width along the edge, default T), `reliefDepth` (how far the slot reaches inside the edge, default T/2), `name`. |
| `miterFlange(edges, opts)` | **Miter Flange** as SolidWorks makes it (also `p.sketch(...).line(...).miterFlange(edges, {...})`): the flange section comes from a profile sketch (`opts.sketch`, default the last sketch): an open chain of lines, with tangent arcs between them setting that bend's radius, on a plane normal to an edge of the sheet and starting on it (the Right Plane at a corner of a Top sketch, or the plane Sheet Metal ▸ Miter Flange opens on a selected edge). The first line is the flange length to the inner virtual sharp (at 90° the height above the face the edge lies on); each further line adds a bend, its length between the lines' virtual sharps on the profile's side of the metal. Runs along `edges` (`[x, y, z]` points; the one the profile starts on is added when missing). Where two meet, a straight 90° profile leaves SolidWorks' corner: both walls stop square T + gap/√2 from the corner, the blank has a square notch (an L profile keeps a 45° mitre: not measured). `opts`: `position` (`"inside"` Material Inside, the default \| `"outside"` \| `"bendOutside"`), `radius`, `miterGap` (default .01 in / .25 mm; 0 at a corner is refused, as SolidWorks), `startOffset` / `endOffset` (the chain's free ends move in; start = the free end of the profile's edge), `relief`, `bendAllowance`, `name` (default `Miter Flange1`). Feature JSON: a `flange` with `miterFlange: true`, `sketch`; an older edge-only `flange` with `miter: true` still builds and edits as before. |
| `hem(L, edges, { gap, reverse, name, hemType, hemPosition, hemRadius, hemAngle })` | A 180° flange folded back over the sheet: bend radius = `gap` / 2 (default T/2, an open hem), length `L` overall from the fold's outer face to the free end (SolidWorks; the straight return is L − (R + T)). Same edge picking and placement as `flange`; unfolds with BA = π·(R + K·T). `hemType`: `"open"` (the default), `"closed"` (flat on the sheet, 0.1 mm between the leaves as SolidWorks), `"teardrop"` / `"rolled"` (a curl of `hemRadius`, default the sheet radius, swept `hemAngle`, default 200° tear drop / 270° rolled; `L` is not used). `hemPosition`: `"outside"` (Bend Outside, the default) or `"inside"` (Material Inside: the hem stays within the flange's height). See *Hems*. |
| `jog(height, length, edges, { angle, reverse, radius, position, name })` | Two opposite bends in one feature: the sheet steps by `height` (measured face to face on the fold side) and continues parallel to the base for `length`. The wall between the bends is solved from the height, radius and angle. Unfolds as UP then DOWN. |
| `jog(offset, { sketch, fixed, offsetRef, angle, position, reverse, radius, bendAllowance, fixLength, name })` | **Jog** as SolidWorks makes it (also `p.sketch(...).line(...).jog(0.5, {...})`): a sketch with exactly one line on a flat face of the sheet; the side away from `fixed` (`[x, y, z]`; default the larger piece) is offset by two opposite bends of `angle` (90). `offsetRef`: `"outside"` (default, Outside Offset: a face of the sheet to the same face of the jogged flange), `"inside"` (Inside Offset: the faces that look at each other across the riser) or `"overall"` (Overall Dimension: far face to far face). `position` places the first bend against the line as Sketched Bend does (`"centerline"` default, `"inside"`, `"outside"`, `"bendOutside"`). The flat keeps the plate (each bend rolls up BA); an offset too small for the bends is an error that gives the smallest (`Kernel.jogMinOffset(A, R, T, ref)`). Feature JSON `fixLength: true` (the PropertyManager's *Fix projected length*; script `fixLength: true`) keeps the jogged side's far end where it was: the sheet is lengthened at the line by the projection the jog takes (2·BA + g − (2R + T) for two 90° bends), so the flat is that much longer; OpenCascade: unsupported (Anvil's solid stays). Jog Offset end conditions (SolidWorks 2024): `offsetEnd: "upToVertex"` (`offsetTarget: [x, y, z]`), `"upToSurface"` (`offsetTarget`: a point on a face parallel to the sheet, or a plane: `"Top"` / a reference plane's id), `"offsetFromSurface"` (that surface, then `offsetDistance` further along the jog, back toward the sheet with `offsetFlip: true`); pass `null` as the offset. The target takes the dimension's end: with Inside Offset the jogged flange's near face lies on it, with Outside Offset / Overall Dimension its far face; heights are measured from the sheet's face on the jog side, so a target on the other side is an error (use `reverse`). Both engines. |
| `loftedBend([sketchA, sketchB], { method, faceting, facetValue, name })` | **Lofted-Bend** (SolidWorks; Sheet Metal ▸ Lofted-Bend): sheet metal lofted between two open profiles (lines and arcs, one chain per sketch, a small gap where the seam goes), e.g. square to round. Needs `p.sheet(T, …)` first; the profiles are the sheet's mid-surface (T / 2 either side). The chains are joined by the triangulation of least total diagonal length (corners fan to their arc). `method: "bent"` (default): flat facets, the flat pattern has a bend line (R 0, UP / DOWN, the angle between the facets) on every diagonal between facets that are not coplanar; `"formed"`: fine facets (chords within 2e-4·r), no bend lines. `faceting` (Bent): `"count"` (`facetValue` chords per arc, default 4), `"length"` (chords at most `facetValue` long), `"chord"` (chords at most `facetValue` from the arc). The flat is the strip developed facet by facet (every edge its true length, area = the mid-surface's exactly); DXF / DWG / drawings read it like any flat; holes cut in a facet after it are laid in the flat by that facet's rigid map (true shape; a cut across a bend line or the blank's edge is not laid yet). OpenCascade: unsupported (Anvil's solid stays). |
| `sweptFlange(profile, path, { flip, radius, flatten, bendAllowance, name })` | **Swept Flange** (SolidWorks; Sheet Metal ▸ Swept Flange): an open profile sketch swept along a tangent-continuous path of lines and arcs. `path` is a sketch, or points on edges of the part (`[[x, y, z], …]`, chained like Edge Flange picks). The profile is sketched on a plane normal to the path through its start; as an open-profile Base Flange, the sketch line is one face of the sheet, T on its right as drawn (`flip: true` = Reverse direction), every sharp corner gets the bend radius (`radius`, default the sheet's), arcs between lines are bends. Needs `p.sheet(T, …)` first. Flat (`flatten: "profile"`, default): a straight path gives the profile developed (flats + BA per bend, the sheet's or the custom `bendAllowance`) × the path length, bend lines mid-bend; a ring (a one-line profile in the path's plane) lays flat as it is. `flatten: "path"` (Flatten along path): a one-line profile along an arc path is developed exactly (cylinder, cone, ring) on the neutral surface K·T from the concave face. A Swept Flange that starts the part on a straight path sketch is built as the open-profile Base Flange it is, so cuts, holes across its bends, Normal Cuts and Edge Flanges after it are in the flat exactly as on a Base Flange; along an arc path, cuts and holes after it are mapped into the development (Edge Flanges on curved edges are refused: linear edges only). A profile with bends along an arc path is not developable (refused), as are the flats of a Swept Flange on a Base Flange, along model edges, or two in one part (not yet). Both engines (OpenCascade: prisms along lines, revolves about arcs). |
| `sketchedBend(angle, opts)` | **Sketched Bend** (SolidWorks): bend the sheet along every straight line of a sketch drawn on one of its flat faces (`opts.sketch`, default the last sketch; `p.sketch(...).line(...).sketchedBend(90, {...})` works too). Each line acts along its whole extension across the face, so it need not reach the edges. The flat is kept: each bend rolls up a strip BA wide (the sheet's method or `bendAllowance`), so the part unfolds back to the plate. `opts`: `fixed` (`[x, y, z]` on the side that stays put; default the largest piece between the lines), `position` (`"centerline"` default: the strip centred on the line, so the flat bend line is the sketch line; `"bendOutside"`: the bend starts at the line on the moving side; `"outside"` / `"inside"`: Material Outside / Inside, the strip set back R / R + T toward the fixed side so the wall's inner / outer face lands on the line, as Edge Flange), `reverse` (fold away from the sketch plane's normal; default toward it), `radius`, `bendAllowance`, `name` (default `SketchBend1`). Several lines bend in one feature: a line on another's moving side rides on it (channels, Z bends); lines must not cross on the sheet or overlap each other's bends. Earlier flanges on a bent side move with it, also in the flat. |
| `closedCorner(faces, { cornerType, gap, ratio, coplanar, closeBend, name })` | **Closed Corner** (SolidWorks): extend the end faces of a flange (`faces`: points on them) across a box-corner gap to the next flange (found automatically). `cornerType` `"butt"` (default) \| `"overlap"` \| `"underlap"`, `gap` (default 0.01 in / 0.25 mm, at least 0.001 in), `ratio` (overlap / underlap, 0–1, default 1), `coplanar` (default true: end faces in the same plane too). `name` default `Closed Corner1`. See *Closed Corner*. |
| `flatPattern({ fixedFace, grain, mergeFaces, simplifyBends, showSlit, cornerTreatment })` | **Flat-Pattern1 options** (SolidWorks' Flat-Pattern PropertyManager): `fixedFace: [x, y, z]` (a point on a flat face of the folded part: the flat is laid in its plane with that face where it is) and `grain: { at: [x, y, z], dir: [x, y, z] }` (a straight edge on a flat face: the flat turns so it runs along X), saved as `model.flatFixed` / `model.flatGrain`; a key left out clears it. `generateScript` writes it after the features. The flat DXF, Flatten, the drawing's flat view and bend table follow (one rigid motion, never mirrored). The check boxes (defaults `true`, `true`, `false`, `true`, as in SolidWorks) go to `model.flatOptions`, only those that differ from the default: `mergeFaces: false` adds `flat.bendRegions` and `flat.regionLines` (tangent lines; not in the DXF), `simplifyBends: false` draws mitre-plane strip ends (mitred hems) as developed curves, `showSlit: true` cuts a rip-gap-wide, R + T long slit where two bend regions meet at a corner, `cornerTreatment: false` leaves the raw corner of a SolidWorks mitre under 90°. |
| `unfold(fixedFace, bends, { name })` / `fold(bends, { name })` | **Unfold / Fold** (SolidWorks): unfold bends about a fixed face so later features (Cut-Extrude, Hole Wizard, sketches on the flat face) work on the flat, then fold them back with the cuts wrapped round the bends. `fixedFace`: a point on a flat face of the sheet that stays put (`null`: the flat pattern's base face); `bends`: points on bends of the folded part (`null` / `[]`: every bend, *Collect All Bends*). `fold(null)` folds every bend its Unfold unfolded; a list folds those and leaves the rest flat. Names `Unfold1` / `Fold1`. See *Unfold / Fold*. |
| `rip(edges, gap, { direction, name })` | **Rip** (SolidWorks): a slot `gap` wide (default 0.004 in / 0.1 mm) through the thickness along corner edges where two walls meet, so they separate. `edges`: points on the corner's outside or inside edges, or `{ at, direction }`; `direction` `"both"` (default: on the corner's bisector) \| `"side1"` \| `"side2"` (which wall keeps the corner). `name` default `Rip1`. See *Rip and Tab*. |
| `vent({ sketch, boundary, ribs, spars, fill, ribWidth, sparWidth, fillWidth, radius, name })` | **Vent** (SolidWorks Sheet Metal ▸ Vent): cuts the sketch's closed `boundary` through the sheet except the `ribs` (strips `ribWidth` wide centred on lines / arcs), the `spars` (strips `sparWidth` wide centred on circles, closed contours, lines or arcs) and the `fill` contour (solid inside, grown by `fillWidth` / 2). The groups are entity indices of the sketch (default: the last sketch); no `boundary` = the largest closed entity, lines as ribs, the other entities as spars. Also `sketch(...).….end().vent(opts)`. `radius` rounds every corner of the openings with true arcs (DT2; a radius that does not fit is an error). `name` default `Vent1`. |
| `wrap({ face, sketch, mode, thickness, reverse, name })` | **Wrap** (SolidWorks Insert ▸ Features ▸ Wrap, Analytical): wraps the closed contours of the sketch (default: the last sketch; Sketch Text outlines and their counters too) onto the cylindrical, conical or planar face under the point `face: [x, y, z]`, keeping their length along the surface. `mode`: `'emboss'` (default, adds `thickness` normal to the face), `'deboss'` (removes it) or `'scribe'` (splits the face along the wrapped contours: no volume change, the face inside is its own face, owned by the Wrap, with real edges). Cylinder: the sketch plane is parallel to the axis (sketch on the plane tangent to it); cone: the sketch plane is tangent to the cone (it is unrolled about its apex); plane: the sketch is projected onto the face. `reverse` turns the normal round. Exact volumes for straight contours on cylinders and planes on both kernels. Feature JSON `{ type: 'wrap', sketch, face: [x, y, z], mode, thickness?, reverse? }`. Also `sketch(...).….end().wrap(opts)`. `name` default `Wrap1`. |
| `insertBends(fixedFace, { radius, kFactor, thickness, autoRelief, name })` | **Insert Bends** (SolidWorks): a part of constant thickness (a shell, a thin extrude) becomes sheet metal. Every sharp corner between two walls of that thickness becomes a bend of inside radius `radius` (the outer faces stay); a bend already rounded in the part (concentric R and R + T faces between two walls T thick: an imported STEP of a bent part, a filleted thin part) is taken as it is, with its own radius; two bends meeting at a square corner each stop R + T short and the (R + T) corner cube is cut out (`autoRelief: "none"` refuses that). `fixedFace`: a point on a flat face (the flat is laid from it). Sets the sheet (T measured at the fixed face unless given; R, K or `bendAllowanceType` / `bendDeduction` / `bendTable` as `sheet()`) when the part has none. Name `Process-Bends1`. See *Insert Bends and Convert to Sheet Metal*. |
| `convertSheetMetal(fixedFace, bendEdges, { thickness, radius, kFactor, ripEdges, gap, autoRelief, name })` | **Convert to Sheet Metal** (SolidWorks) on a solid: the fixed face and the faces reached across `bendEdges` (points on the solid's edges) are kept, the rest removed by a shell of `thickness` inward, the edges between kept faces ripped (`ripEdges`, default found; `gap` default 0.004 in / 0.1 mm), then Insert Bends on the bend edges. A part already `thickness` thick at the fixed face (a thin channel, a shelled box) is not shelled again. Name `Convert-Solid1`. `Kernel.convertSMCollect(body, fixedFace, { thickness, units })` gives the edges *Collect All Bends* picks (`{ bends, rips, T, wall, rounded }`). |
| `tab({ sketch, reverse, name })` | **Tab** (Base Flange/Tab on a part that already has a sheet): the last sketch, on a flat sheet face, extruded the sheet's thickness into the sheet and merged (`reverse`: away from the face). The thickness stays linked to the sheet. `name` default `Tab1`. |
| `breakCorner(size, corners, { kind, faces, name })` | **Break-Corner/Corner-Trim** (SolidWorks): chamfer (`kind: "chamfer"`, the default; `size` along both edges) or round (`"fillet"`, `size` = radius) outside sheet corners through the thickness. `corners`: points on corner edges (the short edges through the thickness); `faces`: points on flat sheet faces (all their outside corners). `name` default `Break-Corner1`. See *Break Corner / Corner Trim*. |
| `cornerRelief(corners, size, { reliefType, centeredOnBend, cornerType, name })` | **Corner Relief** (SolidWorks: *Corners ▸ Corner Relief*, 2 Bend corners): a relief where two edge-flange bends on neighbouring edges of one face meet. `corners`: points near the corners (`Kernel.cornerReliefCandidates(model, build)[i].at`, the corner of the two bends' start lines on their inside face); `size` (null = 2·T); `reliefType` `"rect"` (default, a square `size` wide) \| `"circular"` (Ø `size`) \| `"obround"` (`size` wide, 2·`size` long along the diagonal) \| `"constantWidth"` (a band `size` wide across the two bend zones' square) \| `"tear"` (just that square); `centeredOnBend` (default true: centred on the bend centrelines' crossing in the flat; false: on the start lines' corner). Cut in the face, both bends (wrapped) and both walls; the flat shows the same outline. Square corners only. Trim side bends: `flange(L, edges, { trimSideBends: true })` / `miterFlange(edges, { trimSideBends: true })` (Material Inside / Outside: an earlier bend running on past an end of the edge is cut back by the same trim, R + T or R). `cornerType: "3bend"` (SolidWorks' *3 Bend corner*): where a bend of the base (a U channel's) meets Edge Flanges on the end edges of its face and of its wall (`Kernel.cornerReliefCandidates(model, build, "3bend")`); `rect` or `tear` (the U bend's stub between the end bends), exact in the folded part and the flat. |
| `weldedCorner(faces, { fillet, addTexture, name })` | **Welded Corner** (SolidWorks: *Corners ▸ Welded Corner*): fills the gap where two walls meet at a corner with weld metal, over the straight walls (the bend region stays open). `faces`: a point on a side face of each corner (a wall's end face, through T, facing the other wall across the gap); `fillet`: radius of a fillet in the weld's inside corner (16 chords; default none); `addTexture`: cosmetic, stored only. The flat pattern leaves the weld out (it is laid out from the part with its Welded Corners suppressed). |
| `hole(planeSpec, x, y, dia, opts)` | Sketch + cut in one call. `depth` makes it blind (default through all); `forward: true` cuts along the normal instead of against it; `bodies` limits it to those bodies. |
| `box(w, d, h, opts)` | Sketched on Top at `at[1]`; occupies `x ∈ [0,w]`, `y ∈ [0,h]`, `z ∈ [0,d]` from `at`, or centred in x/z with `centered: true`. `cut: true` subtracts (`bodies` scopes it); `merge: false` keeps it as a separate body. |
| `cylinder(r, h, opts)` | `axis` `"y"` (default), `"x"` or `"z"`; `at`; `cut`; `merge`; `bodies`. |
| `last() → id` | Id of the most recently added feature, for `feature:` references. |
| `helix(sketch, { defineBy, pitch, revolutions, height, startAngle, clockwise, reverse, taper, taperOut, name }) → id` | **Helix and Spiral** (Features ▸ Curves): from a sketch with one circle (its centre is the axis, its radius the start radius). `defineBy` `"pitchRev"` (default), `"heightRev"`, `"heightPitch"` or `"spiral"` (in the sketch plane; `reverse` spirals inward). Starts at `startAngle` (degrees, from the sketch x toward y) and grows along the sketch normal (`reverse`: the other way); counterclockwise = right-handed. `taper` (degrees) narrows it, `taperOut` widens it. A reference curve: no body, a Sweep `path`. |
| `projectedCurve(sketch, { faces: [[x,y,z], …], reverse } \| { sketch2 }) → id` | **Projected Curve**: the sketch projected along its normal onto the faces at the points (exact on planes and cylinders; `reverse` the other way), or with `sketch2` the 3D curve where the two sketches' extrusions meet. |
| `compositeCurve([ref, …]) → id` | **Composite Curve**: model edges `{ edge: [x,y,z] }`, sketch entities `{ sketch, entity, seg? }` and curves `{ curve: id }` joined end to end in any order; a gap is an error. |
| `xyzCurve([[x,y,z], …], { closed }) → id`, `refPointCurve([ref, …], { closed }) → id` | **Curve Through XYZ Points / Reference Points**: the C1 cubic (chord-length Catmull-Rom) through the points; refs as `point()`'s. `Kernel.parseCurveFile(text, units)` / `formatCurveFile(points, units)` read and write the .sldcrv-style text (`x y z` a line, units optional). |

### SketchBuilder methods

`line(x1, y1, x2, y2)`, `rect(x, y, w, h)`, `centerRect(cx, cy, w, h)`, `circle(cx, cy, r)`, `polygon(cx, cy, r, n, rotDeg?)`, `slot(x1, y1, x2, y2, width)`, `arc(cx, cy, r, a0Deg, a1Deg)`, `polyline(points, closed = true)`, `point(x, y)`, `spline(points, closed = false, tans?)`, `styleSpline(controlVertices, { degree })`, `ellipse(cx, cy, rx, ry, rotDeg)`, `partialEllipse(cx, cy, rx, ry, rotDeg, a0Deg, a1Deg)` (see *Sketch tab ▸ Spline, Ellipse, Text*). All return the sketch builder. `id()` returns the sketch's feature id, `end()` returns the part, and `extrude`, `cut`, `revolve`, `revolveCut` delegate to the part with this sketch preselected.

Editing helpers on the same builder: `mirrorEntities(axis, { entities, copy })` mirrors entities (all by default) across `"x"`, `"y"`, a line entity index, or `{ x1, y1, x2, y2 }` — axis-aligned mirrors keep rectangles, arcs and regular polygons stay true entities, anything else becomes a polyline; `patternEntities({ dx, dy, count, dx2, dy2, count2, entities })` repeats entities along one or two step vectors; `trim(index, u, v)` removes the span of entity `index` around sketch point `(u, v)` between its neighbouring intersections (a circle becomes an arc, a rectangle an open polyline, a slot is split into lines and arcs first, an entity nothing crosses is deleted). The results are plain entities, so scripts regenerated from the model contain the final geometry.

`dim(index, kind, { i, off, ang, value })` places a Smart Dimension on entity `index`: kinds are `"len"`, `"dx"`, `"dy"` (lines), `"w"`, `"h"` (rectangles; `i` = edge 0 bottom, 1 right, 2 top, 3 left), `"dia"` (circles), `"r"` (arcs, polygons), `"len"`, `"w"` (slots), `"seg"` (polylines, `i` = segment) and `"curv"` (splines, `i` = a point with a curvature control: its radius of curvature, `R`; the value sets the control, keeping the side it bends to). `off` is the offset of the dimension line from the entity (linear) or the label distance from the centre (radial, with `ang` in degrees). `value` drives the entity to that size first — `p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -20, 40, 20).dim(0, "w", { value: 50 })` makes the rectangle 50 wide — and `Kernel.setDimValue` is what the value box in the UI calls: a line keeps its start point and direction, a rectangle keeps the edge opposite the one you dimensioned, an open polyline carries the rest of its chain along.

`rel(type, refs, value | { value, off } | { at })` adds a sketch relation (or a two-entity driving dimension) and re-solves the sketch; `solve()` re-solves it. See *Sketch relations* below. Written back by `generateScript` after the relations: `.construction([i, …])`, `.sharp([i, …])` (points that are virtual sharps, see *Sketching*), `.driven([i, …])` and `.dimText([[i, t], …])` (the label of Smart Dimension i sits `t` along its dimension line from the middle, where it was dropped).

Profiles are found automatically: closed entities (rectangles, circles, polygons, slots, closed polylines) are loops, open entities (lines, arcs, open polylines) are chained end to end into loops. Loops nested inside other loops alternate between hole and island. Open chains that never close are ignored with a warning. An entity with `construction: true` is drawn but does not contribute.

### Feature JSON

`Kernel.newModel()` gives `{ name, units, density, color, features: [], nextId }`. Each feature has `id`, `type`, `name` and type-specific fields:

| type | fields |
| --- | --- |
| `sketch` | `plane` (`{ ref, offset }` \| `{ featureId }` \| custom frame), `entities: [{ t: "line" \| "rect" \| "crect" \| "circle" \| "poly" \| "slot" \| "arc" \| "polyline" \| "spline" \| "ellipse" \| "pellipse" \| "point", … }]`, optional `dims: [{ e, k, i, off, ang }]` (placed Smart Dimensions; ignored by the rebuild) |
| `plane` | `ref`, `offset` |
| `refaxis` | `kind` (`"line"` \| `"planes"` \| `"points"` \| `"cyl"` \| `"pointFace"`), `refs: [ref, ref?]` |
| `refpoint` | `kind` (`"arc"` \| `"face"` \| `"intersect"` \| `"project"` \| `"on"` \| `"along"`), `refs`, `distance`, `percent`, `reverse` |
| `csys` | `origin`, `x`, `y`, `z` (refs, all optional), `flipX`, `flipY`, `flipZ`; `hidden` on any of the three hides it |
| `helix` / `projCurve` / `compositeCurve` / `xyzCurve` / `refPtCurve` | the curve features (see `helix()` … above for the fields: `sketch`, `defineBy`, …; `mode` `"sketchOnFaces"` \| `"sketchOnSketch"`, `faces: [{ at }]`, `sketch2`; `edges: [ref]`; `points`, `closed`). Rebuild puts `{ refgeo: "curve", kind, pts, edges, runs, closed, length, t0, t1, helix? }` in `build.planes[id]`; `sweep.path` may be a curve's id |
| `extrude` | `sketch`, `depth`, `end` (`"blind" \| "through" \| "mid"`), `reverse`, `cut`, `dir2`, `depth2`, `end2`, `draft` (degrees, optional), `draftOut`, `normalCut` (boolean, cuts on sheet-metal parts; `generateScript` emits `normalCut: true` only when set), `merge` (boolean, default true; `false` on a boss keeps it a separate body), `bodies` (cuts only: the indices of the bodies to cut, absent = all; see *Multi-body parts*), `contours` (optional, `[[u, v], …]`: a point inside each sketch region to use, see *Selected Contours*; absent = the whole sketch; also on `revolve`) |
| `revolve` | `sketch`, `angle`, `axis`, `axisRef` (reference Axis id), `reverse`, `cut`, `merge`, `bodies` |
| `lpattern` | `feature`, `d1`, `rev1`, `s1`, `n1`, `d2`, `rev2`, `s2`, `n2`, `merge`, `bodies` |
| `cpattern` | `feature`, `axis`, `center`, `axisRef` (reference Axis id), `n`, `angle`, `equal`, `merge`, `bodies` |
| `mirror` | `plane`, `offset`, and either `feature` or `body: true`; `merge`, `bodies` |
| `chamfer` / `fillet` | `d` or `r`, `edges: [{ at: [x, y, z], chain }]` (edges are re-resolved against the body at rebuild time, so they survive upstream edits as long as the point still lies near an edge) |
| `flange` | `length`, `angle`, `reverse`, `radius` (`null` = sheet radius), `edges: [{ at }]`, `position` (also `"tangentToBend"`, Tangent to Bend, 90° and up: the bend starts R + T back), `lengthEnd` (`"blind"` \| `"upToVertex"` with `lengthVertex: [x, y, z]` and `lengthVertexMode` `"normal"` (end square to the wall through the vertex, default) \| `"parallel"` (top level with the vertex)), `offset` (`{ end: "blind", distance }` or a number: the flange that far out, the sheet running on flat to it), `reliefRatio` (a custom relief's width and depth as ratio × T), `toward` (optional `[x, y, z]`), `miter` (boolean, default false), `miterGap` (default 0), `relief` (`"none"` \| `"rect"` \| `"obround"`, default `"none"`), `reliefWidth` / `reliefDepth` (`null` = T / T/2), `lengthRef` (`"outer"` | `"inner"` | `"tangent"` | `"wall"`, absent = `"wall"`, the legacy measure; ignored on hems)\| `"inner"` \| `"tangent"`, absent = `"tangent"`, the legacy measure; ignored on hems), `position` may also be `"virtualSharp"`; `hem: true` for hems, which keep `angle: 180` and `position: "bendOutside"` and add `hemType` (`"open"` \| `"closed"` \| `"teardrop"` \| `"rolled"`, absent = open), `hemPosition` (`"inside"` \| `"outside"`, absent = outside; when present it is the hem's placement and `position` is not read), `gap` / `radius` (open hems only), `hemRadius` (`null` = sheet radius) and `hemAngle` (default 200 tear drop, 270 rolled; those two only). `generateScript` emits only the non-default values (`lengthRef`, `hemType`, `hemPosition` whenever they are set). |
| `jog` | `height`, `length`, `angle`, `reverse`, `radius`, `position`, `edges: [{ at }]` |
| `sketchedBend` | `sketch` (id), `angle` (°, default 90), `position` (`"centerline"` \| `"inside"` \| `"outside"` \| `"bendOutside"`, default `"centerline"`), `reverse` (default false), `radius` (`null` = sheet radius), `fixed` (`[x, y, z]` or `null` = the largest piece), optional `bendAllowance`. Its rebuild-cache key includes the whole sheet (the bend allowance changes the solid). |
| `closedCorner` | `faces: [[x, y, z], …]` (points on the end faces to extend), `cornerType` (`"butt"` \| `"overlap"` \| `"underlap"`), `gap`, `ratio` (0–1), optional `coplanar: false`; body-editing (adds the extensions, trims the matching wall), see *Closed Corner* |
| `unfold` / `fold` | `fixedFace: [x, y, z] \| null` (unfold only), `bends: [[x, y, z], …]` (points in the folded state; empty = all); body-replacing rebuild cases, see *Unfold / Fold* |
| `rip` | `edges: [{ at: [x, y, z], direction? }]`, `gap`, `direction` (`"both"` \| `"side1"` \| `"side2"`); a cut (see *Rip and Tab*) |
| `extrude` + `tab: true` | a Tab: `sheetBase: true, tab: true, linkT: true`, `reverse` = away from the face; depth and direction are resolved at rebuild (`Kernel.tabFeature`) |
| `breakCorner` | `kind` (`"chamfer"` \| `"fillet"`), `size` (distance or radius), `corners: [[x, y, z], …]` (points on corner edges), optional `faces: [[x, y, z], …]` (points on flat sheet faces); a cut (see *Break Corner / Corner Trim*) |
| `cornerRelief` | `corners: [[x, y, z], …]`, `reliefType` (`"rect"` \| `"circular"` \| `"tear"` \| `"obround"` \| `"constantWidth"`), `size`, optional `centeredOnBend: false`, optional `cornerType: "3bend"` (`rect` \| `tear`); a registered cut (its bend parts ride to the flat as `foldCutouts` records). Edge / Miter Flange: optional `trimSideBends: true`. |
| `weldedCorner` | `faces: [[x, y, z], …]`, optional `fillet` (> 0), `addTexture: true`; a registered boss (not a cut); `flatPattern` lays out the part without it. |
| `flange` / `jog` / `sketchedBend` (hem included) | optional `bendAllowance: { type: 'kfactor' \| 'allowance' \| 'deduction' \| 'table', value }`: the feature's Custom Bend Allowance (value = K, BA or BD; none for `table`), overriding the sheet's method |
| `sweep` | `profile` (sketch id), `path` (sketch id or `[[x, y, z], …]`), `cut`, `merge`, `bodies` |
| `loft` | `sketches: [id, …]` (two or more, in order), `cut`, `merge`, `bodies` |
| `boundary` | `dir1: [sketch id, …]`, `dir2: [sketch id \| { sketch, entity } \| { pts }]`, `cut`, `tangency1` / `tangency2: { start, end }`, `close`, `mergeTangent`, `thin: { t, mid, reverse }`, `merge`, `bodies`; names Boundary-Boss1 / Boundary-Cut1 |
| `shell` | `thickness`, `faces: [{ at: [x, y, z] }]` (faces to remove; may be empty) |
| `draft` | `angle` (deg, 0–89), `neutral: { face: [x, y, z] }` or `{ plane: "Front", "Top", "Right" or a plane id, offset? }`, `faces: [{ at: [x, y, z] }]`, `reverse?`, `propagate?: "tangent"` |
| `rib` | `sketch` (open lines / a chain), `thickness`, `side` (`"both"` \| `"one"` \| `"other"`), `direction` (`"parallel"` \| `"normal"`), `flip`, `extension` (`"linear"` \| `"natural"`); a boss (see `rib(...)` above) |
| `intersect` | `bodies` (indices, default all), `planes` (`"Front"` \| plane feature id \| `{ ref, offset }`), `regions` (keep flags in `Kernel.intersectRegions` order), `merge` (default true), `mode` (`"create"` \| `"internal"`); replaces the selected bodies (see `intersect(...)` above) |
| `deleteBody` | `bodies` (`[{ at: [x, y, z] }]` points on / inside the bodies, or body indices), `keep` (true: keep the listed bodies, delete the rest); see `deleteBody(...)` above |

An `extrude` with `sheetBase: true` is the base flange; `model.sheet = { thickness, bendRadius, kFactor, bendAllowanceType?, bendAllowance?, bendDeduction?, bendTable?, gaugeTable?, gauge? }` holds the sheet parameters (`Kernel.makeSheet` writes them in that order). `merge` and `bodies` are written only when set (`merge: false`, a non-empty `bodies` list), and `generateScript` emits them only then; `bodies` indices refer to the body list as it stands just before that feature.

Feature names auto-number per type (`Sketch1`, `Boss-Extrude1`, `Cut-Extrude1`, `Revolve1`, `LPattern1`, `CirPattern1`, `Mirror1`, `Plane1`, `Chamfer1`, `Fillet1`, `Sweep1`, `Cut-Sweep1`, `Loft1`, `Cut-Loft1`, `Shell1`, `Edge-Flange1`, `Hem1`, `Jog1`, `SketchBend1`). A flange accepted from the Miter Flange PropertyManager is named `Miter-Flange1`; scripted mitred flanges keep the `Edge-Flange` name unless `name` is given.

### Kernel API (no UI needed)

```js
var model = Kernel.newModel("Part1");
Kernel.runScript(model, code);              // executes the script against model
var b = Kernel.rebuild(model, prevBuild.cache); // { body, bodies, bodyFeatures, tools, planes, errors: {id: msg}, warnings, time, cache, reused }; pass the previous cache to rebuild only from the first changed feature
                                            // bodies: one CSG per solid body in creation order; bodyFeatures: the id of the feature that started each body; body = combineBodies(bodies)
var mesh = Kernel.meshFromBody(b.body);     // { positions, normals, faces: [{ normal, w, start, count, centroid, area, body }], triCount } — faces are grouped by plane and body; face.body is the body index
Kernel.combineBodies(bodies);               // one solid of every body's polygons (the body itself for one, null for none); every polygon gets polygon.body = its index
Kernel.bodyInfo(b, model);                  // [{ index, featureId, name, volume }] — name is the creating feature's name (Body<n> if it is gone)
Kernel.bodyNearPoint(bodies, [x, y, z]);    // index of the body with the vertex nearest the point (−1 for none): how picked features find their body
var mp = Kernel.massProps(mesh, model.density); // { volume, area, centroid, bbox: { min, max }, mass, polygonal?: { volume, area }, curved? } — exact for true curves (see below)
Kernel.curveEdges(meshOrBody);              // [{ kind: 'circle' | 'arc', center, normal, radius, xdir, a0, a1, surface, chords }] — the true circles and arcs bounding curved faces
Kernel.curveSurfaces(meshOrBody);           // [{ key, surf, polys }] — one entry per cylinder / cone / sphere / torus, with its facet fragments
Kernel.setTessellation({ chordTol, minSegs, maxSegs, maxAngle, legacy }); Kernel.segsForRadius(r);   // chord tolerance of the display / boolean mesh ('default' restores 0.05 mm)
var q = Kernel.meshQuality(mesh);           // { triangles, vertices, minAngle, needles, slivers, openEdges, nonManifoldEdges, tJunctions, closed }
Kernel.exportSTL(mesh, name);               // ASCII STL text
Kernel.exportDXFSketch(sketchFeature, units);   // circles and arcs stay true entities; every DXF writer puts out coordinates at 14 significant digits (not rounded to 6 decimals)
Kernel.exportDXFFace(mesh, faceIndex, units);   // OUTLINE / HOLES layers; round holes become CIRCLEs
Kernel.faceOutline(mesh, faceIndex);        // { loops: [[u, v], …], plane }
Kernel.bodyEdges(mesh);                     // [{ a, b, mid, dir, len, faces: [i, j], normals, convex, smooth }] — smooth = tangent (< 22°)
Kernel.resolveEdges(specs, edges, tol);     // specs [{ at, chain }] → the edges a chamfer/fillet/flange will use
Kernel.flatPattern(model, b, mesh);         // { loops (outer first, CCW), bends: [{ a, b, label, up, angle, R, ba, bd, method, source }], bbox, size: [w, h], sheet, note, warnings, etch, etchLayer, bendRegions?, regionLines? }
Kernel.sheetCutList(model, b, mesh);        // SolidWorks' sheet-metal Cut-List Properties: { rows: [{ name, value, text, unit }], values: { 'Bounding Box Length', 'Bounding Box Width', 'Sheet Metal Thickness', 'Bend Radius', 'K-Factor' (or 'Bend Allowance' / 'Bend Deduction' / 'Bend Table'), Bends, 'Cutting Length-Outer', 'Cutting Length-Inner', 'Cut Outs', 'Bounding Box Area', 'Bounding Box Area-Blank', 'Sheet Metal Gauge', Material, Mass (kg), Description: 'Sheet' }, error }; in the app: Cut list(1) ▸ Sheet<1> ▸ Properties…, or anvil.cutList(); a drawing note / title block field links them as $PRPSHEET:"Bounding Box Length" (a File Property of that name wins)
                                            //   etch: the ETCH marks (Etch text strokes, entities on a noProfile layer other than BEND) of sketches on a sheet face, in flat coordinates (wall marks unfold, bottom-face marks mirror)
                                            //   holes, slots and notches cut before a flange / sketched bend stay cut through the bend (wrapped by BA) and appear once in the flat
Kernel.exportDXFFlat(flat, units, opts);    // OUTLINE / HOLES / BEND / NOTES layers, bend labels and "T= R= K=" as TEXT; ETCH marks as LINE / ARC / CIRCLE on layer ETCH (opts.etch: false leaves them out); off by default: boundaryBox (BOUNDARY rectangle), bendLayers: 'split' (BEND_UP / BEND_DOWN), reliefLayer (relief notch edges on RELIEF), hiddenEdges (forming tools' far side on HIDDEN)
Kernel.snapFlatCircle(loop, tol);           // a flat loop that is a circle within tol becomes a true circle (Kernel.flatCircleTol(units, r): 0.003 in / 0.075 mm, at most 3 % of r), so a round hole across a bend is one CIRCLE in the flat DXF
Kernel.pickTol(units, size);                // how far a recorded fillet / chamfer / shell pick may sit from its edge or face: max(0.5 mm in the model's units, size)
Kernel.cleanFlatLoop(loop);                 // drops duplicate, collinear and spike points (within 1e-9 × size, no point moves); run on every flat loop
Kernel.bendAllowance(angleRad, R, T, K);    // angle · (R + K·T), the neutral-axis arc length
Kernel.normalCutTool(tool, body, model, dir); // the Normal-cut replacement for a cut tool: the outlines of the sheet faces it enters (n·dir < 0), re-extruded straight through the thickness; rebuild applies it to cuts with normalCut on a part that has model.sheet
Kernel.flangeProfile(angleRad, R, T, L, ov); // the 2D section a flange sweeps along its edge (o across the edge, u along the fold), incl. the overlap strip o ∈ [−ov, 0]
Kernel.loopSelfCrossing(pts); Kernel.checkProfile(profile);   // first self-intersection of a closed loop | throw "crosses itself" for any self-crossing loop, "Sketch contours intersect" for two loops that cross
Kernel.offsetLoop(pts, d);                  // 2D: mitred offset of a closed loop, +d = outward for CCW
Kernel.filletPolyline(pts, i, r);           // 2D: replace vertex i of a closed loop with a tangent arc
Kernel.sketchPath(sketch, plane, info);     // chain a path sketch's lines/arcs into 3D points; info.dropped = unconnected entities
Kernel.dimGeometry(entity, dim);            // { type: 'linear', P, Q, dir, value } | { type: 'radial', C, r, value, prefix } | null
Kernel.dimValue(entity, dim); Kernel.setDimValue(entity, dim, v);   // read / drive a Smart Dimension (throws on v <= 0)
Kernel.dimForEntity(entity, [u, v]);        // which dimension a click at (u, v) means: { k, i }
Kernel.buildSweep(f, model, ctx); Kernel.buildLoft(f, model, ctx);   // tool bodies (also reached through buildTool)
Kernel.resolveFaces(specs, mesh, tol);      // [{ at }] → mesh face indices (plane distance + outline containment)
Kernel.buildShell(f, { body });             // { body, inner, removed } — the shelled body and the cavity solid
Kernel.buildDraft(f, { body, planes, units }); // rfDX: { body, faces, pull, removed, added } — the drafted body; Kernel.draftNeutral / draftSide give the pull direction
Kernel.generateScript(model);               // model → script text
Kernel.packBuild(b); Kernel.unpackBuild(obj);   // rebuild result ↔ structured-clone-safe object of typed arrays (unpacked builds have cache: null and carry profiles); the separate bodies and bodyFeatures travel, and unpackBuild recombines body with combineBodies so the face tags survive the worker; a packed build with only a body (no bodies list) unpacks to one body
Kernel.applyProfiles(model, profiles);      // put the sketch profiles computed elsewhere (the worker) back on the model's sketch features
Kernel.cacheFromBuild(model, build);        // { steps } for Kernel.rebuild from a build that came back from the worker: one step per feature (key, tool, plane, flange, profile, error, warning), the body only on the last step, `partial: true` on the others — an unchanged model reuses every feature, an appended feature reuses the previous count, a change in the middle reuses nothing
Kernel.cutDirectionHint(model, sketchId, body); // true = a new cut on that sketch should be reversed to go into the body, false = forward already goes into it, null = undetermined (no body, unknown sketch, the probe along the sketch normal misses the body, or the sketch plane lies inside the material). Never throws.
csg.pack(); CSG.unpack(obj); CSG.packTransferables(obj);   // one solid ↔ typed arrays (exact volume, same polygon order), and its ArrayBuffers for postMessage transfer
csg.getBounds();                            // { min: [x, y, z], max: [x, y, z] } of the solid's vertices
```

### True curves

Circles, arcs, cylinders, cones, spheres and tori are exact in the model (run cloud-2026-09-25p); the polygon mesh is only their tessellation.

- **Sketch profiles** keep the curves: `profile.curves` lists every circle and arc of the sketch (`{ c, r, a0, a1, full, step }`, sketch coordinates) and `loop.seg[i]` is the curve index of loop segment i → i+1 (−1 for a straight segment).
- **Solids** carry an analytic record on every facet of a curved face: `polygon.surf = { s: surface, … }`, the surface being `{ t: 'cyl', o, d, r }`, `{ t: 'cone', o, d, r, k }` (radius r + k·z along the axis), `{ t: 'sph', o, d, r }` or `{ t: 'tor', o, d, R, r }`; all facets of one face share it. Extrudes (drafted ones become cones), holes, revolves (a line segment of the profile makes a cylinder or cone, an arc a sphere or torus) and fillets make them (between planar faces a cylinder; on a circular edge a torus, its chamfer a cone, revolved through the edge's own vertices so the cut leaves no slivers; spherical box corners). Sheet-metal bends carry the cylinders of their inner (R) and outer (R + T) surfaces about the bend line: edge flanges, hems of every type, jogs, Miter Flanges, Sketched Bends, Insert Bends / Convert to Sheet Metal and the bends Fold rebuilds, so a K 0.5 part's Mass Properties volume equals its flat area × T. A loft through coaxial full circles on parallel planes carries the cone (or cylinder) of each span. CSG splits, clones, transforms (patterns, mirrors, assembly placements) and `pack` / `unpack` keep them, so every fragment of a curved face knows its exact surface.
- **Mass properties** are exact: `Kernel.massProps` adds to the facets' volume, first moment and area the sliver between each facet fragment and its true surface (columns perpendicular to the axis for cylinders and cones, a second level along the arc's normal for spheres and tori, integrated by Gauss-Legendre on each fan triangle; side walls of the slivers that lie in planar faces correct those faces' areas). A cylinder, a hole, a counterbore, a revolve or a filleted edge measures πr²h, 4/3πr³, 2π²Rr² … to 1e-12, whatever the tessellation; `mp.polygonal` keeps the mesh's own volume and area. The columns are exact where the neighbouring faces are perpendicular to the axis (or contain it, at the ends of a partial revolve); where a plane cuts a cylinder face obliquely (a mitre between two equal rounds, a pocket wall, a sheet-metal flange's mitre) the columns are bounded by that plane (the volume between the radial wall and the plane is added edge by edge), so those are exact too; a hole through a slanted face or two crossing cylinders is exact to the order of the sliver's curvature. Sweeps, lofts, shells of round faces and fillets along curved edges other than circles square to their faces (an ellipse where a hole meets a slanted face, a spline edge) are still polygonal; so are corners where three edges with unequal radii, or not mutually perpendicular, meet (caps / mitres, no spherical blend). The result is kept per mesh object (a fresh copy per call; `Kernel.massOptions.cache`), so asking the same mesh again costs nothing.
- **Edges**: `Kernel.curveEdges` returns the true circles and arcs where a curved face meets a face perpendicular to its axis (caps, hole rims, steps, bottoms) and the profile arcs at the ends of a partial revolve, each with the mesh chords it replaces.
- **Tessellation** follows a chord tolerance: each chord of a full circle is within `chordTol` (default 0.05 mm; the old rule, clamp(round(8√r), 16, 72), was about 0.077 mm) of the circle, with n a multiple of 8 between 16 and 128, so circles have vertices on their quadrant points and concentric circles share every 45° vertex. Revolves use the same count for their largest radius. `Kernel.setTessellation` changes it globally, `model.tessellation = { chordTol }` per document (it is part of the rebuild cache key, and the worker gets it with the model), and `{ legacy: true }` reproduces the meshes made before true curves bit for bit. `{ maxAngle: 10 }` adds an angular limit (no chord of a circle or spline span turns more than 10°, at least 40 segments per circle); it is off (0) by default until the tessellation-sensitive outputs it disturbs are fixed.
- **Viewport**: curved faces shade smooth (`Kernel.analyticNormals(mesh)`, through `UI.analyticNormals`, cached as `mesh.smoothNormals`; `mesh.normals` stays flat for exports): faces with a record take its exact normal, other curved faces (fillets on round edges, splines, lofts, sweeps) are smoothed over their facets with a 20° crease while planes stay flat, and a revolve closes at its axis with the true pole normal, seams between facets of one surface are neither drawn nor pickable, and every circle and arc from `curveEdges` is drawn as one curve sampled for the screen (at least 128 segments per turn, re-sampled as the zoom changes so a hole stays round zoomed in) (hovering a chord lights the whole curve). *Tools ▸ Options ▸ Image Quality* sets the document's chord tolerance (Coarse 0.1 / Default 0.05 / Fine 0.02 / Finer 0.01 mm, stored as `model.tessellation.chordTol`, one undo step); `anvil.tessellation()` reads it, `anvil.tessellation('fine' | 0.02)` sets it.
- **Dimension precision** (SolidWorks' Tolerance/Precision box): a sketch dimension's PropertyManager shows the tolerance type and unit precision as two icon-labelled lists; precision is None, .1 … .12345678 or "<document value> (Document)" (the default). The part's own precision is Tools ▸ Options ▸ Document Properties ▸ Dimensions (task pane *Document properties* ▸ *Dimensions*: Primary precision and Angular precision, defaults inch .123, mm .12, angles .12), stored as `model.dimPrecision = { linear, angular }`, one undo step; script `p.dimPrecision({ linear: 4 })` (written by `generateScript`). Drawing dimensions offer the same list with the drawing's own Units ▸ Decimal places as "(Document)".

**Boolean early-out.** Before a boolean clips anything, `csg.js` compares the two operands' bounding boxes (`getBounds`). Fully disjoint operands short-circuit: a union is the two polygon lists concatenated, a subtract returns the body untouched, an intersect is empty. Otherwise each polygon whose own bounding box does not overlap the other operand's box skips the BSP clipping: it is kept as it is for a union and for the body side of a subtract, and dropped for the tool side of a subtract and for both sides of an intersect. Only the polygons near the other operand go through the tree, so a small cut or boss on a large part costs about what the small feature costs. The result is identical to the full clip (the `csg early-out` kernel tests pin volumes, centroids and polygon counts, and every Design Library part's volume, before and after), and `tests/bench.html` prints the sum of the per-part medians as one before/after number.

**Crack healing (rfFL).** BSP booleans take a vertex within 1e-5 of a plane as on it without moving it, so at tangent joints (a fillet running into a straight edge, a hole tangent to a wall) two faces can miss each other by a few 1e-6 and the mesh has open edges. After a boolean in which that happened, `csg.js` `healCracks` pairs the result's edges, drops exact T-junctions and inserts each near-miss vertex (within 2e-5) into the open edge beside it; a result it cannot improve comes back unchanged, and a watertight one is never touched. `CSG.options.heal = false` turns it off; `healWide`, `healFill`, `healDrop` are stronger, off by default (see the comment above `healCracks`). `CSG.healCracks(csg)` heals one solid; `CSG.healStats` counts what it did. Every BSP tree walk is iterative, so finely faceted parts (3° Image Quality) no longer overflow the call stack.

**Exact retry and shared split points (rfFQ).** Where two nearly parallel faces meet (a vent fillet tangent to a spar circle, a small round on a dish wall, a hole through the edge of a boss) the 1e-5 band leaves cracks up to 1e-5 / sin(angle) wide, more than healing may bridge. A boolean still open after healing is run once more with those vertices on their true side and the better result is kept (fewer open edges, and no more sub-1e-6 slivers unless it is closed); a watertight boolean is never redone. `CSG.options.retryExact = false` turns it off; `CSG.retryStats` counts retries and wins. A polygon edge cut by a plane gets the same split point in both polygons that share it (computed from both ends, the lexicographically smaller kept), so the meshes' 1e-6 weld never splits a twin.

**Far pieces, slivers and twin points (rfFV).** The classic boolean (full BSP trees) splits each solid's polygons by its own planes; the pieces lying far from the other solid now go back whole like the clipped ones (`CSG.options.restoreFar`), since nearly parallel planes made them overlap (a U-bracket with every edge R0.1: 51 open edges, now closed with a sixth of the polygons and in an eighth of the time). A convex piece under 1e-6 wide left where a fillet chord meets a circle chord almost straight (the vent at 3°) is merged into its coplanar neighbour (`fatten`, `CSG.fatStats`), and vertices within 1e-6 (the weld cell) of each other near the cut become one point (`snapTwins`): the same corner computed from both solids could straddle a line of the meshes' 1e-6 weld grid and open.

A feature that fails to build is recorded in `errors` and the rest of the tree still builds. Errors are meant to be shown to people: "Sketch has no closed profile.", "Nothing to cut yet.", "Revolve profile crosses the axis.", "Pattern needs at least 2 instances." Non-fatal problems land in `warnings` by feature id, for example an open contour in a sketch or a cut whose tool never touched the body ("Cut removed no material. Try Reverse direction.").

Degenerate geometry is diagnosed before it reaches the CSG kernel:

- A closed contour with zero area (a rectangle of zero width, a polyline whose points are collinear) is dropped with the warning "1 zero-area contour was ignored." on the feature; if nothing is left, the feature errors with the usual "no closed profile".
- A contour that crosses itself (a bow-tie polyline, a rectangle whose edges cross an open chain) errors with "Sketch profile crosses itself near (x, y)." and the loop stays visible in the sketch so it can be trimmed. `Kernel.loopSelfCrossing(pts)` returns the first crossing point of a closed loop, or `null`.
- A closed contour that crosses another one (a hole circle drawn over the outline's corner) errors with "Sketch contours intersect near (x, y)" in Boss / Cut-Extrude, Base Flange/Tab and every other profile feature, as SolidWorks refuses it, instead of building from part of the sketch. Contours that only touch (a circle tangent to an edge, two contours sharing an edge or a corner, a hole wholly inside) still build. Such loops carry `meet` (the crossing point) and the smaller of the two `meetInner`; the sketch DXF keeps that one on HOLES as drawn.
- A feature built from an over-defined sketch (a dimension left driving after *Make Dimension Driven?*) still builds, but the sketch and the feature get a rebuild warning ("This sketch is over defined …" / "Built from Sketch1, which is over defined."), shown in the FeatureManager, the rebuild chip and the status line (the UI adds it to each applied build; the kernel and worker do not run the solver).
- A non-finite number where a dimension is expected (`extrude(NaN)`, `revolve(undefined + 1)`, a sweep path with a missing coordinate) errors with "… must be a number." instead of building an empty or infinite solid: extrude depth and draft, revolve angle, flange length and angle, jog height / length / angle, sweep path coordinates.

### Hole Wizard

`holewizard.js` adds the `hole` feature: standard holes at the points of a sketch on a planar face, in two standards, **ANSI Inch** and **ISO** metric.

```js
p.holeWizard({ face: "Top", offset: 30, points: [[-15, 0], [15, 0]], type: "cbore", size: "M6", depth: 16 });
var s = p.sketch("Top", 30).point(0, 0).id();
p.holeWizard({ sketch: s, type: "tap", size: "M8", depth: 16, threadDepth: 12 });   // or end: "through"
// inch part
p.holeWizard({ face: "Top", offset: 1, points: [[0.5, 0.5]], standard: "ANSI Inch", type: "tap", size: "1/4-20 UNC", depth: 0.75, threadDepth: 0.5 });
p.holeWizard({ face: "Top", offset: 1, points: [[0, 0]], type: "cbore", size: "1/4", fit: "normal", end: "through" });
p.holeWizard({ face: "Top", offset: 1, points: [[1, 0]], type: "hole", sizeType: "drill", size: "F", end: "through" });
```

Feature JSON: `{ type: 'hole', standard: 'ANSI Inch' | 'ISO', holeType: 'cbore' | 'csk' | 'hole' | 'tap', size, fit: 'close' | 'normal' | 'loose', sizeType: 'drill' (an ANSI Inch Hole sized by drill, else a screw clearance), end: 'blind' | 'through', depth, threadDepth, sketch }`. `size` is `'M2'`…`'M20'` (ISO), a screw `'#2'`…`'#12'`, `'1/4'`…`'1'` (ANSI clearance, counterbore, countersink), a thread `'#2-56 UNC'`…`'1-12 UNF'` (ANSI Straight Tap) or a drill `'#1'`…`'#60'`, `'A'`…`'Z'`, `'1/16'`…`'1'` in 64ths (ANSI Hole with `sizeType: 'drill'`). Without `standard` an `M…` size is ISO and any other ANSI Inch; with neither, the part's units choose (ANSI Inch in an inch part). The tables are in each standard's own unit and `HoleWizard.spec(f, units)` converts to the part's, so an ANSI hole in a metric part and an ISO hole in an inch part are the right size. ANSI Inch data: clearance holes close / normal / loose per ASME B18.2.8 (1/4: 17/64, 9/32, 19/64), Straight Tap UNC / UNF (ASME B1.1, class 2B) with the ~75 % thread tap drill (1/4-20: #7 = .201), Counterbore for socket head cap screws per the ASME B18.3 drill and counterbore table (close / normal drill, counterbore Ø; depth = head height = D; the loose fit takes the B18.2.8 loose drill), Countersink 82° for flat countersunk head machine screws, Ø = ASME B18.6.3 head Ø max (theoretical sharp). There is no counterbore for #12 or 9/16 (no ASME B18.3 socket head cap screw in those sizes); #12 and 9/16 clearances follow B18.2.8's steps, and the 7/8 and 1 countersinks come from B18.6.2 slotted flat cap screws (B18.6.3 stops at 3/4). `HoleWizard.callout(f, units)` gives the drawing callout lines: `Ø.281 THRU`, `⌴ Ø.438 ↧ .250`, `⌵ Ø.385 X 82°`, `1/4-20 UNC - 2B ↧ .500` (inch values with three decimals and no leading zero; mm values as `Ø6.8 ↧ 16`, `M8x1.25 ↧ 12`). The PropertyManager's Type tab has *Standard* (ANSI Inch by default in an inch part), for an ANSI Hole *Hole type* (Screw clearances / Drill sizes), *Size* (the list follows standard and type; a size the new list lacks goes back to its default) and *Fit* (not for taps or drill sizes); the spec line under it is the callout with the drill named. Holes run into the face (against the sketch plane's normal); a blind depth is measured from the face to the flat bottom and includes the counterbore / countersink. Each hole is a revolve cut of its half section about its axis, so walls are exact cylinders and the 90° countersink an exact cone (`massProps` matches the formulas to 1e-9). Sizes: clearance Ø by fit (ISO 273), counterbore Ø / depth for ISO 4762 socket head cap screws, countersink Ø for ISO 10642 flat heads, tap drill for the coarse thread (`HoleWizard.spec(f)`, `HoleWizard.ISO`). Straight Tap holes carry a cosmetic thread (`HoleWizard.threads(model)`): a dashed circle at the major diameter in the viewport and in drawing views along the axis, and the drawing hole callout adds `M8x1.25 ↧ 12` / `1/4-20 UNC - 2B ↧ .500` (or `THRU`) under the tap drill line (`threads` entries carry `label`, `drill`, `standard`, `size`). The UI command is *Features ▸ Hole Wizard* (`UI.cmdHoleWizard`); double-clicking the tree row edits it; the positions sketch sits under the feature in the FeatureManager.

`Kernel.registerFeature(type, { build(f, model, ctx), cut, script(f, varName, idVar, optsStr) })` is the hook other files use to add a tool feature type: rebuild builds it like an extrude or revolve, `isCut` reads `cut`, `generateScript` writes `script`'s line.

### Sheet metal and flat patterns

A sheet-metal part starts with `sheet(T, R, K, opts)` and a `baseFlange`, then `flange` calls on edges of the flat face. `flatPattern` takes the largest face parallel to the base sketch plane, unfolds each flange into a rectangle of length *bend allowance + wall* spliced into the outline, and reports bend lines at the middle of each allowance with `UP`/`DOWN` labels. The flat DXF follows the shop convention: outline on `OUTLINE`, holes on `HOLES` (round holes become `CIRCLE`s), bend lines on `BEND`, labels and the sheet parameters on `NOTES`.

```js
p.sheet(2, 2, 0.71);                                  // 2 mm steel, R2, K = 0.71 (an older part's K; see below)
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -60, 100, 60).end().baseFlange(2);
p.hole("Top", 50, -30, 10, { offset: 2 });
p.flange(20, [[0, 2, 30]]);                           // up along the x = 0 edge
p.flange(15, [[50, 2, 0]], { reverse: true });        // down along the z = 0 edge
// flat pattern: (100 + 20 + BA) × (60 + 15 + BA) with BA = π/2 · (2 + 0.71·2) ≈ 5.372
```

Unfolding is recursive: each flange contributes a bend strip and then its wall face is mapped into the flat with its real outline, so holes cut through a wall and flanges attached to a wall (a Z or hat section) all reach the blank. Flanges accept `position: "bendOutside"` (default, the bend sits beyond the edge), `"outside"` (Material Outside: the wall's inner face runs through the base's top edge) or `"inside"` (Material Inside: the wall's outer face does); at every angle, as SolidWorks (measured at 30° to 135°): the base is trimmed back so the bend starts R·tan(θ/2) from the edge for `"outside"` (the outer virtual sharp T·tan(θ/2) past the edge) and ((R + T) − R·cos θ) / sin θ for `"inside"` (the outer virtual sharp T·cot θ behind it): R and R + T at 90°, where the envelope keeps the base dimensions. `"virtualSharp"` (SolidWorks' *Bend from Virtual Sharp*) puts the bend's outer virtual sharp on the edge: the base is trimmed back by (R + T)·tan(θ/2), so the base keeps its outside dimension at any angle (at 90° it is Material Inside); it needs θ < 180°.

**Flange length reference** (`lengthRef`, SolidWorks' *Flange Length* group). `L` is measured along the wall to its end from: `"outer"` (*Outer Virtual Sharp*, SolidWorks' default and the UI's for a new flange: the intersection of the extended outside faces of sheet and wall; a 90° flange of 1.5 is 1.5 tall outside), `"inner"` (*Inner Virtual Sharp*, the inside faces), `"tangent"` (*Tangent Bend*: up to 90° the same as the outer virtual sharp; beyond 90° the line tangent to the bend's outer surface and square to the wall — the bend's outermost point along the wall, R + T before the wall starts — the same convention as the outside setback, OSSB = R + T beyond 90°, so the flat is still A + B − BD) or `"wall"` (`L` is the straight wall after the bend, so a 90° flange stands L + R + T outside — what Anvil always did, and what a flange **without** the field keeps, so older parts do not move). The straight wall is L − (R + T)·tan(θ/2) to the outer sharp, L − R·tan(θ/2) to the inner one and L − OSSB for Tangent Bend. For θ above 90° the virtual sharp is still the intersection of the extended faces (it lies beyond the bend, (R + T)·tan(θ/2) from the tangent lines), so the length runs along the wall from that point; it is undefined at 180° (use `"tangent"`), and a length shorter than the bend to its reference is an error. Example, 16 ga (T = R = 0.0598), 135°, `L` 1.5 Tangent Bend, bend outside: wall 1.5 − 0.1196, top of the wall's outside face 0.1196 + 0.1196·cos 45° + 1.3804·sin 45° = 1.1803 above the base's outside face. (SolidWorks' help pictures the Tangent Bend dimension along the flange; if a SolidWorks part disagrees at an obtuse angle, this is the place to change.) Hems ignore `lengthRef`. With `"outer"` the flat is the outside dimensions minus the bend deduction, as a shop print reads: a 4 × 1.5 Material Inside 16 ga L unfolds to 4 + 1.5 − BD.

**Hems** (`hem`, SolidWorks' *Hem* types and positions). The length `L` of an open or closed hem is SolidWorks' overall length, from the fold's outer face to the free end (lane DI2; the straight return is L − (R + T); before 2026-10-27 `L` was the straight return, so older parts' hems come out R + T shorter). `hemType: "open"` (absent = open) folds at radius `gap` / 2. `"closed"` lays the return flat on the sheet: a solid needs some radius, so it folds as SolidWorks does with 0.1 mm between the leaves (inner radius 0.05 mm, 0.00197 in) and its flat uses that radius, BA = π·(0.05 mm + K·T); give the hem a Custom Bend Allowance for the shop's own closed-hem value. `"teardrop"` sweeps `hemAngle` (more than 180°, less than 270°, as SolidWorks; default 200°) at inside radius `hemRadius` (default the sheet radius), then a straight tail runs back toward the sheet and stops 0.2 mm short of it, measured along the tail (SolidWorks' tear drop, measured at 200°); `"rolled"` is the curl alone (`hemAngle` 1°–359°, no tail). A roll that curls into its own sheet (past 300° at R = T) joins it, one solid, and the blank stays one piece as SolidWorks'; other metal in its way is refused. Their flat is the swept angle's allowance, BA = θ·(R + K·T), plus the tail. `hemPosition: "outside"` (Bend Outside, absent = this) puts the hem beyond the edge, so the flange grows by R + T; `"inside"` (Material Inside) trims the flange back by R + T first, so the hem folds within the flange's height and the outside height does not change. When `hemPosition` is set it is the hem's placement and its `position` field is not read.

**Bend allowance and bend deduction** (SolidWorks' *Bend Allowance* group). Every bend's strip in the flat is its bend allowance BA; the bend deduction is BD = 2·OSSB − BA, so a part with outside flange dimensions A and B unfolds to **A + B − BD**. Definitions (θ the bend angle, 90° for an L; R the inside radius; T the thickness):

- **K-Factor** (`bendAllowanceType: "kfactor"`, the default): BA = θ·(R + K·T), K the position of the neutral axis as a fraction of T from the inside face. Physical values lie between 0 and 0.5; new parts default to 0.5 (SolidWorks' default).
- **Outside setback**: up to 90° the outside dimensions run to the virtual sharp, OSSB = (R + T)·tan(θ/2); beyond 90° they run to the tangent of the outside of the bend (its outermost point, square to each flat), OSSB = R + T. At 90° both give R + T. `Kernel.outsideSetback`, `Kernel.bendDeduction(θ, R, T, BA)`.
- **Bend Allowance** (`"allowance"`, `bendAllowance: BA`) and **Bend Deduction** (`"deduction"`, `bendDeduction: BD`): the entered value applies unchanged to every bend whatever its angle, as in SolidWorks; the flat pattern warns when such a value lands on a bend that is not 90°. Use a bend table for angle-dependent values.
- **Bend Table** (`"table"`, `bendTable: { kind: "BD" | "BA", name?, tolerance?, rows: [{ thickness, radius, angle, value }] }`): a bend uses the rows whose thickness and radius match its own within the tolerance (default 0.5 % of the value, so a 0.0598 sheet finds 0.060 rows; the closest rows win) and interpolates linearly between their angles. A bend outside the rows falls back to the sheet's K-factor, with a warning in `flat.warnings`. `Kernel.bendTableLookup(table, T, R, angleDeg)`.
- **Custom Bend Allowance** per feature: `flange`, `hem`, `jog` and `sketchedBend` take `bendAllowance: { type, value }` (and their own `radius`), which wins over the sheet's method for that feature's bends.
- **Gauge tables** (`gaugeTable`, `gauge`): `Kernel.gaugeTables` ships the published standard thicknesses (inches) for steel (Manufacturers' Standard Gauge: 16 ga = 0.0598), aluminium (Brown & Sharpe) and stainless (U.S. Standard Gauge). Their bend radius (= T) and K (0.5) are **examples, not shop data**; the PropertyManager labels them "example — replace with your shop's values", and *Edit gauge table…* saves the shop's own table in the part (`gaugeTable` then holds the table object: `{ name, units, rows: [{ gauge, thickness, radius, kFactor | bendDeduction }] }`).
- **K above 0.5.** Older Anvil parts were made with K = 0.71 (the shop's steel constant). That constant belongs to the 90° formula MIB = k·T + 1.57·R, i.e. K = 0.71 / 1.5708 = 0.452; used as a true K it makes each bend 0.405·T longer. Parts keep the K they stored (and a saved part with no stored K keeps 0.71), but the flat pattern warns about a K outside 0–0.5 and gives the conversion.

`flatPattern` reports `size: [w, h]` (the blank's bounding box, shown as *Flat size W × H* in the Flatten dialog, the Sheet-Metal PropertyManager and the status bar), `note` (the parameters for the DXF / drawing note, e.g. `T=0.06 R=0.06 BD=0.1`) and `warnings`; each bend carries `ba`, `bd`, `method`, `source` (`sheet` or `custom`) and `label`, the shop bend note `Kernel.bendNoteText(bend, units, { prec }?)`: direction, angle and inside radius as the drawing dimensions read them, `UP 90° R.060` in inches (3 places, trailing zeros kept, no leading zero) and `DOWN 90° R1.5` in mm (up to 2 places, trailing zeros dropped). The flat DXF writes that text centred on each bend line and running along it, a quarter of the text height beside it (`Kernel.bendNotePlace(bend, h)` → `{ p, rot }`; DXF TEXT with rotation 50 and centre justification 72 = 1), so an end flange's note follows its vertical bend line instead of crossing the outline. In the app: *Base Flange/Tab* (new part) and *Sheet-Metal* (`UI.cmdSheetMetal`, also from the Flatten dialog, the Edge-Flange PropertyManager and Custom Properties) have the *Sheet Metal Gauges*, *Sheet Metal Parameters* and *Bend Allowance* groups (bend table grid with paste from a spreadsheet); Edge-Flange, Miter-Flange, Hem, Jog and Sketched Bend have *Custom Bend Allowance*. A Sketched Bend's record per line is an edge-flange record at the start of its bend strip (so the unfold is the same), and it moves earlier flange records on a bent side with that side; the unfold starts from the fixed side. *Base Flange/Tab* with no sketch asks for a plane or face, opens a sketch on it and comes back when the sketch is exited (an empty sketch is discarded), as SolidWorks does. The Edge-Flange relief group is *Custom Relief Type*. *Sketched Bend* on the Sheet Metal tab (`UI.cmdSketchedBend`) takes the sketch (the open one, the selected one or the last unconsumed one), a click on the face to keep fixed, Bend position, Reverse direction, Bend angle, the radius and Custom Bend Allowance, with a preview of what moves.

```js
p.units("in");
p.sheet(0.06, 0.06, 0.5, { bendAllowanceType: "deduction", bendDeduction: 0.1 });   // 0.060 sheet, R .060, BD .100
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -3, 4, 3).end().baseFlange();
p.flange(1.5, [[0, 0.06, 1.5]]);                                        // A = 4.12, B = 1.62: flat 4.12 + 1.62 − 0.1 = 5.64
p.flange(1, [[2, 0.06, 0]], { bendAllowance: { type: "deduction", value: 0.09 } });   // this flange's own BD
```

**Mitred corners.** `flange(L, edges, { miter: true })` (the *Miter Flange* ribbon button presets it) closes the corners of one feature wherever two selected edges share a corner. A 90° flange at a square corner does what SolidWorks' auto miter does, which is really a Butt corner with the bend closed (M03, M24, 2026-11-02): both walls stop square, gap/√2 short of the other wall's inside face (T + gap/√2 from the outer corner, gap = `miterGap`, or SolidWorks' default 0.01 in / 0.25 mm when it is absent or 0); each bend's end runs from the base corner at the bend's start to the wall's end at the bend's end, straight in (angle, t), a helicoid; in the flat every strip stays square out to the wall's end line, so the blank is a plus with a square notch at the wall ends, 12 corners on a tray. So blank × T is not the folded volume here, in SolidWorks either. Other angles and corners that are not square follow SolidWorks too (120° on a square corner, 90° on a 71.6° trapezoid corner, report 2026-11-02 5d, rfIS): the same default gap; each wall is cut square to itself along the line where the bisector plane, moved gap/2 off, meets its inside face; each bend's end runs straight in (angle, t) from the base corner to that line; in the flat each strip runs the wall's line on straight across its bend zone until the two strips' lines meet past the corner, so the blank is SolidWorks' to the digit and the folded part reads under blank × T as SolidWorks' does (8.1e-4 in³ at 120°). A hem and a Miter Flange's later stages keep the bisector mitre (each bend + wall clipped by that plane). Edges that are collinear or fold in opposite directions are not mitred.

**Flatten and the FeatureManager (lane DC).** A sheet part's tree shows a *Sheet-Metal* row just above the Base Flange (double-click or Edit Feature: the Sheet-Metal PropertyManager) and a *Flat-Pattern* folder at the end with *Flat-Pattern1*, greyed while the part is folded (suppressed). *Sheet Metal ▸ Flatten* (`UI.toggleFlatten(on?)`) toggles the part's flat state: `model.flatState: true` is saved with the document and is one undo step; the graphics area then shows `Kernel.flatBody(model, build, flat?)` (the blank T thick on the Base Flange's sketch plane, `{ body, flat, plane, h0, h1 }`) with dashed bend lines, the folded part's faces are not picked, and the flat pattern, DXF and mass properties still come from the folded part. Flat-Pattern1 ▸ Unsuppress / Suppress does the same; ▸ Edit Feature (`UI.cmdFlatPatternEdit`) shows the Flat-Pattern PropertyManager: *Fixed face* (click a flat face: the flat is laid in its plane with that face where it is; empty = the Base Flange's face) and *Grain direction* (click a straight edge: the flat turns so it runs along X), saved as `model.flatFixed` / `model.flatGrain` and followed by the flat DXF, Flatten, the drawing's flat-pattern view and its bend table (one rigid motion, never mirrored: the bend notes do not change); Anvil always merges faces and keeps bend arcs, so those boxes are not shown; the flat size; ▸ Flat Pattern… is the old dialog (`UI.cmdFlatten`). A feature made while flat goes in above Flat-Pattern1 and acts on the folded part (the flat is redrawn), as in SolidWorks; *Insert ▸ Sheet Metal ▸ Flatten* toggles like the ribbon button (checked while flat). OpenCascade builds a sketch jog with exact bends. In the Cut-Extrude PropertyManager of a sheet part, *Link to thickness* (Direction 1, checked for a new cut, with *Normal cut*) makes the cut Blind at the sheet thickness; the Sheet-Metal PropertyManager moves those depths with T and the kernel reads T for `linkT` cuts anyway.

**Normal cut.** A cut sketched on a plane that is not parallel to the sheet (a face of another wall, a tilted reference plane) would normally leave an oblique window whose walls follow the sketch direction. With `normalCut: true` (the *Normal cut* checkbox in the Cut-Extrude PropertyManager's *Sheet Metal* group, on by default for a new cut on a sheet-metal part) the kernel intersects the plain tool with the body, takes the outlines of the sheet faces the tool enters through, and re-extrudes them straight through the thickness, so the window has walls square to the sheet and the same size on both faces — what a laser or punch would cut in the flat. A blind normal cut always goes through the sheet. When the sketch is parallel to the sheet the result is the plain cut; when the tool crosses no sheet face at all the plain tool is used and the feature gets the warning *Normal cut: the cut does not cross a sheet face; a plain cut was made.* The option is ignored on parts without sheet parameters.

```js
p.sheet(2, 2, 0.71);
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -60, 100, 60).end().baseFlange(2);
p.flange(30, [[100, 2, 30]]);                      // wall at x in [102,104]
// a 45° plane outside the wall: the 12 × 8 sketch lands as a 12 × 11.3 window with square walls
p.sketch({ custom: true, origin: [115, 3, 30], x: [0, 0, 1], y: [-0.7071, -0.7071, 0], n: [0.7071, -0.7071, 0] }, 0)
  .rect(-6, -4, 12, 8).cut("through", { reverse: true, normalCut: true });
```

**Bend reliefs.** `relief: "rect"` or `"obround"` cuts a slot from the base before the flange is added, at every flange end where the sheet continues beyond the end (a wall that stops part-way along a plate edge, or an edge that meets a tab or a wider region). The slot is `reliefWidth` wide along the edge (default T), starts `reliefDepth` inside the edge (default T/2) and runs out past the bend zone (to R + T + `reliefDepth`), through the full thickness; `"obround"` rounds the inner end. Ends at convex sheet corners and ends shared with another edge of the same feature (mitred or collinear) get no relief.
