The relief appears in the flat pattern as a notch beside the bend strip (the flange record carries `reliefs: [{ w, d0, d1, obround } | null]` per end, so both kernels' flats get it): `reliefWidth` wide past the flange end, from `reliefDepth` inside the edge line (plus the Material Inside / Outside trim) out to `reliefDepth` beyond the flat bend zone, max(BA, R + T) — so in the blank the relief always clears the whole bend allowance, even where BA is longer than the solid's slot. On the 16 ga channel (side flanges Material Inside, an end flange Material Inside with a Rectangle relief) each notch is T wide and 4.5·T deep and separates the end flange from the side flanges' bends. **Auto Relief** (SolidWorks): the sheet's `relief` (`"rect"` | `"obround"`) and `reliefRatio` are the relief of every Edge Flange / Miter Flange without a relief of its own (`relief` absent; hems and jogs never take it): ratio·T wide and ratio·T deep (`reliefWidth` / `reliefDepth` on the flange still win). A new part's *Base Flange/Tab* starts with Rectangular, ratio 0.5, as SolidWorks; a sheet without `relief` (every older part, every script that does not ask for it) has none, so older parts do not change. The *Auto Relief* group is in the Base Flange/Tab and Sheet-Metal PropertyManagers; the Edge-Flange's *Custom Relief Type* check (off for a new flange) gives the flange its own.

```js
p.sheet(2, 2, 0.71);
p.sketch("Top").polyline([[0, 0], [100, 0], [100, 30], [60, 30], [60, 60], [0, 60]], true).end().baseFlange(2); // L plate
p.flange(20, [[0, 2, 30], [50, 2, 0]], { miter: true });                 // two walls joined at the (0, 0) corner
p.flange(10, [[60, 2, -45]], { relief: "obround", reliefWidth: 2 });    // wall on the notch edge, relief where it meets the wider plate
```

**Break Corner / Corner Trim** (SolidWorks' *Corners ▸ Break-Corner/Corner-Trim*; `p.breakCorner(size, corners, { kind, faces })`, Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Break-Corner*). Rounds (`kind: "fillet"`, `size` = radius) or cuts off (`kind: "chamfer"`, the default, `size` = the distance along both edges) outside corners of the sheet's flat faces — the base flange, tabs, flange walls — through the thickness. `corners` are points on corner edges (the short edges through the thickness; re-found by position within 0.75·T, like flange edge picks); `faces` are points on flat sheet faces, each taking every outside corner of its face. A corner is a convex outline vertex of at most 150° whose two edges are free sheet edges, so corners where a wall meets its bend, corners inside bends and corners of holes are never broken; the corners a chamfer leaves (135°) can be broken again. It is a cut whose tool carries true cylinder records, so volumes are exact; the flat pattern shows each fillet as equal chords that the flat DXF writes as one `ARC` (both kernels; OpenCascade builds the arc exactly). A corner that is no longer there, a face without corners, a break longer than an edge or two that overlap on one edge are rebuild errors. A later flange on a broken edge takes the shortened edge. The PropertyManager has the Corner Edges / Faces box (a click on a corner edge or a face adds it, a second click removes it; a long edge is refused), the Break type icons and Distance / Radius; it previews the material that goes, remembers the type and size for the session and reopens with its picks from Edit Feature.

```js
p.units("in");
p.sheet(0.0598, 0.0598, 0.5);
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -3, 4, 3).end().baseFlange();
p.breakCorner(0.25, [[4, 0.0299, 0], [4, 0.0299, 3]], { kind: "fillet" });   // two R.25 corners: volume (12 − 2·(1 − π/4)·.0625)·T
p.breakCorner(0.125, [], { faces: [[2, 0.0598, 1.5]] });                     // the top face's two sharp corners chamfered .125
```

**Closed Corner** (SolidWorks' *Corners ▸ Closed Corner*; `p.closedCorner(faces, { cornerType, gap, ratio, coplanar })`, Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ Corners flyout ▸ *Closed Corner*). Where two flanges meet at a box corner and leave a gap, the end faces of one flange (the faces to extend: points on the narrow faces through the thickness where it stops) are extended to the next flange, whose inside face across the gap (the matching face, within 10·T + 2·R) is found automatically. Only the wall above the bend is extended unless `closeBend: true` (the PropertyManager's *Open bend region* unchecked, the default for a new corner there; in a script `closeBend: true`, absent = open so older scripts keep their geometry): the bend's end is then extended the same distance too, less whatever other material it meets and the gap in front of it, and in the flat that flange's bend strip runs on by the same length (the notch beside the tab closes; what is left at the corner is the relief square between the two strips). A corner without `closeBend` (every corner made before) keeps the bend region open. *Butt* stops the extended wall `gap` short of the matching wall's inside face and makes the matching wall end flush with the extended wall's outside; *Overlap* runs the extended wall `ratio`·T into the matching wall (1: flush with its outside) and stops the matching wall `gap` short of the extended wall's inside face; *Underlap* is Butt with the matching wall covering `ratio`·T of the end (1: the same as Butt). A matching wall that stops short (Bend Outside / Material Outside flanges, where both walls end at the corner) is extended across, one that runs past is trimmed. With `coplanar` (default) every end face in the picked face's plane that has a matching face is closed too. The flat needs nothing new: the extended walls unfold with their new outlines, so the corner notches close to the gap. A gap below 0.001 in (the walls would join and the flat could not unfold), a face that is no flange end (wider than 1.5·T) or has no flange across, a gap wider than the opening and an extension that would run into other material are rebuild errors. Both kernels (OpenCascade cuts and fuses the same planar prisms). The PropertyManager has Faces to Extend (a click on an end face adds it, a second removes it; a face that is not one says why), Corner type icons, Gap distance, Overlap/underlap ratio, Open bend region and Coplanar faces; it previews the added material (and, red, what is trimmed), remembers type / gap / ratio for the session and reopens with its picks from Edit Feature. Not built: corners between flanges that are not square to each other, Welded Corner.

```js
p.units("in");
p.sheet(0.0598, 0.0598, 0.5);
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -3, 4, 3).end().baseFlange();
[[2, 0.0598, 0], [4, 0.0598, 1.5], [2, 0.0598, 3], [0, 0.0598, 1.5]].forEach(function (e) { p.flange(1, [e], { position: "inside", lengthRef: "outer" }); });
// each later wall stops R short of the earlier one: close all four corners, the walls' ends .010 off the next wall
p.closedCorner([[0.0299, 0.6, 0.1196], [3.8804, 0.6, 2.9701], [0.0299, 0.6, 2.8804]], { cornerType: "butt", gap: 0.01 });
```

**Cross-Break** (SolidWorks' *Cross-Break*; Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Cross-Break*). `p.crossBreak([x, y, z], { radius, angle, reverse, name })` marks an X, corner to corner, on the planar sheet face at the point (default radius the sheet's bend radius, angle 1°). It is cosmetic: the solid, the volume and the blank do not change. `p.sketch(...).line(...).crossBreak(face, opts)` uses the sketch's lines instead of the X (the PropertyManager's *Edit Cross-Break Sketch*). In the flat DXF the lines are on BEND with one `CROSS BREAK UP 1° R.060` note on NOTES; `reverse`, or the face on the other side of the sheet, reads DOWN.

**Forming Tools** (SolidWorks' Design Library forming tools; Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Forming Tool* flyout, `UI.cmdFormingTool(id)`). `p.formingTool(tool, [x, y, z], { at, angle, flip, ...dims, name })` presses a tool into the flat sheet face at the point: `dimple` (`diameter` at the face, `height`, `sideAngle` from square), `extrudedHole` (`holeDiameter` inside the collar, `height` of the collar, inside `radius`), `louver` (`length` of the lance, `width` hinge to lance, `height` of the open edge, hinge `radius`), `lance` (a bridge lance: `length`, `width`, `height` > T, `legAngle`), `rib` (`length` end to end, `width`, `depth`, `sideAngle`; round ends). `at: [u, v]` places the centre in the face's sketch frame instead (Top face: u = X, v = −Z), `angle` turns the tool's length direction, `flip` forms it into the face. Defaults: `Kernel.FORMING_TOOLS` / `Kernel.formingToolDefaults(id, units)`. The form keeps the sheet's thickness (a shell; exact volume) and is refused when it does not fit on its face (off an edge, over a hole, across a bend). The flat shows the plain blank with the tool's outline and a centre mark on layer FORM and a note (`DIMPLE Ø.75 X .125 UP`) on NOTES; lance cuts are 0.01 in (0.254 mm) slits on HOLES, and an extruded hole gets its developed pre-pierce hole (collar + the 90° bend's BA back from the bend start). Feature JSON: `{ type: 'formingTool', tool: 'dimple', face: [2, 0.0598, 1.5], at: [2, -1.5], angle: 0, flip: false, diameter: 0.75, height: 0.125, sideAngle: 30, name: 'Dimple1' }`. OpenCascade does not build it (Anvil's solid is kept).

```js
p.units("in");
p.sheet(0.0598, 0.0598, 0.5);
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -3, 4, 3).end().baseFlange();
p.formingTool("louver", [2, 0.0598, 1.5], { angle: 90 });
p.formingTool("extrudedHole", [0.6, 0.0598, 0.6], { holeDiameter: 0.201 });
```

**Rip and Tab** (SolidWorks' *Rip* and *Base Flange/Tab* on an existing sheet; Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Rip* / *Base Flange/Tab*). Rip (`p.rip(edges, gap, { direction })`) cuts along each picked corner edge (the outside or the inside edge of a corner where two walls meet), the corner's whole length as SolidWorks: past the walls through a floor the corner runs on across. `side1` / `side2`: one wall keeps the corner square and the other is cut back `gap` from its inside face (removes gap·T per length); `both`: both walls stop square T + gap/√2 from the outer corner, and a floor loses that square (`gap` is what is left between the two inside corners; off 90°, T + gap·sin(θ/2)). Wall 1 / wall 2 follow the edge direction d (largest component positive) with (n1 × n2)·d > 0. A corner already open on one side (a Closed Corner's gap) is ripped on that side whatever the direction: the gap widens to `gap`. T is the sheet's, or (no sheet: a shelled part) measured at the edge. A registered cut, planar prisms on both kernels; the PropertyManager has Edges to Rip (a click adds / removes an edge, one that is no corner says why), Change Direction (the last picked edge) and Rip gap. Tab: Base Flange/Tab on a part that already has a Base Flange opens the Tab PropertyManager (no sheet parameters; the thickness is linked) and makes `Tab1`, a closed sketch on a flat sheet face extruded T into the sheet (flush with that face) and merged; Reverse direction takes it away from the face. A sketch plane that is no face of the part is a rebuild error. The flat pattern includes the tab.

**Insert Bends and Convert to Sheet Metal** (Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Insert Bends* / *Convert to Sheet Metal* / *No Bends*). Insert Bends finds every convex edge between two flat faces where two plates exactly T thick meet (inside corner T behind, both plates running on past R + T) and replaces the sharp corner with a bend of inside radius R: the outer faces stay where they were, the inside corner fills to the R arc. The plates must form a tree from the fixed face's plate: an unripped box is refused (rip its corners first). Where two bends meet at a square corner each stops R + T short and the (R + T) cube there is cut out (a square corner relief); a Rip takes the floor's corner, so after one they do not meet. The feature (`insertBends` / `convertSM`, `sheetBase: true`) carries the fixed face; the sheet (`model.sheet`) carries T, R and the bend allowance, so the Sheet-Metal row, Flatten, Flat-Pattern1 and the flat DXF work as on a Base Flange part, and later flanges, hems, cuts and rips do too. The bend records are edge-flange shaped (Material Inside from the plate nearer the fixed face), so the flat unfolds them as flanges: the ripped 2 × 2 × 1 box (T = R = .06, K .5) gives one blank 1.76 + 2 × .88 + 2·BA = 3.8027 square, its four corners off (SolidWorks' cross). Convert to Sheet Metal = Shell (the faces not reached from the fixed face across the bend edges, T inward) + Rip (`both`, gap) + Insert Bends on those edges. A part already T thick at the fixed face (walls of a thin channel or a shelled box) skips the Shell; one 2·T thick or less but not T is refused with its wall thickness. *Collect All Bends* (the button under the Bend Edges list, enabled once the fixed face is picked; `Kernel.convertSMCollect`) fills the list: every sharp convex edge with the inside corner T behind both faces (the outside corner of two walls T thick), reached from the fixed face breadth-first; an edge that would close a ring (a box's vertical corner) is left for Convert to rip, so the part is the one the same edges picked by hand give, and the edges are stored as hand picks. When none qualify at the typed T but do at the part's own wall, T is set to the wall. Rounded bends (concentric faces R and R + T joining two walls T thick) are collected too (stored as a point on the bend's outside face) and keep their own radius. A solid block and an inside fixed face collect nothing and say so. In the PM, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y undo / redo changes to the Bend Edges list (a click, Clear, Collect) before cancelling the command. *No Bends* (`model.noBends`, display state, undoable) shows the part without its bends; the flat refuses meanwhile. **Rounded bends** (lane rfIH): Insert Bends (and Convert, on a part already T thick) recognises every pair of concentric cylinders, inner R > 0 and outer R + T, on one axis over the same angle (under 180°), with a flat wall T thick running on tangent from each end; the solid is left exactly as it is and the bend's record carries the part's R (the PM's radius is for sharp corners only, as in SolidWorks), so the flat uses the part's K-factor / BA / BD with that radius. Sharp and rounded corners mix in one part; a Z's web (the outside of one bend, the inside of the next) is one wall. Found from the facets' cylinder records, which Anvil's arcs, fillets and STEP import carry (OpenCascade: `shapeToCSG` with records). Both kernels (OpenCascade: exact bend arcs). Bends meeting at a corner that is not square (flared walls, a hexagonal tray) each stop where their bend zone leaves the other's and their corner kite is cut out over that length. Convert of a solid whose edges are filleted (radius r > T) shells them into bends of inside radius r − T; in the PM a click on a rounded face adds it to Bend Edges, and Insert Bends lists the rounded bends it found. Convert's *Reverse thickness* (`reverse: true` on the feature) thickens the kept faces outward instead, so the solid's faces become the inside of the sheet. Not built: a corner relief other than these.

```js
p.units("in"); p.sheet(0.06, 0.06, 0.5);
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, 0, 4, 2).end().baseFlange();
p.sketch("Top", 0.06).rect(4, 0, 1, 1).end().tab();      // + 1 × 1 × .06, flat 5 × 2
```

**Unfold / Fold** (SolidWorks' *Unfold* / *Fold*; `p.unfold(fixedFace, bends)` / `p.fold(bends)`, Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Unfold*, *Fold*). Unfold lays the picked bends (or all of them) flat about the fixed face, which stays where it was: with every bend picked the part becomes the flat pattern's blank × T, exactly; with some, the walls past the bends left folded keep their shape and ride along. Features after it work on the flat. Fold folds the part back: what was cut between the two is taken from the folded part, each flat face's piece moved back rigidly and each bend strip's through-cut wrapped round its bend by the bend allowance (the flat length s goes to the angle A·s/BA, the same map a cut made before a flange takes), and the cuts ride on the bend records, so the flat pattern of the folded part shows each cut exactly as it was made (a 0.25 × 1.0 slot cut flat across a bend line comes back as a 0.25 × 1.0 hole; a round hole as one CIRCLE). Only through cuts cross a bend; blind cuts are carried on flat faces; material added between Unfold and Fold is not folded. One Unfold is open at a time (a second needs a Fold first). The PropertyManagers: Unfold has Fixed face (a click on a flat face) and Bends to unfold (a click on a bend adds it, a second removes it; *Collect All Bends*); Fold has Bends to fold (a click on a flat bend strip; *Collect All Bends*; none = every unfolded bend); both preview the resulting part (built in the worker) and reopen with their picks from Edit Feature. Kernel helpers: `Kernel.sheetBends(model, build, mesh)` (the bends with the `at` points the features store), `sheetBendAt(bends, P, T)`, `unfoldedBendAt(unfoldRecord, P, T)`, `unfoldPreview(model, build, f)`. While the part is unfolded its flat pattern (DXF, drawing view) keeps the unfolded bends' lines and notes, as SolidWorks does. OpenCascade builds both exactly (the folded part's faces moved flat, the cut wrapped through an exact cylindrical cutter); a Fold of some bends only stays Anvil's (*unsupported*).
**Unfold / Fold** (SolidWorks' *Unfold* / *Fold*; `p.unfold(fixedFace, bends)` / `p.fold(bends)`, Sheet Metal ribbon ▸ *Unfold*, *Fold*). Unfold lays the picked bends (or all of them) flat about the fixed face, which stays where it was: with every bend picked the part becomes the flat pattern's blank × T, exactly; with some, the walls past the bends left folded keep their shape and ride along. Features after it work on the flat. Fold folds the part back: what was cut between the two is taken from the folded part, each flat face's piece moved back rigidly and each bend strip's through-cut wrapped round its bend by the bend allowance (the flat length s goes to the angle A·s/BA, the same map a cut made before a flange takes), and the cuts ride on the bend records, so the flat pattern of the folded part shows each cut exactly as it was made (a 0.25 × 1.0 slot cut flat across a bend line comes back as a 0.25 × 1.0 hole; a round hole as one CIRCLE). A blind cut across a bend strip folds back at its depth, and material added between Unfold and Fold folds too (lane DJ): a Boss-Extrude or Tab on the flat is cut into layers of constant depth and each wrapped round the bend the same way, radially (a tab in the sheet's plane past the part's edge bends exactly as the sheet does, so with K = 0.5 a 0.5 × 0.5 × T tab across the bend line adds exactly 0.25·T on OpenCascade, and the flat pattern of the folded part shows the tab and runs the bend line across it; a boss on the inside of a bend is compressed toward the bend's axis, refused when it would reach past it). Several Unfolds can be open: a second Unfold folds the open one back (with what was cut or added since) and unfolds its bends and the new ones together; each Fold of some bends folds just those, a Fold with none picked folds every open one, in any order. The second Unfold's PropertyManager offers the bends still folded, picked where the part shows them. The PropertyManagers: Unfold has Fixed face (a click on a flat face) and Bends to unfold (a click on a bend adds it, a second removes it; *Collect All Bends*); Fold has Bends to fold (a click on a flat bend strip; *Collect All Bends*; none = every unfolded bend); both preview the resulting part (built in the worker) and reopen with their picks from Edit Feature. Kernel helpers: `Kernel.sheetBends(model, build, mesh)` (the bends with the `at` points the features store), `sheetBendAt(bends, P, T)`, `unfoldedBendAt(unfoldRecord, P, T)`, `unfoldPreview(model, build, f)`. While the part is unfolded its flat pattern (DXF, drawing view) keeps the unfolded bends' lines and notes, as SolidWorks does. OpenCascade builds both exactly (the folded part's faces moved flat, the cut wrapped through an exact cylindrical cutter; added material and blind cuts through cylindrical shells at their depth), including a Fold of some bends, an Unfold of a bend that has a cut across it and a second Unfold; a round hole cut in the flat across the bend and into both flats (rfFT M26) folds back with its exact circle on the flats and its exact wrapped ellipse on the bend (the through void is taken out of the cut as an exact prism, so no chord-to-arc crescent is left to wrap). A blind pocket or a boss that runs onto a bend keeps the flat whole (the metal left at mid-thickness rides on the bend record). Not yet: a partial Unfold of a folded part that had material added across a bend leaves out the added part of that strip.

```js
p.units("in");
p.sheet(0.0598, 0.0598, 0.5);
p.sketch("Top").rect(0, -4, 6, 4).end().baseFlange();
p.flange(1.5, [[3, 0.0598, 0]], { position: "inside", lengthRef: "outer" });
p.unfold([1, 0.0598, 2], null);                                   // the base top stays put; every bend flat
p.sketch("Top", 1).rect(2.5, -0.1741, 1, 0.25).cut("through", { reverse: true });   // a slot centred on the bend line
p.fold();                                                          // the slot now wraps the bend
```

### Geometry Nesting (Transform tab)

*Transform ▸ Geometry Nesting* (`UI.cmdTNesting()`, Mastercam's nesting) lays parts out on sheets for the laser or punch.

- **Parts**: *Add flat pattern* (the open sheet-metal part's flat), *Add sketch* (the closed profiles of the open sketch, or of a sketch selected in the FeatureManager: each outer loop with the loops right inside it as holes is one part), *Add DXF…* (the file's closed profiles, converted to the document's units); a quantity each, ✕ to remove. The first part added is nested at once; a quantity typed re-nests when typing pauses (or on Enter). The list and the values stay while the document is open (re-open the PM to Save DXF again or add a part); a new or opened document starts with an empty list.
- **Sheet**: 48 × 96, 60 × 120, 48 × 120 in or custom (Length X × Width Y, in the document's units; an mm document names the presets "1219 × 2438 mm (48 × 96 in)"), material and thickness (a note kept on the Nest document).
- **Enter** in any box of this PropertyManager nests (the values apply); only the ✓ button makes the Nest document, since that leaves the part for a new document.
- **Parameters**: part spacing, sheet margin, rotation (none, 90°, 45° or every n°), allow mirror, nest in holes (small parts go into big parts' holes when they fit), start corner (lower / upper, left / right), direction (rows along X or columns along Y), max sheets, accuracy (the raster cell: the sheet's longer side / 1000, 2000 or 3000).
- **Results**: *Nest* (re-nests live as values change while a nest takes under 0.4 s), the sheet with its parts count and utilisation (◀ / ▶ between sheets), a preview in the PropertyManager and one over the graphics area with a tab per sheet, *Save DXF* (one file per sheet, `<part>-nest-sheet<k>.dxf`, LINE / ARC / CIRCLE on layer CUT, R12 or R2000, sheet coordinates with 0,0 at the lower-left corner).
- **✓** makes a new document *Nest*: one sketch per sheet (`Sheet1`, `Sheet2`, … on the Top plane, side by side along X), the placed geometry on layer CUT and the sheet outline as construction lines; `model.nest` records the sheet, spacing, quantities and utilisation.

Engine (`nest.js`, no DOM, runs under Node):

```js
var part = Nest.part({ name: "Bracket", loops: flat.loops });          // outer + holes; point loops are fitted back to lines / arcs
var res = Nest.run([{ name: "Bracket", loops: part.loops, qty: 12 }], {
  width: 96, height: 48, spacing: 0.25, margin: 0.5, rotation: 90, mirror: false, holes: true,
  maxSheets: 10, corner: "bl", direction: "x" });                      // cell, tol: raster cell and chord tolerance
res.placements;   // [{ part, name, copy, sheet, x, y, angle, mirror }]: p -> rot(angle)·(mirror ? (-x, y) : p) + (x, y)
res.sheets;       // [{ index, width, height, placements, partArea, utilization (%) }]; res.unplaced, res.utilization
Nest.check(res);  // { ok, minGap, problems }: exact polygon distance / containment tests, margin
Nest.sheetDXF(res, 0, "in", { version: "R2000" });
Nest.partsFromLoops(Kernel.sketchProfile(sketch).loops, "Sketch1");
```

How it works: every orientation of a part (rotation steps × mirror) is rasterised once on a grid of square cells. A cell is marked when its centre lies inside the part, or when the whole cell comes within spacing / 2 + the chord tolerance of the part's chord polygon, so two parts whose marked cells are disjoint are at least the spacing apart (true arcs included). Holes stay free inside that band. The margin is kept exactly: a placement must have the part's exact extents inside the sheet less the margin. Copies go in order of priority and then area, largest first. Each copy goes on the first sheet where it fits, at the bottom-left-most free position (counted from the start corner, in the fill direction) over its orientations. A table of free and occupied runs lets the search jump across filled stretches, and every orientation's search resumes where its last one stopped. Several deterministic strategies run: bottom-left, lowest top edge, and each orientation preferred first. The best is kept: most parts placed, then fewest sheets, then the shortest used length.


## Sketching

The sketcher behaves like SolidWorks' (`ui-sketch.js`, `ui-relations.js`, `sketch-solver.js`).

- **No grid.** Nothing is drawn and nothing snaps to an invisible grid. A point lands where it is clicked, and the sketcher infers instead, in this order: an existing sketch point within 9 px (end, midpoint, centre, corner, quadrant, intersection, the origin; an orange ring) is taken exactly; then a point on a line, circle or arc within 7 px (rectangle, polygon and polyline edges too; a slot's are not); otherwise the point stays under the cursor, aligned (within 6 px) horizontally / vertically with the previous point or with any sketch point the cursor passed over (dotted blue inference lines). Each free coordinate is rounded to the roundest value inside the pixel under the cursor (25.4 rather than 25.3971), so nothing moves visibly. *View ▸ Sketch Grid* (off by default) brings back a grid that free coordinates snap to, like SolidWorks' Grid/Snap; points, curves and alignment still win, and the grid step follows the zoom. Every tolerance is in screen pixels at the cursor, so a snap behaves the same at any zoom, and two targets at the same distance go to the first in sketch order.
- **Snap to Model** (*View ▸ Snap to Model*, on by default; `App.view.snapModel`). Sketching on a face of a solid, or on a plane above or below one, points also snap onto the model, projected onto the sketch plane: vertices, straight-edge midpoints, centres and quadrants of round edges square to the sketch (holes, round ends), the area centre of each planar face parallel to the sketch, the part centre (the middle of the part's outline in the sketch) and the centre of mass. They come after sketch points (a sketch point at the same spot wins) and before sketch curves; a point on a model edge comes last, and where one inference line crosses a model edge the point lands on the crossing. Model snaps show a magenta ring and a label (Vertex, Midpoint, Center, Face center, Part center, …), become points the dotted inference lines can align with, and add no relation (sketches have no external references). Computed once per rebuild and sketch plane.
- **Inference while drawing.** Relation glyphs ride on the cursor, the status bar shows a live readout (L / A, R, W, …) next to the coordinates, the entity under the cursor is pre-highlighted, the cursor is a pencil with the active tool's glyph (an arrow with a dimension glyph for Smart Dimension), the active sketch's end and centre points are drawn as small dots in the entity's colour, and the inferred relations are added: Coincident where an end lands on a point or curve, Concentric for centres, Midpoint, Horizontal / Vertical, Tangent / Perpendicular for a line leaving the end of a line or arc (6 px lock). As in SolidWorks, Symmetric is never inferred while drawing (Mirror Entities, Dynamic Mirror and Add Relations make it), and only the points a click placed infer Coincident / Concentric / Midpoint / Tangent, for every drawing tool: a Corner Rectangle started on a line gets one Coincident for the clicked corner, not one for each corner that happens to lie on the line, and a Centerpoint Straight Slot's computed far end or a polygon's other corners infer nothing where they land (`SketchSolver.infer(sketch, i, { placed: [[x, y], …], symmetric })`; ui-sketch.js `addEntity` passes the tool's `st.pts`, plus a sliding tangent line's start; the preview glyphs follow the same rule; a chain's closing click is not in `st.pts`, so the closing segment infers at every point; without `placed` every point of the entity infers, as for scripts and paste). With the Line tool near a circle or arc, the end locks onto the exact point where a line from the first point touches it (within 6 px, Tangent glyph), and a line started on a circle or arc locks onto its tangent there; either click adds Tangent with the end's Coincident (when both ends could lock, the end's tangent point wins and the start keeps its Coincident). A relation that would over-define the sketch is skipped. A dragged end point or circle / arc centre dropped on another point (9 px), a line's or rectangle edge's midpoint (Midpoint) or a curve (7 px, never its own entity or a curve it already lies on) merges with it the same way; a merge that would over-define the sketch is refused with a status-bar message and the point stays where it was dropped.
- **Line tool.** Click point after point, or press and drag for a single line; the chain ends where it closes on its first point, on a double-click (the Line tool stays), or on Esc (which also puts the tool down); a right-click while drawing opens SolidWorks' short menu (*End Chain (double-click)*, *Select*, *Smart Dimension*, *Exit Sketch*; `UI.chainMenu(x, y)`). A double-click ends the chain without the stub its second click would make, as in SolidWorks. A line started on a circle or arc starts where it is clicked, with Coincident, as in SolidWorks. **Tangent start** (off by default; **T** with the Line tool toggles it, also after the start is clicked, and it stays as set for the session; `UI.setLineTangentStart(on)`, `UI.lineTangentStart()`): a line started on a circle or arc (the on-curve snap, not one of its points) stays tangent to it like Mastercam's Line Endpoints with *Tangent*: the start follows the cursor round the curve and the click adds Tangent with the start's Coincident. **N** flips the sliding start to the other tangent point (Mastercam's choice of tangent; continuity then keeps it on that side), and a length typed while the start slides is measured from the sliding tangent point (a typed angle picks the tangent point whose line passes nearer the cursor). A chain that goes on from a point where the previous segment ends on a circle or arc locks onto the tangent there too and gets Tangent with the curve (the solver follows the chain's Coincidents to it); near its start a tangent line hugs its curve, so there the tangent lock beats the snap onto the curve, while a pick on the curve away from the start still lands on it. `A` (or moving back over the end point) switches the next segment to a tangent arc; after it the chain goes on with lines.
- **Typed values while drawing.** With a point placed, typing a number opens a box at the cursor for the live values — L and A for a line, W and H for a rectangle, R for a circle, the chord (L, A) then R for a 3 Point Arc, … Tab moves to the next value, Enter places the point, Esc closes the box; a value may be shop input (`1-1/2`, `3/8`, `25mm`, `2*3/8`, see *Units*) or an equation. A circle offers R and Ø (Tab to Ø for a hole typed by its diameter; typing one clears the other), a polygon Ø (its *Circle diameter*, as the PM shows it) and A, an arc slot R, then A (the sweep), then W, an ellipse R and A (major), then R (minor), and a partial ellipse then A (the sweep), and a typed radius, diameter or slot width is the entity's exact value. A typed value holds while the mouse supplies the rest (type a length, then swing the line round), and each typed size becomes a driving dimension unless it would over-define the sketch. *Tools ▸ Sketch Settings ▸ Enable on-screen numeric input on entity creation* (off by default, kept per browser in `localStorage` `anvil.sketchNumericInput`; `UI.sketchNumericInput(on?)`) gives every entity drawn its driving dimensions, typed or not.
- **3 Point Arc** (Sketch tab; `A` twice): start, end, then a point the arc passes through; from the end of a line or arc it locks tangent to it as the cursor comes near (Tangent glyph). Three points in a line are refused. **Centerpoint Arc** gives the same tangent guidance (move the centre out square to a line or arc end) and sweeps the way the mouse moves.
- **Smart Dimensions.** See *The workspace* for placing them. A first click on a point that lies on no curve (a circle's or a slot's centre, a sketch point) starts a distance: the second click names the other point or line. A slot's typed length and width are placed outside it (beside its side, beyond its first end). The Modify box looks like SolidWorks' (a *Modify* title, ✓ / ✗ / Rebuild buttons, then the value with its unit and spin arrows; Rebuild previews the typed value): the wheel over it, the arrows or ↑ / ↓ step the value by 1 mm (0.1 in, 1°), ×10 with Ctrl, ÷10 with Alt, and the sketch follows live; Esc restores the old value and only the final value makes an undo step (`UI.spinDimEdit(sign, mods)` from code). The box shows the value rounded to 4 decimals; ✓, Enter or a click elsewhere with that text untouched changes nothing (no undo step, the exact value kept: a √13 line stays 3.6055513, not 3.6056). With one sketch entity selected (one that has a dimension of its own: a line, circle, arc, rectangle, slot …) or two, Smart Dimension starts from them: the dimension (or the distance / angle between the two) is on the cursor and the next click places it. Overlapping labels slide apart, and a label that would sit on another dimension's extension or dimension line slides clear of it too (the full layout is settled 150 ms after the view stops moving; `UI.dimLabelClashes()` counts the ones left); dragging a dimension's drag dot (Select tool) drives it like typing. A dimension edit solves only the part of the sketch connected to it.
- **Sketch Fillet** of two lines meeting at a corner (also the corners of a Line-tool chain; a rectangle first becomes four related lines) makes trimmed lines and a tangent arc with Tangent / Coincident relations and a driving R dimension. The vanished corner becomes a **virtual sharp**: a point drawn with thin extension lines to the lines, which keeps the corner's relations and the lines' length / dx / dy dimensions, so a dimensioned rectangle stays Fully Defined after filleting; it survives scripts (`.sharp([i])`).
- **Offset Entities** keeps circles, rectangles, slots and polygons as true entities, offsets lines and arcs too, and with *Add dimensions* (on by default) adds a driving offset dimension: the radial distance for circles and arcs, the parallel distance for lines; an offset rectangle or polygon comes out fully defined. **Extend Entities** (next to Trim) lengthens the end of a line or arc nearest the click to the first entity in its way; hovering previews the extension in orange. **Mirror Entities** mirrors the entities in its *Entities to Mirror* box (the selection when it opens plus entities clicked while it is open; a second click removes one; nothing is mirrored by default) about the sketch Y or X axis or a line: pick it in the list, or click the *Mirror about* box (outlined pink) and then a line. About a sketch line it adds Symmetric relations and carries the dimensions along. While Mirror Entities or Linear Sketch Pattern is open, a plain click selects into its box instead of opening the entity's own PropertyManager. Sketch circles, arcs and slots are drawn as true curves at any zoom: the viewport tessellates them for the screen (a chord sagitta of about 0.15 px, 32 to 2048 segments per turn) and redraws them finer as you zoom in, independently of the model's chord tolerance that the solid itself is built with. **Linear Sketch Pattern** repeats the entities in its *Entities to Pattern* box only (the selection when it opens, plus whatever is clicked while it is open; nothing selected means nothing is patterned), each copy spacing away from the seed along the direction angle, and with *Add spacing dimensions* (on by default) adds a driving distance from the seed's centre (or a line's start) to the same point on its first copy in each direction, so the spacing reads seed-to-copy and editing it moves the pattern.
- **Trim Entities** has SolidWorks' five options (the last one used is remembered). *Power trim* (the default): press and drag, and a grey trail follows the cursor; every entity segment it crosses goes at once, back to its neighbouring intersections (lines, arcs, circles, rectangle edges, slots; an entity nothing crosses is deleted), one undo step per drag. Pressing on the free end of a line or an arc and dragging extends or shortens it along itself; it lands exactly on an entity in its way (within 8 px) with a Coincident. *Corner* extends or trims two lines / arcs until they meet, keeping the sides clicked (the result is previewed dashed). *Trim away inside* / *outside*: click two bounding entities, then each entity to cut between them or outside them. *Trim to closest* (and a click in Power trim) removes the segment under the cursor. In every option the part that would go is highlighted in orange before the click. Trimmed pieces keep the relations that still hold (Horizontal, Tangent, a point on the piece it lies on, ...), new ends get a Coincident where they were cut, and dimensions are re-pointed. On a 90-entity sketch with relations a Power trim move costs about 1 ms on average (10 ms at worst, when it trims), and the sketch is redrawn once per frame.
- **Relations and status.** Relation icons sit clear of the dimension labels and of each other (chain-end Coincidents are not shown); hovering one highlights it, right-click ▸ Delete removes it. Display/Delete Relations lists conflicts first, follows the hover and the keyboard, and filters by a clicked entity. Conflict messages name the dimension or relation that blocks a change. Sketches show (-) when under defined and (+) when over defined in the FeatureManager.
- **Exiting.** The confirmation corner (top right of the graphics area) has *Exit Sketch* and *Discard Changes and Exit*, which asks first when the sketch was changed. Right-click in a sketch opens the sketch menu (Smart Dimension, Line, Add Relation, Display/Delete Relations, Construction Geometry, Delete, Zoom to Fit, Exit Sketch, Discard Changes and Exit). Inside a sketch each action is its own undo step.
- **Dragging** solves only the connected part of the sketch, once per frame (a 55-entity sketch: 10.9 → 3.6 ms per move, 0.6 ms in an unconnected piece); dragging a circle or arc edge changes its radius. A slot's end cap (an arc centred on the end of a construction line or arc and Tangent to the slot's sides; `SketchSolver.slotCap`) moves that end instead: its centre follows the mouse and its radius holds, so the slot stretches with its width kept (`drag(…, { cap: true })`); dragging a slot's side holds its centre line (`slotAxisOf`, `drag(…, { hold })`), so the width changes symmetrically (rfY).
- **Sketch Settings** (*Tools ▸ Sketch Settings*, checkable, kept per browser in `localStorage` `anvil.sketchSettings`; `UI.sketchSettings.get(k) / set(k, on) / toggle(k) / reset()`): *Automatic Relations* (`autoRelations`, on: off adds no inferred relation and shows no inference glyphs; a Line chain's segments still share their ends), *Automatic Solve* (`autoSolve`, on: off makes a drag move without solving, as No Solve Move), *No Solve Move* (`noSolveMove`: a drag moves the point or entity alone, its relations and dimensions are left unsolved until something else solves the sketch), *Enable Snapping* (`snapping`, on: off places points under the cursor with no point / curve snaps, inference lines or drop targets, as Ctrl held; the sketch grid still rounds), *Detach Segment on Drag* (`detachOnDrag`: a line or arc dragged whole loses the Coincident / Midpoint on its ends and Tangent with an entity it shared an end with), *Override Dims on Drag/Move* (`overrideDims`: the dimensions on the dragged entity let go during the drag and drive again at the new values), *Ghost image on drag* (`ghostOnDrag`, on, rfAY: while sketch geometry is dragged every entity that has moved is drawn faded where it was when the drag began, until the release; `UI.sketchGhostCount()`).
- **Rapid Sketch** (rfAY; Sketch tab toggle, off by default, kept as `rapidSketch` in `anvil.sketchSettings`; `UI.toggleRapidSketch(on?)`, `UI.rapidSketchOn()`): with it on and no sketch open the sketch-entity tools are not greyed; picking one arms it (`UI.rapidSketchArmed()`): planes and planar faces light up under the cursor and the first click on one opens a sketch there without turning the view, the entity's first point where the click was. While a drawing tool is on and nothing is in progress, another plane / face lights up under the cursor (not near the sketch's own geometry, not the sketch's own plane) and a click there exits this sketch (kept, or discarded when empty) and opens one on it with the same tool, the entity starting there. Esc (or putting the tool down) ends it; turning Rapid Sketch off disarms a waiting tool.
- **Quick Snaps** (Sketch tab flyout, and the right-click menu while a Line chain is drawn; `UI.quickSnap(kind | null)`, `UI.QUICK_SNAPS`): Point, Center Point, Midpoint, Quadrant Point, Intersection, Nearest, Tangent, Perpendicular, Parallel (hover a line first), Horizontal/Vertical Lines, Horizontal/Vertical to Points, Length (the grid step), Grid, Angle (15°). The next pick snaps to that kind only, within 20 px (hovering a line or circle takes its midpoint, centre or quadrant), then inference goes back to normal; picking one with the Select tool starts the Line tool, as in SolidWorks.
- **SketchXpert** (*Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ SketchXpert*, and *SketchXpert…* in the *Make Dimension Driven?* question). *Diagnose* lists the solutions, each a set of relations / dimensions whose deletion leaves an over-defined sketch solvable and not over defined (single items first; pairs only when no single deletion works); ◀ ▶ step through them with what would go highlighted, *Accept* deletes it in one undo step. *Manual Repair* lists every relation and dimension involved with its own *Delete*. `SketchSolver.diagnose(sketch, { max, maxCandidates, pairs })` → `{ over, candidates: [{ src, index, name, state }], solutions: [{ remove: [{ src: 'rel' | 'dim', index }], names, status, dof }] }` (pure: trials run on copies with the items suppressed), `SketchSolver.diagnoseApply(sketch, remove)`.
- **Scan Equal** (*Tools ▸ Relations ▸ Scan Equal*): lines of equal length and arcs / circles of equal radius within the *Tolerance* (0.001 in, 0.01 mm by default; *Find* scans again), one checked row per set; *Set Equal* or ✓ adds Equal from each set's first member to the others (one already equal is skipped). `SketchSolver.scanEqual(sketch, { tol, noConstruction, entities })` → `{ groups: [{ kind: 'line' | 'radius', value, members }] }`, `SketchSolver.applyEqual(sketch, members)`.

## Sketch tab

### Line, Centerline, Midpoint Line, rectangles, Point (`ui-sketch-lines.js`)

- **Insert Line.** Line, Centerline and Midpoint Line (the Line flyout) open *Insert Line* at once, and it stays open while you draw: *Message*, *Orientation* (As sketched, Horizontal, Vertical, Angle), *Options* (For construction, Infinite length, Midpoint line) and, for a set orientation, *Parameters* (Length, and Angle for Angle) that drive the next line (a length of 0 follows the cursor). Centerline is For construction; Midpoint Line makes the first click the line's midpoint (Midpoint relation where it lands on a sketch point or the origin). As sketched chains; a set orientation, a midpoint line or an infinite line is one line per two clicks. Infinite length draws the line across the whole graphics area through the two points, to be trimmed back. ✓, ✗ or Esc puts the tool down and closes the PropertyManager.
- **Parallel Line** (Line flyout; `UI.cmdParallelLine()`, like Mastercam's Line Parallel): pick a sketch line (or a rectangle / polygon / polyline edge) or a straight model edge, then place the new line *Through point* (the usual inference, model snaps included), at a *Distance* (Flip side, Both sides) or *Tangent to arc* (click a circle or arc; the side nearest the click). *Length*: Same as reference (its ends projected across) or a typed Length centred on the reference's midpoint. Off a sketch line it adds Parallel and, with *Add dimensions*, the line-to-line distance; off a model edge it adds nothing. The reference stays picked for the next line; each placement is one undo step.
- **Splines, ellipses and infinite lines in the tools** (refinement pass): Trim (all options but Corner) and Extend work on splines and ellipses (a trimmed ellipse becomes a partial ellipse, a trimmed spline keeps its shape within 0.025 mm); a point can be Coincident on a spline or ellipse (Add Relations, inference, drag); Smart Dimension of a spline gives its end-to-end distance or the distance from its nearer end to a point; Move / Copy / Rotate / Scale, the patterns and Mirror carry splines, ellipses and arc slots; Offset of a spline or ellipse gives a spline copy. *Infinite length* makes a true infinite line through its two points (2 degrees of freedom, drawn across the view at any zoom, no profile or DXF output); Trim turns it into rays or segments kept Collinear; scripts `.infinite([i])`. Dragging a sketch point snaps to the model too; holding Ctrl while drawing or dragging suppresses every snap. Relation icons keep clear of small entities after zooming out.
- **Sketch Text** (`sketch-font.js`, `ui-sketch-text.js`): Anvil's own single-stroke engraving font (85 characters, lines and true arcs; nothing downloaded). *Etch* text is single strokes on the ETCH layer, never part of a profile, written to DXF as LINE / ARC for laser marking; *Cut* text outlines every stroke as a capsule with true round ends, joined into closed loops (islands such as O's centre are kept, not bridged). Text can follow a line or arc (*Curves*), with alignment, height, width factor, spacing, rotation and mirror; click to place, click / double-click to edit, drag to move. Move / Copy / Rotate / Scale / patterns / Mirror carry text (`Kernel.transformText`); Trim refuses it. Script `.text(x, y, str, opts)`.
- **Levels Manager** (`ui-levels.js`, Mastercam style): part-wide numbered levels in a *Levels* tab of the left pane (Number, Visible X, Name, Entities, Level Set; the main level highlighted), *Main level* number / name (a new number creates the level), All on / All off, Display filter (All / Used / Named / Used or named), level sets that toggle together, right-click Make main / Hide / Select all / Move or Copy selected to this level / Rename / Delete / Hide all others / Clear unused, and a *Level* chip in the status bar. New geometry goes on the main level; hidden levels are not drawn, picked, snapped or box-selected; the main level cannot be hidden. Stored as `model.levels` + `model.mainLevel` and synced into each sketch's layers (entities keyed by level name, or number when unnamed; level 1 is the default layer), so profiles, DXF layers and the CUT / ETCH / BEND presets keep working. Script `p.levels(list, main)`.
- **Dynamic Mirror, Intersection Curve, Fillet Chain**: Dynamic Mirror (Mirror flyout) mirrors every entity drawn about a selected line while on, with Symmetric relations; Intersection Curve (Convert flyout) makes sketch curves where the sketch plane cuts picked faces (lines, true circles / arcs, ellipses / partial ellipses where a cylinder is cut at an angle, else line chains), On Edge; Sketch Fillet's *Fillet all corners of the chain* rounds every corner at one R with Equal relations. Trim ▸ Corner works with splines.
- **Ordinate dimensions** (Smart Dimension flyout: Horizontal / Vertical Dimension, Horizontal / Vertical Ordinate): pick the zero, the text line, then each point or edge; values stand on one line with jogged leaders when crowded; deleting the "0" removes the set. Fully Define Sketch has an *Ordinate* scheme. Drawings take sketch ordinate sets as ordinate model items, carry each sketch dimension's tolerance, precision, prefix / suffix (2X, TYP, REF, MIN / MAX, Basic) and name — on entities, on location dimensions (distance relations marked for drawing), on each value of an ordinate set (its member's) and on each part of a slot callout (the slot's width, overall length or arc radius dimension: `2X .250 ±.005 X 1.25 SLOT`; a "2X" leads the callout) — and call out arc slots as `R1.500 ARC SLOT .375 WIDE 60°`; a slot drawn as lines and arcs (the Slot tools, or exploded by Fillet / Trim) brings the same parts from its construction centerline's length (an end arc's diameter), the distance relations between its sides or across its end arcs, and an arc slot's construction arc R and the radial distance between its inner and outer arcs (rfAF). Script `.ordinate([...])`.
- **DWG**: File ▸ Open and *Insert DXF / DWG* take .dwg through the local server's `api/dwg2dxf` (ODA File Converter or LibreDWG); the published static copy says to use the desktop shortcut. Paste between inch and mm parts scales the geometry and dimensions. Tools ▸ Sketch Tools holds Check Sketch for Feature, Repair Sketch and Insert DXF / DWG.
- **Replace Entity (rfCR).** Right-click a sketch entity ▸ *Replace Entity…* (as SolidWorks' Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ Replace Entity): pick the entity to replace, then the entity that replaces it; ✓ moves the first's relations and dimensions onto the second (a whole-entity relation to the new entity, each end to its nearest end, a centre to the centre; a dimension keeps its value and the new entity is solved to it) and deletes the first, or keeps it as construction geometry with *Make construction*. A relation the new entity cannot take is dropped; a replacement that would over-define the sketch is refused and nothing changes. One undo step (`SketchSolver.replaceEntity(sketch, i, j, { construction })` → `{ ok, index, moved, dims, error }`).
- **Placing and locking an imported blank (rfAP).** *Modify Sketch* (Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ Modify…, or right-click in a sketch) moves the whole sketch with its relations and dimensions: Translate by ΔX / ΔY, *Lower-left to origin*, *Center on origin*; Rotate and Scale about the sketch origin or a point; *Flip Horizontal* / *Flip Vertical* (arcs' ends and rectangles' corners keep their relations); a drag in the graphics area moves it and Shift+drag turns it; each Apply is one undo step and ✗ puts the sketch back. Relations to the origin and Fixed are deleted, since the origin does not move with it (`SketchSolver.modifySketch(sketch, { dx, dy } | { rot, cx, cy } | { s, cx, cy } | { flip: 'h' | 'v', cx, cy })`). *Constrain All* (right-click, Tools ▸ Relations ▸ Constrain All, `anvil.constrainAll(opts)`, `SketchSolver.constrainAll`) adds the relations the geometry already obeys within a tolerance (default 1e-5 of the sketch's size, 0.01°): Coincident ends (one on the origin), Concentric centres, Horizontal / Vertical lines, Tangent arcs; those that would over-define are left out; then Fully Define Sketch adds baseline dimensions, so an imported blank is fully defined in one undo step (`{ fullyDefine: false }` for relations only, `tol`, `angle`, `scheme` or `hScheme` / `vScheme`, `hPlace` / `vPlace`). From the menu (no options) it opens a PropertyManager as SolidWorks' DXF-import step does: *Relations only* (the default, so Autodimension picks the scheme afterwards) or *Relations and dimensions* (horizontal / vertical scheme and placement), a message previewing what will be added ("Will add 6 Coincident, 2 Horizontal, 2 Vertical, 2 Tangent.", `UI.constrainAllPreview()`), ✓ one undo step, ✗ leaves the sketch. *Segment* (Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ Segment…) divides a line, arc or circle into n equal segments with Coincident sketch points, or splits a line / arc into n equal pieces (`SketchSolver.segmentEntity`). *Equation Driven Curve* (Spline flyout): Explicit y = f(x) over x1..x2 or Parametric x(t), y(t) over t1..t2 (pi, sin, cos, sqrt, ^ …), made as a spline through the sampled points with the equation kept on it (`e.eq`, scripted as `.spline(pts, false, null, { eq })`); select it and run the command again to edit the equation.
- **Search Commands and the Tools menu (rfAP).** The menu-bar box searches every CommandManager command (every tab, flyouts and stacks opened up) and every menu row by name and SolidWorks' other words ("stretch entities", "constrain all", "modify sketch", "flat pattern", "hole table"), lists the matches under the box with their icons and where they live, and Enter runs the first that can run now (`UI.searchCommands(q)`). Tools ▸ Sketch Entities, Sketch Tools, Relations and Dimensions list every sketch command, in SolidWorks' order, greyed outside a sketch.
- **Refinement round (2026-10-08).** Drawing tools commit through `UI.afterSketchCommit(f, n0, st)` (`UI.sketchCommitHooks`), which is how Dynamic Mirror mirrors what every tool and command places in the same undo step; `UI.sketchToolDefs[tool].noOwnSnap(st)` keeps a tool's own points out of its snapping (arc slots, ellipses). Ordinate edits hold the zero and move only the target. A Sketch Text's insertion point is a solver point (Smart Dimension, Coincident / Horizontal / Vertical / Symmetric, drag that snaps). Sketch Fillet / Chamfer work on every pair (line–circle, arc–circle, circle–circle, a line or arc meeting a spline end); polygons and straight slots are exploded first (`SketchSolver.explodePoly` / `explodeSlot`). Straight Slot's Overall Length drives the typed L and the placed dimension. Status messages carry the part's unit (`R.250 in`). A dimension's other driving dimensions on the same entity hold their values when one is edited (a line's horizontal 3.000 stays 3.000 when its length is typed). The Dimension PM's *Mark for drawing* (`rel.mark`) chooses which location dimensions reach drawings as Model Items.
- **Sketch tab extras (lane CV).** Right-click a sketch text ▸ *Dissolve Sketch Text* turns it into plain lines / arcs (Etch strokes on ETCH; a Cut text's outline with exactly the text's profile, `Kernel.dissolveText(e)`). Right-click a dimension ▸ *Link Values…* (Shared Values dialog) gives dimensions one named value (`link` on a dim / distance / angle; script `.dimProps([[i, { link: "T" }]])` / `.rel(…, { link: "T" })`; `SketchSolver.linkValues / unlinkValue`); linked values show ∞, change together, and reach the part's other sketches when the sketch is left; *Unlink Value* frees one. Right-click a spline ▸ *Show Minimum Radius* (script `.showMinRadius([i])`). Tools ▸ Sketch Settings ▸ *Create dimension only when value is entered*. A line splitting a closed contour no longer warns "Open contour ignored".
- **Sketch Text extras.** *Stencil* (Cut mode: bridges of a set width keep the islands of O, A, B, 8 … attached for the laser); text follows splines, ellipses and partial ellipses too; DXF import turns TEXT / MTEXT into Etch text (DIMENSION / HATCH are counted and skipped). Drawings' flat-pattern views draw the flat's etch marks (*Show etch marks*, on an ETCH layer in DXF / SVG / PDF), marks on a face a later Sketched Bend moves follow it, and the Layers PM's Rename renames the level across the part.
- **Construction tools** (`ui-sketch-construct.js`, AutoCAD / Mastercam style): *Tangent Line* (two circles / arcs, nearest of up to 4 solutions, N or *Next solution* cycles; Angle mode), *Perpendicular Line* (a line or model edge, then a point), *Bisector* (two lines; construction by default, kept bisecting by a small tangent construction circle), *Tangent Circle* (Tangent-Tangent-Radius with an R dimension, Tangent-Tangent-Tangent for lines and circles) in the Circle flyout, *Split Entities* (click points on a line, arc, circle or spline — two on a circle or a closed spline; *Split all at intersections*) and *Join Entities* (collinear lines, co-radial arcs) in the Trim flyout. Relations are added so results stay parametric; each placement is one undo step. `UI.sketchConstruct` holds the geometry helpers.
- **DXF import, Check and Repair** (`dxf-import.js`, `ui-sketch-import.js`): *File ▸ Open* of a .dxf makes a new part with the DXF in Sketch1 on the Top Plane; *Insert DXF* adds one to the open sketch. *DXF/DWG Import*: file units and override, a layer checklist, position (as in file, lower-left at origin, centred) with offset and rotation, merge tolerance (Merge points closer than, 0.0005 in / 0.01 mm), Repair after import with *Close gaps up to* (sketchgaps-2: 0.001 in / 0.025 mm by default, so a customer flat with duplicate edges and small gaps comes in as one closed profile ready for Boss-Extrude or Base Flange; a gap of exactly the tolerance closes), Coincident relations (off above 300 joints). The pipeline is `DxfImport.prepare(parsed, { partUnits, units, layers: { NAME: false }, origin, dx, dy, rot, tol, gap, repair })` (no DOM). LINE, ARC, CIRCLE, (LW)POLYLINE with bulges, SPLINE, ELLIPSE, POINT and nested scaled / rotated / mirrored blocks; (rfAZ) *Import blocks as blocks* (Options, on by default, shown when the file has INSERTs that can be): each INSERT comes in as a sketch block instance (Tools ▸ Blocks: rigid, one definition per block name, a name the sketch already uses for other geometry gets `-2`; not repaired, no Coincident relations inside it), off explodes them as before (`UI.importDXFText(text, { blocks: false })`); DXF layers become sketch layers (ETCH / MARK / SCRIBE / ENGRAVE take the ETCH preset). *Check Sketch for Feature* lists open ends, gaps, shared endpoints, duplicates and crossings with markers and Next / Previous; *Repair Sketch* closes gaps, merges duplicates, joins collinear pieces, removes tiny entities, splits at crossings, never moving a point more than the tolerance. `DxfImport.parse / check / repair`, `UI.importDXFText(text, opts)` (opts.gap: Close gaps up to).
- **Layers, regions, copy / paste** (`ui-sketch-layers.js`): an open sketch shades every closed region and marks open ends with red squares (*View ▸ Sketch Regions*). *Layers* PropertyManager: colour, visibility, current layer, New / Rename / Delete, Move selected; presets CUT, ETCH (green, makes no solid) and BEND (dashed, makes no solid). ETCH / BEND entities stay out of profiles, sweep paths, Sketched Bend lines and Hole Wizard points; DXF export writes each layer with its colour and line type. Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V (and Edit ▸ Copy / Paste) copy entities with the relations and dimensions among them into any sketch, placed at a click.
- **Line Properties** (a selected line): *Existing Relations* (with the line's state), *Add Relations* (only those that apply), *Options* (For construction, Infinite length), *Parameters* (Length, Angle) and *Additional Parameters* (Start X/Y, End X/Y, Delta X/Y). A typed value drives the line through the solver with every relation and dimension kept (a driving length dimension is changed instead); a value that would over-define the sketch is refused with a message naming what it conflicts with (`SketchSolver.driveEntity(sketch, i, n, f, { guess, ents })`).
- **Rectangle.** Every rectangle tool opens *Rectangle*: *Rectangle Type* (Corner, Center, 3 Point Corner, 3 Point Center, Parallelogram; the buttons switch the tool without leaving the PropertyManager), *Add construction lines* (on for the center types) with *From Corners* / *From Midpoints*, and *Parameters*: the four corners, and the center for center types, live while drawing and afterwards driving the rectangle just drawn. Corner and Center Rectangles are four lines as soon as they are placed, as SolidWorks draws them: 2 Horizontal + 2 Vertical and Coincident corners (line k runs from corner k to k + 1: bottom, right, top, left), the construction lines tied to the corners (or the sides' midpoints) and, for the Center Rectangle with construction lines, a center point (Midpoint of the first one, Coincident with the origin when started there). An edge click opens *Line Properties*, Display/Delete Relations lists the H / V (delete one and the rectangle can skew), and typed W / H are the bottom and left lines' driving lengths. 3 Point Corner (two corners, then the height), 3 Point Center (center, a side's midpoint, then the height) and Parallelogram (three corners) are four lines with Coincident corners, Parallel opposite sides and, for rectangles, a Perpendicular corner (a 3 Point Center Rectangle with construction lines also gets a center point, Midpoint of the first one), so Smart Dimension, Sketch Fillet, Trim, profiles and DXF treat them as lines; the on-screen box takes L / A then H (W / A then H for the center type; L / A of the second side for a parallelogram), and typed sizes become driving dimensions. A Corner / Center Rectangle entity made by a script (`.rect()`, `rect` / `crect` in the feature JSON) stays one entity; selected, it opens its relations, For construction and the corner Parameters. The Polygon and Straight Slot tools draw lines and arcs too (see *Circle, Arc, Polygon, Slot*); scripts' `poly` / `slot` entities stay whole (Sketch Fillet and Trim make them lines and arcs first).
- **Point.** The Point tool opens *Point* (Parameters X, Y), filled in with the point just placed and moving it; a selected point adds Existing / Add Relations.
- Hooks for the other sketch-tool files: `UI.sketchToolHooks[tool]` (ui-sketch.js: `entities`, `steps`, `commit`, `after`, `single`, `numFields`, `liveValues`, `typedPoint`, `readout`, `live`); a PM opened with `spec.sketchTool` closes when the tool is put down. `UI.lineMode()`, `UI.lineOptions()`, `UI.cmdMidpointLine()`, `UI.lastSketchRectangle()`, `UI.driveSketchLine(i, key, v)`, `UI.driveSketchRectangle(k, axis, v)`.
### Sketch tab ▸ Dimensions and relations

`ui-sketch-dims.js` (solver side in `sketch-solver.js`).

- **Smart Dimension picks.** As in SolidWorks:
  - A line gives its length; above / below or beside a slanted line gives its horizontal or vertical length.
  - Two parallel lines give their distance. Two other lines give their angle, or its supplement when the label is placed outside them.
  - Two points give a horizontal, vertical or aligned distance, picked by where the label goes (above / below them, beside them, or neither). Point to line is the perpendicular distance.
  - A circle gives its diameter and an arc its radius. Arc length is the arc, then its two end points (`arclength` relation, label `⌒12.57`).
  - Two circles / arcs give their centre distance; the origin and model edges (brought in as fixed construction geometry) work as picks too. A round model edge square to the sketch (a hole's rim) comes in as its true circle or arc, so a click on it dimensions the Ø / R or the centre, not the facet line under the cursor; the facet corners along it are not vertices.
  - A dimension that would over-define the sketch asks *Make Dimension Driven?*.
- **Dimension PropertyManager.** It opens when a dimension is placed or clicked, next to the Modify box. Each change is one undo step.
  - *Tolerance/Precision*: None / Basic (boxed) / Bilateral / Limit / Symmetric / MIN / MAX, the variations, and the decimal places.
  - *Primary Value*: the name (`D1@Sketch1` by order unless renamed), the value, *Driven*, and *Reverse direction*. The value is shown at the dimension's precision (else the document's: inch .123, mm and angles .12) and follows a Modify-box edit; a value typed here is applied at full precision.
  - *Dimension Text*: text before / after the value, with 2X / TYP / REF buttons.
  - *Leaders*: Radius / Diameter display for circles and arcs (the Modify box then takes the value shown). For distances to whole circles / arcs, *First / Second arc condition*: Center / Min / Max (`rel.ac`).
  - The properties are stored on the dimension (`name`, `tol: { type, plus, minus }`, `prec`, `prefix`, `suffix`, `show`). Scripts keep them: `.dimProps([[i, {…}]])` for Smart Dimensions, extra keys in `.rel(…, { … })` for distances.
- **Instant2D** (rfAV; Sketch tab toggle, on by default, `UI.toggleInstant2D(on?)`, `anvil.instant2D(on?)`). Hover a dimension (its value or its lines): a ball appears at its moving arrow end (a linear dimension's second arrow, a radius / diameter's arrow on the curve, an angle's second arrow). Drag the ball: a ruler appears along the dimension (an arc for an angle) and the value follows live, snapped to the ruler's ticks; the nearer the pointer to the ruler, the finer the ticks (about 5 px apart on it, 14 px beside it, 40 px away; never finer than the document's precision, .001 in / 0.01 mm / 0.01°). Releasing makes one undo step, Esc during the drag puts the value back. Driven dimensions and the grey driven values show no ball; chamfer, path length, arc length and running dimensions take their values from the Modify box. Instant3D makes no dimension dots in an open sketch (rfBA: none are made; Instant3D acts on the part only, as SolidWorks). Test hooks: `UI.instant2DHandle(key)` (hover the dimension drawn with `UI.dimKey` key `d0` / `r3`), `UI.instant2DPoint(value, px)`, `UI.instant2DStepAt(px)`, `UI.instant2DDrag()`.
- **Modify box.** Enter accepts and Esc restores. An angle (two lines) and an Angular Running value read in ° with a 1° spin step; an angle stays above 0 and at most 180°, a running value from 0 up to 360° (the spin goes round, 359.5 + 1 = 0.5); a value outside is refused with the reason (`UI.dimEditUnit()`). The spin arrows, wheel and ↑ / ↓ step the value. It takes shop input (`25mm` in an inch part, `1-1/2`) and `=` equations. The ⇄ button reverses the sense of the dimension: the free geometry goes to the other side, and fixed geometry refuses with a message (`UI.reverseDimSense({ kind, index })`).
- **Add Relations.** The PropertyManager has three groups:
  - *Selected Entities*.
  - *Existing Relations*, each with its state icon (Satisfied / Over Defining / Not Solved / Dangling / Suppressed). A click selects a relation, hover lights it, and × deletes it.
  - *Add Relations*: only the relations that apply, from Horizontal, Vertical, Merge (two points), Coincident, Midpoint, Intersection (a point and two lines), Collinear, Parallel, Perpendicular, Tangent, Concentric, Coradial, Equal, Equal Curve Length (two of line, arc, circle, spline, ellipse: one length along each; `.rel('equalcurve', [a, b])`), Symmetric and Fix.
  - *Options* has *For construction*.
  - Other sketch PMs get the same two groups from `UI.sketchRelationGroups(sketch, refs, { onChange })`. `refs` are sketch refs or entity indices; without `onChange` the open PM is re-rendered.
- **Display/Delete Relations.** Filters: All in this sketch, Dangling, Overdefining/Not Solved, External (to model edges), Defined in context / Locked / Broken (none in a part sketch), Selected Entities.
  - *Suppressed* turns the selected relation off (`rel.suppressed`, analysed as `'suppressed'`).
  - Deleting a relation solves the sketch again (rfAV). The solver never takes a line of real length (or a circle / arc of real radius) to nothing: Horizontal plus Vertical on one line is a conflict and the line keeps its length; deleting either turns the line about its middle at that length.
  - *Entities* lists the selected relation's entities with their state.
  - *Delete All* deletes what the filter lists.
- **Status.** Under defined is blue, fully defined black, over defined red (entities, icons, dimensions, rows). Construction geometry is dashed. The status bar reads Under / Fully / Over Defined, and the FeatureManager shows (-) / (+).
- **Fully Define Sketch.** Find it on the Sketch tab or under *Tools ▸ Dimensions*.
  - It applies Horizontal / Vertical relations to lines within 1° of an axis.
  - It adds sizes (diameters, radii, slot widths), then horizontal and vertical distances of every point from the datums (the origin, or a picked point). The scheme is Baseline or Chain, placed below / above and left / right.
  - A point level with one already placed gets a Horizontal / Vertical relation instead of a dimension.
  - Each candidate is kept only if it removes a degree of freedom, so nothing is over-defined.
  - *Calculate* applies it; Cancel restores the sketch.
  - Code: `SketchSolver.fullyDefine(sketch, opts)`, `anvil.fullyDefine(opts)`, `anvil.dimProps(kind, i, props?)`.
- **Autodimension** (rfAQ; *Tools ▸ Dimensions ▸ Autodimension* and the Smart Dimension flyout). The Fully Define Sketch engine with dimensions only, in SolidWorks' groups: *Entities to Dimension* (all / selected), *Horizontal Dimensions* and *Vertical Dimensions*, each with its check box, Scheme (Baseline / Chain / Ordinate), Origin datum (the sketch origin, a point, or a line square to the direction: a vertical line for the horizontal dimensions; Fully Define Sketch takes a datum line too) and placement (above / below, left / right). *Apply* adds them (one undo step). Code: `UI.cmdAutodimension()`; `SketchSolver.fullyDefine` takes `skipH` / `skipV`.
- **Ordinate Dimension** (rfAQ). Horizontal / Vertical Ordinate with the direction left open: the zero, then where the values go (above / below the zero: a horizontal set; beside it: a vertical one), then the members. `UI.cmdOrdinateFree()`.
- **Chamfer Dimension** (rfAQ). The chamfer line, the line next to it, then where the label goes: `8.000 X 45°` on a leader. Both values drive: the distance (the chamfer's length along the second line) from the Modify box, the angle from the *Angle* field of the Dimension PM. A chamfer dimension that would over-define comes in driven. Relation `{ type: 'chamfer', refs: [chamfer line, adjacent line], v, cang, lo }` (`lo`: the label's offset from the chamfer's middle).
- **Path Length Dimension** (rfAQ). Pick the lines and arcs of the path (a click toggles one; *Select Chain* takes every line and arc joined end to end), ✓, then click where the label goes (✓ again: the default place). The total length drives the chain (driven when it would over-define). Relation `{ type: 'pathlength', refs: [lines / arcs / circles], v, lo }`.
- **Angular Running Dimension** (rfAQ). Click the 0° line near the end the angles are measured at (the vertex), click where the arcs go, then each line (its far end from the vertex) or point / hole: every angle counter-clockwise from the 0° line, 0° to 360°, one set with a `0°` at the datum. Each value drives. Click the `0°` to select the set; Delete deletes it. Dragging any value sets the arcs' radius for the set. Relations `{ type: 'runangle', refs: [vertex, datum point, point], v, off, run }`.
- Scripts keep these through `.rel(type, refs, value)` plus `.rfaq([[relIndex, { cang, run, lo }], …])`; code: `SketchSolver.addChamferDim(sketch, chamferRef, adjacentRef, { lo })`, `setChamferAngle(sketch, i, deg)`, `addPathLength(sketch, refs, { lo })`, `addRunningAngle(sketch, { vertex, datum, target, run?, off? })`, `runningSets`, `deleteRunningSet`; from the console `anvil.chamferDim`, `anvil.pathLength`, `anvil.runningAngle`, `anvil.runningSets`.
- **Tools ▸ Dimensions** lists Smart, Horizontal, Vertical, Ordinate, Horizontal Ordinate, Vertical Ordinate, Chamfer, Path Length and Angular Running Dimension, Autodimension and Fully Define Sketch; each runs its command.
### Sketch tab: Circle, Arc, Polygon, Slot

`ui-sketch-curves.js`. Each tool opens its SolidWorks PropertyManager at once and keeps it open while drawing (it does not take the keyboard, so typed values still go to the on-screen box). The Parameters follow the entity being drawn, then show the one just placed, where they can be edited until the next one starts. ✓ / ✗ / Esc put the tool down with its PM. A selected circle, arc, arc slot (or a polygon / slot entity a script made) opens the same Parameters (and *For construction*) to edit; an edit is one undo step, and the sketch is re-solved with the edited entity moving least (a value a relation holds comes back, with a status-bar message). *Existing Relations* / *Add Relations* appear when `UI.sketchRelationGroups` exists.

- **Circle** (ribbon flyout *Circle* / *Perimeter Circle*): *Circle Type* switches the tool in place; *Options*; *Parameters* Radius and Diameter (rfIC: no centre boxes; the two are linked, both take units and expressions; a size typed before the first click makes the next circle exactly that size, the click on the rim only sets its direction; Perimeter Circle with a typed size puts its third point on that circle and refuses a second point farther than the diameter). **Perimeter Circle** takes three points on the circle (after two, the circle on them as a diameter follows the cursor); a point placed on an existing sketch point or the origin holds it on the circle (Coincident).
- **Arc** (flyout *Centerpoint Arc* / *Tangent Arc* / *3 Point Arc*): *Arc Type*; *Parameters* Center X / Y, Start X / Y, End X / Y, Radius, Angle (an end point edit keeps the centre). **Tangent Arc** starts on the end of a line or arc (any other first click is refused) and uses SolidWorks' intent zones: leaving the end point (10 px) along the line gives a tangent arc, back over it the reverse tangent, square to it a normal arc; back onto the end point chooses again. Tangent arcs get Coincident with the clicked end and Tangent. Typed R and A.
- **Polygon**: *Options*; *Parameters* Number of sides, *Inscribed circle* (default, the second click is the middle of a side) / *Circumscribed circle* (a corner), Center X / Y, Circle diameter, Angle; *New Polygon*. As SolidWorks draws it, the polygon placed is its n sides (lines, side k from corner k to k + 1) and a construction circle — through the corners (Circumscribed) or touching the sides (Inscribed) — held by Coincident corners and one **Polygon** relation (`SketchSolver.explodePoly`; relation `{ type: 'polygon', refs: [circle, side 0, …, side n-1], ins }`: the corners, where consecutive sides meet, extended, sit at n equal turns about the circle's centre, so a filleted corner keeps its virtual sharp and the polygon stays regular; 4 degrees of freedom: centre, size, turn; made by the tool only, never offered in Add Relations; script `.rel("polygon", refs, { ins: true })`). A side selects as a line; the on-screen box takes the circle's diameter (Ø, rfY), which becomes its diameter dimension. The tool PM's Parameters drive the polygon just drawn through the solver (its relations hold); Number of sides / circle type rebuild its sides and keep the circle with its relations and dimensions. A `poly` entity a script makes (`.polygon(cx, cy, r, n, rot, { inscribed: true })`) stays whole: its `r` is always the corner radius; `ins: true` marks an inscribed circle, which its R dimension and the PM's diameter measure to the flats. Switching the circle type keeps the diameter and angle and moves the corners.
- **Slot** (flyout *Straight Slot*, *Centerpoint Straight Slot*, *3 Point Arc Slot*, *Centerpoint Arc Slot*): *Slot Types* with *Add dimensions* (length and width for a straight slot, arc radius and width for an arc slot, on finishing) and the length type *Center to Center* / *Overall Length* (Smart Dimension kind `olen`, cap end to cap end; a slot's other dimensions hold while one is edited); *Parameters* Center X / Y, Slot width, Slot length (arc slots: Arc radius, Arc angle, Slot width). The **Straight Slot** and **Centerpoint Straight Slot** place what SolidWorks draws (`SketchSolver.explodeSlot`): two sides, two end arcs (Coincident and Tangent at the four joins, Equal) and a construction centerline from the first end's centre to the second's (the arcs centred on its ends; a slot drawn level or upright gets Horizontal / Vertical on it). Typed or *Add dimensions* sizes become the centerline's length (Center to Center), the distance across the arcs (Overall Length) and the distance between the sides (width), all driving; the tool PM's Parameters drive the slot just drawn through the solver (a slot started on the origin grows from there). A **Centerpoint Straight Slot** adds its center point held at the centerline's middle (Midpoint). A `slot` entity a script makes (`.slot(x1, y1, x2, y2, w)`) stays whole until Sketch Fillet or Trim works on it: then it is exploded the same way (rfAF: an arc slot `aslot` too), its width / overall length dimensions becoming distance relations that keep their tolerance, precision, text and name. **Arc slots** (rfY) are drawn the same way (`SketchSolver.explodeArcSlot`, through `explodeSlot`): the outer arc, the end arc at the slot's end, the inner arc, the end arc at its start and a construction centre-line arc (its centre is the slot's centre point); the outer / inner arcs are centred on the construction arc's centre, the end arcs on its ends, the four joins Coincident and Tangent; the fourth join's Coincident is `implied: true` (it repeats part of what the other three say, as SolidWorks' solver accepts it: `analyze` does not count an implied relation that adds no rank as over-defining; scripts keep the flag, `.rel("coincident", refs, { implied: true })`). *Add dimensions* / typed sizes become the construction arc's R and the radial distance between the inner and outer arcs (width); the tool PM's Parameters drive the slot just drawn with its other parameters held. A script's `.arcSlot()` is the entity `{ t: 'aslot', cx, cy, r, a0, a1, w }` (centre-line arc counter-clockwise a0 → a1 in degrees, width w): four true arcs in profiles, extrudes (exact volume θ·r·w + π·w²/4 per unit depth), the sketch DXF (four ARCs) and trims; refs `c`, `p1` / `p2` (cap centres, sketch points) and the whole entity as its centre-line arc (Concentric, Equal, R); Smart Dimension on a side gives R, on a cap the width; Mirror, Move, Offset (radial offset dimension) keep it an arc slot. Script: `.arcSlot(cx, cy, r, a0, a1, w)`.
- Options the tools remember (per browser, `localStorage` `anvil.sketchCurves`): `UI.sketchCurveOptions({ sides, ins, slotDims, overall })` sets and returns them.
- Hooks for drawing tools defined outside `ui-sketch.js`: `UI.sketchToolDefs[tool] = { steps, entities(pts, cur, st), accept(st, p, typed), after(f, firstIndex, count, st), fields(n, st), live(pts, p, st), typed(pts, cur, v, st), readout(pts, p, fmt), refine(info, raw, st, h, grid) }`, with `UI.sketchGeom` (tangentArc, threePointArc, smoothDirs, …).
### Sketch tab ▸ Spline, Ellipse, Text

`ui-sketch-spline.js` (tools, PropertyManagers, curvature comb), `kernel-sketch.js` (geometry, profiles), `kernel-script.js` (DXF, script), `occt-kernel.js` (true edges).

- **Spline** (Sketch tab, *S* shortcut bar, Search Commands "spline"): click point after point; a double-click, a right-click or **Esc** ends it (Esc also puts the tool down), a click on its first point closes it. The *Spline* PropertyManager opens with the tool (Options: *For construction*, *Show curvature*, *Maintain internal continuity*) and shows the spline just drawn.
