Selecting a spline opens its PropertyManager on the point clicked: Existing / Add Relations, Options (also *Closed spline*), Parameters (*Number of points*, *Spline point number*, *X / Y Coordinate*, *Tangent Driving* with *Tangent Weighting* and *Tangent Radial Direction*, *Insert Point* / *Delete Point*). Spline points are sketch points: they snap, take relations (`3.p1`, `3.p2`, `3.v1` …) and drag. *Show curvature* draws SolidWorks' comb. Entity `{ t: "spline", pts: [[x, y], …], closed?, tans?: [[angle°, weight, weightIn?] | null, …], local?, showCurvature? }` (`weightIn`: the speed of the span arriving at the point when it differs from the one leaving it, SolidWorks' asymmetric tangent weighting; Split Entities and Trim set it so a cut spline keeps its exact shape).
  - Geometry (`Kernel.splineGeom(e)` → `{ bez: [[B0, B1, B2, B3], …], knots, closed }`): the C2 interpolating cubic through the points, parameterised by chord length (knot i = the summed distances to point i), natural (curvature-free) free ends, periodic when closed. A tangent-driving point fixes the first derivative there to weight · (cos, sin) of its angle (1 = the natural chord-length speed; the curve is C1 there). *Maintain internal continuity* off (`local: true`): each point's tangent comes from its neighbours only (chord P[i+1] − P[i−1] over its knot span), so moving a point reshapes only the two spans beside it (C1).
- **Ellipse** (centre, end of the major axis, a point whose distance from the major axis is the minor radius) and **Partial Ellipse** (the Ellipse ▸ flyout: centre, major axis end, the start point — it sets the minor radius — then round the way the arc should run to the end point). One *Ellipse* PropertyManager for both: Existing / Add Relations, *For construction*, Parameters *Center X / Y*, *Major radius*, *Minor radius*, *Angle*, and *Start angle* / *End angle* for a partial one (parameter angles: the point at u is centre + rot·(rx·cos u, ry·sin u)). A minor radius typed larger than the major swaps the axes. Refs: `3.c`, axis ends `3.v0` (major), `3.v1` (minor), `3.v2`, `3.v3`, partial ends `3.p1` / `3.p2`; Smart Dimension on an ellipse gives its major / minor radius (`dim(i, "rx" | "ry")`). Dragging an axis end turns and stretches it about its centre; dragging a partial end slides it along. Entities `{ t: "ellipse", cx, cy, rx, ry, rot }` and `{ t: "pellipse", cx, cy, rx, ry, rot, a0, a1 }` (degrees, a0 < a1, counterclockwise).
- **Profiles and volumes.** Splines and ellipses are profiles for Extrude / Cut-Extrude like any loop (alone when closed, or chained end to end with lines and arcs). Their polygon is *area-true*: the points sit off the curve by about two thirds of a chord sagitta so the polygon encloses exactly the curve's area, so an extruded ellipse is πab·h and a spline region its exact (Green's theorem) area times the depth to rounding, whatever the tessellation (chord tolerance as for circles). `profile.free` / `loop.fseg` carry the true curves: the OpenCascade kernel builds ellipses as `gp_Elips` edges and splines as the B-spline of their Bézier spans (the same curve), one face each.
- **DXF.** Sketch DXF (*DXF/DWG Output ▸ Sketch*, `Kernel.exportDXFSketch`): R2000 writes a spline as `SPLINE` (degree 3, its spans' control points, knots = Anvil's chord-length knots with interior multiplicity 3) and an ellipse as `ELLIPSE` (major axis vector, ratio ≤ 1, start / end parameters); R12 has neither, so they go out as `ARC`s through the curve that chain end to end, within 0.001 mm (0.00004 in) of it (`DXF_TOL.freeArc`). Face and flat-pattern DXF read the mesh, so there a spline or ellipse is its tessellation (a polyline within the chord tolerance).
- **Split Entities** on a spline (`SketchSolver.freeSplit(sketch, i, points)`): an open spline splits at one or more clicks, a closed one at two (two open splines). The pieces run through the original points and the split point and are exactly the original curve (the split point and the original points beside it are tangent-driving with the original's direction and per-side speeds), joined by Coincident; they leave the split point at one angle, so the join stays smooth. A style spline's pieces are its own Bézier sub-spans of the same degree, joined by Coincident and Tangent (their end legs stay in line). Relations and dimensions follow the pieces as Trim keeps them; one undo step per click. *Split all at intersections* splits splines at their true crossings too.
- **Split Entities** on an ellipse or a partial ellipse (rfAS, `UI.splitEllipse(sketch, i, points)`; the Split Entities command in `ui-sketch-construct.js` handles clicks on ellipses, partial ellipses and conics itself since rfAW and hands the split to it): a full ellipse splits at two clicks into two partial ellipses, a partial ellipse at one click into two. The pieces are the same curve (centre, radii, angle), joined end to end by Coincident; relations on its points (centre, axis ends, ends) go to the same point of a piece, a point lying on it to the piece under it, its major / minor radius dimensions to the first piece. One undo step per split.
- **Sketch Picture** (rfAW, `ui-sketch-picture.js`; the Sketch tab's *Sketch Picture* button, Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ Sketch Picture…): pick a PNG, JPG or BMP (read from the file, nothing fetched; over 2048 px a side or a BMP is re-encoded). It lies on the sketch plane behind the geometry, lower-left corner at the sketch origin, about two thirds of the graphics area across, and shows whenever its sketch shows. The *Sketch Picture* PropertyManager: *Origin X / Y* (its lower-left corner), *Angle* (about that corner), *Width / Height* with *Lock aspect ratio*, *Enable scale tool* (click two points a known distance apart — they snap to sketch points, e.g. the ends of a line traced along the print's scale bar — and type the real distance in the box that opens: the picture scales about the first point), *Flip horizontal / vertical*, Transparency *None* / *From file* / *Full image* (slider) / *User-defined* (a colour, its matching tolerance and the slider). ✗ on a picture just placed takes it out again. Right-click on the picture: *Edit Sketch Picture*, *Delete*. Stored on the sketch as `sketch.pictures = [{ name, file, src: data URL, px, x, y, w, h, ang, flipH, flipV, tmode, alpha, key, keyTol }]`, never part of a profile, a region or a DXF; it goes through save / load, undo and the script as `.picture({ … })` (kernel-sketch.js). Hooks: `UI.insertSketchPicture(src, name, { noPM })` (Promise of its index), `UI.editSketchPicture(i)`, `UI.deleteSketchPicture(i)`, `UI.scaleSketchPicture(i, a, b, distance)`, `UI.sketchPictureAt(mx, my)`.
- **Tools ▸ Spline Tools** (rfAS; also on a spline's right-click menu): *Insert Spline Point* (click on a spline: the point becomes a spline point and the curve keeps its shape exactly; its neighbours become tangent-driving as Split Entities makes them; open splines), *Simplify Spline* (Tolerance, Smoothing: interior points dropped, the one that changes the curve least first, while the curve stays within the tolerance of the original; *Number of points* shows before → after; ✗ puts it back), *Fit Spline* (the selected lines, arcs and polylines, one chain end to end such as an imported DXF polyline, become one spline within *Tolerance* of them: it runs through the chain's corners and arc midpoints and gets more chain points wherever it strays further; *Closed spline*; *Delete geometry* (default) / *Constrained* (the geometry stays as construction, the spline's ends Coincident with its ends) / *Unconstrained*), *Show Spline Handles* (every spline's tangent handles, a third of the span long), *Show Curvature* (the comb on the selected splines; its *Curvature Comb* PropertyManager has *Scale* and *Density*), *Relax Spline* (inner tangent-driving off and internal continuity on: the natural C2 curve through the points). rfAW: *Insert Spline Point* works on closed splines too (the point goes in after the span clicked, the old points keep their numbers before it, only the new point and its two neighbours become tangent-driving; the curve stays the same within 1e-6). *Add Tangency Control* (click on a spline: a spline point there with its own tangent driving, and the Spline PropertyManager opens on it to turn or weight the handle), *Add Curvature Control* (the same, with the spline's curvature comb on and the point given the curvature the curve had there as a driving value; rfAY, below), *Show Inflection Points* (a diamond where the bend changes side: the simple roots of B' × B'' in each cubic span and sign changes at the joins; `e.showInflection`, `UI.splineInflections(e)`). All three are also on a spline's right-click menu. Test hooks `UI.fitSpline(sketch, idx, { tol, closed, mode })`, `UI.insertSplinePointAt(sketch, i, uv)`, `UI.simplifySpline(e, tol, smoothing)`.
- **Curvature Control** (rfAY): `tans[i] = [angle°, weight, weightIn | null, κ]` makes spline point i tangent-driving with the signed curvature κ (1 / radius in sketch units; + bends to the left of the way the points run, 0 is flat), held on both sides of the point (G2 at that value). The Spline PropertyManager has *Curvature Control* (on: the curvature the curve has there now, the tangent made driving first), *Radius of curvature* (keeps the side it bends to) and *Curvature* (signed); `UI.splineCurvatureControl(index, pt, 'on' | 'off' | { r } | { k })` is one undo step; `Kernel.splineCurvatureAt(e, i)` -> `{ out, in, k }` measures it. `Kernel.splineGeom` fits it exactly: each side's curvature is one linear condition on the neighbour's tangent (`cross(T, M_next) = 3·cross(T, Δ)/h − κ|T|³h/2`), solved with internal continuity on as the least-bending-energy spline (KKT) meeting every condition, so the curve stays C2 at every other point except one bend component at the controlled point's neighbours; off (local), the neighbour's tangent turns by what is needed. A neighbour that is tangent-driving itself (Insert Spline Point leaves them so) has no free tangent: the controlled point's leaving / arriving speed gives the value instead, and where no speed can (κ = 0, or the curve bends the other way) the neighbour's tangent lets go. Mirror negates κ, Scale divides it, Rotate keeps it; a save / load keeps it (it is in `tans`). Not yet: the script line `.spline(pts, closed, tans)` drops `tans[i][3]` (kernel-script.js `SketchBuilder.spline` keeps three entries), Split / Trim pieces drop it, and a curvature is not a sketch dimension (it is set in the PropertyManager).
- **Parabola** and **Conic** (rfAS, the Ellipse ▸ flyout, Search Commands "parabola" / "conic"). Parabola: click the focus, then the apex (it opens towards the focus), then the start and end points (each lands on the parabola square across its axis from the click). Conic: the start point, the end point, the top vertex (where the end tangents meet), then a point that sets rho (its place along the line from the chord's middle to the top vertex; 0.5 a parabola, less an elliptical arc, more a hyperbolic one). Entity `{ t: "spline", pts: [start, shoulder, end], conic: rho, parabola? }`: the rational quadratic Bézier from start to end through the top vertex T = M + (shoulder − M) / rho with middle weight rho / (1 − rho) (`Kernel.conicData(e)`, `Kernel.conicPoint(c, t)`). Its three points are sketch points (relations, drags, Smart Dimension of the ends): any change keeps a conic of the same rho. `Kernel.splineGeom` gives the other consumers cubic spans (two exact ones for a parabola, 16 within about 1e-7 of the size for a conic), so a region extrudes to the conic's area, sketch DXF writes `SPLINE` (R2000) or chained `ARC`s (R12), and drawings and OpenCascade take it as a spline. Its PropertyManager: Existing / Add Relations, *For construction*, *Start / End / Top vertex X, Y*, *Rho* (a parabola's is fixed at 0.5). Script: `.conic(start, topVertex, end, rho)`, `.parabola(start, topVertex, end)`; `generateScript` writes them as `.spline(pts, false, null, { conic: rho, parabola: true })`. Split Entities on a conic or a parabola makes conics of the same curve (each piece its own rho; `UI.splitEllipse` takes them too). Not yet: Trim of a conic other than a parabola does not keep its shape (SketchSolver.freePiece maps spline spans to points one to one; a conic has 16 spans on 3 points).
- Script: `.spline(points, closed = false, tans?, { local: true }?)`, `.ellipse(cx, cy, rx, ry, rot°)`, `.partialEllipse(cx, cy, rx, ry, rot°, a0°, a1°)`; `generateScript` writes them back.
- **Style Spline** (Spline ▸ flyout, Search Commands "style spline" / "bezier"): SolidWorks' single-span Bézier curve shaped by its control polygon. Click control vertex after vertex; it ends by itself at degree + 1 vertices (a double-click, right-click or **Esc** ends it sooner: the vertices placed are elevated to the degree chosen, the same curve). The curve runs through the first and last vertex, tangent to the control polygon there. Its PropertyManager (on the tool, and on a style spline selected: a click on its curve or within 9 px of a vertex) has *Spline Degree* (Degree 3 / 5 / 7: raising it elevates the curve exactly, lowering it fits the nearest curve of that degree with the same ends and end tangent directions), Options (*For construction*, *Show curvature*), Parameters (*Control vertex number*, its *X / Y*) and *Control Polygon* (Horizontal / Vertical on the leg after the vertex picked). A click within 5 px of a control polygon line (nearer than any curve) selects that leg (`3.e0`, `3.e1` …, a line for relations): with a line or arc Ctrl+clicked, Add Relations offers Horizontal / Vertical, Parallel, Perpendicular, Collinear and **Tangent** (to a line: the leg runs along it; to an arc at a shared end vertex: square to the radius there). The control polygon is drawn in the sketch; the vertices are sketch points (`3.p1`, `3.v1` …, `3.p2`): they snap, drag and take relations (Coincident, Horizontal / Vertical between two of them, Tangent of the curve to a line or arc at a shared end, which lines it up with the end leg). Entity `{ t: "spline", style: true, pts: [control vertices] }` (degree = pts.length − 1).
  - Everywhere a spline works: a point Coincident on it, Smart Dimension of its ends, Trim (the piece kept is a style spline of the same degree: the Bézier's own sub-span, `Kernel.bzSub`), Extend Entities and Corner (it carries on along its own polynomial, its vertices redrawn; relations on the inner vertices go), Offset (a spline through the offset points), Mirror / Move / Rotate / Scale / patterns, profiles for Extrude / Cut (area-true as other splines), sketch DXF, the script and drawings through the model. Split Entities splits it into style splines of the same degree.
  - Geometry: `Kernel.bzPoint(P, t)` / `bzDeriv` / `bzSub(P, a, b)` / `bzElevate` / `bzDegree(P, m)`; `Kernel.splineGeom` gives the other consumers (profiles, OpenCascade, drawings) one exact cubic span for degree 3 and 8·n C1 cubic spans within about 1e-7 of its size for degree 5 / 7. Sketch DXF: R2000 writes one `SPLINE` of its own degree (n + 1 control vertices, clamped knots `0 × (n + 1), L × (n + 1)`); R12 chained `ARC`s along the exact curve (`DXF_TOL.freeArc`). Script: `.styleSpline(points, { degree })` (a degree other than points − 1 elevates or fits them); `generateScript` writes it back.
- Not yet: Add Tangency / Curvature Control, Show Inflection Points, Insert Spline Point on a closed spline by click (its PropertyManager's *Insert Point* adds one mid-span). (Equation Driven Curve, Parabola and Conic came later; Sketch Text, Trim / Extend of splines and ellipses, a point Coincident on them and Smart Dimension of a spline came later: see *Sketch tab* ▸ *Splines, ellipses and infinite lines in the tools* and *Sketch Text* above.)
### Sketch tab ▸ Tools

The sketch tools (`ui-sketch-tools.js`, geometry in `sketch-solver.js`) open their SolidWorks PropertyManager at once, with SolidWorks 2024's groups, fields and defaults, and sit on the ribbon as SolidWorks groups them: *Sketch Fillet ▸ Sketch Chamfer*, *Trim Entities ▸ Extend / Split / Join Entities*, *Convert Entities ▸ Intersection Curve*, *Offset Entities*, and the small *Mirror Entities ▸ Dynamic Mirror*, *Linear Sketch Pattern ▸ Circular Sketch Pattern* and *Move Entities ▸ Copy / Rotate / Scale / Stretch* flyouts. Every tool is all or nothing: one that fails (a radius too large, a conflict with the sketch's relations) leaves the sketch exactly as it was and takes its undo step back, and a relation a tool moved or made that would over-define the sketch is not kept.

- **Sketch Fillet** (*Entities to Fillet*; *Fillet Parameters*: Radius, *Keep constrained corners* on, *Dimension each fillet* off). Click a vertex where two lines or arcs meet (line–line, line–arc, arc–arc), a rectangle's, polygon's or polyline's corner, or two entities one after the other (they need not meet: each is cut back or extended to the fillet, the side clicked kept; several selected when the command starts, by a box over a contour or a rectangle, polygon or slot picked, have every corner where they meet one another rounded, one radius with Equal relations; (rfAZ) such a selection, or two entities selected, is previewed, not made: its corners are listed in *Entities to Fillet* and the fillets drawn at the current radius, a new Radius (or option) redraws the preview, ✓ makes them as one undo step (refused, the PropertyManager staying open, when they do not fit), ✗ leaves the sketch untouched, and a pick in the graphics area makes them first: `SketchSolver.selectionCorners(sketch, list)`, `filletCorners(sketch, corners, r, opts)`). A line and a spline that do not meet work the same way: the line is extended (or cut back) to the fillet and the spline cut back to its tangent point; when the line's extension misses the spline, the spline is carried on past a free end (its end span's own curve, as Extend Entities carries it; a style spline's own polynomial; not past an end joined to other geometry, nor a conic) to the fillet, its old end staying a spline point, as SolidWorks does. The fillet is tangent to both, joined by Coincident / Tangent; the vanished corner becomes a virtual sharp carrying its relations and length dimensions (*Keep constrained corners* off: they go with the corner). The first fillet gets a driving radius; with *Dimension each fillet* off the later ones get Equal to it. ✗ takes back every fillet made while the PropertyManager was open. `SketchSolver.filletPair(sketch, spec, r, { keepCorner, dimension, equalTo })`, `curveCorner(sketch, uv, tol)`.
- **Sketch Chamfer** (*Chamfer Parameters*: Angle-distance / Distance-distance, *Equal distance* (default), Distance 1, Direction 1 Angle, Distance 2): lines at a corner or two picked lines; the chamfer line is Coincident with both, and a virtual sharp holds the chamfer's driving dimensions (the distances along the lines, or the first distance and the angle). `SketchSolver.chamferPair(sketch, spec, { type, d1, d2, angle, equal })`.
- **Offset Entities** (*Parameters*: Offset distance, Add dimensions, Reverse, Select chain, Bi-directional, Cap ends ▸ Arcs / Lines, Construction geometry ▸ Base geometry / Offset geometry): the selection (clicks add / remove) is previewed as it changes. Lines and arcs joined end to end are offset as a chain (*Select chain* takes the whole chain from one pick): smooth joins stay smooth, corners are extended or cut back to meet (sharp, as SolidWorks makes them), closed chains go outward by default; one *Offset* relation holds the chain (refs: the bases then the copies, `chain: { n, dirs, closed, joins, bi, cap }`), with Parallel / Concentric and one driving offset dimension on the first copy, so editing that dimension or the base moves the whole chain. Circles, rectangles, slots and polygons stay true entities; closed polylines and a face's outline (nothing selected in a sketch on a face) become offset polylines. `SketchSolver.offsetSelection(sketch, idx, opts)` (`preview: true` returns the geometry only).
- **Trim Entities** adds *Keep trimmed entities as construction geometry* (the span removed stays as a construction line / arc joined to the cut; an entity nothing crosses becomes construction) and *Ignore trimming of construction geometry* (construction entities are left alone by clicks and the Power trim trail, and still cut the others); both are remembered with the option. `trimAt / powerTrim / trimBetween(…, ctx)` take `ctx.keepConstruction` / `ctx.ignoreConstruction`.
- **Convert Entities** (*Entities to Convert*, *Select chain*, *Inner loops*): click model edges or faces; each edge is projected onto the sketch plane, circular edges square to the sketch as true circles / arcs, straight pieces of one edge joined into one line, each held by an *On Edge* relation (solved like Fix). A face brings its edges (*Inner loops* off: the outer loop only); *Select chain* brings an edge's whole loop.
- **Move / Copy / Rotate / Scale / Stretch Entities** (*Entities to Move* … with *Keep relations*; *Parameters*: From/To — click the start point, then where it goes — or X/Y with ΔX, ΔY and *Repeat*; Rotate: Center of rotation (a click) and Angle; Scale: Scale about, Scale Factor, *Copy* (default) and Number of copies). Keep relations: the geometry tied to the selection follows (Stretch always does: joined lines stretch); off: those ties are deleted first. A quarter turn swaps Horizontal / Vertical; rectangles turn as four lines; copies keep the relations among themselves (not dimensions). `SketchSolver.transformEntities(sketch, idx, { dx, dy } | { rot, cx, cy } | { s, cx, cy }, { copy, count, keepRelations })`. *Stretch* after a window or crossing box (drawn before the command or while *Entities to Stretch* is active) works as SolidWorks': the points inside the box move, an entity wholly inside moves, a line / slot / polyline / spline with an end inside stretches, an arc with one end inside keeps its radius and tangents (its other end slides if the relations need it), a rectangle stretched along a side stays a rectangle; distances between entities and the stretched entities' own dimensions read the new size; entities picked by clicks move whole as before (`SketchSolver.stretchEntities(sketch, idx, boxPolygon, { dx, dy })`, the box in `UI.lastSketchBox`).
- **Linear Sketch Pattern** (*Direction 1 / 2*: X-axis / Y-axis or a clicked line, Reverse direction, Spacing, Dimension X / Y spacing, Number of instances, Angle, Dimension angle between axes; *Entities to Pattern*; *Instances to Skip*: click the box, then the instance dots) and **Circular Sketch Pattern** (*Parameters*: Reverse direction, Center X / Y (the origin by default), Spacing (360°), Equal spacing, Dimension radius, Dimension angular spacing, Number of instances (4), Radius, Arc angle; the same two boxes). The copies stay tied to their seeds by one *Pattern* relation (`pat: { nd, m, inst }` linear, `{ circ, m, inst }` circular): editing the seed, a spacing, the radius or the angular spacing moves and sizes the whole pattern. Linear: the first copy along each direction is held Horizontal / Vertical (an axis direction) and by its spacing dimension; circular: two construction lines from the centre (Equal, the angle between them) carry the radius and spacing dimensions. `SketchSolver.linearPattern(sketch, idx, spec)`, `circularPattern(sketch, idx, spec)`.
- **Jog Line** (rfAT; Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ Jog Line, `UI.cmdJogLine()`): click a line where the jog starts, move along it and off it (the preview follows), click to place; or type *Width* / *Depth* in the PM and press ✓. The line becomes five connected lines — the two outer pieces *Collinear*, the jog's top *Parallel* to them, its sides *Perpendicular*, joined by Coincident — in one undo step; the line's relations stay on its pieces (its second end's on the last piece), its length dimension goes. `UI.jogLine(i, t1, t2, depth)` does it without the mouse.
- **Close Arc** and **Show Endpoints** (lane BS, Mastercam's): *Close Arc* (Sketch tab, an Anvil extra beside the modify tools; Tools ▸ Sketch Tools ▸ Close Arc; `UI.cmdCloseArc()`, or `UI.closeArcs([i, …])` without the PM) turns each arc in *Arcs to Close* (the selection plus picks; other entities are ignored and the PM says so) into the full circle it lies on — same centre, radius, construction flag and level — and a partial ellipse into its ellipse, in one undo step; the radius dimension stays (the circle's, still read R), whole-curve and centre relations (Concentric, Coradial, Tangent, Equal …) stay, relations and dimensions on the end points go and the status line counts them. *Show Endpoints* (Heads-up View toolbar, before View Settings; View ▸ Display ▸ Show Endpoints; `UI.toggleShowEndpoints(on?)`) hides the end-point dots of the sketch being edited (centre dots, sketch points, the origin and snap markers stay; ends still select and snap); `App.view.showEndpoints`, remembered in `localStorage` `anvil.showEndpoints`, on by default.
- **Blocks** (rfAT; Tools ▸ Blocks ▸ Make… / Edit Block / Insert… / Explode / Save…, `ui-sketch-blocks.js`, engine in `kernel-sketch.js`). *Make Block* turns the selected entities into a named definition (*Block name*, *Insertion point* X / Y, or click it; a rectangle goes in as four lines) and its first instance in place; their relations and dimensions go, as the block holds them. *Insert Block* places instances by clicking (the block rides on the cursor) with *Angle* and *Scale*, or at typed X / Y with ✓; *Browse…* loads a saved `.sldblk`. With an instance selected, Insert Block opens its *Block* properties (Insertion X / Y, Angle, Scale, live; Edit Block, Explode, Save Block… buttons). An instance is ordinary sketch geometry tagged `blk` plus its insertion point `{ t: 'point', blk, blkDef, blkAng, blkS }`: dimension or relate the insertion point (or sketch geometry to the instance's points) and the instance follows as one rigid piece; dragging any of its lines moves the whole instance. (rfAU) The solver holds each instance as one rigid body: its insertion point carries three unknowns (x, y and the angle `blkAng`; the scale is fixed) and every parameter of its geometry is tied to them, so an instance counts 3 DOF (two instances in an empty sketch: 6, Under Defined), a dimension or relation to any of its points places or turns the whole instance (Horizontal on a turned instance's line turns it level), and one that would bend it is over-defining like any other: a dimension between two of its points is redundant, a second Horizontal on its upright line is a conflict whose message names *Block Name (a block is rigid)*, and SketchXpert offers to remove it. A direct edit that moved a whole instance (Move Entities, a typed coordinate) takes its insertion point along at the next solve (`Kernel.syncBlocks`). *Edit Block* (on the selected instance; menu again or leaving the sketch to finish) frees that instance's geometry for the sketch tools — what is drawn meanwhile joins the block — and then every instance follows the new definition. (rfAZ) The relations and dimensions on the other instances stay on the same pieces (matched by their place in the definition, or by kind and position when the edit took pieces out), as do relations to any insertion point and a Horizontal / Vertical / Fix on one of the edited instance's own pieces; the edited instance's relations among its own pieces (or on what was drawn in the edit) go into the definition; a relation on a piece the edit took out goes and is counted in the status line (`redefineBlock` returns `{ ok, dropped }`). *Explode* turns instances into ordinary geometry (the last instance takes its definition with it). Definitions live on the sketch as `sketch.blocks = { Name: { ents } }` in block coordinates and round-trip through scripts as `.blocks({ defs, tags })`. (rfAU) Sketch DXF output writes each definition once in the BLOCKS section (block coordinates, base point 0, 0; entities on the default layer on layer 0, so they take the INSERT's layer) and each instance as an INSERT (insertion point, rotation, uniform scale) on HOLES or OUTLINE, in R12 and R2000 (R2000: a BLOCK_RECORD per block, owner handles on its BLOCK, ENDBLK and entities); `exportDXFSketch(sketch, units, { explode: true })` writes the exploded geometry instead. `DxfImport.parse(text, { blocks: true })` reads a flat, uniformly scaled, unmirrored INSERT (and each cell of a MINSERT) back as a block instance (nested blocks exploded into its definition; others exploded as before); `Kernel.adoptBlocks(sketch)` (run by every solve and by Insert Block) gives instances whose definition the sketch lacks their definition, `Kernel.blockDefs(sketch)` without changing it. Kernel API: `makeBlock(sketch, idx, name, [x, y])`, `insertBlock(sketch, name, x, y, ang, s)`, `setBlockInstance(sketch, id, { x, y, ang, s })`, `redefineBlock(sketch, id, extra)`, `explodeBlock(sketch, id)`, `blockInstances(sketch)`, `syncBlocks(sketch, skipId)`, `blockFile(sketch, name)` / `addBlockDef(sketch, obj, name)`.
- **Line tool arcs** (rfAT): the Line tool's tangent arc (back over the end point and out, or **A**) uses the same intent zones as Tangent Arc — out along the line / arc: tangent; back over it: reverse tangent; square to it: normal (no Tangent relation); back within 10 px of the end point lets the next way out choose again. `UI.lineArcZone()` reports the zone.

The Pattern and chain Offset relations round-trip through scripts as `.rel("pattern", refs, { pat })` and `.rel("offset", refs, { chain })`; On Edge as `.rel("onEdge", [i], { at })`.

### Transform tab ▸ Offset and Layout (`ui-transform-layout.js`)

Mastercam's Transform tab acts on the open sketch's geometry (outside a sketch: the toast *Open a sketch: Transform acts on sketch geometry*). Each command takes the selection when it starts plus what is clicked or window-selected while its PropertyManager is open, previews live, ✓ applies as one undo step and leaves the result selected, ✓+ (*Apply*, beside ✗) applies and keeps the PropertyManager open for the next selection. Plain copies: no relations tie them to the original (Mastercam wireframe).

- **Offset ▸ Entity** (`UI.cmdTOffsetEntity()`): click one entity, then the side (the preview follows the cursor until the click; *Flip side* turns it); *Move* / *Copy*, *Distance*, *Number of copies* (each copy is the one before offset again). Lines, arcs, circles, rectangles, slots and polygons stay true entities (`SketchSolver.offsetEntity`); ellipses and splines give splines. *Move* deletes the original.
- **Offset ▸ Chains** (Mastercam Offset Contour, `UI.cmdTOffsetChains()`): a click on a line or an arc takes the whole chain joined to it end to end (a click on a taken chain drops it; a circle is a chain of its own; a window takes the chains among what it selects). A closed chain runs counter-clockwise, so *Left* is inside and *Right* outside; an open chain runs the way the picked entity is drawn; *Reverse* turns them all. *Move* / *Copy*, *Distance*, *Left* / *Right* / *Both*, *Number of copies* (at 1 ×, 2 × … the distance), *Incremental depth* (3D: ignored in a sketch), *Corners*: *None* (outside corners sharp: the copies extended to meet), *Sharp* (an arc of radius = distance, centred on the corner, on outside corners whose included angle is under *Max angle*, default 135°; the others sharp), *All* (an arc on every outside corner). The result is exact (`SketchSolver.contourOffset(E, items, closed, d, { corners, maxAngle })`, `sketch-solver.js`): lines stay lines, arcs stay concentric arcs, inside corners are trimmed where the copies cross, an arc that shrinks to nothing goes and its neighbours meet, and the loops a closed chain's copy makes where it crosses itself are cut away (every piece nearer the chain than the distance goes).
- **Layout ▸ Rectangular Array** (`UI.cmdTRectArray()`): *Direction 1* (*Number of instances*, *Distance*, *Angle*) and *Direction 2* (the same, its angle measured from direction 1, default 90°), each *One way*, *Reverse* or *Both ways*. The original is instance 1, 1; copies only (relations among the copied entities are copied with them).
- **Layout ▸ Distribute** (`UI.cmdTDistribute()`): the selected entities fall into groups (entities whose boxes touch or overlap are one group: an outline with its holes), ordered along *X*, *Y* or *At an angle* (typed, or read from a clicked line). *Evenly between the first and the last* (those two stay) or *Fixed spacing*; *By centers* (the groups' box centres) or *By gaps between boxes*. Only the position along the direction changes. `UI.transformLayout.distributeMoves(f, values)` gives the moves without making them.
### Transform tab: Translate to Plane, Project, Roll, Stretch, Scale (`ui-transform-3d.js`)

Mastercam's Transform functions act on sketch geometry (Anvil's wireframe) in the open sketch; outside one they say *Open a sketch: Transform acts on sketch geometry* (Roll ▸ Unroll also runs outside a sketch). Every PropertyManager has a live preview, ✓ (one undo step, the result selected) and ✓+ (apply and keep it open). Geometry that changes plane goes through a 2D affine map (`UI.transform3D.mapEntity(e, A)`, `frameMap(Fs, Ft)`): lines, points, polylines and the control points of Style Splines and conics map exactly; circles, arcs and ellipses stay circles / arcs under a similarity and become exact ellipses / partial ellipses otherwise (a line when seen edge-on); rectangles and polygons become closed polylines and slots their lines and caps under a non-similarity.

- **Translate to Plane** (`UI.cmdTTranslateToPlane()`): *Entities to Translate* (the selection), *Method* Move / Copy, *Target Plane* (the *Plane* list: standard planes, reference planes and the other sketches; or click a plane, a planar face or another sketch's entity), *Into the sketch on that plane (new if none)* / *Into a new sketch*, *Edit the result sketch after ✓*; *Origin*: source origin to target origin, or From point (clicked in the sketch, or typed) to To point (clicked on the target plane, or typed). The plane-relative coordinates are kept. Move deletes the originals; the relations that tied them to geometry left behind are dropped and counted in the status bar. One undo step covers both sketches.
- **Project** (`UI.cmdTProject()`): click entities of other sketches, model edges or faces (their boundary edges); they are projected square onto the open sketch's plane (*To plane*) or into the sketch on the parallel plane at *Depth* (*To depth*: an existing sketch there, else a new one — `{ ref, offset + depth }` for a standard plane). A circle on a tilted plane comes in as an exact ellipse; collinear pieces of straight model edges join. Move takes sketch entities out of their sketch (model edges stay).
- **Roll** (`UI.cmdTRoll()`): *Unroll* — pick a cylindrical face or a circular edge of one; every edge on that face is laid flat with arc length s = R·θ along the circumference and the axial distance across (*Diameter* defaults to the face's, *Seam angle*, *Direction*, *Axis along Y / X*, *X / Y* offset, *Seam lines* close a full tube's blank). Circles square to the axis come out as single lines from R·θ to R·θ of their ends, lines along the axis as lines, a point where a planar cut (a window, a saw cut) meets the cylinder is put back on the true cylinder (A cos φ + B sin φ = C on its circle) so the facets do not shift it, and any other edge (a radial hole's saddle outline) as a spline through its unrolled points. A partial face (a bend) starts at its own edge. Into the open sketch, or into a new sketch on the chosen plane. *Roll* — Anvil's sketches are flat (it holds no 3D curves and its Features ▸ Wrap button has no command behind it), so the selection wrapped round a cylinder of *Diameter* whose axis runs along the sketch's X or Y through *Axis X / Y* is drawn as seen square to the sketch: a point at distance x from the axis goes to R·sin(x / R); lines along the axis stay lines, lines across it within a quarter turn stay lines, the rest become splines through the mapped points.
- **Stretch** (`UI.cmdTStretch()`): window the region (drag a box; right to left is a crossing window) before or while it is open, then *Delta X / Y* or *From / To points*; what lies inside the window moves and what crosses it stretches (`SketchSolver.stretchEntities`, relations kept). ✓+ moves the window with the geometry so the next ✓ stretches the same region again.
- **Scale** (`UI.cmdTScale()`): *Uniform* or *XYZ* (X and Y factors), *Scale factor* or *Percentage*, about the *Origin*, a clicked or typed *Point* or the *Selection centre*; *Move*, *Copy* or *Join* with *Number of copies* (each copy scaled again from the one before; Join adds a line from each end to its copy). Uniform keeps entity types (`SketchSolver.transformEntities`); XYZ turns circles into ellipses. Move drops the relations to unselected geometry and counts them.
## Transform tab (Mastercam)

Mastercam 2024's Transform tab, after Sheet Metal: **Position** (Dynamic, Translate, Translate to Plane, Rotate, Project, Move to Origin, Mirror, Roll), **Offset** (Offset Entity, Offset Chains), **Layout** (Geometry Nesting; Rectangular Array and Distribute stacked), **Size** (Stretch, Scale), each group's caption under its buttons. Every button runs `UI['cmdT' + name]()` and toasts "… is not available yet" when that command is not loaded; outside a sketch the commands toast "Open a sketch: Transform acts on sketch geometry" (Geometry Nesting excepted). Transforms act on sketch geometry in the open sketch: the selection when the command starts, plus what is clicked or box-selected while its PropertyManager is open. Search Commands finds them (Transform's Mirror as *Mirror (Transform)*).

The PropertyManagers share Mastercam's options (`ui-transform.js`): **Move / Copy / Join** (Join: the copies plus lines joining corresponding end points of consecutive instances), *Number of copies* with *Distance between / Total distance* (Rotate: *Angle between / Total sweep*), a live preview, ✓ (one undo step; the result is selected) and **✓+** (apply and keep the PM open, the result in its box for the next). Moving drops the relations to unselected geometry (the status says how many); copies carry no relations.

Shop use (lane BO, `test-reports/2026-10-13-transform-audit.md`): every value box takes shop input (`1/2`, `1-1/2`, `.25`, `25mm`) and **Enter is ✓**; Esc or ✗ cancels and says so in the status bar. As in Mastercam, each command **remembers its last-used values** for the next run (Move / Copy / Join, copies, distances, angles, the mirror axis, the offset distance and corners, the array counts, the scale factor …) in the session and in `localStorage` (`anvil.transform`); points picked on screen, picked entities and the picked mirror line are asked for again. `UI.transformMemory` (`get(name)`, `recall(name, values, forget)`, `keep(name, values, forget)`, `reset()`). The **preview is drawn in the Transform result colour** (magenta, `UI.transformPreviewColor()`; the sketch tools keep the sketch blue), so what the command will make can be told from what is there, in the light and the dark theme. After ✓ the status bar keeps the command's result line ("Copied 1 entity × 3.", "Offset: 3 copies at .500 in.", "Rectangular Array: 6 instances …") with the result selected.

- **Translate**: Delta X / Y, Polar (Length, Angle) or From / To points clicked on screen.
- **Rotate**: Center (the origin, or a point clicked), Angle, copies; *Rotate* or *Translate* (the copies travel round the centre keeping their direction).
- **Mirror**: about the X axis (at a Y), the Y axis (at an X), a line at an angle through a point, a sketch line clicked, or two points; Move / Copy; *Mirror text* (off: text stays readable, moved to its mirrored place).
- **Move to Origin**: click a point; the selection (nothing selected: the whole sketch) moves so it lands on the origin, at once.
- **Dynamic**: Mastercam's dynamic gnomon at a point placed with its *Origin* box or by dragging the ball: X (red), Y (green) and Z (blue) arrows with cone heads and axis letters, a grey ball at the centre, and three quarter-arc rotation handles between the axis pairs (each in the colour of the axis it turns about, white ticks along it), drawn as an SVG overlay that projects 3D points every frame, so it keeps its size on screen (110 px axes) at any zoom and follows the view's rotation. Drag an arrow to move along it (a guide line runs along the axis), an arc to turn about its axis (a dotted yellow ring with direction arrows and the swept wedge); the value box next to the cursor shows the distance or the angle (`-25.00°`), and a number typed while dragging, or into the box after it, sets the move exactly (Enter; Tab moves on to the PM). Angles snap to 1° (15° with Shift, or the PM's *Angle increment*), distances to the document's precision and to a sketch point the gnomon lands on; the ball snaps to sketch points and the origin and moves the gnomon without moving the geometry. A click on the X or Y letter, then on a line, aligns that axis with the line (*X axis angle* in the PM). In a sketch only in-plane moves apply: the Z arrow and the two out-of-plane arcs are faded and inert ("Only in-plane moves apply in a sketch"). The moves add up (*Delta X / Y*, *Angle*) until ✓; the gnomon travels with the geometry. Tests: `UI.pmSpec().dynamic` (`geo()`, `hitPart(p)`, `typed(text)`, `pose()`, `state()`).

Engine, for every Transform command: `UI.transformEntities(f, idx, xf, opts)` moves (or with `opts.copy` / `opts.join` copies) sketch entities by a 2D affine `[a, b, c, d, e, f]` in SVG order (x' = a·x + c·y + e, y' = b·x + d·y + f), or by a list of them (one per copy; one matrix with `opts.count` copies by its powers). Every entity type: lines, arcs, circles, ellipses and partial ellipses, splines (style splines and conics too), slots and arc slots, polygons, rectangles (four lines once they turn off the axes), polylines, text (`opts.mirrorText`) and block instances (a copy is a new instance; reflected or unevenly scaled they become ordinary geometry). An uneven scale turns circles into ellipses and arcs into partial ellipses; text cannot be stretched unevenly. Returns `{ ok, error, entities | added, joins, dropped, message }`; `opts.undo` (default true: one undo step) and `opts.select` (default true). `UI.transformPreview(f, idx, xf, opts)` gives the entities it would make; `UI.transformAffine` the matrix helpers (`translate`, `rotate`, `scale`, `mirror`, `mirrorDir`, `mul`, `apply`, `power`, `classify`); `UI.transformPM(def)` the shared PropertyManager.

## Sketch relations

A sketch may carry `rels`, geometric relations and two-entity driving dimensions solved together with its Smart Dimensions (`dims`) by `sketch-solver.js`. Relations name pieces of entities by index:

| Ref | Means |
| --- | --- |
| `"O"` | the sketch origin (fixed) |
| `"3"` | entity 3 as a whole: a line, circle / arc, slot axis, or a point entity |
| `"3.p1"`, `"3.p2"`, `"3.mid"` | start / end point (lines, slots, arcs), midpoint (lines, slots) |
| `"3.c"` | centre (circles, arcs, polygons, rectangles, slots) |
| `"3.v0"`, `"3.e0"` | corner / edge i of a rectangle, polygon or polyline (an edge is a line) |
| `"3.e0.mid"` | midpoint of edge i of a rectangle, polygon or polyline, or of side 0 / 1 of a straight slot (a point: distances, Coincident, Smart Dimension's midpoint pick) |

Types (SolidWorks names): `coincident` (point–point, point on line, point on circle), `horizontal` / `vertical` (a line, or two points), `parallel`, `perpendicular`, `collinear` (two lines), `tangent` (line–circle/arc, circle–circle; at a shared end point it is written as an angle condition there), `concentric`, `equal` (two lines or two circles/arcs), `midpoint` (point, line), `symmetric` (two points, two lines or two circles / arcs and the centerline they mirror about, refs `[a, b, axis]`: each mirrored pair has its midpoint on the axis and its chord perpendicular to it; lines pair their ends as they mirror when the relation is made; circles also get equal radii), `fix` (`at` holds the fixed values), and the driving dimensions `distance` (point / line / circle centre pairs; between a line and anything it is the perpendicular distance) and `angle` (two lines, degrees). Stored as `{ type, refs, v?, off?, at? }`:

```js
p.sketch("Front")
  .line(0, 0, 58, 1).line(58, 1, 59, 22).line(59, 22, 1, 19).line(1, 19, 0, 0)   // drawn by eye
  .dim(0, "len", { value: 60 }).dim(1, "len", { value: 20 })
  .rel("coincident", ["0.p2", "1.p1"]).rel("coincident", ["1.p2", "2.p1"])
  .rel("coincident", ["2.p2", "3.p1"]).rel("coincident", ["3.p2", "0.p1"])
  .rel("coincident", ["0.p1", "O"])
  .rel("horizontal", ["0"]).rel("vertical", ["1"]).rel("horizontal", ["2"]).rel("vertical", ["3"])   // 60 x 20, Fully Defined
  .rel("distance", ["0", "2"], 25)          // over-defines: flagged, not applied
  .extrude(10);
```

Distance relations take `dir: 'h' | 'v'` (a horizontal / vertical distance between points or centres, what Smart Dimension makes when placed above / beside them) or `dir: 'r'` (two concentric circles or arcs: the radial distance, what Offset Entities and Smart Dimension on concentric circles make), and `tx` (the label position along the dimension line); angle relations take `sup: true` (the supplementary angle) plus `q` / `ta` (the quadrant and label position the cursor chose). All of them round-trip through `.rel(type, refs, { value, dir, tx, sup, q, ta })`.

**Solver.** Every entity parameter is an unknown (arc angles in degrees). Each relation and dimension is one or two residual equations; damped Gauss-Newton (Levenberg-Marquardt) takes minimum-norm steps `dx = -Jᵀ (J Jᵀ + λI)⁻¹ r` (normal equations when there are more equations than unknowns), so an under-defined sketch moves as little as possible. Smart Dimensions are driving at the value the geometry has when the solve starts, unless an edit gives a new one. After a solve the Jacobian is reduced row by row: DOF = unknowns − rank; a relation whose rows add no rank is *redundant* (yellow), one left unsatisfied is a *conflict* (red, and the geometry stays where it was); an entity whose unknowns are all pinned is fully defined (black), otherwise under defined (blue). The sketch is *Under Defined*, *Fully Defined* or *Over Defined* (status bar).

**Driven dimensions.** A distance / angle relation or a Smart Dimension with `driven: true` is a reference dimension: it adds no equation, `analyze` reports it as `'driven'`, and its value follows the geometry (`updateDriven` after every solve writes the measured `v` of driven relations; a Smart Dimension holds no value, so it is always current). `setDim` / `setRelValue` refuse driven dimensions. `overDefines(sketch, 'dim' | 'rel', i)` says whether a dimension taken as driving would over-define the sketch (it removes no degree of freedom or adds a conflict); `setDriven(sketch, kind, i, on)` switches it. In the app, a new Smart Dimension (single entity or two-entity) that would over-define the sketch opens the *Make Dimension Driven?* modal (*Make this dimension driven* / *Leave this dimension driving*, `#drv-driven` / `#drv-driving`); driven dimensions are grey and in parentheses, and clicking one offers *Make driving*. Script: `.driven([i, …])` after the `.dim()` calls, `.rel("distance", refs, { driven: true })`.

**Construction geometry.** An entity with `construction: true` is solved like any other but is left out of profiles and drawn dashed. *Construction Geometry* (Sketch tab, next to Add Relation) toggles the selected entities; *Centerline* draws construction lines with the Line tool; the entity PropertyManager has *For construction*. A line drawn across a centerline with its ends mirrored infers only what any line would (a level one gets Horizontal): SolidWorks never infers Symmetric while sketching, and neither does Anvil (`SketchSolver.infer(…, { symmetric: true })` still finds it for a line, point or circle that mirrors an existing one); Mirror Entities and Dynamic Mirror add it, and *Add Relations* offers Symmetric for two like items and a line (a centerline is preferred as the axis). Script: `.construction([i, …])`. `anvil.construction(i | [i…])`, `anvil.driven('dim' | 'rel', i, on?)`.

**API** (global `SketchSolver`, no DOM): `solve(sketch, { dimTargets, relTargets, mob })`, `analyze(sketch)` → `{ dof, status, entities[], rels[], dims[], redundant, conflicts }`, `addRelation(sketch, type, refs, { value, off })` → `{ ok, rels, status, analysis }`, `deleteRelation`, `setDim(sketch, index | dim, value)` and `setRelValue(sketch, index, value)` (re-solve; refused and unchanged when the value conflicts or the dimension is driven), `overDefines`, `setDriven`, `updateDriven`, `measure(sketch, type, refs)`, `applicable(sketch, refs)` (what Add Relations offers), `drag(sketch, ref, to, startEntities)`, `infer(sketch, i)` / `inferPreview(sketch, entity)`, `removeEntity(sketch, i)` / `remap(sketch, before)` / `clean(sketch)` (relations follow entity edits), `describe`, `refName`, `pointRefs`, `curveRefs`. kernel.js is not edited for any of this: the file adds `rel` / `solve` to the SketchBuilder prototype and wraps `Kernel.generateScript` to write `.rel(...)` lines before each sketch's `.id();`, so relations round-trip through scripts; a model without `rels` produces the same script and geometry as before.

**UI.** Sketching infers relations the way SolidWorks does (Coincident where a point lands on an existing point or the origin, Concentric for centres, Midpoint, Horizontal / Vertical for level lines), with yellow inference icons next to the entity being drawn; an inferred relation that would over-define is skipped. Relation icons sit next to the entities (*View ▸ Sketch Relations* hides them; click one to select it, Delete removes it). *Add Relation* (Sketch tab, *Tools ▸ Relations*) opens the Add Relations PropertyManager: pick entities and points in the graphics area, the applicable relations are offered as buttons, *Existing Relations* lists the ones already on them. *Display/Delete Relations* lists every relation with its state (Satisfied, Over Defining, Not Solved), filters *All in this sketch / Overdefining/Not Solved / Selected Entities*, and deletes one or all. Smart Dimension on a second entity or point makes a distance (or, for two non-parallel lines, angle) dimension; editing any dimension re-solves the whole sketch. With the Select tool, dragging an entity or point moves it while every relation and dimension holds, so fully defined geometry does not move; a dragged end point or centre dropped on another sketch point (9 px) or curve (7 px) snaps there and takes a Coincident (Concentric, Midpoint), dropped on a model vertex, centre or edge (sketch on a face, *Snap to Model* on) it lands there exactly with no relation, and with **Ctrl** held it lands where it is dropped (no snaps, as the drawing tools). Selecting two or more entities or points with Ctrl / Shift + click or a box opens the *Properties* PM with *Add Relations* (see *Selection and Instant3D*). `anvil.relations()`, `anvil.addRelation(type, refs, value?)`, `anvil.deleteRelation(i)`, `anvil.sketchStatus()`, `UI.editRelDim(i, text)`.

## Multi-body parts

A part is a list of separate solid bodies, as in SolidWorks. Most parts have one; a second appears when a boss is made with *Merge result* off, or when a boss lands clear of everything already there.

- **Merge rule.** A boss (extrude, revolve, sweep, loft, pattern, mirror) unions with every existing body whose bounding box its own overlaps or touches; when it touches several they collapse into the first one's slot. A boss that touches nothing starts a new body. `merge: false` (the *Merge result* checkbox unchecked) always starts a new body, even where it overlaps another.
- **Feature scope.** A cut subtracts from every body unless it carries `bodies: [i, …]`, the *Feature Scope* group of the Cut PropertyManager (*All bodies* / *Selected bodies* with one checkbox per body, shown when the part has two or more bodies). A body a cut empties is removed from the list. The warning *Cut removed no material* appears only when no body in the scope changed.
- **Picked features.** Shell, fillet, chamfer, edge flange, hem and jog act on the body nearest their first pick point (`edges[0].at` / `faces[0].at`; a shell with no faces takes body 0); a sketched bend on the body nearest its first bend line. `mirror` with `body: true` mirrors the whole part and then applies the merge rule.
- **Solid Bodies folder.** The FeatureManager's *Solid Bodies(n)* folder holds one row per body, named after the feature that created it (`Boss-Extrude1`, `Mirror1`, …); it opens by default when there is more than one. Clicking a row selects the body (`UI.selectBody(i)`, `App.sel = { kind: 'body', index }`), and a body can be hidden from the viewport with `UI.setBodyHidden(i, hidden)` / `UI.isBodyHidden(i)` (`App.hiddenBodies`) without changing the mesh, the mass properties or the exports. *Mass Properties* lists each body's volume when there are two or more.
- **Automation.** `anvil.bodies()` returns `bodyInfo` with volumes to 3 decimals, `anvil.selectBody(i)` selects one, and `anvil.summary().bodies` is the count. The *Two-body clamp* example in the Design Library shows all of it.

```js
p.box(60, 30, 10);                                   // base: one body
p.box(60, 30, 6, { at: [0, 15, 0], merge: false }); // a cap 5 mm above it, kept as body 1
p.hole("Top", 10, -15, 6, { offset: 21, bodies: [1] }); // through the cap only; the base below stays solid
p.fillet(3, [[5, 0, 0]]);                            // picked near a base corner: rounds the base only
```

## Equations

Global variables, as in SolidWorks' *Equations, Global Variables, and Dimensions*: a part keeps an ordered list `model.equations = [{ name: "T", expr: "10" }, { name: "W", expr: "T*2" }]`. Each expression may use the variables above it; the list is evaluated top to bottom on every rebuild.

- **"=" fields.** Any numeric field of a feature (extrude `depth` / `depth2`, `angle`, `draft`, pattern spacing and count, fillet `r`, chamfer `d`, shell `thickness`, flange `length`, plane and sketch-plane `offset`, sheet parameters, nested fields such as `dir2.depth`) may hold a string that starts with `=` instead of a number: `{ type: "extrude", depth: "=W+1" }`. The model keeps the text; rebuild evaluates a resolved copy. Sketch geometry and references (`entities`, `profile`, `sketch`, `edges`, `faces`, `bodies`, `feature`, `id`, `name`) are never expressions: sketch sizes are plain numbers.
- **Grammar.** Numbers (`1.5`, `.5`, `2e3`), `+ - * /`, `^` (or `**`, right-associative: `2^3^2` is 512; unary minus binds looser, so `-2^2` is −4), parentheses, variables, the constants `pi` and `e` (a variable of that name wins), and functions: `sin cos tan asin acos atan sinh cosh tanh` (radians), `sind cosd tand asind acosd atand` (degrees), `deg(x)`, `rad(x)`, `sqrt cbrt abs floor ceil round trunc sign exp log log10 log2`, `atan2(y, x)`, `pow(a, b)`, `min(…)`, `max(…)`, `hypot(…)`. No `eval`: a hand-written parser.
- **Errors.** An unknown name, a wrong argument count, a stray character, division by zero or a non-finite result is an error. A failing variable (or a duplicate or invalid name) is left out of the variables and reported in `build.equationErrors` (`{ name: message }`); a feature whose field fails gets the error `"<field>: <message>"` (`"depth: Unknown variable \"Q\""`) and leaves the body alone. Rebuild never throws for either.
- **Rebuild cache.** Features are keyed by their resolved values, so changing `T` rebuilds only the features whose numbers change; editing an equation's text without changing its value reuses everything.
- **Script.** `p.equations({ T: 10, W: "=T*2" })` (object form; a leading `=` is dropped) or `p.equations([{ name: "T", expr: "10" }, …])` (array form, kept as written; what `generateScript` emits) replaces the list; numeric arguments of script calls accept `"=…"` strings: `.extrude("=W")`, `p.sketch("Front", "=H")`, `p.linearPattern({ spacing: "=L/4", count: "=N" })`. Sizes that become sketch geometry (`p.box` width and depth, `p.cylinder` radius, `p.hole` diameter) are evaluated when the script runs.
- **Kernel API.** `Kernel.isExpression(v)`, `Kernel.evalExpression(expr, vars)`, `Kernel.resolveEquations(model)` → `{ vars, errors }`, `Kernel.resolveFeature(f, vars)`, `Kernel.resolvedModel(model)` → `{ model, vars, errors, featureErrors, sheetError }`, `Kernel.hasExpressions(f)`; a rebuild result carries `equations` (the values) and `equationErrors`, and both survive `packBuild` / `unpackBuild`.
- **UI.** *Tools ▸ Equations…* (also a double-click on the FeatureManager's *Equations* folder) opens a table of name / value-or-equation / evaluates-to rows with add, move up / down and delete; each keystroke re-evaluates the list, rows in error turn red and OK is refused until they are fixed; OK is one undo step. PropertyManager number fields accept `=`: typing `=W+1` turns the box into an equation, the hint beside it shows the value (red with the message when it fails, and OK is disabled). The sketch *Modify* box accepts `=T*3` or plain arithmetic and stores the resulting number. Equation errors show in the status bar as *Equation W: …*.
- **Automation.** `anvil.equations()` returns a copy of the list; `anvil.equations({ T: 12 })` or `anvil.equations([...])` replaces it and returns a Promise of the summary; `anvil.summary().equations` and `.equationErrors` hold the evaluated values and errors. `UI.showEquations()`, `UI.pmSetField(key, text)`, `UI.pmExpressions()`, `UI.evalInput(text)`, `UI.equationVars()` and `UI.editDim(i, text)` drive the same paths from code. The *Parametric plate* example in the Design Library uses all of it.

```js
p.equations({ L: 120, W: 80, T: 8, E: 15, D: 8 });
p.box("=L", "=W", "=T");                                  // thickness stays live
p.hole("Top", 15, -15, "=D", { offset: "=T" });
p.linearPattern({ dir: "x", spacing: "=L - 2*E", count: 2, dir2: "z", spacing2: "=W - 2*E", count2: 2 });
```

## Configurations

Named sets of values for the global variables, as in SolidWorks' *ConfigurationManager*: one part, several sizes. A configuration overrides the expressions of the variables it names; the others keep their base expression from `model.equations`.

```js
model.configurations = [{ name: "Small", values: { T: "6", N: "2" } }, { name: "Large", values: { T: "12", D: "T-4" } }];
model.activeConfiguration = "Small";   // absent, null or an unknown name: the base equations
```

- **Values** are expression strings without the leading `=`. They are evaluated in the equations' order, so a value may use the variables above it (`D: "T-4"` sees the configuration's `T`). A value for a name that is not an equation is ignored.
- **What follows a switch.** Every live feature field (extrude and cut depth, plane and sketch-plane offset, pattern spacing and count, fillet `r`, chamfer `d`, shell thickness, …) resolves against the active set on every rebuild. Sizes that a script turns into sketch geometry (`p.box` width and depth, `p.cylinder` radius, `p.hole` diameter, sketch dimensions typed as `=…`) are evaluated once, when the script runs, with the configuration active at that line; they do not change on a later switch.
- **Rebuild cache.** Cache keys follow the resolved values, so switching configuration rebuilds only the features whose numbers change (`build.reused` counts the reused steps), and switching back gives the identical body.
- **Script.** `p.configurations([{ name, values }], active?)` replaces the list (numbers become strings, a leading `=` is dropped; an empty list removes the configurations and the active name). `p.activeConfiguration(name)` activates one and throws for an unknown name; `p.activeConfiguration(null)` returns to the base equations. `generateScript` writes both right after `p.equations(...)`.
- **Kernel API.** `Kernel.configurationList(model)`, `Kernel.findConfiguration(model, name)`, `Kernel.activeConfiguration(model)` → the object or `null`, `Kernel.effectiveEquations(model)` → `[{ name, expr }]` with the active overrides, `Kernel.captureConfiguration(model)` → `{ name: expr }` (what a new configuration starts from), `Kernel.applyEquations(model, list)` (writes an edited list back: without an active configuration it replaces `model.equations`; with one, a variable the configuration names changes in that configuration only and any other variable changes in the base; a name dropped from the list leaves every configuration). `resolveEquations`, `resolvedModel` and a rebuild result carry `configuration` (the active name or `null`), and it survives `packBuild` / `unpackBuild`.
- **UI.** The *ConfigurationManager* tab above the FeatureManager lists the configurations; the active one is marked, and the buttons add, rename, delete and activate (each one undo step). The first *Add* on a part without configurations creates *Default* (a copy of the current values) plus the new one and activates the new one. The Equations dialog edits the active configuration's values, and the part row of the tree reads *Name (Config)*.
- **Automation.** `anvil.configurations()` → `{ active, list: [{ name, values }] }`; `anvil.configurations(list, active?)`, `anvil.activateConfiguration(name)` and `anvil.configurations.add / rename / remove / activate` return a Promise of the summary; `anvil.summary().configuration` is the active name and `.configurations` the names. `UI.configurations` has the same operations, and `UI.setFmTab('config')` opens the tab. The *Configured spacer* example in the Design Library has three configurations.

```js
p.equations({ T: 10, N: 3, S: 20, D: "=T/2" });
p.configurations([
  { name: "Small", values: { T: 6, N: 2, D: 3 } },
  { name: "Large", values: { T: 12, N: 4, S: 18, D: "=T-4" } }
], "Small");
p.sketch("Front").rect(0, 0, 80, 40).extrude("=T");
p.sketch("Front", "=T").rect(10, 10, 10, 20).cut("=D", { reverse: true });
p.linearPattern({ dir: "x", spacing: "=S", count: "=N" });
```

### Suppress, per-configuration suppression and the Design Table

- **Feature JSON.** `suppressed: true` on a feature suppresses it in every configuration that does not say otherwise; `configuration.suppressed = { "<feature id>": true | false }` overrides it in that configuration (`false`: unsuppressed there). A feature whose parent (its sketch, pattern seed, sketch plane or face, reference axis — `Kernel.featureParents`) is suppressed is suppressed with it. `Kernel.resolvedModel` hands the rebuild copies flagged `suppressed`, so Anvil's rebuild and OpenCascade both skip them; `build.suppressed` (`{ id: true }`, also through `packBuild`) lists them. Mass properties, drawings and exports read the build, so they follow.
- **Kernel API.** `Kernel.setSuppressed(model, ids, on, scope)` with scope `'all'` (f.suppressed; overrides of those features cleared), `'this'` (the active configuration) or `[names]` (the others keep what they show); `Kernel.suppressedIds(model, cfg?)`, `Kernel.isSuppressed(model, id, cfg?)`, `Kernel.featureChildren` / `featureAncestors`.
- **Script.** `p.suppress(ref, configs?, opts?)` / `p.unsuppress(ref, configs?, opts?)`: a feature id (script variable), name or list; configs omitted / `null` = all, `'this'`, or a name / list (an unknown name throws). As in the UI (`Kernel.suppressWithChildren`), Suppress also suppresses the children (a fillet on a hole's edge goes with the hole), Unsuppress also the suppressed features it needs, `{ dependents: true }` its children too; `{ exact: true }` sets just the listed features. `generateScript` writes the states after the features with `{ exact: true }`, by variable, so renumbered ids round-trip.
- **UI.** FeatureManager right-click and *Edit*: *Suppress*, *Unsuppress*, *Unsuppress with Dependents*; with configurations each has *This Configuration* (the plain row), *▸ All Configurations* and *▸ Specify Configurations…* (a check box per configuration). Suppress also suppresses the children (by id, and the features whose face / edge picks lie on faces the feature made — a fillet on a hole's edge goes with the hole), each stored suppressed as SolidWorks does; Unsuppress unsuppresses the feature and what it needs, *with Dependents* also its children. One undo step each; suppressed rows are grey. The ConfigurationManager rows show *n suppressed*.
- **Design Table** (*Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Design Table…*, ConfigurationManager *Design Table…*): rows = configurations, columns = global variables (`W`), feature dimensions (`D1@Boss-Extrude1` in `Kernel.DIM_FIELDS` order, or `depth@Boss-Extrude1`; Apply drives the field through a new variable `D1_Boss_Extrude1` with `dim` naming the column) and `$STATE@<feature>` (S / U). Editable cells, × on a row / column, *Add configuration*, *Add column…*, *Copy* / *Paste…* as tab-separated text (Excel), a multi-cell paste into a cell fills from there. Apply / OK check the grid first: a bad cell turns red with its message and nothing changes; a good grid is one undo step. `Kernel.designTable(model)` → grid, `Kernel.applyDesignTable(model, grid)` → `{ ok, errors: [{ row, col, message }], created }`, `Kernel.gridToTSV` / `tsvToGrid`; `anvil.designTable()` / `anvil.designTable(grid | tsv)`.
- **Automation.** `anvil.suppress(refs, scope?)`, `anvil.unsuppress(refs, scope?, dependents?)` → summary with `suppressed` (names); `anvil.suppressed(cfg?)`; `UI.setSuppression(ids, on, scope, dependents)`.
- **Assemblies.** A component shows one configuration of its part: `c.config` (`a.insert(src, { config: "NoHole" })`, right-click ▸ *Configuration ▸ <name>*, `anvil.componentConfig(comp, name)`); `c.suppressed` suppresses a component (right-click *Suppress*, `anvil.suppressComponent`). Assembly configurations: `model.configurations = [{ name, components: { <id>: { config, suppressed, hidden } } }]` with `activeConfiguration` (`a.configuration(name, { components: { "<name>": {…} } })`, `a.activeConfiguration(name)`), listed above the exploded views in the assembly's ConfigurationManager (*Add…* / *Show* / *Delete*); a component change made while configurations exist goes to the active one, and Hide / Show is recorded there. `Assembly.compState`, `setComponentState(model, id, patch, scope)`, `partConfigurations(source)`. The part-build cache is keyed by source + configuration; a drawing of the assembly shows the active configuration.

## Assemblies

An assembly document places parts (*components*) in space and positions them with *mates*, as in SolidWorks. `assembly.js` (global `Assembly`, no DOM, loaded after `kernel.js`) holds the model, the mate solver, the script API and mass properties; the UI switches its ribbon, tree and commands when the open document is an assembly.

### Model JSON

```js
{ type: "assembly", name: "Assem1", units: "mm",
  features: [],                        // always empty, so part-oriented code stays safe
  components: [{ id: 1, name: "Plate<1>", source: { example: "Plate with bolt holes" }, fixed: true,
                 transform: { position: [0, 0, 0], rotation: [1, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 1] } }],
  mates: [{ id: 3, name: "Concentric1", type: "concentric", a: Ref, b: Ref, alignment: "aligned", suppressed: false },
          { id: 4, name: "Distance1", type: "distance", a: Ref, b: Ref, distance: 15, flip: true }],
  nextId: 4 }                          // shared id counter for components and mates
```

- **Transform.** `world = R · local + position`, `R` a row-major 3×3 proper rotation. The rebuild writes the solved transforms back into the model, so a saved assembly keeps its solved positions and re-solving it changes nothing (beyond 1e-9).
- **Source** — exactly one of `{ example: name }` (a Design Library part, or an assembly entry: a sub-assembly), `{ script: "p.cylinder(4, 20);" }` (a part script, or an assembly script), `{ model: { …part or assembly JSON… } }` (an embedded copy) or `{ file: name }` (a **live link** to a document in the document store, see *Sub-assemblies and live links*). A string source is an example name.
- **Names.** A component is named after its part model plus an instance number: `Plate<1>`, `Plate<2>`, `Pin<1>`. Mates are `Coincident1`, `Concentric2`, `Distance1`, `Angle1`, … (next free number per type).
- **Fixed.** The first component of an empty assembly is fixed (shown `(f)` in the tree); later ones float (`(-)` while under-defined, no prefix once fully defined, as in SolidWorks) unless inserted with `fixed: true`.

### References

A reference (`Ref`) names geometry on a component by id (the script also accepts a name), in the component's **local** coordinates; without `component` it is the assembly origin.

| Ref | Resolves to |
| --- | --- |
| `{ component, face: [x, y, z] }` | the planar face of that component containing the point |
| `{ component, plane: "Front" \| "Top" \| "Right" }` | the component's standard plane through its origin (normals +Z, +Y, +X) |
| `{ component, cylinder: [x, y, z] }` | the axis of the cylindrical (faceted) face through or nearest the point |
| `{ component, axis: "x" \| "y" \| "z" }` | the component's local axis through its origin |
| `{ plane: "Top" }`, `{ axis: "y" }` | the assembly origin planes and axes (fixed in the world) |

### Mates

- **Coincident** — two planes (faces or planes) coincide. `alignment: "anti-aligned"` makes the normals opposite (faces touching), `"aligned"` makes them equal. Default: anti-aligned when both refs are faces, otherwise aligned.
- **Coincident offset** — `offset: d` (≥ 0) holds B's plane `d` along A's normal instead of on it; `flip: true` puts it on the other side (−d). An offset of 0 is not stored.
- **Concentric** — two axes (cylinders or axes) coincide. Directions equal (`"aligned"`, default) or opposite (`"anti-aligned"`). A cylinder's direction is canonical: the sign whose largest-magnitude local component is positive.
- **Distance** — two planes (faces or planes) parallel, `distance: d` (≥ 0) apart: B's plane lies `d` along A's normal, or −d with `flip: true` (*Flip dimension*). Alignment defaults as for Coincident (anti-aligned for two faces). Distance 0 is Coincident.
- **Angle** — `angle: deg` (0–180) between the directions of two refs (faces, planes, cylinders or axes; the second times the alignment sign, default aligned), measured as a signed angle about the mate's *angle axis* k; `flip: true` measures it the other way round (−deg). k is the line normal to both directions when the mate is solved, with its sign made canonical (so re-solving keeps the same side); when the two directions are parallel it is the hinge axis earlier mates leave the moving component (e.g. a Concentric), else any normal. The angle removes one rotation: a free block with only an Angle keeps 5 DOF; a bar with Coincident + Concentric (hinge) + Angle is fully defined.
- **Parallel** — the directions of two refs (faces, planes, cylinders, axes) parallel: two planes or two axes `dA = s·dB` (removes 2 rotations), a plane and an axis: the axis lies parallel to the plane (removes 1).
- **Perpendicular** — two planes or two axes square to each other (removes 1 rotation); a plane and an axis: the axis along the plane normal (removes 2).
- **Tangent** — a cylinder and a plane: the axis parallel to the plane and one radius above it (on the side the plane normal points to; `flip: true` behind it); two cylinders: axes parallel and `rA + rB` apart (external; `flip: true` internal, `|rA − rB|`). At least one ref must be a cylindrical face. The radius and axis come from the cylinder's exact surface record (`polygon.surf`, cloud-2026-09-26r), so the offset is the true radius at any turn about the axis; a mesh without records (e.g. an imported STL) falls back to the least-squares fit of the facets (`r·cos(π/n)` for an n-gon). Concentric and Distance between cylinders use the exact axes the same way (`Assembly.findCylinder(build, p)` → `{ point, dir, radius, exact }`).
- **Width** (Advanced) — `a` and `a2` are the two width faces (parallel, one component), `b` and `b2` the two tab faces (another component), or `b` alone a cylindrical tab: the tab's mid-plane (or axis) is held on the mid-plane of the width faces. A block in a slot keeps 3 DOF (slide along the slot, lift, turn about the width direction); a cylindrical tab keeps 4.
- **Distance between cylinders** — a Distance with two cylindrical faces or axes: axes parallel, centre distance `distance` (Flip does not apply). A cylinder and a plane is still an error.
- **Limit Distance / Limit Angle** (Advanced) — `min` ≤ value ≤ `max` instead of one value: the plane gap / axis distance (Limit Distance, refs as for Distance) or the signed angle (Limit Angle, refs as for Angle, 0 ≤ min ≤ max ≤ 180°). Inside the range the residual is 0 and the component moves freely (a Limit mate removes no DOF); outside, the solver pulls it to the nearest bound. `flip: true` puts the range on the other side.
- **Gear** (Mechanical) — two rotating components, refs are axes (cylinders or axes) on each; `ratio: [a, b]` (a number r means [r, 1]): when A turns θ about its axis, B turns −θ·a/b about its axis (`flip: true` = Reverse, same direction). Each turn is measured about the ref's own axis direction from the `phase` [θA, θB] (degrees) stored on the mate the first time it is solved, so an assembly reopens where it was. Drag or rotate gear A and gear B follows (the second component is the driven one). Limitation: A's turn is taken within ±180° of its phase, so a non-integer ratio slips if A is spun more than half a turn in one step.
- **Profile Center** (Advanced, the first of SolidWorks 2024's Advanced Mates; rfJA) — two planar faces, one on each component: the faces coincide (anti-aligned by default) or lie `offset` apart (`flip: true` the other side) and their profiles' centres lie on one normal. A profile's centre is the face's area centroid (`Assembly.faceProfile(geom, p)` → `{ center, normal, axis, area }`), so a rectangle's or a circle's centre; a face with holes is centred on its area centroid. 1 DOF left (the turn about the normal); `lock: true` (*Lock rotation*) holds the turn as well (0 DOF): the in-plane angle of B's profile against A's is stored in `phase` [degrees] at the first solve, snapped so the long sides are parallel when both profiles are oblong. A cylindrical pick is refused (*Profile Center needs two planar faces…*). Name `ProfileCenter1`; script `a.profileCenter(faceA, faceB, { offset, flip, lock })`.
- **Linear/Linear Coupler** (Advanced, as in SolidWorks 2024; rfIV) — a direction on each of two components (a planar face or plane: its normal; a cylinder or axis: its direction); `ratio: [a, b]` is SolidWorks' *Ratio* a : b: when A moves a along its direction, B moves b along its own (a number r means [1, r], so ratio 2 moves B twice as far); `flip: true` is *Reverse*. Travel is measured in the assembly frame from the `phase` [sA, sB] (each ref's position along its direction) stored the first time the mate is solved, as Gear's; editing the ratio or Reverse in the PM starts from where the parts stand. It removes one degree of freedom between the two (each part keeps its rails' DOF); dragging either moves the other. No alignment. Name `LinearCoupler1`. *Reference component* is not offered: travel is always relative to the assembly.
- **Screw** (Mechanical, rfKA) — two cylindrical faces or axes (the screw and the nut): Concentric, and B's travel along A's axis is `pitch` per revolution of B about it, measured against A (`flip: true` is *Reverse*). `pitch` is SolidWorks' *Distance/revolution*; `pitchBy: "revolutions"` shows it as *Revolutions/mm* (1 / pitch; the script option `revolutions: n` sets both). The travel and turn when first solved are kept in `phase` [travel, degrees]; the coupling is periodic (a whole turn more is one pitch further), so a nut dragged one revolution round moves exactly one pitch, and a nut pulled along turns. 1 DOF left. Alignment as Concentric (Closest by default).
- **Rack Pinion** (Mechanical, rfKA) — `a` the rack (a linear edge `{ component, edge: [[x, y, z], [x, y, z]] }`, its two ends in the part's frame, or an axis / cylinder: its direction), `b` the pinion (a cylindrical face or axis; a circular edge picked in the PM is its cylindrical face): the rack travels `pitch` along its direction per revolution of the pinion about its axis, both relative to each other (a pinion on a carriage rolling along a fixed rack turns too). `pitch` is *Rack travel/revolution*; `pitchBy: "diameter"` shows it as *Pinion pitch diameter* (pitch / π; the script option `diameter: d` sets both, and the PM fills it from the picked pinion's diameter). `flip: true` is *Reverse*; no alignment (as Gear); `phase` as Screw. Removes 1 DOF. *Rack Pinion: the pinion must be a cylindrical face or axis*; *Rack Pinion needs a linear edge, axis or cylindrical face (the rack) …* for a planar face.
- **Closest alignment.** The newer mates (and a Distance / Limit Distance with cylinders) default to `alignment: "closest"`: whichever sign the two directions have when the mate is solved, so nothing flips over; Aligned / Anti-Aligned can still be set.
- **Errors, never exceptions.** Wrong reference kinds give *Coincident needs two planar faces or planes* / *Concentric needs two cylindrical faces or axes* / *Distance needs two planar faces or planes*; bad values *Distance must be a number ≥ 0*, *Angle must be between 0° and 180°*, *Offset must be a number ≥ 0*; a mate the solver cannot satisfy (over-defined or conflicting, or between two fixed components or two refs on one component when not already satisfied) gets *Mate cannot be satisfied (over-defined or conflicting)*; a face point that lies on no face and a part that fails to build are errors on the mate or component. Suppressed mates are ignored.
- **Degrees of freedom.** Each component reports `dof` (0–6, 0 when fixed) and `underDefined`: a pin concentric with a hole has 2 (slide and spin), adding a coincident face leaves 1 (spin).
- **Solver.** Fixed components never move. Floating components are placed mate by mate, then refined by damped least squares over all mates; `dof` comes from the rank of each component's Jacobian.

### Script API

An assembly script sees `a` (and `asm`, the same Builder) and `Math`. `Assembly.isAssemblyScript(code)` recognises one (`a.insert`, `a.mate`, `a.coincident`, `a.concentric`, `a.distance`, `a.angle`, and the calls added with the newer mates, patterns and explode steps), and `anvil.run(code)` / *File ▸ Open* open such a script as an assembly.

| Method | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| `name(s)`, `units(u)` | Chainable. |
| `insert(source, opts) → id` | `opts`: `name`, `position` (alias `at`), `rotation` (9 numbers, row-major), `fixed`, `flexible` (sub-assemblies), `transparent` (Change Transparency). |
| `transparent(c, on = true)` | Change Transparency of a component (id or name; what `generateScript` writes for pattern instances). |
| `ignoreInterference(a, b, { coincident })` | Ignore an interference between two components (ids or names) or bodies (`"Part<1>/Body2"`); kept in `model.ignoredInterferences` as component-id keys (`"3\|7"`, `#c` for a coincidence). |
