| `mate(type, a, b, opts) → id` | `type` `"coincident"` \| `"concentric"` \| `"distance"` \| `"angle"` \| `"parallel"` \| `"perpendicular"` \| `"tangent"` \| `"width"` \| `"gear"` \| `"limitdistance"` \| `"limitangle"` \| `"symmetric"` \| `"slot"` \| `"hinge"` \| `"linearcoupler"` \| `"profilecenter"` \| `"screw"` \| `"rackpinion"`; `opts`: `alignment`, `name`, `suppressed`, `distance`, `angle` (degrees), `offset`, `flip`, `min`, `max`, `ratio`, `pitch`, `pitchBy`, `phase`, `a2`, `b2`. |
| `screw(a, b, pitch, opts)`, `rackPinion(rack, pinion, pitch, opts)` → id | rfKA. `screw`: pitch = distance per revolution, or `{ revolutions }` per unit; `rackPinion`: pitch = rack travel per revolution, or `{ diameter }` (pinion pitch diameter); `{ flip }` Reverse, `{ phase }`. |
| `parallel(a, b, opts)`, `perpendicular(a, b, opts)`, `tangent(a, b, opts)` → id | Shortcuts. |
| `width(widthFace1, widthFace2, tab1, tab2 \| null, opts)` → id | Width; `null` for a cylindrical tab `tab1`. |
| `gear(a, b, ratio, opts)`, `linearCoupler(a, b, ratio, opts)`, `limitDistance(a, b, min, max, opts)`, `limitAngle(a, b, min, max, opts)` → id | `linearCoupler`: ratio `[a, b]` or a number r (= [1, r]), `{ flip }` Reverse. Gear (`ratio` `[a, b]` or a number), limits. |
| `symmetric(plane, a, b, opts)`, `slot(slotFace, pin, opts)`, `hinge(a, b, a2, b2, opts)` → id | `a.symmetric(plane, a, b, opts)`: two faces / planes or two cylinders / axes mirrored about a plane or planar face (`a2`, may be on a third component); only the selected entities are mirrored, alignment Closest; two entities both on one side of the symmetry plane (where they stand in the model) are refused as SolidWorks does: *Symmetric: the entities to mate are both on the same side of the symmetry plane* (rfJA). `a.slot(slotFace, pin, { constraint: "free" \| "center" \| "distance" \| "percent", distance, percent, flip })`: `slotFace` is a side (`face`) or end arc (`cylinder`) of a straight slot, found from the part's two end cylinders and two flat sides; along-slot runs between the end arc centres, from the picked end (a side face: the lower end), `flip` from the other; Free stops at the ends; 3 DOF left (Free) or 2. **Arc slots** (rfIV; the sketch's `arcSlot`, SolidWorks' Arc Slot) are found from their two end cylinders and the inner and outer walls about the arc's centre axis: the pin rides on the centre-line arc, along-slot is the arc length from the picked end (a wall: the end the slot runs counter-clockwise from), Percent is of the sweep. `a.hinge(a, b, a2, b2, { limit: { a, b, min, max } })`: Concentric Selections `a` / `b`, Coincident Selections `a2` / `b2`; the angle limit is signed degrees between `limit.a` and `limit.b` (saved as `a3`, `b3`, `limit: true`, `min`, `max`) about the first axis; 1 DOF left. The PM for these three is at the end of `ui-assembly.js` (ui-commands.js' `cmdMate` has no room for their selections): `UI.cmdMate`, `UI.editMate` and `UI.mateSelect` are wrapped there and the two PMs swap as the type changes, keeping the picks. The tree icon is set on the row by `UI.gvRowIcon`, since ui-panels.js' row map does not know the three. |
| `linearPattern(seeds, opts)`, `circularPattern(seeds, opts)`, `mirrorComponents(seeds, opts)` → pattern id | Component patterns; `opts` as the pattern fields, plus `name` and `instanceNames` (what generateScript writes to keep the names). |
| `explode(components, direction, distance, { view, name })` → step id | An explode step; `direction` `"x"`, `"-y"`, … or `[x, y, z]`; `view` a name (made when missing) or the first view. |
| `exploded(view \| true \| false)` | Show a view exploded / collapse. |
| `coincident(a, b, opts)`, `concentric(a, b, opts)` → id | Shortcuts for `mate`. |
| `distance(a, b, d, opts)`, `angle(a, b, deg, opts)` → id | Distance / Angle mate with its value. |
| `fix(c)`, `float(c)` | Fix or float a component (id or name). |

```js
a.name("Pinned Plate").units("mm");
var plate = a.insert({ example: "Plate with bolt holes" }, { fixed: true });
var pin = a.insert({ script: 'p.name("Pin"); p.cylinder(4, 20);' }, { position: [40, 30, -20] });
a.concentric({ component: pin, cylinder: [4, 10, 0] }, { component: plate, cylinder: [14, 4, 10] });
a.coincident({ component: pin, face: [1, 0, 1] }, { component: plate, face: [50, 0, 30] }, { alignment: "aligned" });
// the pin now stands in the (10, 10) hole with its bottom flush: origin [10, 0, 10], axis +Y, 1 DOF (spin) left
```

```js
// Distance and angle mates (Design Library example): a block 15 above a base, a bar hinged on the base corner at 30°
a.distance({ component: block, face: [10, 0, 10] }, { component: base, face: [50, 10, 30] }, 15);
a.coincident({ component: block, face: [0, 10, 10] }, { component: base, face: [0, 5, 30] }, { alignment: "aligned", offset: 30 });
a.distance({ component: block, face: [10, 10, 20] }, { component: base, face: [50, 5, 60] }, 10, { alignment: "aligned", flip: true });
a.angle({ component: bar, face: [20, 2, 0] }, { component: base, face: [50, 5, 0] }, 30);
```

`Assembly.generateScript(model)` writes the same form back (numbers rounded to 1e-6, rotation only when not identity, alignment only when it differs from the default, `distance` / `angle` / `offset` / `flip` when set).

### Kernel API

| Call | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `Assembly.isAssembly(model)`, `Assembly.newAssembly(name?)` | Type test; a fresh assembly (`Assem1`). |
| `Assembly.partModel(source)` | A fresh model for a source (a part, or an assembly for a sub-assembly source); throws `Unknown Design Library part "X"` / `Cannot find the referenced document "X"`. |
| `Assembly.insert(model, source, opts?)`, `Assembly.addMate(model, type, a, b, opts?)` | Add a component / mate (mutates, returns it). |
| `Assembly.removeComponent(model, id)` (and its mates), `Assembly.removeMate(model, id)`, `Assembly.setFixed(model, id, fixed)` | Edit. |
| `Assembly.findComponent(model, idOrName)`, `Assembly.findMate(model, idOrName)` | The object or `null`. |
| `Assembly.rebuild(model, cache?, opts?)` | `opts.explodeT` (0..1, the explode animation), `opts.drag = { id, point, target }` (free drag: the grabbed local point pulled toward the world target as far as the free degrees of freedom allow, moving the components mated to it — a linkage follows its crank), `opts.quick` (a drag frame: solved to a micron, the release exactly), `opts.poseOnly` (placements only, for drag frames). Result: `{ assembly: true, body, bodies, bodyFeatures, bodyNames, components: [{ id, name, index, fixed, dof, underDefined, error }], mates: { id: { ok, residual, error } }, errors, warnings, partBuilds, cache, … }`. `body` is every component in world space with `polygon.body` = component index (so `Kernel.meshFromBody(build.body)` faces carry `face.body`); volumes add, components are never merged. Pass `build.cache` back to reuse the part builds. |
| `Assembly.solve(model, partBuilds)`, `Assembly.resolveRef(model, partBuilds, ref, transforms?)` | Solver; a ref as `{ kind: "plane", point, normal }` or `{ kind: "axis", point, dir }` in world coordinates (throws a readable Error). |
| `Assembly.refFromPick(model, build, faceIndex, worldPoint, mesh)` | The Ref for a viewport pick: `{ component, cylinder }` on a cylindrical face, else `{ component, face }`, in local coordinates rounded to 1e-6; `null` when nothing applies. |
| `Assembly.moveComponent(model, id, delta)`, `Assembly.rotateComponent(model, id, axis, deg, pivot?)` | Move / Rotate Component: change a floating component's stored transform (translate by a world delta; turn about `"x"`\|`"y"`\|`"z"` or a vector through `pivot`, default the component origin). Throw *X is fixed: Float it to move it*. The next rebuild solves the mates again, so the component keeps what its free DOF allow. |
| `Assembly.componentCenter(model, build, id)` | World centre of the component's bounding box (the pivot the UI rotates about). |
| `Assembly.interference(model, build?, { minVolume }?)` | Interference Detection: `{ pairs: [{ a, b, aName, bName, aIndex, bIndex, volume, body }], volume, checked, tolerance }`. Every pair whose world boxes overlap is intersected with `CSG.intersect`; pairs whose common volume exceeds the tolerance (1e-9 · bbox diagonal³) are listed, largest first, with the common solid as `body`. Touching faces are not interferences; components whose part failed are skipped. |
| `Assembly.collision(model, build, moving, { against, skip, minVolume }?)` | rfIV, Collision Detection: the components `moving` (ids) against the others at the transforms the model holds now (no world bodies rebuilt; `build.partBuilds` gives the bodies): `{ hit, pairs: [{ a, b, aName, bName, aIndex, bIndex, volume, points }], checked }`; `against` = *These components* (pairs inside the set only); touching faces are no collision. |
| `Assembly.collisionStep(model, cache, prev, { against, draggedOnly, dragged, stop }?)` | rfJA, a Rotate Component frame: the model as it stands (solved), the components that moved from `prev` ({ id: transform }, the last pose without a collision) checked against the others; on a collision each moved component is turned back toward its `prev` placement (rotation about its centre interpolated, the mates solved again at each try, 12 bisections). Returns the pose build with `collision`. |
| `Assembly.collisionDrag(model, cache, drag, { against, draggedOnly, stop, quick, from }?)` | *Stop at collision*: `rebuild(model, cache, { drag, poseOnly, quick })`, but when the parts the drag moves would collide the target is pulled back toward the grabbed point's place before the step (samples at half the smallest part size, so a fast drag cannot pass through a thin part, then 12 bisections: contact to 1/4096 of the step). Returns the pose build with `collision: { hit, t, pairs, stopped }`; pairs already overlapping before the step do not stop it; `stop: false` only reports. |
| `Assembly.setMateValues(mate, { distance, angle, offset, min, max, ratio, flip })`, `Assembly.MATE_TYPES` | Set a mate's values, dropping keys its type does not use. |
| `Assembly.setTransparent(model, id, on)` | Change Transparency, kept on the component (`component.transparent`): saved, undone with the model, written by the script; pattern instances that survive a regeneration keep it, new ones are opaque. |
| `Assembly.interferenceKey(a, b, coincident?)`, `setInterferenceIgnored(model, key, on)`, `migrateIgnored(model)` | Ignored interferences (`model.ignoredInterferences`); keys by component id, older name keys are migrated on rebuild, a deleted component's keys go with it. |
| `Assembly.massProps(model, build)` | `{ volume, area, mass, centroid, bbox, components: [{ id, name, index, volume, mass, density, centroid }] }`: mass = Σ volume · density with each part's own density, centroid mass-weighted. |
| `Assembly.runScript(model, code)`, `Assembly.generateScript(model)`, `Assembly.isAssemblyScript(code)` | Script round trip. |
| `Assembly.transformPoint(T, p)`, `inverseTransformPoint(T, p)`, `transformDir(T, d)`, `IDENTITY` | Transform helpers. |
| `Assembly.MATE_GROUPS`, `Assembly.MATE_NAMES` | `{ standard, advanced, mechanical }` type lists as grouped in the Mate PM; the base names (`LimitDistance`, …). |
| `Assembly.addPattern(model, 'linear' \| 'circular' \| 'mirror', seeds, opts)`, `removePattern`, `findPattern`, `setPatternValues(model, p, opts)`, `syncPatterns(model)` | Component patterns (below). `syncPatterns` makes the instance components match the patterns; rebuild calls it. |
| `Assembly.addExplodedView(model)`, `addExplodeStep(model, view, comps, direction, distance, opts?)`, `removeExplodeStep`, `removeExplodedView`, `findExplodedView`, `findExplodeStep`, `setExploded(model, view \| true \| false)` | Exploded views (below). |
| `Assembly.explodeOffsets(model, view, t?, centers?)`, `Assembly.explodedBodies(model, build, view?, t?)` | `{ offsets: { id: [dx, dy, dz] }, lines }` for a view part-way (`t` 0..1 runs the steps in turn); the world bodies of a view exploded, without re-solving — **the hook for drawings** (a "Show in exploded state" view option in `drawing.js` can draw these instead of the collapsed bodies). |

### Component patterns and Mirror Components

`model.patterns = [{ id, name, type, seeds: [component ids], … }]`. A pattern makes **instance components**: ordinary entries of `model.components` with `{ pattern, seed, instance }` whose transforms are recomputed from the seed's solved transform on every rebuild, so moving (or re-mating) the seed moves the instances. The solver never moves an instance; mates may reference one (the assembly is solved again once the instances are placed). Instances are named like inserted components (`Pin<2>`, `Pin<3>`, …), listed in the BOM and in Mass Properties, and refuse Move / Rotate (*Pin<3> is a pattern instance: move its seed*). Deleting a seed deletes its instances (and the pattern when no seed is left); lowering a count deletes the extra instances and the mates on them.

| Type | Fields | Name |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `linear` | `direction` Ref (a face or plane: its normal; a cylinder or axis: its direction), `spacing`, `count` (instances, seed included), `reverse`; optional `direction2`, `spacing2`, `count2`, `reverse2` (a grid) | `LocalLPattern1` |
| `circular` | `axis` Ref (a cylinder or axis), `angle`, `count`, `equal` (`angle` is then the total: 360 → 360/count apart), `reverse` | `LocalCirPattern1` |
| `mirror` | `plane` Ref (a face or plane), `oppositeHand` | `MirrorComponent1` |

Mirror Components places a **copy** by default: the seed's rotation with its own axis nearest the plane normal flipped, positioned so the bounding-box centre is reflected (a part symmetric about that local plane lands exactly on its mirror image, the rotation stays proper). `oppositeHand: true` makes the reflected (left-hand) version: the transform has det −1 and the body's polygons are turned round, so volumes and normals stay right. Errors are keyed by the pattern id (*Pattern axis must be a cylindrical face or an axis*, *Number of instances must be a whole number ≥ 1*, …); `build.patterns[id]` is `{ ok, error, instances }`.

### Exploded View

`model.explodedViews = [{ id, name: "ExplView1", steps: [{ id, name: "Explode Step1", components: [ids], direction: [x, y, z] (unit, world), distance }] }]` and `model.exploded` = the id of the view shown exploded (absent: collapsed). A component moves by the sum of its steps' `direction · distance`, in step order. The explode is display state: component transforms and mates stay collapsed; `rebuild(model, cache, { explodeT })` adds the offsets to the world bodies (`explodeT` 0..1 for the animation, default 1) and returns `build.explode = { view, name, t, offsets, lines }`, where the **explode lines** are one segment per step and moved component through its centre (drawn dashed orange in the viewport). `refFromPick` takes the offset off, so mates picked in the exploded view still land on the right local point. Drawings are made from the current mesh, so a drawing made while exploded shows the exploded assembly; `Assembly.explodedBodies` is the hook for a per-view option.

### Sub-assemblies and live links

A component whose source is an assembly is a **sub-assembly**: it is built by `Assembly.rebuild` in turn (its own mates solved, collapsed) and placed as one body. Its part build is that assembly build (`build.partBuilds[id].model` is the sub-assembly model with its solved inner transforms, `.partBuilds` its parts), so everything that walks components can go down a level. Every polygon of an assembly body carries the part it came from (`polygon.part`, numbered through every level, each body of a multibody part counted as one; CSG clone / transform / split and pack / unpack keep it) and `Kernel.meshFromBody` never groups polygons of different parts into one face (`face.part`), so exactly coplanar faces of two parts in a sub-assembly stay two faces on either kernel (OpenCascade face ids are offset per inner component as well).

- **References inside a sub-assembly.** A ref may carry `path`: `{ component: sub, path: [inner id or name, …], face | cylinder | plane | axis }` names geometry of the component `path` leads to, in that component's own coordinates (`path: ["Arm<1>"]`, or two levels down `[2, "Pin<1>"]`). `resolveRef` carries it out through the inner transforms; `refFromPick` on a sub-assembly's face returns the path ref of the part under the pick (nearest face, preferring the picked normal). A ref without `path` on a sub-assembly is in the sub-assembly's own frame.
- **Rigid / Flexible.** A sub-assembly is rigid (moves as one body, SolidWorks' default). `flexible: true` (`a.insert(src, { flexible: true })`, `Assembly.setFlexible(model, id, on)`, *Solve as Flexible* in the component menu) solves its own mates in the assembly: its fixed components become one rigid base, each floating component takes part in the solve (virtual id `'S/k'`), and parent mates to parts inside it by `path` drive them. The in-context inner transforms are stored on the component as `flexState: { "<inner id>": { position, rotation } }` (relative to the sub-assembly), so the sub-assembly's own document never changes; *Solve as Rigid* drops them and the parts go back to the document's layout. Pattern instances inside a flexible sub-assembly stay with the base; a flexible sub-assembly's own sub-assemblies move rigidly; a pattern instance of a sub-assembly is always rigid.
- **Live links.** `{ file: name }` reads the document `name` from `Assembly.Documents` on every rebuild; the cached build is reused only while the document (and every document it links to in turn) is unchanged, so editing a referenced part updates every assembly that uses it on its next rebuild. A component is named after the file (`Pin<1>`). The store has a backing (in memory by default; the app backs it with the documents saved in the browser, *File ▸ Save*, `localStorage` `anvil.files`) and a layer of edited, unsaved (dirty) documents that already show in assemblies. A document that contains itself is an error (*Circular reference: X contains itself*), not a loop.
- **Broken references.** A `{ file }` the store does not have is **suppressed with a warning**, not an error: `build.warnings[id]` = *Cannot find the referenced document "X"*, the component status has `suppressed: true, broken: true`, no body, 0 DOF; mates on it are left out of the solve (`build.mates[id]` `{ ok: true, suppressed: true, warning }`); BOM and mass properties leave it out. It comes back as soon as the document exists.
- **Across levels.** `Assembly.massProps` adds the sub-assemblies' own mass properties (mass-weighted centroid through every level; a sub-assembly row carries its `components` with world centroids). `Assembly.interference` checks every part at every level by default (pairs inside one sub-assembly too, named `Hinge<2>/Arm<1>`, with `aPath` / `bPath`); `{ subAssembliesAsComponents: true }` checks each sub-assembly as one body (the union of its parts). `Assembly.bom(model, build?, { type })` lists `'top'` (Top-level only: a sub-assembly is one row), `'indented'` (each sub-assembly followed by its own rows, items `2.1`, `3.2.1`, qty per parent) or `'parts'` (Parts only: quantities multiplied through the levels); rows `{ item, level, name, kind, key, qty, components, file? }`, identical sources counted together. Drawing BOMs of assemblies use it for *Indented* and *Parts only* (`Drawing.bomRows(model, mesh, { type: 'indented' | 'parts' }, { build })`, `Drawing.BOM_TYPES`). Interference volumes and Clearance distances between analytic bodies are exact (`Evaluate.exactVolume`, `Evaluate.exactClearance`: parallel cylinders, a cylinder inside a hole, a cylinder and a plane parallel to its axis).
- **Pack and Go.** `Assembly.packAndGo(model)` → `{ model, embedded, missing }`: a copy with every `{ file }` replaced by `{ model }` (linked sub-assemblies packed in turn), so it builds with an empty store; missing documents stay links and are listed.
- **Exchange.** STEP export writes each sub-assembly as its own product with NEXT_ASSEMBLY_USAGE_OCCURRENCEs under it (a flexible instance as its own product, *Hinge (Hinge<2>, flexible)*); 3MF writes it as a components object; OBJ writes every part flat. `Exchange.docParts` keeps `instances` flat (every part in world placement) and adds `top` / `subs` for the nesting.

| Call | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `Assembly.Documents.use(backing \| null)` | Back the store with `{ get(name) → model \| null, put(name, model), remove(name), list() }` (null: a fresh in-memory store). |
| `Assembly.Documents.get / has / put(name, model, { dirty }) / save(name) / revert(name) / remove / list / isDirty / dirty()` | Read (a copy), write (saved, or dirty: in memory only), save or drop a dirty edit. |
| `Assembly.references(model)`, `Assembly.saveReferences(model)` | The document names a model links to (through linked sub-assemblies); save the dirty ones (File ▸ Save of an assembly). |
| `Assembly.resolveSource(source)` | `{ kind: 'part' \| 'assembly', model, file? }` (throws; a missing document with `broken: true`). `Assembly.partModel(source)` is its `model`. |
| `Assembly.setFlexible(model, id, on)`, `Assembly.isSubAssemblySource(source)` | Solve as Flexible / Rigid (throws for a part). |
| `Assembly.bom(model, build?, { type })`, `Assembly.leafBodies(model, build, asComponents?)`, `Assembly.packAndGo(model)` | As above. |

```js
// Hinged cover (Design Library): a base and two Hinge sub-assemblies mated by faces of their leaves; Hinge<2> is Flexible
var h2 = a.insert({ example: "Hinge sub-assembly" }, { position: [60, 30, 20], flexible: true });
a.coincident({ component: h2, path: ["Leaf<1>"], face: [-20, -5, 10] }, { component: base, face: [60, 10, 30] });
a.angle({ component: base, face: [60, 10, 30] }, { component: h2, path: ["Arm<1>"], face: [20, 5, 10] }, 30);   // opens its arm
var pin = a.insert({ file: "Pin" }, { position: [0, 20, 0] });   // a live link to the saved document "Pin"
```

### UI

- **File ▸ New** → *New assembly* (or `anvil.newAssembly()`; *New part* / `anvil.newPart()` goes back) opens an empty assembly; the ribbon shows the *Assembly* tab in SolidWorks' order (*Insert Components*, *Mate*, *Linear Component Pattern*, *Move* / *Rotate Component*, *Hide/Show Components*, *Exploded View*, *Bill of Materials*; *Fix* / *Float* last) and *Evaluate*; part-only commands refuse with a message. Sketches are not available in an assembly document.
- **Insert Components** PropertyManager (`UI.cmdInsertComponent()`): pick a Design Library part, the last open part, or *Browse…* for a part JSON file, and the X/Y/Z position of its origin. The first component is fixed; later ones float until mated.
- **Mate** PropertyManager (`UI.cmdMate(existing?)`): *Mate Selections* takes two viewport picks (a cylindrical face gives an axis, a flat face a plane, an origin plane its plane: the planes are not drawn over the model while the PM is open, they are picked in the flyout FeatureManager at the top left of the graphics area, whose rows show their plane on hover; editing a mate lights its faces); *Standard Mates* defaults to *Concentric* for two cylinders and *Coincident* otherwise, and offers *Distance* and *Angle* with their value box (starting at the current distance / angle between the selections), *Offset* for Coincident, and *Flip dimension*; *Mate alignment* Aligned / Anti-Aligned. The graphics area previews the solved result (the component glides to its mated place), ✓ commits it, Cancel restores the model; double-clicking a mate row reopens it. As in SolidWorks the PropertyManager stays open after ✓, empty, for the next mate. The picks sit in one *Entities to Mate* list (two for Width; hover shows the face, Delete / right-click removes one, *Clear Selections*), and once both are made the Mate pop-up toolbar appears by the pointer with the applicable types, *Flip Mate Alignment* (not for a cylindrical Width tab), ✓ and ✗. Clicking a mate in the FeatureManager selects its two faces; Delete deletes it; the mate's menu has *Flip Mate Alignment*. Tangent is proposed for a cylinder and a plane.
- **Move Component / Rotate Component** (Assembly tab, `UI.cmdMoveComponent()` / `UI.cmdRotateComponent()`): with no command open, or with this PropertyManager open, pressing on a floating component and dragging moves it in the screen plane (Rotate: turns it about its centre, 0.5° per pixel); a right-drag on a floating component rotates it. The drag **follows the mates** as SolidWorks' free drag does: a hinged arm swings, a linkage or a gear train turns, a floating part inside a Flexible sub-assembly drags in context. Each frame solves the placements only and moves the drawn components (`UI.asmPose`; Gear train 55 → 8–11 ms per frame, a 30-pin plate 292 → 6–7 ms); the release rebuilds once, reusing each part's edges, seams and smooth normals, and is one undo step. Dragging a fixed or fully held component rotates the view as usual and says why (*Base<1> is fixed: Float it to drag it*), with no undo step. *By Delta XYZ* moves / rotates the selected component by typed amounts. `UI.componentDragHook` is what `ui-sketch.js` asks on a press. **Collision Detection** (rfIV, SolidWorks' *Options* group): *Standard Drag* / *Collision Detection*, *Check between* *All components* / *These components* (*Add Selected* adds the selected component to the list), *Stop at collision* (on), *Dragged part only*; *Advanced Options* ▸ *Highlight faces* (on), *Sound* (off; a short beep). While a Move drag runs with it, every frame and the release solve through `Assembly.collisionDrag`, so the part stops at first contact; the status bar names the pair and, after the drop, the touching faces are highlighted (`App.command.faceList`). The settings are kept between opens (`UI.asmCollision.options`; `UI.asmCollision.last` the last collision). Rotate Component drags (and the right-drag rotate) are checked too (rfJA, `Assembly.collisionStep`): the part stops turning at first contact. The groups are added to ui-commands.js' PM by a wrapper at the end of `ui-assembly.js`.
- **SmartMates.** Alt + drag a face of a floating component onto a face of another component: while the cursor is over a face that can take a mate (plane on plane: Coincident, cylinder in cylinder: Concentric, cylinder on plane: Tangent) the component is shown mated there; dropping adds the mate (one undo step), dropping anywhere else changes nothing. At the drop the **Mate pop-up toolbar** offers the other types for the pair (Coincident / Parallel / Perpendicular for two planes, Concentric / Tangent for two cylinders), *Flip Mate Alignment*, ✓ (Enter, or a click elsewhere) and ✗ (Esc: the mate is taken back).
- **Component context toolbar.** A click on a component in the graphics area (not the end of a drag) or on its FeatureManager row shows a small toolbar by the pointer: *Mate* (the Mate PM opens with the clicked face as its first selection), *Hide Components*, *Change Transparency*, *Isolate*, *Fix* / *Float*, *Edit Part*. It fades as the pointer moves away and goes when it is far. **Tab** hides the component under the pointer, **Shift+Tab** shows the hidden one there.
- **Change Transparency** draws a component see-through with faint edges (clicks go through it to what is behind) and is kept on the component (`component.transparent`): one undo step without a rebuild, saved, written by the script, kept through Hide / Show. **Isolate** hides every other component until *Exit Isolate* (a bar at the bottom of the graphics area) puts the earlier hidden state back; Esc leaves Isolate too. Both are also in the component menu.
- **Insert Components**: the chosen part follows the pointer (its origin on the face under it, X / Y / Z following) and a click drops it there.
- Over-defined components show `(+)` in the FeatureManager (`anvil.components()[i].overDefined`); 'closest' alignment never locks the start orientation and the solver retries half turns before it rejects a mate (every example solves from 40 of 40 random starts); components that share no mates are solved separately, and floating clusters of six or more are solved with the hub held.
- **Mate** PropertyManager groups, as in SolidWorks: *Standard Mates* (the *Mate type* select: Coincident, Parallel, Perpendicular, Tangent, Concentric, Distance, Angle), *Advanced Mates* (Width, Limit Distance with Maximum / Minimum, Limit Angle) and *Mechanical Mates* (Gear with Ratio A / Ratio B and Reverse); choosing a type in one group shows "—" in the others. Width shows *Width selections* and *Tab selections* and takes four picks in that order (three when the tab is a cylindrical face); limits start at the measured value. Tree rows get their own icons.
- **Linear Component Pattern** (Assembly tab), with **Circular Component Pattern** and **Mirror Components** stacked beside it (`UI.cmdLinearComponentPattern()`, `UI.cmdCircularComponentPattern()`, `UI.cmdMirrorComponents()`, edit: `UI.cmdComponentPattern(type, pattern)`): the selected component is the seed; *Selecting* chooses whether picks add components or set the direction / axis / mirror plane (after the direction is set picks go to the components); Spacing / Instances (Direction 2 optional), Angle / Instances / Equal spacing, *Create opposite hand version*. The graphics area previews the instances; ✓ is one undo step. The FeatureManager shows a `LocalLPattern1` / `LocalCirPattern1` / `MirrorComponent1` folder after the Mates with the instance rows inside (double-click edits, context menu Edit Feature / Delete). Dragging an instance is refused with a message.
- **Exploded View** (Assembly tab, `UI.cmdExplodedView(step?)`): pick components, choose X / Y / Z or *Along selected face* (then pick a face: its normal), *Reverse direction*, type the distance — or drag one of the step's components in the graphics area and the distance follows the mouse along the direction. *Done* adds the step and starts the next; ✓ keeps them in `ExplView1` and leaves the assembly exploded. The **ConfigurationManager** of an assembly shows *Default [ Assem1 ]* ▸ *ExplView1* ▸ *Explode Step1*…, with *New Exploded View…*, *Explode* / *Collapse* and *Animate explode* / *Animate collapse* (the steps play one after another); double-click the view to explode / collapse, a step to edit it; context menus Edit / Delete. Explode lines are drawn dashed while exploded.
- **Interference Detection** (Evaluate tab, `UI.cmdInterference()`): lists *Interference1 — 2000 mm³ / Base<1> · Block<1>*, …, largest first, or *No Interferences*; highlights the interfering components and draws the common volumes in red on top; clicking a result shows that pair only (again for all); *Calculate* runs it again. *Options* as in SolidWorks: *Treat coincidence as interference* (touching components listed with 0 volume), *Show ignored interferences* (a result can be ignored from its row; ignored ones drop out of the count and show greyed when this is on; saved in the model, undoable), *Include multibody part interferences* (the bodies of one part against each other) and *Ignore hidden bodies/components* (on by default).
- **FeatureManager.** One row per component, `(f) Plate<1>` when fixed, `(-) Pin<1>` while under-defined and the bare name once fully defined, then a *Mates* folder with a row per mate, each with its mate-type icon. Every row keeps its own icon (part, sub-assembly with a blue mark when Flexible, hollow when hidden, grey when suppressed); a failed mate gets a red ✗ badge and red name, and so do the Mates folder, the components it over-defines (`(+)`) and the assembly's top row; a component with a warning (broken link, a Flexible mate that failed, a part or sub-assembly with rebuild errors) gets an amber ⚠. Right-click ▸ *What's Wrong?* lists them, with *Go To* and *Edit Mate* (a mate or an over-defined component), *Edit* (a pattern) or *Edit Part*. Clicking a component row selects its body; hovering the row lights the component in the graphics area, and the row of the component under the pointer (or owning a selected face) is lit in the tree; hover reads *Component: Pin<1>*.
- **Mass Properties** lists each component with its own volume, density and mass, and the mass-weighted totals.
- Every edit is one undo step; saving and *File ▸ Open* round-trip the assembly JSON and script.
- **Sub-assemblies in the FeatureManager.** A sub-assembly row (assembly icon; tooltip *sub-assembly, solved as Rigid / Flexible*) expands into its own components (`(f)` / `(-)` as in its document, every level down) and its *Mates* folder; a linked component's name carries ↗ and its tooltip *linked to "Pin"* (*edited, not saved* while a change is only in memory); a broken reference is shown grey, *Pin<1> (suppressed)*, its icon with an amber ⚠ badge and the message; its mates are greyed.
- **Insert Components** also lists the Design Library assemblies (*Hinge sub-assembly (sub-assembly)*) and every document saved in this browser as a link (*Pin (linked, saved in this browser)*); *Browse…* accepts assembly JSON too. The Mate PM names a pick inside a sub-assembly *Face@Arm<1>@Hinge<1>*.
- **Component menu:** *Open Part* / *Open Assembly* (a linked document), *Edit Part* (the component's part, linked or embedded, opened in the context of the assembly), *Solve as Flexible* / *Solve as Rigid*, *Make Linked Document…* (save an embedded part or sub-assembly in the browser and link every component with that source to it). Opening puts the assembly on a document stack; a banner *Editing "Pin" in the context of Assem1* has **Return to Assem1**: a changed linked document stays in memory (dirty) and already shows in the assembly, a changed embedded part replaces the embedded copy (one undo step); the assembly keeps its undo history. **File ▸ Save** of an assembly also saves the edited documents it links to; saving the part itself drops the edited copy.
- **File ▸ Pack and Go…** downloads the assembly with every linked document embedded (lists what it embeds and what is missing). The *Bill of Materials* button (Assembly tab) opens a BOM with *BOM Type* Top-level only / Indented / Parts only and a *Description* column: the part's *Description* property (links evaluated), else the model's `description`, else its Design Library name (`Assembly.bom` rows carry `description`).

### Automation

`anvil.newAssembly(name?)`, `anvil.newPart(name?)`, `anvil.isAssembly()`; `anvil.insert(source, opts)` and `anvil.mate(type, a, b, opts)` return a Promise of the summary; `anvil.components()` → `[{ id, name, index, fixed, dof, underDefined, position, rotation, volume, error }]`, `anvil.mates()` → `[{ id, name, type, ok, error, suppressed }]`; `anvil.fix(idOrName, fixed)`, `anvil.removeComponent(id)`, `anvil.removeMate(id)`; `anvil.moveComponent(idOrName, delta)` and `anvil.rotateComponent(idOrName, axis, deg)` (about the component's centre) return a Promise of the summary; `anvil.interference()` → `{ count, volume, pairs: [{ a, b, aId, bId, volume }] }`; `anvil.mates()` rows carry `distance` / `angle` / `offset` / `min` / `max` / `ratio` / `flip` when set; `anvil.pattern(type, seeds, opts)`, `anvil.patterns()` → `[{ id, name, type, seeds, instances, error }]`, `anvil.removePattern(id)`; `anvil.explode(components, direction, distance, opts)` (one step, left exploded), `anvil.exploded()` → `{ view, offsets, lines }` or `null`, `anvil.exploded(true | view | false)`, `anvil.animateExplode(explode, ms)`, `anvil.explodedViews()`; `anvil.components()` rows also carry `overDefined`; `anvil.transparent(idOrName, on)` (Change Transparency, one undo step); `anvil.documents()` (`[{ name, type, dirty }]`), `anvil.saveDocument(name, model?)` (default: the open document), `anvil.removeDocument(name)`, `anvil.flexible(id, on)`, `anvil.openPart(componentOrName)`, `anvil.editPart(component)`, `anvil.returnToAssembly()`, `anvil.docStack()`, `anvil.bom('top' | 'indented' | 'parts')` (rows `{ item, level, name, description, kind, qty, file }`), `anvil.packAndGo()`, `anvil.saveReferences()`; `anvil.mass()` is `Assembly.massProps` in an assembly; `anvil.script()`, `anvil.run(code)`, `anvil.model()` and `anvil.load(json)` work on assemblies; `anvil.summary().type` is `"part"` or `"assembly"`, with `components` and `mates` counts. `UI.isAssembly()`, `UI.selectComponent(id)`, `UI.componentIndex(id)` and `UI.bodyName(i)` (the component name) drive the same paths. The *Pinned plate assembly* example in the Design Library is a fixed plate with two pins mated into its holes and a cap seated on it; *Distance and angle mates* holds a block 15 above a base (Distance, offset Coincident, flipped Distance: block at [30, 25, 50]) and hinges a bar on the base corner at 30° (Ry(−30°)), every component fully defined; *Interference check* sinks a block 5 into a base and a pin 8 into it (interferences 2000 and 8·A(Ø8) mm³) with a second pin only touching; *Gear train and patterns* couples two gears on shafts by a Gear mate (12 : 24, Gear A inserted at 45° turns Gear B −22.5°), patterns three dot pins round Gear B (they turn with it), a row of four pins (Linear Component Pattern) and a mirrored pin, centres a slider in a slot (Width) with Limit Distance 10..60, lays a roller on the plate (Tangent + Perpendicular) and has a three-step exploded view; *Hinge sub-assembly* is a fixed leaf with an arm hinged on its top edge (1 DOF left: it swings), and *Hinged cover (sub-assemblies)* places two of them on a base by faces of their leaves (path refs) — Hinge<1> rigid with its arm standing, Hinge<2> Flexible with an Angle mate that opens its arm to 30° (88000 mm³; BOM Indented *Base, Hinge ×2 ▸ Leaf, Arm*, Parts only *Base 1, Leaf 2, Arm 2*).

## Background rebuilds

By default (when the page is served over HTTP) the rebuild runs in a Web Worker: `worker.js` loads `csg.js` and `kernel.js`, receives `{ type: 'rebuild', seq, model }`, keeps its own rebuild cache between messages and answers `{ type: 'built', seq, build, edges, curves, normals }` with `Kernel.packBuild(build)` (typed arrays, transferred rather than copied) plus the mesh's `Kernel.bodyEdges`, curve info and smooth normals, then `{ type: 'volume', seq, volume }` — or `{ type: 'error', seq, message }`. The main thread unpacks the result with `Kernel.unpackBuild`, puts the sketch profiles back with `Kernel.applyProfiles`, and only then swaps the mesh, tree and status. While a build has been in flight for more than 250 ms the status bar shows *Rebuilding…*; rapid edits are coalesced, so only the latest model is built. PropertyManager previews go to the same worker as `{ type: 'preview', seq, model, upto, op, feature }` (`op` `'tool' | 'shell' | 'edge' | 'flange' | 'jog'`: the feature being edited built on the model without joining the part) and come back as `{ type: 'previewed', seq, tools, info, ms }` (with `base: n` the reply also carries `base`, the packed part of the first n features with its edges, for Edit Feature's rollback; `op: 'base'` builds only that); a newer request supersedes an older one, and previews of a truncated model keep a cache of their own. *Edit ▸ Rebuild in background (Web Worker)* toggles the mode (`App.workerOn`, persisted in `localStorage` under `anvil.worker`); with it off every rebuild is synchronous, as before, which is also the fallback when workers are unavailable (`file://`).

For code that drives the app, `UI.rebuild(opts)` and `UI.pmOK()` return Promises, `UI.whenIdle()` resolves once no rebuild is queued or in flight, and `anvil.run` / `append` / `load` / `rebuild` return Promises resolving to the summary they used to return directly (`anvil.busy()` says whether a build is in flight, `anvil.workerOn(bool)` reads or sets the mode). In synchronous mode the work is done before the Promise is returned, so `anvil.summary()` is already up to date when the call returns.

## OpenCascade kernel

Anvil's own kernel is a BSP mesh CSG with exact records for circles, cylinders, cones, spheres and tori. With *Tools ▸ OpenCascade kernel* on — the default whenever `vendor/occt/opencascade.full.wasm` is installed (`powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File vendor\get-occt.ps1`) and no choice was stored — every rebuild is also built by OpenCascade 7.6 (opencascade.js, WebAssembly) in `occt-worker.js`:

- **The feature tree is read exactly as Anvil reads it** (`OcctKernel.build`): the resolved model (equations, configurations), sketch profiles with their exact arcs, planes, extrude ranges, revolve axes, cut scopes, merge / bodies options. Supported: sketches and planes; extrude / cut (all end conditions, draft, two directions) and revolve; linear / circular patterns and mirror; fillet and chamfer (edges picked by point, tangent propagation as SolidWorks); shell (faces removed, or a closed hollow); loft (smooth) and sweep (exact line / arc paths, 3D polylines); Hole Wizard holes; sheet metal (base flange, edge flange with every length reference and hem of every type with mitres and reliefs, jog, normal cut — via `Kernel.flangeLayout`; Sketched Bend via `Kernel.sketchedBendLayout`; bends exact; the flat pattern groups OpenCascade's coplanar B-rep faces per body as Anvil's mesh does); the Imported feature when it kept its STEP / IGES file. Every body is checked with `BRepCheck_Analyzer`; faces split by booleans are merged back.
- **Its solid drives the part** when every feature was built, the solid is valid and its volume agrees with Anvil's (relative 1e-4; 2e-2 when Anvil only approximates: sweeps, lofts, flange bends, mesh imports, unchained edge picks) — or when Anvil's kernel failed features OpenCascade built (a fillet on the saddle edge of a pipe tee). `OcctKernel.toBuild` turns it into an Anvil build: every polygon tagged with its B-rep face (`polygon.face`), its face's surface record and its feature, exact mass properties on the body (`body.exact`, used by `Kernel.massProps`). The viewport, picking (whole B-rep faces; coplanar faces stay apart), edges, drawings, flat patterns, exports and evaluate tools then work on OpenCascade's geometry. Otherwise Anvil's result stays and the status bar says why: *OpenCascade ✓ 12 faces*, *(+1 Anvil could not)*, *needs a mesh import*, *volume differs*, *invalid solid*, *not installed*.
- **After an edit** (*Tools ▸ OpenCascade kernel ▸ …*, or *Options* in the Task Pane; remembered per browser in `anvil.occtSwap`): *Show the quick result first* (the default) shows Anvil's own result at once (15–20 ms after an edit of the Mounting bracket) and cross-fades OpenCascade's exact solid in over it when it lands (a copy of the frame on screen fades out over 180 ms above the new one; camera, selection and pre-selection do not move; a click, wheel turn or key ends the fade at once). *Wait for the exact solid* keeps the previous exact solid on screen until the new one is ready (up to 1.5 s, estimated from per-feature times, with a *Rebuilding…* pill; ~215 ms on the Mounting bracket): no faceted frame ever shows, but edits feel slower. Quick is the default because the change shows about ten times sooner and the facets → exact change is a soft fade, not a flash. Chip colours: green, OpenCascade's solid is shown; blue with a pulsing dot, rebuilding; amber, Anvil's result is shown because OpenCascade cannot build the part yet, or the engine is not installed (click to pick it); red, it failed or disagrees. `UI.occt.swapMode()` → `'quick' | 'wait'`; `UI.occt.swapMode(mode, { remember: false }?)`.
- **STEP and IGES** come straight from the B-rep while it drives (*File ▸ Save As* STEP AP214 with exact B-spline faces, IGES 5.3, `anvil.exportIges()`); STEP / IGES files are read by OpenCascade (any surfaces) for IGES, while the kernel is on, or when Anvil's own reader refuses a file, and the Imported feature keeps the file so OpenCascade rebuilds it exactly.
- **Assemblies**: every distinct part (sub-assemblies' too) is built by OpenCascade and checked against Anvil's build of it; the assembly is then rebuilt around OpenCascade's parts (`Assembly.setPartBuilder`), mates, patterns, interference and BOMs as ever.
- **A round or chamfer that uses up a face** (R50 on the top edge of a block 50 deep: the round ends on the face's far edge and the face is gone, as SolidWorks builds it): OpenCascade's own fillet refuses that, so the faces beyond the far edge are pushed out along the vanishing face, the round is made on the widened body and the added material cut away again — exact radius, no sliver face (straight edges between planar faces, convex or concave; `kernel/rfHU.js`, occt-tests "rfHU").
- **Sketches with many loops** (a perforated panel) are joined and cut in one Boolean per nesting level, not one per loop: the Cut-Extrude of 600 Ø.25 holes went from 120 s to 4.7 s on the desktop engine (200 holes in the browser engine: 53 s to 3.8 s). The display mesh of the perforated faces (`BRepMesh`, ~17 s for 600 holes on the desktop engine), the final validity check and the cut itself grow faster than the hole count and are now the slow part (1800 holes: cut 22 s, check 27 s, mesh 96 s).
- **Rebuild cache**: the worker keeps a snapshot after each feature (per part in assemblies); editing the last feature of the Tray rebuilds in ~50 ms instead of ~1 s.
- **Off the main thread**: the worker also turns its solid into Anvil bodies, edges, curve info and smooth normals (`OcctKernel.toBodies` there, `OcctKernel.fromPacked` on the page), so swapping it in costs 8–13 ms of main-thread time instead of 20–60; unchanged assembly parts are not re-meshed. The swap waits for a pause in editing and keeps the selection: a face or edge is restored by component, feature and B-rep face. The chip names a feature OpenCascade could not build (*OpenCascade ✗ Fillet1*; click it for *What's Wrong*), and lofts through three or more sections stay smooth on OpenCascade, as in SolidWorks (Anvil's ruled loft then does not drive).

**Engine integrity.** Before the engine runs, `occt-worker.js` checks the SHA-256 of both the loader (`opencascade.full.js`, `8016d504…`) and the WebAssembly binary (`opencascade.full.wasm`, `4b6a7439…`) against the official opencascade.js 2.0.0-beta.b5ff984 build, and refuses anything else: the chip reads *OpenCascade ✗ integrity check failed* and the part stays on Anvil's kernel; switching the kernel on again starts a fresh worker. The binary may also be served gzipped (`opencascade.full.wasm.gz`, unpacked in the worker with `DecompressionStream`, then checked), which keeps a published copy under a 15 MB per-file limit; `serve.ps1` serves `.gz`. `vendor/get-occt.ps1` checks the same two fingerprints when it downloads. *Help ▸ About Anvil* names the engine, its LGPL-2.1 licence, its source and the fingerprints.

**Engine file.** A copy of Anvil whose server has no engine (the published copy) can still use OpenCascade: *Tools ▸ OpenCascade kernel ▸ Engine file…* opens a dialog to pick `opencascade.full.wasm` (or `.wasm.gz`) from `vendor/occt` after running `get-occt.ps1`, or from the `dist` folder of the opencascade.js 2.0.0-beta.b5ff984 npm package. The file is checked against the official SHA-256 before it is kept, kept in this browser's IndexedDB (`anvil-occt` ▸ `files` ▸ `engine`; nothing is uploaded), sent to the worker, which checks it again, and the kernel is switched on. A served engine always wins over a kept one. On later visits a kept engine counts as installed, so the kernel is on by default unless it was switched off. *Forget kept engine* removes it. The loader (`vendor/occt/opencascade.full.js`, 0.4 MB) must still be on the server. `UI.occt.engineDialog()`, `UI.occt.useEngineFile(file)` (a `File` / `Blob`; resolves once kept, rejects a wrong file), `UI.occt.storedEngine()` (the kept bytes or `null`), `UI.occt.forgetEngine()`.

`anvil.occt()` → `{ on, state, driving, faces, volume, anvilVolume, volumeDiff, beyond, unsupported, loadMs, ms }`; `anvil.occt(true | false, { remember: false }?)` switches it; `anvil.occtIdle()` resolves when OpenCascade has answered; `UI.occt.pending()` is true while a build of the current model is queued, running or held back. The engine loads in 1–3 s once per session and only while the switch is on.

## Performance

Measured during the refinement rounds (2026-10-02 / 03) on the user's Windows machine in Chrome, often with other agents running, so absolute numbers jitter; before → after.

| Area | Measure | Before → after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rebuilds (`tests/bench.html`, `tests/BENCHMARKS.md`) | Sum of medians, 23 examples, paired run | 381 → 165 ms (Funnel 259 → 76, Mug 32 → 20, Handlebar 17 → 7) |
| | `bench.html`, 30 parts incl. stress | 1044 → 216 ms |
| | Mass properties: Flange curve correction / `anvil.summary()` (r11) | 28 → 10 ms / 93 → 1 ms |
| Viewport | Draws per second while idle | 60 → 0 (1 s safety redraw) |
| | Hover pick, 30.8k-triangle part (median) | 6.7 → 1.9 ms |
| | FeatureManager refresh, 433 rows | 31.6 → 12.1 ms |
| PropertyManagers | Main-thread time per spin of the Funnel's Shell thickness | 99 → 0.1 ms (long tasks 1221 ms → 0) |
| | Opening the Funnel's Shell for editing | 222 → 1 ms |
| Sketcher | Drag, 55-entity sketch, per move | 10.9 → 3.6 ms (0.6 ms in an unconnected piece) |
| | Dimension edit commit | 11.9 → 3.8 ms |
| | Dimension and relation-icon redraw | 5.3 → 2.6 ms |
| OpenCascade | Main-thread time of the swap | 20–60 → 8–13 ms |
| | `Kernel.bodyEdges` on the Flange | 135 → 14–33 ms |
| Assemblies | Drag per frame: Gear train / 30-pin plate | 55 → 8–11 ms / 292 → 6–7 ms |
| | Drag per frame: 30-pin Flexible / Hinged cover | 160–216 → 6–13 ms / 14–56 → 5–10 ms |
| | Release of a drag, 30-pin plate | 60–70 → ~30 ms |
| | Solve, 30 pins mated separately | 107 → 24 ms |
| | Rigid sub-assembly edges after a translation (12 pins) | 166 + 86 → 8–13 ms |
| Drawings | Dragging a view with its dimensions, per move | 14.1 → 1.4 ms |
| | Dragging a dimension, per move | 12.9 → 4.6 ms |
| | Mug drawing, dashed pieces → paths | 964 → 75 |
| | SVG of an aluminium section (hatch) | 104 → 35 KB |

## Automation from the browser console

`ui-app.js` exposes `window.anvil` for driving the running app. `run`, `append`, `load` and `rebuild` return Promises (see *Background rebuilds*); `await` them, or `await UI.whenIdle()` after any UI action, before reading `summary()`.

| Call | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `anvil.run(code)` / `anvil.append(code)` | Replace or extend the model from a script. Returns a Promise that resolves to a summary, or rejects with the first feature error. |
| `anvil.model()` / `anvil.load(json)` | Get or set the feature JSON (`load` returns a Promise of the summary). |
| `anvil.rebuild()` | Rebuild and resolve to the summary. |
| `anvil.busy()`, `anvil.workerOn()` / `anvil.workerOn(bool)` | Whether a rebuild is in flight; read or set background-rebuild mode. |
| `anvil.script()` | Script regenerated from the model. |
| `anvil.stl()`, `anvil.dxfFace(i)`, `anvil.dxfSketch(id)` | Export text. |
| `anvil.dxfOutput(opts)`, `anvil.saveAs('dxf' \| 'dwg', base?, opts)` | The DXF/DWG Output PropertyManager's file without the PropertyManager: `opts` is `{ export: 'faces' \| 'views' \| 'sheet', face, views: ['Front', 'Top', 'Right', 'Isometric'], current, bendLines, notes, etch, formingTools, hiddenEdges, boundaryBox, bendLayers: 'split', reliefLayer }` (the PropertyManager's defaults apply when left out); `saveAs` downloads it, and for `dwg` returns a Promise (the DWG is written in the page by `dwg-writer.js`, see *Exports ▸ DWG*). `UI.cmdDxfOutput({ format, name })` opens the PropertyManager (see *Exports*). |
| `anvil.mass()`, `anvil.faces()`, `anvil.features()` | Mass properties; face list with index, normal, area, centroid; feature list with errors. |
| `anvil.select(i)`, `anvil.view(name)`, `anvil.fit()` | Select a face, set a standard view, zoom to fit. |
| `anvil.bodies()`, `anvil.selectBody(i)` | The solid bodies (`[{ index, featureId, name, volume }]`, volumes to 3 decimals); select one. |
| `anvil.theme()` / `anvil.theme("dark" \| "light")` | Read or set the workspace theme (same as *View ▸ Theme*). |
| `anvil.png()` | Data URL of the current viewport. |
| `anvil.equations()` / `anvil.equations(list \| { T: 10 })` | Read (a copy) or replace the global variables; setting returns a Promise of the summary (see *Equations*). |
| `anvil.summary()` | `{ type, name, features, errors, volume, bbox, faces, bodies, rebuildMs, equations, equationErrors }`, plus `components` and `mates` counts; `type` is `"part"` or `"assembly"`. |
| `anvil.newAssembly(name?)`, `anvil.newPart(name?)`, `anvil.isAssembly()`, `anvil.insert(…)`, `anvil.mate(…)`, `anvil.components()`, `anvil.mates()`, `anvil.fix(…)`, `anvil.removeComponent(id)`, `anvil.removeMate(id)` | Assembly documents (see *Assemblies*). |
| `anvil.drawing.*` | Drawing documents: `make()`, `summary()`, `svg()`, `dxf()`, `pdf()`, … (see *Drawings*). |
| `anvil.examples` | The Design Library entries. |
| `anvil.selection()`, `anvil.selectionFilter(opts?)`, `anvil.boxSelect(a, b, mods?)`, `anvil.lassoSelect(points, mods?)`, `anvil.selectionMode(mode?)`, `anvil.selectHidden(on?)`, `anvil.clearSelection()`, `anvil.instant3D(on?)` | The selection list, the Selection Filter, a box selection in viewport pixels, Instant3D handles (see *Selection and Instant3D*). |
| `anvil.transparent(idOrName, on)`, `anvil.bom(type)` | Change Transparency of a component; BOM rows with `description` (see *Assemblies*). |
| `anvil.drawing.tangent(viewId, mode)` | Tangent Edges Visible / With Font / Removed for a drawing view (see *Linework and the sheet*). |
| `anvil.occt(on?, opts?)`, `anvil.occtIdle()` | The OpenCascade kernel switch and status (see *OpenCascade kernel*). |

UI entry points added during the refinement rounds, for scripts and tests (all on `window.UI`):

| Call | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `UI.viewAnimation` (`false` turns view transitions off), `UI.finishViewAnimation()`, `UI.viewAnimating()`, `UI.requestRender()` | View transitions and the render-on-demand loop (see *Viewport and PropertyManagers*). |
| `UI.previewIdle()`, `UI.holdFrame(ms)`, `UI.featurePreview(fid)` | Resolves once the newest live PropertyManager preview is shown; keep the last frame; preview only one feature (Instant3D). |
| `UI.pmHandles()`, `UI.pmSetValue(key, v)`, `UI.pmToggle(key)`, `UI.pmFocusField(key)`, `UI.pmHoverItem(item)`, `UI.pmSpinStep(field)` | The open PropertyManager's on-model handles, fields, selection-list hover and spin step. |
| `UI.selectOtherAt(x, y)`, `UI.instant3DRulerPoint(handle, value, px)` | Select Other's face list at a viewport point; the screen point over a handle's ruler that snaps to a value. |
| `UI.sketchNumericInput(on?)`, `UI.spinDimEdit(sign, mods)`, `UI.dimEditValue()`, `UI.sketchMenu(x, y)`, `UI.confirmCancelSketch()`, `UI.sketchChanged()`, `UI.sketchTreePrefix(feature)` | Sketcher: the numeric-input option, the Modify box spin and value, the sketch right-click menu, Discard Changes and Exit, the (-) / (+) prefix. |
| `UI.cmdExtendEntities()`, `UI.cmdOffsetEntities()`, `UI.cmdSketchFillet()`, `UI.cmdTrimEntities()`, `UI.cmdMirrorEntities()`, `UI.cmdSketchPattern()`, `UI.cmdConvertEntities()` | The sketch-tool PropertyManagers. |
| `UI.smartMateHook`, `UI.asmMatePopup(o)`, `UI.asmPopup()`, `UI.asmClosePopup(kind?)`, `UI.flipMateAlignment(id)`, `UI.asmIsolate.isolate(id)` / `.exit()` / `.active()`, `UI.asmGhost.set(id, on)` / `.is(id)` / `.list()`, `UI.asmPose` | SmartMates, the Mate pop-up and context toolbar, Flip Mate Alignment, Isolate, Change Transparency, placements-only drag frames. |
| `UI.occt.engineDialog()`, `UI.occt.useEngineFile(file)`, `UI.occt.storedEngine()`, `UI.occt.forgetEngine()`, `UI.occt.pending()`, `UI.occt.swapMode(mode?)` | The OpenCascade engine file, the pending flag and the after-an-edit option (see *OpenCascade kernel*). |

## Materials and custom properties

`materials.js` (global `Materials`) holds a material library in SolidWorks' shape, a tree of classes: *Steel* (Plain Carbon Steel, AISI 1020, AISI 1045, AISI 4130, Galvanized Steel), *Stainless Steel* (AISI 304, AISI 316, 17-4 PH), *Aluminium Alloys* (1060, 5052-H32, 6061-T6 (SS), 7075-T6 (SN)), *Copper Alloys* (Brass, Copper, Aluminium Bronze), *Titanium Alloys* (CP Grade 2, Ti-6Al-4V) and *Plastics* (ABS, PLA, Nylon 6/6, Acetal (POM), Polycarbonate, Acrylic (PMMA)). Each has a mass density (kg/m³), elastic modulus, Poisson's ratio, tensile and yield strength (N/mm²) and an appearance colour. `Materials.find` also takes short names (`"6061"`, `"304"`, `"delrin"`, `"Steel, plain carbon"`).

- **Part material.** `model.material` holds the library name; applying one also sets `model.density` and `model.color`, so everything that reads `model.density` (Kernel and assembly mass properties, the worker) keeps working and the viewport takes the material's colour. Removing it restores 7850 kg/m³ and the default grey. A typed density that is not the material's unlinks it. Older models with `materialName` (the task pane's earlier list) are read through the same lookup.
- **Per-body materials.** A multi-body part can give a body its own material: `model.bodyMaterials = { "<feature id>": "Brass" }`, keyed by the feature that created the body (stable across rebuilds). Mass Properties weighs each body with its own density, and the viewport colours each body with its material.
- **Mass.** `Materials.massProps(model, build?, mesh?)` returns Kernel.massProps' fields (`volume`, `area`, `centroid`, `bbox`, `density`, `mass` = volume · density in model units) plus `massKg`, `material` and `bodies: [{ index, featureId, material, density, volume, massKg, centroid, own }]`; with per-body materials the centroid is mass-weighted and `density` is the average. Without them the mass is exactly `Kernel.massProps(mesh, model.density)`'s. Assemblies: `Assembly.massProps` uses each component's own part material (and its body materials) and lists `material` per component.
- **Custom properties** (*File ▸ Properties*): `model.properties = { custom: [{ name, type, value }], config: { "<configuration>": [...] } }`, types *Text*, *Date*, *Number*, *Yes or no*. A value may link to system values in SolidWorks' form, quotes included: `"SW-Material"`, `"SW-Mass"` (grams for mm parts, pounds for inch parts), `"SW-Density"`, `"SW-Volume"`, `"SW-Surface Area"`, `"SW-File Name"`, `"SW-Configuration Name"`; an `@file` suffix is ignored and an unknown link stays as typed. Configuration Specific rows override Custom rows of the same name while that configuration is active. Assemblies have properties too (`a.property`).

```js
p.material("6061");                                   // 6061-T6 (SS), 2700 kg/m³, its colour
var e = p.extrude(10, { merge: false }).last();
p.bodyMaterial(e, "ABS");                             // that body is ABS
p.property("Description", "Mounting bracket");
p.property("Mass", '"SW-Mass"');                      // evaluates to "203.00 g"
p.property("Revision", "B", { config: "Large" });     // Configuration Specific
```

```js
Materials.massProps(model, build, mesh).massKg;       // 0.203
Materials.property(model, 'Mass');                    // "203.00 g" (evaluated), null when not defined
Materials.sheetProperty(model, 'Material');           // the property, else (Material / Mass) the part's own
Materials.evaluate(model, 'rho "SW-Density"');        // "rho 2700 kg/m^3"
Materials.setProperty(model, 'Vendor', 'Acme');       // null removes; { config: 'Large' } for configuration rows
```

`generateScript` writes `p.material(...)` (then `p.color(...)` when the colour is not the material's), or `p.density(...)` / `p.color(...)` when they are not the defaults, and every property right after `p.name(...)`; `p.bodyMaterial(fN, ...)` lines go at the end. A model without any of them writes the same script as before.

**UI.** The FeatureManager's *Material* row shows the material (*Material <not specified>* without one): double-click opens **Edit Material** — the class tree on the left, *Properties* (model type, units, category, name, and the property table) and *Appearance* on the right, *Apply* / *Remove Material* / *Close*; right-click lists the favourites (Plain Carbon Steel, AISI 304, 6061-T6, Brass, ABS, Nylon 6/6) and *Remove Material*. A *Solid Bodies* row's right-click menu has *Material ▸ Edit Material…* and *Material ▸ Use Part Material*. The task pane's *Properties* panel lists the library by class. *File ▸ Properties…* opens the **Summary / Custom / Configuration Specific** dialog: Property Name (with the usual names offered), Type, Value / Text Expression (the `"SW-…"` links offered), Evaluated Value updated as you type, *Add Property*, a delete button per row; a document with no properties yet starts with Description, Part Number, Material (`"SW-Material"`), Mass (`"SW-Mass"`), Vendor and Revision. OK is one undo step. *Mass Properties* shows the material and each body's (or component's) material and density. In an assembly each component keeps its part's colour and the component tooltip names its material.

In the app: `anvil.material()` / `anvil.material(name | null)`, `anvil.bodyMaterial(i)` / `anvil.bodyMaterial(i, name | null)`, `anvil.materials()` (the library), `anvil.properties()` (`{ active, custom, config, evaluated }`), `anvil.properties(rows, { config })`, `anvil.property(name)` / `anvil.property(name, value | null, { type, config })`, `anvil.editMaterial({ body })`, `anvil.fileProperties(tab)`; every setter is one undo step and resolves to the summary, which gains `material`, `massKg` and `bodyMaterials`. `anvil.mass()` goes through `Materials.massProps` for parts.

## Drawings

*File ▸ Make Drawing from Part* opens a drawing document of the active part (SolidWorks placement; *Make Drawing from Assembly* in an assembly, see *Drawings of assemblies* below): a **View Layout** ribbon tab with the drawing views only, as SolidWorks 2024 (*Standard 3 View*, *Model View*, *Projected View*, *Auxiliary View*, *Section View*, *Detail View*, *Break*, *Crop View*), an **Annotation** tab (*Smart Dimension*, *Model Items*, balloons, *Bill of Materials*, *Revision Table*, symbols, *Document Properties*); *Save Drawing as SVG / DXF / DWG / PDF* and *Close Drawing* are in the *File* menu, *Sheet Properties* in *Tools* and the sheet's right-click menu, a drawing FeatureManager (*Draw1* ▸ *Annotations*, *Sheet1* ▸ *Sheet Format1*, *Drawing View1…n* with the part under each), the sheet in place of the viewport with a *Sheet1* tab and a tab back to the part, and drawing menus (*File ▸ Save Drawing as…*, *Insert ▸ Drawing View*, *Window* switches between the part and its drawing). Opening or starting another document (*File ▸ New*, *Open*, a Design Library entry, *Edit Part*) puts the drawing away first, and closing a drawing cancels only a PropertyManager of its own. The first drawing is made with *Standard 3 View*: Front, Top and Right, third-angle by default (Top above Front, Right to its right; *Sheet Properties ▸ First angle* puts Top below and Right to the left), plus an Isometric in the free corner. The sheet shows at once: border, title block and a soft placeholder where each view goes are drawn in the first frame (~10–25 ms), and the hidden-line removal of the views runs in `drawing-worker.js`, each view dropping in as it is done (model items and Auto insert marks once all are); a click, an export or any `anvil.drawing` call finishes the rest on the spot, so scripts always read the whole sheet. When the part changes under an open drawing (a rebuild, OpenCascade's exact solid, undo) the last sheet stays on screen until the new views are worked out the same way, then the new sheet replaces it in one go. A view placed with the mouse (model, projected, section, detail) is worked out the same way: a placeholder at once, its linework when ready (a section's cut no longer holds the page for 15–60 ms). On screen every line is kept at least about a pixel wide while zoomed out (exports keep the paper widths); dimension arrowheads are 3 × 1 mm (ASME Y14.2). Text is drawn as SolidWorks' ANSI standard does: Century Gothic (installed look-alikes, then Arial, where it is missing; nothing is downloaded), dimensions and notes with 3.5 mm capitals; linear and aligned dimension text is centred in a break of the dimension line (horizontal for vertical dimensions too); when text and arrows do not fit between the extension lines the arrows go outside and the text beyond them on the line run on (drag the text to move it); diameter, radius and hole-callout leaders end in a 4 mm shoulder with the text after it, centred on it. The sheet is A3 for mm parts and ANSI B for inch parts, with a border and a title block (part name, drawing number, scale, units, date, projection, sheet 1 of 1); the scale is the largest standard one (10:1 … 1:200) at which the three views fit, or a fixed one from *Sheet Properties*; a view may have its own scale (shown under it). **Zones** (ASME Y14.1): an ANSI sheet's margin carries zone numbers (right to left from the lower right, top and bottom) and letters (bottom to top, both sides) with a tick at each zone line: A 2 × 2, B 4 × 2, C 4 × 4, D 8 × 4. *Sheet Properties ▸ Zones* turns them off or on (`anvil.drawing.setSheet({ zones: false })`, `'auto'` = by format; ISO sheets have none unless asked, then about 50 mm fields); they are an item of the sheet format (*Edit Sheet Format* can delete them) and go to SVG / DXF / PDF on the SHEET layer. `Drawing.zoneAt(doc, p)` names the zone of a paper point. A Revision Table row with no ZONE typed shows the zone of each Revision Symbol that names it (`summary().revisions.rows[i].zoneAuto`); it follows the symbol when it moves. A Bend Table or Hole Table placed on free paper keeps clear of where the sheet's dimensions go (rfJR). Changing the size in *Sheet Properties* (rfJX) lays the standard views out again and keeps every other view, the sheet's own notes and placed Hole Tables at the same place relative to the border (a view that would then cover another or run off goes to the nearest clear spot, same scale; `Drawing.refitSheet(doc, mesh, oldSize, units)`). Every ANSI size has the same title block (MATERIAL, THICKNESS, SCALE, SIZE, FINISH, SHEET …); on ANSI A, too narrow for both blocks beside it, the general tolerances stay and COMPANY / DRAWN / CHECKED go.

Views are **projected from the mesh, not rendered**: every edge where faces meet at more than 23° (the kernel's coarsest round facets turn 22.5°) and every silhouette of a faceted curved face (a smooth edge between a face turned toward the viewer and one that is not) is clipped exactly against the front-facing triangles. Along an edge, "inside the triangle's projection" and "behind the triangle's plane" are both linear, so each triangle hides one parameter interval; the rest is visible. Collinear pieces are merged (also across gaps or overlaps under 0.03 mm, so both kernels draw a cut cylinder's edge as one line), a hidden piece under a visible one is dropped, and closed chains of equal chords on one circle come back as true circles. Orthographic views default to *Hidden Lines Visible* (dashed), pictorial views to *Hidden Lines Removed*; right-click a view to switch. Model dimensions (*Model Items*, on by default) are the overall width and height on the Front view, the depth on the Top view and every distinct visible circle diameter once (`4X Ø8` for a pattern), with leaders pointing out of the view. *Smart Dimension* (D) works as SolidWorks' drawing Smart Dimension: a first pick (an edge, a circle or arc, a vertex / end point, or a circle's centre) in an orthographic view, an optional second pick in the same view, then a placement click; a preview follows the pointer from the first pick, Esc cancels. One edge gives its length (an oblique edge horizontal, vertical or aligned by where the pointer is: above / below, beside, elsewhere), a circle Ø, an arc R; two points or centres their distance (horizontal / vertical / aligned by the pointer); a point, centre or circle and an edge the distance square to the edge (hole centre to edge); two circles their centre distance (Shift on a circle pick: to the side clicked, min or max); two parallel edges their distance; two edges at an angle the angle in the pointer's quadrant (arc, arrows, `45°`). Values are the model's (true size whatever the view scale) and the dimension line goes through the placement point. The Dimension PropertyManager (value, tolerance, precision, text, arrows) opens on the placed dimension (the next pick closes it with its edits and the tool goes on; ✓ or Esc ends the tool), on a double-clicked one, and on a single click (no drag) on any dimension with no tool running, as SolidWorks: the keyboard stays on the sheet (Delete deletes the dimension), a click elsewhere closes it with its edits, and a model change (rebuild, undo / redo) or Delete closes it unapplied so the sheet always follows the part. A two-pick dimension keeps its references (`d.refs`) and is measured again after every rebuild, dangling when one is gone; an angle can be dragged in or out. `Drawing.smartDim(pick1, pick2, at, { scale, minmax })`, `Drawing.addSmartDim(doc, viewId, pick1, pick2, at, opts)` (picks `{ edge: [a, b] }`, `{ circle: { c, r } }`, `{ arc: { c, r, a0, a1 } }`, `{ point: [u, v] }`, `{ center: { c, r } }`, view model units), `anvil.drawing.smartDim(viewId, pick1, pick2, at)`. **DWG**: *File ▸ Save Drawing as DWG…* and the button in the DXF save dialog write the active sheet's R2000 DXF as an AutoCAD 2000 DWG in the page (`dwg-writer.js`, as *DXF/DWG Output* does; no server); `anvil.drawing.dwg()` returns a promise of `{ name, bytes }` (or null). Drag a view to move it (Top and Right stay aligned with Front and follow it); Delete removes a selected view or dimension (a removed model dimension stays hidden); the wheel zooms smoothly about the cursor, dragging the empty desk or the middle button pans, a middle double-click or F fits the sheet. In an assembly document the same menu slot reads *Make Drawing from Assembly* (see *Drawings of assemblies*), and the *Window* menu lists the open part or assembly and its drawings. Drawings are stored on the document (`model.drawings`), follow every rebuild, and every drawing edit is one undo step.

**ANSI inch drawings (rfT).** An inch drawing uses the ANSI scale set (8:1, 4:1, 2:1, 1:1, 1:2, 1:4, 1:8, 1:16, 1:32, 1:64; `Drawing.scalesFor('in')`, metric keeps the ISO list `Drawing.SCALES`) in the automatic scale and in *Sheet Properties* / *Model View*; the automatic scale is the largest one at which the three views fit with a quarter view gap of margin and the isometric fits its corner at half that scale or more (a 6 × 4 sheet-metal bracket lands on ANSI B at 1:1). The dimension style follows the drawing's units: ANSI inch .125 in text, .125 × .042 in arrows, a .0625 in gap from the part and .125 in extension past the dimension line; ISO millimetre 3.5 mm text, 3 × 1 mm arrows, 1.5 mm gap, 2 mm extension. *Document Properties* has a *Dimensions* page (text height, arrow length / width, extension-line gap and extension, in the drawing's units) stored in `doc.options` (`dimText`, `arrowLen`, `arrowWidth`, `extGap`, `extBeyond`; `Drawing.dimSizes(doc)` in drawing units, `Drawing.dimGeometry(doc)` in paper mm, `sheet.dimStyle.paper` in sheet units). An unset material leaves the title block's MATERIAL cell blank (SolidWorks' `Material <not specified>` is tree text, never printed).

**Model Items PropertyManager (rfT).** *Model Items* (View Layout / Annotation ribbon, *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Model Items…*) opens SolidWorks' PropertyManager: *Source/Destination* (Entire model or Selected feature; Import items into all views or the selected view), *Dimensions* (Marked for drawing, Hole Wizard locations, Hole callout), *Annotations* (centre marks and centrelines) and *Options* (Eliminate duplicates). ✓ inserts the items in one undo step and keeps the choice in `doc.modelItems` (`Drawing.modelItems(doc)`): Marked for drawing is the overall sizes, radii, slots, the sketches' marked location dimensions and ordinate sets and each 90° Edge Flange's length measured outside (from the outer face of the sheet it rises from to its end, in the view that looks along its edge; a 1.5 flange on 16 ga with R .060 reads 1.620); Hole Wizard locations locate every hole centre not already located by a sketch dimension from the view's left and bottom edges, one per line of holes; Hole callout is the diameters (`2X Ø.281`); **(rfAJ)** a Hole Wizard hole gets a hole callout annotation instead (`an.mi`, `Drawing.insertWizardCallouts(doc, model, ids, { on, dedupe, feature })`), one per hole feature in the first projected view that looks along it, with the feature's text (`2X Ø.281 THRU ALL`, `Ø.201 THRU ALL` / `1/4-20 UNC - 2B THRU ALL`, `Ø.281 THRU ALL` / `⌵ Ø.507 X 82°`) and no Ø dimension on its circles. Any hole callout whose circle centre lies on a Hole Wizard position (view axis along the hole, within .003 in / .08 mm) reads `HoleWizard.callout` (Through All as `THRU ALL`, `nX` for several positions) rather than the faces' analysis, so a CSK whose cone goes through a thin sheet still reads its drill and countersink, as in SolidWorks. A selected feature brings only its own dimensions (its sketch's marked dimensions, an Edge Flange's length). Sheet-metal bends no longer get their outside radius (R + T) as a model item, only the bend radius. Drawings made before (no `doc.modelItems`) keep the old set. **(rfAB)** Marked for drawing also brings the sketches' own Smart Dimensions on entities (a line's length / dx / dy, a rectangle's width / height, a polyline segment: the typed 10 × 6 of a blank drawn with Corner Rectangle), marked unless `mark: false` (driven ones only when marked), each in the first view that looks square at its sketch (the Top view for a blank sketched on Top), between the points it measures (`d.sketchDim` 'sketchId:dimIndex'); diameters and radii come as hole callouts and radii, and a dimension with both ends inside one slot (a slot drawn as lines and arcs: its centre distance, width, overall length) is left to the slot callout, which is recognised from the view's linework, so tool-drawn (exploded) straight and arc slots keep their `SLOT` / `ARC SLOT` callouts. A **flat-pattern view** is a configuration of its own: Model Items with it among the destination views (all views, or the flat view selected, as when it was just placed) insert there the hole callouts, slots and radii it shows, the dimensions and ordinate sets of the sketches lying on the base (parallel to the flat, within a thickness of the Base Flange's sketch plane: the flat is that plane's 2D, so they land where they lie) and the hole locations from the flat's left / bottom edges; never the bend radius or bend deduction (bend notes and the Bend Table carry those). A Selected feature that is a base sketch goes to the flat as well. `anvil.drawing.modelItems({ source, feature, views, marked, holeLocations, holeCallouts, annotations, dedupe })` does what the PM's ✓ does; `modelItems(true | false)` still switches the default set on and off. **(rfBA)** Marked for drawing also brings the sketches' angular dimensions, each with its tolerance, precision, text and name: an angle between two lines (the arc between them as drawn, or its supplement as the sketch shows it), each Angular Running value up to 180° (from the datum ray) and a Chamfer Dimension (`2X .500 X 45°`), in the first orthographic view that looks square at the sketch (`Drawing.sketchAngularItems(doc, projections)`, `d.sketchAng` 'sketchId:relIndex', `d.relSrc`); **(rfDD)** a Path Length dimension comes as the drawing's Path Length dimension (kind `pathlen`, its lines and arcs in the view, the value with the sketch dimension's tolerance / precision / text on a leader from the first). A sketch Smart Dimension brought as a model item (`d.sketchDim`) takes its own properties by its key, also in a flat view; location dimensions to the origin or to a converted model edge carry theirs like any other distance.

**Projected, Section and Detail views** (*Drawing* tab and *Insert ▸ Drawing View*):
- *Projected View*: select a view (or click one after pressing the button), move the pointer above, below, left or right of it and click. The new view looks from that side (third angle; the opposite side in first angle), has the parent's scale, stays aligned with it (dragging it slides it along the projection only) and follows the parent when that moves. Front + down is *Bottom*; a projection that is not a standard view (up from *Top*: the back, upside down) is named *Projected*.
- *Section View*: the PropertyManager picks a *Horizontal* or *Vertical* cutting line and the label; click in a view to place the line (it snaps to hole and boss centres within 8 px), then click on the side where the view goes. The part is cut exactly (every body intersected with a box that ends on the cutting plane, so the cut faces are true faces of the result), the cut faces are hatched with their material's pattern (see *Linework and the sheet* below; ANSI31 without a material) at 45° (each other body, and in an assembly each part, in its own style — see *Drawings of assemblies*; 2.5 mm apart on paper, anchored to the sheet, ending on the drawn round edges), and the parent shows a thick chain line past the view with arrows pointing along the line of sight and the letters. The view is aligned with its parent, placed on the viewer's side in third angle and on the arrow side in first angle, labelled *SECTION A-A*, hidden lines removed. Letters run A, B, C… skipping I, O and Q. Sheet-metal bends, which Anvil's kernel builds as faceted prisms with no analytic record, are drawn as exact arcs (runs of four or more equal chords with equal turns, `Drawing.fitArcs`), so both kernels draw the U-bracket's section as the same 8 lines and 4 arcs.
- *Section View ▸ Section Type* (the PropertyManager's first box): *Full Section* as above; *Half Section* (click the centre where the line turns, then the side; *Cut half* picks Right / Top or Left / Bottom): only that half of the line cuts, so the quarter on the viewer's side is taken away — one half of the view is hatched section, the other the part seen from outside — and the parent shows the L-shaped cutting line (the cut leg to the centre, then square to it out of the part) with one arrow and the letter; *Offset Section* (click each leg, 2 or 3, on the holes it should pass through, then outside the view on the viewer's side): a stepped line, each step halfway between the clicks, every leg hatched, the steps not drawn in the view; *Aligned Section* (click the centre, then a point on the second leg, then the side of the first leg): what lies behind the second leg is turned about the centre into the first leg's plane, so both cut faces show at true size (a bolt circle's holes at different angles in one view), with an arrow square to each leg. The cutting line is a chain line with thick corners (`cutLine` primitives on the parent's layer, DXF `PHANTOM`).
- **PropertyManagers and previews (rfW).** *Model View*: *Orientation* (standard-view buttons, *(A) Flat pattern* first on sheet metal, the named views in *View*), *Options ▸ Auto-start projected view* (on: after the view is placed the Projected View tool goes on from it, one projection per click, until Esc), *Display Style* (Hidden Lines Visible / Removed), *Scale* (*Use sheet scale* / *Use custom scale*), *Dimension Type* on a pictorial view (Projected, or *True*: rfZ, its linear dimensions read the true 3D length of the edge or between the vertices they measure, and Smart Dimension works on it, a length always along what it measures;
