rfAL: a hole seen as an ellipse can be picked and reads its true Ø, a part arc of one its true R, the arrow landing on the ellipse — the ellipse's semi-major axis, `Drawing.ellipseRadius(ax, ay)`; the dimension keeps `ell: { ax, ay }` and follows the hole when the part changes; also in the view's Properties). *Section View*: *Section Line* (type, horizontal / vertical, cut half; rfZ *Partial section*: a full section's cutting line runs between two clicks, stops at its ends on the parent, and the view keeps only that band; *Scale with model changes*: the line, its ends and legs keep their place on the part as fractions of the parent's extents, so a part made bigger moves them with it; both also in the view's Properties), *Section View* (label, *Flip direction*: the view looks the other way), *Scale* (*Use parent scale* / custom). *Detail View* and *Auxiliary View* open their PropertyManager with the tool (keyboard left on the sheet): Detail *Style* (rfZ: *Per Standard* / *Without Leader*: the letter beside the circle; *With Leader*: further out on a thin leader; *Broken Circle*: the letter in a gap of the circle; *Connected*: a thin line from the circle to the detail view), *Label*, *Circle* / *Profile* (rfZ: a rectangle by two opposite corners; the detail shows the parent inside it), *Detail View ▸ Full outline* (the circle / rectangle drawn round the detail view) / *Jagged outline* (a zig-zag one; neither: the lines just end), *Scale* (twice the parent's / custom); style and outline also in the view's Properties; Auxiliary *Arrow* label and *Flip direction*. While placing, the sheet previews what a click will make: every leg of a half / offset / aligned cutting line (the next leg at the pointer), the auxiliary view's outline sliding along its arrow (where it lands), the crop rectangle or circle, the break lines.
- *Dimensions in a broken view* keep their model place: dragging one maps the pointer's move back through the break (`w.breakMap.fwd / inv`, sheet x / y with and without the breaks), so a dimension's text dragged out of a gap lands under the pointer and the value stays the true one.
- *Detail View*: click the centre of the detail circle in a view, click again for its radius, click where the view goes. It shows the parent inside the circle at twice the parent's scale (change it in the view's properties): lines are clipped to the circle, circles it cuts become arcs, a section's hatch is redrawn at the detail's scale. The parent shows the circle and the letter (just outside the circle, upper right unless that covers linework, hatching or a label; on hatching the hatch stops round it) and its own label moves below the circle; the view is labelled *DETAIL B* / *SCALE 4:1*.
- *Detail View* from a sketched profile (rfCQ): sketch a closed profile in the parent view with the drawing's *Sketch* tab (a circle, or lines, arcs and splines end to end), select it (one of its entities is enough), then *Detail View*: the profile is the detail's boundary and the tool asks only where the view goes. The detail shows only what lies inside the profile (lines cut where they cross it, circles into arcs, hatching clipped to it); the parent shows the profile itself with the letter, and *Full outline* draws it round the detail (*Jagged outline* zig-zags along it). The sketch entities become the detail's (`view.detail = { shape: 'profile', pts: [[u, v] …] (the parent's frame, counter-clockwise), a, b (its box), c, r }`; a sketched circle makes an ordinary circle detail). Script: `anvil.drawing.detailView(null, { sketch: [entityId] })`, engine `Drawing.addDetailView(doc, mesh, parentId, { pts })`, `Drawing.sketchLoop(doc, ids)`.
- **View labels (rfCQ)**, as SolidWorks: section, detail and auxiliary views are labelled by default (*SECTION A-A*, *DETAIL B / SCALE 2 : 1*, *VIEW C-C*); model, projected, relative and flat-pattern views have no label unless *Display label* is ticked (*Model View* ▸ *Options*, the view's *Properties* ▸ *Label*, *Section View* / *Detail View* / *Auxiliary View* PMs), then they read the view's name and the scale it is drawn at (*ISOMETRIC / SCALE 1 : 2*). `view.showLabel` is stored only when it differs from the default; `Drawing.viewLabelShown(v)`, `Drawing.setViewLabel(doc, id, on | null)`, `anvil.drawing.viewLabel(id, on)`, `showLabel` in every view-adding call and in `summary().views`.
- *Auxiliary View* (after *Projected View*; *Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Auxiliary*): click a straight edge in a view (an angled flange seen edge-on), then click on the side where the view goes. The view looks square onto the edge (its faces are drawn at true size and it can be dimensioned like an orthographic view), lies along the edge's normal on the sheet with its projection lines square to the edge, slides only along that axis when dragged and follows its parent; the parent shows an arrow pointing along the line of sight with the letter, the view is labelled *VIEW A-A* (DXF layer `AUXILIARY`).
- *Break* (*Broken View* PropertyManager; *Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Break*): pick vertical or horizontal break lines, the gap (10 mm / 0.4 in) and *Zig Zag Cut* or *Straight Cut*, then click where the two break lines go. The geometry between them is removed and the two sides close up to the gap (each moves in by half, so the view keeps its centre); dimensions across the break keep the true length; the break lines are thin lines on the view's layer. Right-click ▸ *Un-Break View* restores it. Several breaks per view are allowed.
- *Crop View*: pick *Rectangle* (two opposite corners) or *Circle* (centre, then a point on it) and click in a view: the view keeps what is inside, clipped exactly (lines, circles into arcs, a section's hatching). Right-click ▸ *Crop View ▸ Remove Crop*.
- *Broken-out Section* (rfAD; *View Layout* after *Detail View*, *Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Broken-out Section*): pick the profile (*Spline* through clicked points, closed by clicking the first point again or OK; *Polyline*; *Rectangle*; *Circle*) and the *Depth*, or tick *Depth reference* and click an edge or circle in another view square to this one (the cut goes down to its line or centre). The material inside the profile, from the front of the part down to the depth, is removed in that view only (an exact CSG cut, like a section); the cut floor is hatched with the material's pattern, the profile is a thin line (also in a detail of the view), the view keeps its place. Several per view; right-click ▸ *Broken-out Section ▸ Remove*. Engine: `Drawing.addBrokenOut(doc, viewId, { shape: 'spline' | 'poly' | 'rect' | 'circle', pts | a, b | c, r, depth | depthRef: { view, p: [u, v] } })`, `setBrokenOut(doc, viewId, id, spec | null)`, `clearBrokenOut`; stored as `view.brokenOut`; in the app `anvil.drawing.brokenOut(viewId, spec)`, `brokenOutEdit(viewId, id, spec | null)`.
- *Area Hatch/Fill* (rfAD; *Annotation* tab after *Centerline*, *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Area Hatch/Fill* then a click on a region, a click on the hatch of a view already selected, or a hatched region's right-click menu): *Material crosshatch* (the default), or the region's own *Pattern* (ANSI31, 32, 33, 34, 37, 38, *Solid*, *None*), *Scale* and *Angle* (added to the pattern's 45°). Each separate piece of a cut face is its own region; a detail follows its parent's regions. Stored per view as `view.hatchRegions: [{ p (a point inside), pattern, scale, angle }]`; `Drawing.setAreaHatch(doc, viewId, p, props | null)`, `Drawing.hatchRegions(sheetView)`; in the app `anvil.drawing.hatchRegions(viewId)`, `areaHatch(viewId, index | [u, v], props | null)`. SVG / PDF / DXF carry the lines; *Solid* is a real fill (rfAG): an even-odd filled path in SVG, `f*` in the PDF, a `HATCH` entity with the SOLID pattern in an R2000 DXF (one polyline boundary path per loop; it survives the DWG conversion), close hatch lines in R12, which has no HATCH. `drawing-render.js` draws every region straight from its own pattern, scale and angle, so a detail letter on an overridden region clears its island too, and a detail circle, a jagged outline or a break clips the fill. (`Broken-out Section` and `Area Hatch/Fill` are tools of their own, `bko` and `ahatch`.)
- *Area Hatch/Fill* per region, lane DW (2026-10-29): the PropertyManager has SolidWorks' groups — *Properties*: *Material crosshatch*, *Hatch* / *Solid* / *None*, *Pattern* (ANSI31–ANSI38: ANSI35 fire brick and ANSI36 marble as acad.pat draws their dash-dot lines), *Scale*, *Angle*; *Apply changes to*: *Region*, *Body* (every region of that solid), *Component* (assemblies). One undo step per OK; Esc / ✗ leaves the drawing as it was. Overrides are keyed so they survive a rebuild that keeps the region: `key` `'b<body>[.<part>]:r<n>'` (the solid, and the region's place among its regions, left to right then bottom to top); a region is found by its stored point while that still lies in a region of the same solid, else by its place; a body or component scope is `'b<body>[.<part>]'` / `'b<body>'` + `comp` (the component id). An override whose region or body is gone is not drawn (it stays in the file). Entries: `view.hatchRegions: [{ p?, key?, scope?: 'body' | 'component', comp?, pattern, scale, angle }]`; old entries without `key` match by the point alone. `Drawing.setAreaHatch(doc, viewId, p | key, { pattern, scale, angle, fill: 'hatch' | 'solid' | 'none' } | null, { key, scope, comp })`; `Drawing.hatchRegions(sheetView)` gives each region's `key`, `solid`, `scope`, `comp`. App / script: `anvil.drawing.hatchRegions(viewId)`, `areaHatch(viewId, index | [u, v] | key, props | null, { apply })`, `hatch(viewId, key, { pattern, scale, angle, fill, apply })`, `view(viewId).hatch(key, props)`.
- *Area Hatch/Fill* on a closed region that is not a cut face (lane DW; the same tool): a click inside a closed sheet-sketch or view-sketch profile (lines, arcs, splines end to end, or a circle), or on a model face of a non-section view, opens the PM (no Material crosshatch, no Apply to) and adds the fill. Stored as `doc.sheet.areaFills` (the sheet's sketch) / `view.areaFills` (`{ id: 'AF1', ids: [sketch ids] }` or `{ id, face: { p: [u, v], n: normal } }`, plus `pattern`, `scale`, `angle`); a face fill finds its face again under `p` at every compute (kept only while the normal is the same). A sketch fill cuts out the closed profiles of the same sketch drawn inside it (islands alternate, as SolidWorks); a face fill covers only the part of the face that shows (bodies in front of it are cut out). Click a fill (a sheet fill anywhere off the views, a view's when the view is selected) to edit it; Delete removes it. Drawn on the view's layer (sheet fills on `ANNOTATIONS`) in SVG / PDF / DXF / DWG; `sheet.areaFills` lists what was drawn. `Drawing.addAreaFill(doc, { ids } | { view, p }, mesh, frame)`, `editAreaFill(doc, id, props | null)`, `areaFills(doc)`, `sketchRegionAt(doc, sheetPoint)`; app: `anvil.drawing.areaFill({ ids } | { view, p }, props)`, `areaFills()`, `areaFillEdit(id, props | null)`, `view(viewId).areaFill([u, v], props)`.
- *Detail View ▸ Jagged outline* (rfAD): the detail's lines and hatching run out to the zig-zag and end on it (the zig-zag is the clip, not the circle).
- Deleting a view deletes the section and detail views cut from it; projected and auxiliary views keep their direction and become free views.

**Dimensions that follow the part.** A Smart Dimension stores the edge or circle it was placed on. After every rebuild it is reattached to the same geometry in the new projection: the unchanged edge; else the best of the collinear edges that overlap it (an edge that grew or shrank) and the parallel edges within a quarter of the view that overlap at least half their union (a face that moved), scored by overlap / union and offset; circles by centre (a hole that changed size), then by radius near the old centre (a hole that moved). The value follows. With no match the dimension is **dangling**: it keeps its last position and is drawn in olive (`#a07c00`, DXF colour 42, like SolidWorks' dangling annotations); undoing the change reattaches it.

Exports: **SVG** at the real sheet size (`width="420mm"`), one `<g data-layer>` per view, hidden edges dashed; **DXF** R12 in paper units with one layer per view (`FRONT`, `TOP`, `RIGHT`, `ISOMETRIC`, `SECTION_A`, `DETAIL_B`, …) plus `DIMENSIONS` and `SHEET`, hidden edges on the `HIDDEN` linetype, cutting lines on `PHANTOM`, holes as `CIRCLE`s and clipped circles as `ARC`s, hatching as `LINE`s (rfIR: the app's *Save Drawing as DXF* in R2000 and *Save as DWG* write each hatched region as one `HATCH` entity with the pattern's ANSI families instead, a 321-line section 37 KB -> 0.6 KB; `Drawing.toDXF(sheet, { version: 'R2000', hatch: true })`); **PDF** 1.4, one page at the sheet size, Helvetica text. Text is the same size in all three and on screen (a text's height is its capitals' height, 3.5 mm for dimensions); the DXF text style names Century Gothic (`GOTHIC.TTF`).

```js
var m = Kernel.newModel('Plate'); Kernel.runScript(m, code);
var mesh = Kernel.meshFromBody(Kernel.rebuild(m).body);
var doc = Drawing.newDrawing(m, { projection: 'third' });      // { format, scale: [1, 2], date, autoDims } optional
Drawing.standard3View(doc, mesh, m.units);                       // Front / Top / Right + Isometric, scale and layout
var sheet = Drawing.compute(doc, mesh, m);                       // paper-space primitives, views, dims
Drawing.toSVG(sheet); Drawing.toDXF(sheet); Drawing.toPDF(sheet);
Drawing.toDXF(sheet, { version: 'R2000' });                      // AC1015: true ELLIPSE, line weights (370); R12 default
Drawing.projectView(mesh, 'Front');                              // { lines: { visible, hidden }, circles, bounds, stats }
Drawing.addProjectedView(doc, mesh, frontId, 'down');            // up | down | left | right; { x, y } optional
Drawing.addSectionView(doc, mesh, frontId, { axis: 'h', at: 4, sign: 1 });   // cut at v = 4 (parent's frame), viewer on +v
Drawing.addDetailView(doc, mesh, topId, { c: [10, -10], r: 8 });  // scale defaults to 2x the parent's
Drawing.addSectionView(doc, mesh, frontId, { axis: 'h', at: 25, sign: 1, type: 'half', c: 50, side: 1 });              // cut only for u > 50
Drawing.addSectionView(doc, mesh, frontId, { axis: 'h', at: 15, sign: 1, type: 'offset', jogs: [{ t: 50, at: 35 }] });   // v = 15 up to u = 50, then v = 35
Drawing.addSectionView(doc, mesh, frontId, { axis: 'h', at: 0, sign: 1, type: 'aligned', c: 0, side: 1, angle: 270 });   // leg 1 to +u, leg 2 at 270° turned into it
Drawing.setSectionType(doc, sectionId, { type: 'full' });        // or half / offset / aligned with the fields above
Drawing.addAuxiliaryView(doc, mesh, frontId, { a: [100, 20], b: [0, 60], sign: -1 });   // edge a-b (parent frame); sign 1: left of a→b
Drawing.setCrop(doc, frontId, { type: 'rect', a: [0, 0], b: [50, 50] });                // or { type: 'circle', c, r }; null removes it
Drawing.addBreak(doc, frontId, { dir: 'vertical', a: 50, b: 150, gap: 10, style: 'zigzag' });   // 'straight'; clearBreaks(doc, id)
Drawing.sectionOf(mesh, [0, 1, 0], 4);                           // { mesh, caps: [face], area }: keeps p.e <= 4
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.make()` (File ▸ Make Drawing from Part; returns the summary), `open(i)`, `close()`, `isOpen()`, `summary()` (`{ name, sheet, views: [{ id, name, orient, kind: 'model' | 'projected' | 'section' | 'detail', label, parent, x, y, scale, display, tangent, extents, box, stats: { visibleLines, hiddenLines, visibleCircles, hiddenCircles, tangentLines? }, hatch, sectionArea }], dims, dangling }`), `standard3View()`, `addView(orient, { x, y, display, scale, dimType })`, `setSheet({ format, scale: '1:2' | 'auto', projection: 'first' | 'third' })`, `projectedView(parentId, dir, { x, y, fit })`, `sectionView(parentId, { axis, at, sign, x, y, label, fit, partial: [from, to], scaleWithModel, excluded: [component ids] })` (rfDN: `excluded`, an assembly drawing's Excluded components, drawn whole and unhatched; engine `Drawing.setSectionExcluded(doc, viewId, ids | null)`, saved as `section.excluded`. `sectionView` and `removedSection` cut in drawing-worker.js and return at once: `await` the result (or `UI.drawing.ready()`) for the summary; reading a property of the result straight away finishes the view on the main thread and reads it, as before; rfDR: `make`, `open`, `standard3View`, `addView`, `projectedView`, `detailView`, `auxiliaryView` and `tangent` return the same way, their hidden-line removal done in the worker), `detailView(parentId, { c, r | shape: 'profile', a, b, style, outline: 'full' | 'jagged', scale, x, y, label, fit })`, `viewOptions(viewId, { dimType: 'true' | 'projected', partial, scaleWithModel, style, outline })` (rfZ; engine: `Drawing.setDimType`, `setSectionOptions`, `setDetailOptions`, `trueLength(mesh, frame, a, b)`), `deleteView(id)`, `auxiliaryView(parentId, { a, b, sign, x, y, label })` (kind `'aux'`), `removedSection(parentId, { a: [u, v], b: [u, v], x, y, label, flip, scale, showLabel })` (rfCX, View Layout ▸ Removed Section: a section view with `section.removed`, the cross-section between the two ends alone, not aligned; engine `Drawing.addRemovedSection`; a section made at its parent's scale carries `section.parentScale` and follows the parent's scale), `crop(viewId, region | null)`, `breakView(viewId, { dir, a, b, gap, style })`, `unbreak(viewId)`, `tangent(viewId, 'visible' | 'font' | 'removed')`, `modelItems(bool | { source, feature, views, marked, holeLocations, holeCallouts, annotations, dedupe })`, `svg()`, `dxf({ version: 'R12' | 'R2000' })`, `pdf()`, `doc()`. *Save as DXF* offers the version in its dialog (`#dw-dxf-version`, remembered for the session): R12 (AC1009; ellipses as polylines) or R2000 (AC1015, laid out as the flat-pattern DXF's R2000: handles, subclass markers, BLOCKS / OBJECTS, a true `ELLIPSE`, each line's weight from the sheet's strokes); both keep one layer per view, true arcs and circles (geometry to 1e-9, so edges that meet on the sheet meet in the file, rfIJ), `SOLID` arrowheads and `TEXT`. (rfT) In R2000 every linear, aligned, diameter and radius dimension is a true `DIMENSION` entity (`AcDbRotatedDimension` / `AcDbAlignedDimension` / `AcDbDiametricDimension` / `AcDbRadialDimension`, the measured value in 42, the sheet text as its override, style `Standard` with the drawing's DIMTXT / DIMASZ / DIMEXO / DIMEXE / DIMDEC / DIMZIN) whose lines, arrows and text sit in its own anonymous block `*D1`, `*D2` …, so a reader without dimension support still shows them; an angle dimension is a 3-point angular DIMENSION (`AcDb3PointAngularDimension`: 13 / 14 on its two lines, 15 the vertex, 10 on the arc; no measured value, which DXF keeps in radians); ordinate, baseline and chamfer dimensions stay exploded, and R12 explodes them all. Angle dimensions are drawn in the drawing's dimension style like the others (ANSI inch .125 text and arrows). `headless/dxf-check.js` reads the BLOCKS section and checks that each DIMENSION's block and dimension style exist (`check(...).dimensions`); LibreDWG converts it to DWG and back with the DIMENSIONs kept. Projected, section and detail views added through these calls fit a full sheet (`fit: true` by default, see below).

### Linework and the sheet

- **Exact linework.** Parts carry true curves, and the projection uses them wherever it can: on OpenCascade solids the intersection curves come from the exact surfaces (chord ≤ diagonal · 1e-5), on Anvil's kernel from the true curve when it lies within 3× the chord tolerance (the hex nut's hyperbolas); torus and sphere silhouettes are exact and cylinder edges snap to their true circles. Open mesh edges within 1e-4 of the diagonal are paired first (`Drawing.pairOpenEdges`), so OpenCascade parts draw without stray facet lines; nearly-full visible rims are kept whole; creases are judged from the true normals (more than 2° apart); face-on circles are never dashed. Lines meet exactly at vertices (chords split at the true curve's extreme point), visible ends cut short by a curved occluder's facets are moved onto its exact outline, and free ends within one facet step (3 × chord tolerance for faceted silhouettes) are joined to the neighbouring line (ends 0.001–0.5 mm short over 23 examples × 4 views: 14 → 1). Smooth chains are written as one SVG / PDF path, so dashes do not restart (Mug: 964 pieces → 75 paths).
- **Tangent edges** per view, as in SolidWorks: *Tangent Edges Visible* (thin solid), *With Font* (thin phantom) or *Removed* (the default), from the view's right-click menu ▸ *Tangent Edge*, the view PropertyManager or `anvil.drawing.tangent(id, mode)` (`view.tangent`; a detail view follows its parent). Tangent edges on true circles — fillet run-outs, blends round a boss, rim fillets between features — are drawn as exact circles, arcs or ellipses. SVG, DXF (`PHANTOM` for With Font) and PDF carry them.
- **Hatch per material.** A part's cut faces use their body's material pattern (`Drawing.hatchPattern(materialName)`, `Drawing.HATCH_PATTERNS`): steel and stainless ANSI32, aluminium ANSI38, copper alloys ANSI33, plastics ANSI34, anything else ANSI31; in an assembly each cut part uses its own part's (or body's) material, at the angle that keeps it apart from its neighbours (`Drawing.asmPartMaterials`). A run of hatch lines is written as one SVG path with relative moves (an aluminium section 104 → 35 KB) and DXF hatch LINEs leave out z.
- **Dimensions.** Radius and diameter texts are placed clear of other dimensions, view geometry, centre marks, other views, the title block and the sheet edge (`Drawing.textRect`); a diameter tries a short leader beside the hole first. A selected dimension shows arrow handles: click one to flip the arrows. Dragging snaps the dimension into line with its neighbours and shows a guide (hold Alt for no snapping). A double-click opens a Modify box over the text: for a model item a new value drives the part (Enter applies, Esc cancels), otherwise it edits the text (`<DIM>` is the value).
- **Title block.** Double-click a title block cell to edit its text in place: the box shows the field's own text or what its property link gives, and an empty box puts the link back (`Drawing.titleFieldAt`).
- **View fitting.** Section, projected and detail views added through the script API (`fit: true`) go along their alignment at their own scale first, then into the nearest free area of the sheet, then at the next smaller standard scales (down to a quarter); at each scale a crowded sheet (ANSI A with four views) is tried again with a 4 mm gap round the view before a smaller scale, and the title block's tolerance / approval blocks count as taken (rfIJ); a detail view never shrinks to its parent's scale, and its name follows its final scale. When nothing fits the view goes beside the sheet with a warning — never over another view. View labels read like SolidWorks' (`SCALE 2 : 1`, `Drawing.scaleLabel`; `scaleText` stays `2:1` for fields).
- **The sheet** is patched layer by layer instead of redrawn; views and their dimensions are dragged by transform (14.1 → 1.4 ms per move); the item under the pointer is pre-highlighted; the sheet waits while OpenCascade is still building (`UI.occt.pending()`), so Anvil's approximation never flashes up; when animation frames are throttled (a background tab) the render falls back to a 60 ms timer. Slots get slot centre marks. In DXF the view layers are colour 7 and the thin lines on them (hatch, tangent edges, centre marks) colour 8, so a plot-by-colour pen table gives thick and thin weights.


**True curves in drawings.** Parts carry true curves (see *True curves*): circles and arcs are drawn as exact circles, arcs (DXF `ARC`) and, in pictorial views, ellipses (SVG `<ellipse>` / elliptical arcs, DXF `POLYLINE`, PDF Béziers); a partly hidden circle splits into visible and hidden arcs; cylinder and cone silhouettes are the exact tangent lines (where both ends of the line sit on a true circle of the same axis; a trimmed surface such as the hex nut's chamfer cone keeps its facet edge); edges between facets of one surface are never drawn; facet lines lying along a drawn exact curve (a mitre seam seen edge-on over a bend's arc) are dropped, and a free line end under 0.03 mm from other linework is carried onto it; in an assembly a circle on another component's coincident surface (a bore on its shaft) is hidden only where its true arc is too; model items add `R` dimensions of true arcs (`2X R5`) and Smart Dimension works on arcs; hole callouts and centrelines use the exact circles. Imported meshes without surface records draw from their facets as before. `Drawing.meshCurves(mesh)`; `projectView` adds `arcs`, `ellipses`, `arcsAll`, `exact`.

### Sheets (rfQ)

- **Several sheets.** The tabs under the sheet are the drawing's sheets, as in SolidWorks: a click activates one, the tab after the last (*Add Sheet*, also *Insert ▸ Sheet…* and the tab's right-click menu) adds a sheet with the active one's format, scale and projection and activates it, right-click a tab for *Add Sheet…*, *Delete* (asks first; the last sheet stays), *Rename* (the tab becomes a text box: Enter applies, Esc cancels) and *Properties…* (activates that sheet, then *Sheet Properties*). Each sheet has its own format size, scale, projection, views, notes, symbols and BOM; the title block SHEET field (`$PRP:"SW-Sheet"`) reads `1 OF 2`, `2 OF 2`. The FeatureManager lists every sheet (the active one with its views; double-click another to activate it). Adding, deleting and renaming are undo steps; switching sheets is not.
- **On screen (rfBI, the SolidWorks 2024 look).** Four scroll arrows before the tabs go to the first, previous, next and last sheet; the status bar reads *Editing Sheet1* (*Editing Sheet Format* while the format is edited), the sheet scale (`1 : 2`) and the unit system (MMGS / IPS) in place of the part's items. The sheet sits white on a grey desk with a small shadow down and to the right. A view's dashed frame is orange under the pointer and blue when selected; a dimension is coloured in place, blue selected and orange under the pointer, and a reference dimension added on the sheet (not a model item) shows grey; notes, symbols and annotations keep a thin frame in the same colours. All of this is screen only (`data-tint` on the live sheet, `drawing.css`): SVG, PDF, DXF and DWG are unchanged.
- **Exports.** *Save as PDF* writes every sheet, one page each at its own size; *Save as SVG / DXF* write one sheet, the active one, and their dialog has a *Sheet* choice when there are several (choosing another activates it).
- **Document.** The active sheet's contents stay where a one-sheet drawing keeps them (`doc.sheet`, `doc.views`, `doc.notes`, `doc.symbols`, `doc.bom`, `doc.layout`), so every engine call works on the active sheet; `doc.sheets` lists the sheets (the active one as `null`, the others as `{ sheet, views, notes, symbols, bom, bomRows, layout }`) and `doc.activeSheet` is its index. A drawing saved before has no `doc.sheets` and is one sheet. View and annotation ids are unique across the sheets; revisions, title block texts, properties and options belong to the drawing.

```js
Drawing.addSheet(doc, { name, format: 'ANSI A', scale: '1:2', projection, activate });   // -> index; activates it unless activate === false
Drawing.activateSheet(doc, 1 /* or 'Sheet2' */);  Drawing.renameSheet(doc, 1, 'FLAT');  Drawing.deleteSheet(doc, 'FLAT');
Drawing.sheets(doc);       // [{ index, name, format, scale, autoScale, projection, views, active }]
Drawing.sheetDoc(doc, i);  // a document whose active sheet is sheet i (work out / write a sheet without switching)
Drawing.toPDF([Drawing.compute(Drawing.sheetDoc(doc, 0), mesh, model), Drawing.compute(Drawing.sheetDoc(doc, 1), mesh, model)]);   // one page per sheet
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.sheets()`, `addSheet(opts)`, `activateSheet(i | name)` (-> the summary), `deleteSheet(i | name)`, `renameSheet(i | name, newName)`; `svg(i)` and `dxf({ version, sheet })` write one sheet (default the active one), `pdf()` every sheet; `summary().sheet` has `index` and `count`, `summary().sheets` the list.

- **Notes and symbols on a view** (rfQ): a Note, Surface Finish, Datum Feature or Geometric Tolerance placed on a view (its leader's tip, or with no leader its position, inside the view's linework box) belongs to that view and moves with it when the view is dragged, laid out again or moved by its parent, live while dragging; one placed on free paper stays on the sheet. `note.view` / `symbol.view` is the view's id (0: on the sheet), `vxy` the view's position the note was placed against (its offset is `p - vxy`); `Drawing.addNote` / `addSymbol` / `editNote` / `editSymbol` take `view: id` to attach and `view: 0` to free it. A note whose view is deleted stays on the sheet. `summary().notes[i].view`, `summary().symbols[i].view`.
- **General tolerances block** (rfQ): a double-click edits it in place as lines: Enter starts a new line, Ctrl+Enter or ✓ applies (one undo step), Esc cancels.

### Sheet format, templates, General Tables, .drawing files (rfAE)

- **Edit Sheet Format**: right-click the sheet ▸ *Edit Sheet Format* (also the **Sheet Format** ribbon tab, *Tools ▸ Edit Sheet Format* and the *Sheet Format1* row's right-click menu). The views and annotations grey out and only the border and title block take clicks: click a line or a text (a title block field's label and text go together), drag it to move it, Delete removes it, *Line* (two clicks; snaps to line ends and horizontal / vertical) and *Note* (a click, then its text; `$PRP:"CompanyName"` / `$PRPSHEET:"…"` links as in the title block, `\n` starts a line) add to the format, double-click edits a note or a field, right-click ▸ *Edit Sheet* goes back. *Reset Sheet Format* returns to the built-in ANSI / ISO format. Each change is one undo step. Stored as `doc.format = { name, hide: { id: true }, move: { id: [dx, dy] }, items: [{ id, k: 'line', a, b, style } | { id, k: 'note', p, text, h }] }` in paper mm, added items measured from the border's bottom-right corner so they follow the title block onto any sheet size. Built-in ids: `border`, `tb`, `tb-row1`, `tb-row2`, `blk`, `blk-row1`, `blk-row2`, `blk-sep`, `col:<row>:<x0>`, `field:<key>`. `sheet.format` lists the items with their paper geometry; their primitives carry `fmt: id`.
- **Drawing templates**: *Sheet Format ▸ Save as Template* keeps the drawing's format, title block texts, properties (company name, DRAWN …), sheet size, projection, scale and options under a name in this browser (localStorage `anvil.drawingTemplates`); *Save Sheet Format* keeps only the format, texts and properties. *File ▸ Make Drawing from Part / Assembly* (a part without a drawing) and *File ▸ New Drawing…* offer the saved templates (SolidWorks' New Document list; the built-in format is the first choice, nothing is asked while none is saved). *Tools ▸ Drawing Templates…* exports one as a JSON file (`Name.drwdot.json` / `Name.slddrt.json`), imports one, deletes one, or (in a drawing) loads one's sheet format.
- **General Table** (*Insert ▸ Tables ▸ General Table…*, Annotation tab): columns, rows and an anchor (top-left / top-right / bottom-left / bottom-right of the border, bottom-right standing on the title block), or none: click where its top-left corner goes. Double-click a cell to type in it (Enter applies, Tab goes to the next cell, Esc cancels; `$PRP` links work), right-click for *Insert ▸ Row Above / Below / Column Left / Right*, *Delete ▸ Row / Column*, *Anchor ▸ …*, *Delete Table*; drag it to move it off its anchor; Delete removes it. Tables belong to their sheet (`doc.tables`, one of `SHEET_KEYS`) and are drawn on the `TABLES` layer in SVG, DXF and PDF; a text longer than its cell is cut with `...`.
- **The .drawing document**: *File ▸ Save Drawing As…* writes `Draw1.drawing`: `{ kind: 'anvil-drawing', v: 1, part: { name, file, type, units }, drawing }`. *File ▸ Open* of a `.drawing` opens it on the open part when that is the part it names (the drawing joins `model.drawings`, replacing one of the same name), else on the part saved in this browser under that file or model name, else asks for the part file. Drawings embedded in the part (`model.drawings`) work as before.

```js
Drawing.addFormatLine(doc, S, units, a, b, 'thick'); Drawing.addFormatNote(doc, S, units, p, 'MADE BY $PRP:"CompanyName"', 3.5);
Drawing.moveFormatItem(doc, 'field:company', dx, dy, units); Drawing.deleteFormatItem(doc, 'tb-row1'); Drawing.resetFormat(doc);
Drawing.formatItemAt(sheet, p, tol);  Drawing.titleFieldAt(S, units, p, doc);   // doc: deleted / moved fields
var t = Drawing.drawingTemplate(doc, 'Shop B');  Drawing.newDrawing(model, { template: t });  Drawing.applySheetFormat(doc, Drawing.sheetFormatData(doc2));
var gt = Drawing.addTable(doc, units, { cols: 3, rows: 2, anchor: 'top-left' | x, y, cells });  Drawing.editTableCell(doc, gt.id, r, c, text);
Drawing.insertTableRow(doc, id, at); Drawing.insertTableCol(doc, id, at); Drawing.deleteTableRow(doc, id, r); Drawing.deleteTableCol(doc, id, c);
Drawing.setTableProps(doc, units, id, { anchor, x, y, rowH, colW }); Drawing.moveTable(doc, id, x, y); Drawing.tableCellAt(doc, units, p);
Drawing.drawingFile(doc, model, fileName);  Drawing.readDrawingFile(text);
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.editSheetFormat(on)`, `formatItems()`, `formatLine(a, b, style)`, `formatNote(p, text, h)`, `formatMove(id, dx, dy)`, `formatDelete(id)`, `formatEdit(id, { text, h, style })`, `resetFormat()`; `templates()`, `saveTemplate(name, { kind: 'template' | 'format' })`, `template(name)`, `deleteTemplate(name)`, `exportTemplate(name)`, `importTemplate(text)`, `loadSheetFormat(name)`, `newDrawing({ template })`; `table(spec)`, `tableCell(id, r, c, text)`, `insertRow`, `insertColumn`, `deleteRow`, `deleteColumn`, `tableProps`, `deleteTable`, `tables()`; `saveFile()`, `openFile(text, { part: { name, data } })`. `summary()` adds `format: { name, edited, editing, template, items }` and `tables`.

### Annotations

The **Annotation** tab (next to *Drawing* in a drawing document, SolidWorks placement; also *Insert ▸ Annotations* / *Insert ▸ Dimensions*) has *Smart Dimension*, *Ordinate / Baseline / Chamfer Dimension*, *Model Items*, *Balloon*, *Auto Balloon*, *Stacked Balloon*, *Hole Callout*, *Center Mark*, *Centerline* and *Document Properties*. Each tool takes clicks on the sheet until Esc (or the PropertyManager's ✓ / ✗). Annotations store the view geometry they were put on (model units in the view frame) and are reattached after every rebuild like Smart Dimensions; one whose geometry is gone is drawn dangling (olive). Centre marks and centrelines use the CENTER linetype (long-short dash, DXF `CENTER`) on an `ANNOTATIONS` layer (DXF colour 3).

- **Center Mark**: *Single* (each hole clicked gets a mark), *Linear* (click the holes of a row, ✓: marks plus connection lines through the centres) or *Circular* (✓: marks, the pitch circle fitted through the hole centres, radial lines and a mark at the pattern centre). A mark is a cross of *Mark size* in the drawing's units (`doc.options.markSize`; SolidWorks' Centerlines / Center Marks default: .1 in on an inch drawing, 2.5 mm on a metric one; an inch drawing's size of 1 or more saved before is read as paper mm, `Drawing.markPaper(doc, size)`) and, with *Extended lines*, centre lines from 1 mm beyond it to 2.5 mm past the circle.
- **Centerline**: click near a cylinder or hole seen from the side (its axis is found from the circular edges at both ends, `Drawing.cylAxes`), or two parallel edges one after the other (the line runs midway); drawn 3 mm past both ends.
- **Auto insert on view creation** (*Tools ▸ Options ▸ Document Properties*, `doc.options`): *Center marks-holes* puts a single mark on every centre of a full visible circle of an orthographic view, *Centerlines* one on every cylinder axis lying in the view plane (coaxial and overlapping ones joined), whenever a view is made (Standard 3 View, Model, Projected, Section, Detail). Drawings made in the app start with both on (SolidWorks' template); `Drawing.newDrawing` without `options` leaves them off.
- **Hole Callout**: click a hole. The callout is read from the solid, not a feature: the smallest circle on the hole's axis is the drill; lines parallel to the axis just outside the drill, just outside each larger circle and halfway between are cut against the mesh — a larger radius whose wall reaches the entry face above the drill's wall is a counterbore (the halfway line stops with the drill wall) or a countersink (it stops higher: a cone, angle snapped to 60 / 82 / 90 / 100 / 110 / 118 / 120 / 130° within 1.5°). The empty gap on the axis says THRU or blind and which face the hole opens to; depths are measured from the entry face (Hole Wizard convention). A hole that goes through reads `THRU ALL` when the cut that makes its size ends Through All (a Cut-Extrude Through All, a Hole Wizard hole with `end: 'through'`) and `THRU` when a blind cut happens to clear the part, as SolidWorks writes it. Identical visible holes of the view (one per centre) give the count: `4X Ø8 THRU ALL`, `Ø6.6 THRU` / `⌴ Ø11 ↧ 6.8`, `Ø6.6 THRU` / `⌵ Ø12 X 90°`, `Ø8 ↧ 10`. Drag it to move the leader; double-click for its text (`<HOLE>` is the reading) and precision. DXF and PDF have no ⌴ ⌵ ↧ glyphs, so they write `CBORE`, `CSK` and `X 6.8 DEEP`.
- **Ordinate / Baseline Dimension**: pick *Horizontal* or *Vertical*, click the base (an edge or a hole: its centre), then every feature to dimension; ✓ ends (one undo step). Ordinate texts (the base reads 0, the others their distance from it) stand on one line 10 mm outside the view, crowded ones jogging sideways; baseline dimensions stack 8 mm apart, nearest first.
- **Smart Dimension flyout** (lane CT; also *Tools ▸ Dimensions*), SolidWorks 2024's drawing order: Smart, **Horizontal / Vertical Dimension** (Smart Dimension's picks, the dimension held horizontal / vertical wherever it is placed: vertex to hole centre placed above-left still reads the X distance; one circle reads its width across, one arc the run between its ends, one edge its run; stored as an ordinary `h` / `v` dimension with `lock`), Baseline, Ordinate, **Horizontal / Vertical Ordinate** (the Ordinate tool with the direction preset), **Angular Running** (click the 0° line, then lines or holes round its vertex: one record reading `0°, 30°, 60°`, clockwise when the first pick is past 180°; `anvil.drawing.angularRunning(viewId, { base, refs, c?, cw?, rad? })`), Chamfer and **Path Length** (click lines and arcs end to end, then where the value goes: the total on a leader from the first edge, the sketch's rules — a line its length, an arc r × sweep, a circle 2πr; the developed outside length of a bent profile; `anvil.drawing.pathLength(viewId, { refs, lead? })`). One undo step per dimension; Esc ends each tool. API: `horizontalDim / verticalDim(viewId, pick1, pick2, at)`, `horizontalOrdinate / verticalOrdinate(viewId, spec)`.
- **Chamfer Dimension**: click the chamfer edge, then the lead-in edge: `2 X 45°` (the setback along the lead-in edge and the angle to it).
- **Dimension editing**: drag a dimension to move its line (and slide the text along it), a diameter / chamfer leader to move its elbow, an aligned dimension to change its offset. Double-click a dimension for the **Dimension PropertyManager**: *Primary Value*, *Tolerance/Precision* (None, Bilateral `+0.10/-0.05` stacked beside the value at 0.7 height, Limit `100.10` over `99.95`, Symmetric `100.00 ±0.05`; *Unit precision* 0–4 decimals), *Dimension Text* (`<DIM>` is the value: `<DIM> TYP`) and *Leaders ▸ Flip arrows* (outside the extension lines). A model item keeps these in `doc.dimProps` under a style key (`view:kind:n`) that survives part changes; a Smart Dimension keeps them on itself. A **model item drives the part**: its value is traced to the sketch dimension (circle diameter, rectangle width / height, line / slot length, arc radius) or blind extrude depth with that value whose direction the dimension measures in the view, the closest one on the sheet winning; a new *Value* writes it (through the sketch solver when the sketch has relations) and rebuilds, as one undo step. A value driven by an equation is refused with its equation named. Smart Dimensions are reference dimensions and do not drive.
- **Balloons**: *Balloon* — click an edge of a component (or of the part: a part drawing's balloon is item 1) in any view, then where the balloon goes. *Stacked Balloon* — the same, then click edges of other items to stack them (direction Right / Left / Up / Down). Drag a balloon to move it (its leader keeps its point); double-click for the **Balloon PropertyManager**: *Show quantity* puts the BOM QTY (`2X`) to its right / left / top / bottom. *Document Properties ▸ Balloons* turns quantity on for new balloons. Auto Balloon still replaces a view's balloons.
- **Magnetic lines** (rfAG; *Annotation* tab after *Auto Balloon*, *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Magnetic Line*): click where the line starts and where it ends (kept horizontal or vertical within 5°); it belongs to the view nearest its middle and moves with that view. A balloon of that view dragged within about 10 px of it snaps on (Alt: no snap) and the balloons on a line spread evenly along it (at 1/2n, 3/2n … of its length), in the order they were dropped, else in the order their leaders' points run along it, so leaders do not cross; dragged off, a balloon stays where it is put. Drag the line to take its balloons along (a view being dragged carries its lines along as it moves, rfAL); click it and press Delete to remove it (its balloons stay where they are). The line is drawn on the screen only, as in SolidWorks: SVG / DXF / PDF / DWG print the balloons where they sit and not the line. *Auto Balloon*'s *Layout* is *Square* (the default), *Top*, *Bottom*, *Left* or *Right*; a side with *Insert magnetic line(s)* (ticked by default) puts the balloons on a magnetic line 14 mm outside that side (the next Auto Balloon replaces that line unless it was dragged), without it they keep to that side. Stored with the drawing: `view.magLines: [{ id: 'ML1', a, b }]` (paper, from the view centre; `{ id, side, auto }` for an Auto Balloon row) and a balloon's `mag` / `magT`. Engine: `Drawing.addMagLine(doc, viewId, { a, b } | { side })`, `setMagLine(doc, id, { a, b } | { shift: [dx, dy] } | null, sheet?)`, `balloonMag(doc, viewId, index, id | null, t?)`, `magLineAt(sheet, p, tol, viewId?)`; `sheet.magLines = [{ id, view, a, b, side, balloons }]`. In the app `anvil.drawing.magLines()`, `magLine(viewId, spec)`, `magLineEdit(id, spec | null)`, `balloonMag(viewId, index, id | null, t)`; `autoBalloon(viewId, { layout, magnetic })`.

```js
var doc = Drawing.newDrawing(m, { options: { autoCenterMarks: true, autoCenterlines: true } });
Drawing.standard3View(doc, mesh, m.units);                                     // marks and centrelines inserted
Drawing.addCenterMark(doc, topId, { style: 'circular', holes: [{ c: [20, 0], r: 3 }, …], ext: true });
Drawing.addCenterline(doc, frontId, { a: [10, 0], b: [10, 8] });
Drawing.addHoleCallout(doc, topId, { c: [90, -50], r: 4 });                     // text worked out at render: '4X Ø8 THRU'
Drawing.holeInfo(mesh, Drawing.orientation('Top'), [0, 0], 5.5);               // { dia, thru, depth, cbore: { dia, depth }, csk: { dia, angle }, entry }
Drawing.addOrdinate(doc, frontId, { dir: 'h', base: { a: [0, 0], b: [0, 8] }, refs: [{ c: [10, -10], r: 4 }, { a: [100, 0], b: [100, 8] }] });
Drawing.addBaseline(doc, frontId, { dir: 'h', base, refs });  Drawing.addChamferDim(doc, rightId, { a, b, ref: { a, b } });
doc.dimProps = { '1:h:0': { tol: { type: 'bilateral', plus: 0.1, minus: 0.05 }, prec: 2, flip: true, at: -15 } };
Drawing.dimLabel({ kind: 'h', value: 100, prec: 2, tol: { type: 'limit', plus: 0.1, minus: 0.05 } }, 'mm').text;   // '100.10/99.95'
var sheet = Drawing.compute(doc, mesh, m);                                      // sheet.annotations = [{ id, view, type, style, text, dangling, box, info }]
var src = Drawing.dimSource(m, build, sheet.views[0], sheet.dims[0]);           // { feature, featureName, kind: 'entity' | 'depth', e, dim, value, what, expr }
Drawing.driveDim(m, src, 120);                                                  // then rebuild
Drawing.addBalloon(doc, asm, isoId, { component: id, attach: [u, v], pos: [dx, dy], stack: [{ component: id2 }], stackDir: 'down', qty: true });
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.options({ autoCenterMarks, autoCenterlines, extLines, markSize, balloonQty })`, `centerMark(viewId, spec)`, `autoCenterMarks(viewId?)`, `centerline(viewId, spec)`, `autoCenterlines(viewId?)`, `holeCallout(viewId, { c, r })`, `slotCallout(viewId, { c, r })` (rfDD: Hole Callout on a slot's end arc, or an arc slot's side arc: `.375 X 1.375 SLOT` / `R1.000 ARC SLOT .250 WIDE 60°`, following the slot's size), `deleteAnnotation(id)`, `ordinate(viewId, spec)`, `baseline(viewId, spec)`, `chamferDim(viewId, spec)`, `dimProps(i, props?)` (dimension i of `summary().dims`), `dimSource(i)`, `driveDim(i, value)` (Promise of the summary), `balloon(viewId, spec)`, `balloonProps(viewId, index, { qty, qtyPos, stackDir, pos })`; `summary()` adds `annotations`, `dimDetails` (kind, text, value, style, tol, prec, flip, dangling, box), `options`, and per balloon `index`, `stack`, `qty`, `manual`. Each call is one undo step.


### Hole Table, Revision Symbol and Cloud, Weld Symbol (rfAN)

- **Hole Table** (*Annotation ▸ Hole Table* after *Revision Table*, *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Hole Table…*): on a view with no holes it is refused with a message, no table and no undo step, as SolidWorks inserts none (rfJU; a Punch Table on a flat without forming tools keeps its empty table); the PropertyManager's *Datum* is picked on the sheet — click a vertex of a view (the origin), or an edge for the X axis and then one for the Y axis (the origin is where they meet); *Holes*: click hole edges in the same view (*Select all holes of the same size*, on by default, takes every hole of that drilled diameter, and holes of that size added to the part later join; *All holes in the view*); *Tag*: `A1, A2 … B1` (a letter per size, sizes smallest first, holes left to right) or `1, 2, 3 …`, *Combine same tags* (one tag per size, the TAG cell merged), *Combine same sizes* (the SIZE cell merged over its rows); *Hole Location Precision* (Document: the drawing's dimension precision, `.500` on an inch drawing). ✓ makes the table beside the view, clear of the other views, the title block and the tables; drag it anywhere, double-click it for its properties, right-click ▸ *Properties… / Follow the view / Delete*, Delete removes it. Columns TAG / X LOC / Y LOC / SIZE: the SIZE of a Hole Wizard hole is its callout (`Ø.281 THRU ALL`, the counterbore line under it), else the hole read from the solid as the Hole Callout reads it, a flat-pattern hole `Ø.281 THRU ALL` (a Hole Wizard feature's callout when one of its size made it). On a **flat-pattern view** the locations are flat coordinates from the picked blank corner (the laser / punch locations). The tags go up and right of each hole and X / Y datum arrows at the origin (ANNOTATIONS layer, moving with the view); the table is on the TABLES layer, in SVG, DXF R12 / R2000 and PDF. Every compute reattaches the origin and the picked holes to the part (a moved hole moves its row; a lost one is drawn dangling). Stored per sheet as `doc.holeTables` (a `SHEET_KEYS` entry), saved with the drawing.
- **Revision Symbol** (*Annotation* tab, the stack after *Geometric Tolerance*; *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Revision Symbol…*): the revision letter in a triangle (ASME Y14.35) with a leader — pick the Revision Table row (the latest by default), click the point the arrow touches, then the symbol. It keeps the row's id (`doc.revisions.rows[i].id`, given to new rows), so editing that row's REV changes the symbol; a symbol whose row is gone keeps its letter.
- **Revision Cloud** (*Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Revision Cloud*): *Rectangle* or *Ellipse* (two opposite corners), *Irregular Polygon* (click the corners, then the first one again or ✓), *Freehand* (press, draw round the change, release); *Maximum arc radius* in paper mm (3). Drawn as 150° arcs bulging outward (true ARCs in DXF), a scallop ending on every corner of a rectangle / polygon.
- **Weld Symbol** (*Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Weld Symbol…*): the PropertyManager's *Arrow Side* (below the reference line) and *Other Side* (above): *Fillet*, *Square / V / Bevel groove*, *Plug / slot*, each with its *Size* (left of the symbol) and *Length-pitch* (`2-6`, right of it); *Weld all around* (the circle at the junction), *Field weld* (the flag, toward the tail), *Tail* (the process / spec after the `<`; no tail without text). Click the joint (the arrow point), then where the reference line starts; the line runs away from the arrow, the fillet's upright leg on the left (AWS A2.4). Double-click a symbol for its PropertyManager.

Revision symbols, clouds and weld symbols are entries of `doc.symbols` like the other shop symbols (drag, Delete, leader tips, follow their view):

```js
var ht = Drawing.addHoleTable(doc, flatId, { origin: { p: [u, v] } | { x: { a, b }, y: { a, b } }, holes: [{ c, r }], sizes: [0.281], all, tagStyle: 'alpha' | 'numeric', combineTags, combineSizes, prec, x, y });
Drawing.editHoleTable(doc, ht.id, { combineSizes: true, x: null /* beside the view again */ }); Drawing.moveHoleTable(doc, id, x, y); Drawing.deleteHoleTable(doc, id);
sheet.holeTables;   // [{ id, view, box, origin, rows: [{ tag, x, y, xText, yText, size, lines, c, r, dangling }] }]
Drawing.viewHoles(sheetView, mesh);   Drawing.holeTableAt(sheet, p);
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'revision', p, leader });   // rev: the latest revision, row: its row id
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'revCloud', shape: 'rect' | 'ellipse', a, b } | { type: 'revCloud', shape: 'polygon' | 'freehand', points, arc: 3 });
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'weld', p, leader, arrow: { type: 'fillet', size: '1/4', length: '2-6' }, other: { type: 'v', size: '1/8' }, allAround: true, field: true, tail: 'GMAW' });
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.holeTable(viewId, spec)` (its id), `holeTableEdit(id, props | null)`, `holeTables()`; the symbols through `anvil.drawing.symbol({ type: 'weld' | 'revision' | 'revCloud', … })`.

**Punch Table** (rfJG; *Annotation ▸ Punch Table* after *Bend Table*, *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Punch Table…*): on a flat-pattern view, one row per Forming Tool (dimple, louver, extruded hole, lance, rib), tagged `A1, A2 … B1` by punch ID (or `1, 2, 3 …`), X LOC / Y LOC of the tool's centre from a vertex of the blank, PUNCH ID = the tool's flat note (`DIMPLE Ø.75 X .125 UP`). The PropertyManager starts with the selected (or only) flat view's lower-left blank corner as the origin; click another vertex to move it, ✓ places the table. It is a hole table record with `punch: true` (same drag / properties / Delete / outputs); refused on a view that is not a flat pattern.

```js
Drawing.addHoleTable(doc, flatId, { punch: true, origin: { p: [u, v] }, tagStyle, combineTags, combineSizes, prec }, sheetView);
Drawing.viewPunches(sheetView);   // [{ form, c, r, id }]
anvil.drawing.punchTable(viewId, { origin? , … });   // its id (origin default: the blank's lower-left corner)
```

A **Bend Table** on a flat view also adds SolidWorks' `Fixed Face` note at the view centre (moved off the blank's lines, holes and form marks; flat item `fixedFace`: drag / hide it like a bend note; `view.fixedFaceNote = false` leaves it off).

**Autodimension on a flat pattern view** (rfJM; *Tools ▸ Dimensions ▸ Autodimension* with the flat view selected, or click it): *Entities to Dimension* All entities in view / Selected entities (click edges, holes and bend lines; again to drop one), *Bend lines (flat pattern)*, and per direction *Scheme* Baseline / Chain / Ordinate (Ordinate is preselected on a flat), *Origin* Left / Right and Bottom / Top edge, *Placement* Above / Below and Right / Left. On the 6 × 4 blank: `0, .750, 1.500, 3.000, 4.500, 6.000` across (hole, bend, holes, bend, edge) and `0, 1.000 … 4.000` up. Bend-line references follow their bend (by its key) when the part changes. A side whose dimensions would run onto the title block, a Bend / Hole / Punch Table, the BOM, another view or off the sheet swaps to the other side; when neither side is clear the flat view slides (2 mm steps, up to 40 mm) until one is, and the status bar says so.

```js
Drawing.flatAutodimension(doc, flatId, sheetView, sheet, { hScheme: 'ordinate', vScheme: 'ordinate', hOrigin: 'left', vOrigin: 'bottom', hPlace: 'above', vPlace: 'right', bends: true, select: [{ c, r } | { a, b }], h: true, v: true, keepPlace: false });   // { n, h, v, moved }
Drawing.autodimension(doc, viewId, proj, { h: { scheme, origin, place }, v: { … }, bends, select, scale, units });   // any orthographic view
```

With *Merge faces* off (Flat-Pattern1, `p.flatPattern({ mergeFaces: false })`) the flat view also draws the two tangent lines of each bend region (`flat.regionLines`) as thin tangent edges on the view's layer (phantom with Tangent Edges With Font), turned with the flat; SVG / DXF / PDF carry them. Merge faces on (the default) draws none.

### Cosmetic Thread, Dowel Pin Symbol, Datum Target, Multi-jog Leader, note borders and lists (rfCU)

All on the Annotation tab (Multi-jog Leader after Note; Datum Target / Cosmetic Thread / Dowel Pin Symbol in the flyout after the weld symbols) and in *Insert ▸ Annotations*. Each opens its PropertyManager; Esc or ✓ ends, every placement is one undo step, double-click edits, Delete removes.

- **Cosmetic Thread**: click a hole's circle in a view (a circle that is not a hole, such as a pin's end, takes an external thread, class 2A, sized by its major diameter). *Standard* ANSI Inch, *Size* (`#2-56 UNC` … `1-12 UNF`; *From the hole* picks the thread whose tap drill the hole is), *End Condition* Through / Blind with *Depth*, *Insert callout*, callout text (`<THREAD>` is the standard text). Drawn as a thin 3/4 circle at the major diameter (a shaft picked at its major diameter: at the minor one, class 2A) with the callout `#10-32 UNF - 2B THRU` / `#10-32 UNF - 2B ↧ .375` on a leader (drag it). It follows the hole as the part changes (dangling when the hole is gone) and is written to SVG / DXF / PDF.
- **Dowel Pin Symbol**: click a hole's circle: the circle, a cross and two opposite quadrants filled, at the hole's size.
- **Datum Target**: click the target point, then where the symbol goes: a circle split across, the size above (the area's, or typed), `A1` below; a point target ends in an X, a circular / rectangular area is drawn in phantom at its view's scale with a dot. The number counts up per datum.
- **Multi-jog Leader**: click the start (on a note: the leader hangs from the note's nearer side and follows it), each bend point, then double-click the end (or ✓ / Enter: the last click is the end). Arrow / Dot / None. Drag a bend point to move it; drag a segment to move the whole leader.
- **Note ▸ Properties…** (right-click a note): *Border* None / Circle / Triangle / Box / Diamond / Hexagon / Pentagon / Flag Triangle / Flag Pentagon / Underline (rfJI added Box's name, Diamond, Pentagon, Flag Pentagon, Underline), *Add to Flag Note Bank* (rfJI), *List* None / Numbered (`1.`, `2.` … on a hanging indent) / Bulleted.
- **Flag notes** (rfJI; *Annotation ▸ Flag Note*, *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Flag Note…*): a note with *Add to Flag Note Bank* on is the drawing's numbered notes block (`NOTES: 1. DEBURR ALL EDGES …`). The Flag Note PropertyManager lists the bank's items; pick one, the *Border* shape (Flag Triangle by default) and *Leader*; with a leader click the feature, then where the flag goes, without one click where it goes; the tool stays on for the next flag. A flag shows its item's number and follows the item (lines inserted above renumber it; a changed text keeps the number; a deleted bank leaves it dangling). The bank draws each flagged item's number in the flag's shape. SVG / DXF / PDF like any note.
- **View ▸ Hide/Show** (rfJI): *All Annotations* (dimensions, notes, symbols, balloons, centre marks, bend notes; tables and the title block stay) and *Sketches* (the drawing sketch) toggle what the drawing shows, saved with it (`doc.display`), one undo step each; the outputs follow the sheet. *Hide/Show Edges* and *Hide/Show Annotations* start the per-item tools.

```js
Drawing.addCosmeticThread(doc, viewId, { c, r, size: '#10-32 UNF', end: 'blind', depth: 0.375, callout: true });
Drawing.addDowelPin(doc, viewId, { c, r });
Drawing.editAnnotation(doc, id, { end: 'through' });
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'datumTarget', p, leader, datum: 'A', area: 'circle', aw: 0.5 });   // num: next for A
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'leader', p, jogs: [[x, y], [x, y]], leader: tip, end: 'arrow', note: noteId });
Drawing.addNote(doc, { p, text: 'DEBURR\nBREAK EDGES', border: 'flag', list: 'number' });
var bank = Drawing.addNote(doc, { p, text: 'DEBURR ALL EDGES\nPAINT BLACK', bank: true });   // rfJI: a Flag Note Bank (numbered)
Drawing.addFlagNote(doc, { bank: bank.id, item: 2, p, leader: tip, border: 'flag' });   // { text: '2', flag: { bank, item, key } }
Drawing.resolveFlagNotes(doc);   // { byNote: { id: { item, dangling } }, banks: { bankId: { itemIndex: border } } }
Drawing.setDisplay(doc, { annotations: false, sketches: false });   // View ▸ Hide/Show; Drawing.displayOf(doc)
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.cosmeticThread(viewId, spec)`, `dowelPin(viewId, spec)`, `editAnnotation(id, props)`, `annotations()`, `datumTarget(spec)`, `leader(spec)`; `note()` / `editNote()` take `border` and `list`.

### Drawing sketch, Line Format, Hide / Show Edges, Relative View (rfAO)

The drawing's **Sketch** tab (`drawing-ui-sketch.js`; engine in `drawing-views.js`) draws Line (chained), Centerline, Corner Rectangle (four lines held Coincident / Horizontal / Vertical), Circle, Centerpoint Arc, Spline, Point and Text on the sheet, or in a view when the first click is inside it (the entity is stored relative to the view and moves with it). While drawing, an end within 8 px of another sketch point snaps there and is held Coincident; a line within 4° of level or plumb gets Horizontal / Vertical. Click selects (Ctrl adds), drag moves an entity or the end under the pointer (what is Coincident follows, H / V hold), Delete removes it with its relations, double-click opens its Properties (relations, Add Relations, Length / Radius, Line Format; a sketch dimension's Modify value). Smart Dimension on a sketch line / circle / arc adds a driving length / Ø / R (in a view it reads model units: paper ÷ view scale). **Line Format** sets style (solid, hidden, center, phantom), thickness (thin, normal, thick) and colour of selected sketch entities, or of one view edge (right-click the edge ▸ Line Format…). **Hide/Show Edges**: right-click a view edge ▸ Hide Edge, the view's Show Hidden Edges, or the Sketch tab tool (click to hide, hidden edges show grey, click to show). Every output carries the sketch on the `SKETCH` layer (SVG group, PDF, DXF layer with HIDDEN / CENTER / PHANTOM line types). **Relative View** (View Layout, Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Relative To Model): pick the face that faces you, then the second face in any view, choose Right / Left / Top / Bottom, OK, place; an oblique result gets Dimension Type True.

```js
var l = Drawing.addSketchEntity(doc, { t: 'line', a: [1, 1], b: [3, 1], rel: 'H' });   // sheet coordinates; a 3rd argument: a view id
Drawing.addSketchEntity(doc, { t: 'circle', c: [5, 5], r: 0.5, style: 'hidden', weight: 'thin', color: '#ff0000' });
Drawing.addSketchEntity(doc, { t: 'arc', c, r, a0: 0, a1: 90 }); Drawing.addSketchEntity(doc, { t: 'spline', pts }); { t: 'point', p } | { t: 'text', p, text, h } | { t: 'line', a, b, cons: true }
var ids = Drawing.addSketchRect(doc, [1, 1], [3, 2], viewId?);                 // 4 lines, 4 Coincident, 2 H, 2 V
Drawing.addSketchRelation(doc, 'coincident', [{ id, end: 'b' }, { id: id2, end: 'a' }]);   // ends: a / b, c, start / end, p
Drawing.moveSketchEntity(doc, id, dx, dy, end?); Drawing.editSketchEntity(doc, id, { b: [4, 1] }); Drawing.deleteSketchEntity(doc, id);
var d = Drawing.addSketchDim(doc, l.id); Drawing.setSketchDim(doc, d.id, 2.5);   // driving length / Ø / R
Drawing.setLineFormat(doc, [id], { style: 'phantom', weight: 'thick', color: '#0000ff' });   // null clears a field
Drawing.hideEdge(doc, viewId, Drawing.edgeKey({ la, lb })); Drawing.showEdges(doc, viewId); Drawing.setEdgeFormat(doc, viewId, key, { style: 'center' });
Drawing.addRelativeView(doc, mesh, { face1, face2, second: 'Top', x, y });   // or { n1, n2 } normals; Drawing.relativeFrame(n1, n2, second); Drawing.faceAt(mesh, frame, [u, v])
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.sketch.add(spec, viewId?)`, `rect(a, b, viewId?)`, `list(viewId?)`, `get(id)`, `move(id, dx, dy, end?)`, `remove(id)`, `relate(kind, refs)`, `dim(id)`, `setDim(id, value)`, `lineFormat(ids, fmt)`, `hideEdge(viewId, key, hide?)`, `edgeFormat(viewId, key, fmt)`, `showEdges(viewId)`, `relativeView(opts)`, `tool(name)`; each is one undo step. Primitives carry `lw` (mm) and `rgb` for a writer that honours a custom thickness or colour; today they are drawn with the nearest sheet style (thin 0.25 / visible 0.5 / thick 0.7, hidden, center, phantom).

### Drawings of assemblies

*File ▸ Make Drawing from Assembly* (the same menu slot as *Make Drawing from Part*, in an assembly document) opens a drawing of the assembly: every view — Standard 3 View, Model, Projected, Section, Detail — shows the components in their solved positions, and hidden lines are removed across all of them (a pin behind the cap is dashed, the hole under the cap is a hidden circle). Edges are kept per component, so a pin standing in a hole keeps its facet lines smooth and a hidden edge lying under another component's visible edge is dropped. Assembly drawings start without model dimensions (*Model Items* adds them).

- **Section views** cut every part — each part inside a sub-assembly and each body of a multibody part on its own — and hatch each cut face in its own style, as SolidWorks does: the styles run 45°, 135°, 30°, 120°, 60°, 150°, then the same angles 1.6× wider apart, and so on (`Drawing.hatchStyle(i)`), taken in tree order, except that a part whose cut face touches (or nearly touches) one already hatched takes the style furthest in angle from its neighbours', preferring one no other part uses: a second pin in a plate is 135° wider apart, not 30° beside the plate's 45° (`Drawing.hatchAssign(caps)`; the summary's `hatches` name the part, e.g. `Hinge<1>/Leaf<1>`). Hatch lines end on the drawn round edges, not on the facets' chords (`Drawing.snapHatch`).
- **Bill of Materials** (*Drawing* tab ▸ *Bill of Materials*, *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Bill of Materials…*; inserted by *Make Drawing from Assembly*): columns ITEM NO., PART NUMBER, DESCRIPTION, QTY.; one row per distinct part — components with the same source (`{ example }`, `{ script }` or `{ model }`) count together — numbered in the order they appear in the tree. PART NUMBER is the part's name; DESCRIPTION is the part model's `description`, else the Design Library entry's name, else the part's size in its own x × y × z (`8 x 20 x 8`). The table is anchored to a corner of the sheet (*Top Right* by default, *Top Left*, *Bottom Right* above the title block, *Bottom Left*); dragging it detaches it, the PropertyManager re-anchors it; Delete removes it. Standard 3 View leaves the table clear (the scale drops a step if it must).
- **Auto Balloon** (*Drawing* tab, *Insert ▸ Annotations*, view right-click menu): on the selected view (default the isometric) one balloon per BOM item that has an edge in the view (*Ignore multiple instances*, the default; unchecked, one per component). A balloon's leader ends with an arrow on a visible edge of its component — the one farthest from the view centre — and the balloons sit on a rectangle 14 mm outside the view, spread apart. Balloons store the component, not the number: the number is looked up in the BOM at every render, so removing or reordering components renumbers them and a new instance of a part only raises its QTY. Click a balloon and press Delete to remove it; the view's *Balloons* row in the tree has *Delete Balloons*.
- **Title block** (A3 / ANSI B, 180 × 40 mm): TITLE, PART NO., REV / DWG. NO., MATERIAL, SCALE, UNITS / DATE, MASS, PROJECTION, SHEET. Each field is a text with links, as in a SolidWorks sheet format: `$PRPSHEET:"Description"` (TITLE, upper case), `$PRPSHEET:"Part Number"`, `$PRPSHEET:"Revision"`, `$PRPSHEET:"Material"`, `$PRPSHEET:"Mass"` read the custom properties of the part or assembly on the sheet (Configuration Specific first); MATERIAL and MASS fall back to the part's material and mass, TITLE and PART NO. to its name. `$PRP:"SW-File Name"`, `"SW-Sheet Name"`, `"SW-Sheet Scale"`, `"SW-Units"`, `"SW-Date"`, `"SW-Projection"`, `"SW-Sheet"` are the drawing's own. *Sheet Properties* lists every field's text for editing (one undo step with the sheet change), and a double-click on a cell edits it in place; `doc.titleBlock = { key: text }` holds the edited ones. Assemblies show the assembly's properties and its total mass. Left of it the sheet format blocks (COMPANY, DRAWN, CHECKED, general tolerances) and, with a Revision Table, a REV from its latest row: see *Sheet-metal drawings* below (rfM).
- **BOM columns and type** (part and assembly drawings; a part drawing gets a BOM from *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Bill of Materials…* or the ribbon): the PropertyManager has *BOM Type* (*Top-level only*, *Indented*, *Parts only*), *Columns* (a checkbox per column to show or hide it) and *Add Column* (a property: Material, Mass, Vendor, Revision, Finish or any custom property of the parts, with an optional title). `doc.bom.columns = [{ type: 'item' | 'partNumber' | 'description' | 'qty' | 'property', prop, title, width, hidden }]` (a plain string is a property column); `doc.bom.type = 'top' | 'indented' | 'parts'` (for assemblies with sub-assemblies, *Indented* and *Parts only* come from `Assembly.bom`). A property column shows each part's own property (its Configuration Specific value first), with Material and Mass falling back to the part's material and mass; PART NUMBER and DESCRIPTION use the *Part Number* and *Description* properties when they are set. *Parts only* lists the solid bodies of a multi-body part as rows of their own (PART NUMBER `Part - Boss-Extrude2`, DESCRIPTION the body's size, its own material and mass; identical bodies counted together, as a cut list), and *Top-level only* lists the part; in an assembly *Parts only* also flattens the sub-assemblies. Balloons keep pointing at the first row of their part.
- **Exports**: the BOM is drawn as lines and text on a `BOM` layer (DXF colour 7), balloons as circles, leaders, arrows and text on `BALLOONS` (colour 3); SVG `<g data-layer="BOM">` / `"BALLOONS"`; the PDF carries the table text.

```js
var m = Assembly.newAssembly(); Assembly.runScript(m, code);
var mesh = Kernel.meshFromBody(Assembly.rebuild(m).body);          // one body per component, solved
var doc = Drawing.newDrawing(m, { bomAnchor: 'top-right' });       // bom: false leaves the table out
Drawing.standard3View(doc, mesh, m.units);
Drawing.autoBalloon(doc, m, isoViewId, { ignoreMultiple: true });   // layout: 'top' | 'bottom' | 'left' | 'right', magnetic: true (rfAG)
var sheet = Drawing.compute(doc, mesh, m);                         // sheet.bom = { rows, box }, sheet.balloons = [{ view, item, component, c, attach }]
Drawing.bomRows(m, mesh);                                          // [{ item, partNumber, description, qty, components: [id…], indices }]
doc.bom.columns = [{ type: 'item' }, { type: 'partNumber' }, 'Material', 'Mass', { type: 'qty' }]; doc.bom.type = 'parts';
Drawing.compute(doc, mesh, m, { build: asmBuild }).bom.cells;       // [[1, 'Plate', 'AISI 304', '371.46 g', 1], …]
Drawing.titleBlock(doc, m, { build: asmBuild });                    // { title, partNo, rev, dwgNo, material, scale, units, date, mass, projection, sheet }
```

In the app: `anvil.drawing.bomColumns()` / `bomColumns(list)`, `anvil.drawing.bomType('top' | 'indented' | 'parts')`, `anvil.drawing.titleBlock()` / `titleBlock({ key: text | null })` (part and assembly drawings; `summary()` adds `titleBlock` and `bom.type`, `bom.columns`, `bom.cells`). `anvil.drawing.make()` in an assembly, `anvil.drawing.autoBalloon(viewId?, { ignoreMultiple })`, `anvil.drawing.bom()` (`{ anchor, box, rows }`), `anvil.drawing.bom('bottom-left' | { x, y } | null)` (anchor, place, remove), `anvil.drawing.deleteBalloons(viewId)`; `summary()` adds `bom: { anchor, box, rows }`, `balloons: [{ view, item, component, c, attach }]` and per view `hatches: [{ body, component, angle, lines }]`, `balloons: [item…]`.

### Sheet-metal drawings: flat patterns, bend notes, notes

- **Flat-pattern view.** On a sheet-metal part (a Base Flange), *Model View* opens on **Flat pattern** (*Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Flat Pattern…* presets it): OK, then click where it goes. The view (`kind: 'flat'`, layer `FLAT_PATTERN`) draws the blank `Kernel.flatPattern` unfolds, seen from the +n side of the Base Flange's sketch plane in that plane's frame (UP bends come toward the viewer), at true size whatever plane the base was sketched on. The kernel's flat comes as polygons; `Drawing.flatCurves(loops, diag)` turns them back into true curves for display, dimensioning and export: a loop whose vertices lie on one circle (tolerance r · 2e-4; a stray vertex or two left by the blank's CSG merge is ignored) is a circle, runs of 3 or more chords on one circle turning the same way under 30° each are arcs (slot ends, rounded corners; an arc the loop's seam split is joined), the rest lines; collinear split vertices are dropped. The flat is worked out on the main thread and cached on the mesh (`Drawing.flatData(mesh, model, build)`; with OpenCascade's mesh on screen the build's own mesh is used). Holes get centre marks (slots a linear mark), Smart Dimension and Hole Callout work on the flat (`4X Ø.281 THRU ALL`: a flat's holes all go through, counted over the flat; THRU ALL from a Through All cut); projected, section and detail views of a flat are refused.
- **Bend lines** run along each bend's centre line 1.5 mm past the outline at both ends: UP on layer `BEND_UP` (thin solid, DXF colour 3), DOWN on `BEND_DOWN` (thin dashed, DXF colour 1, linetype `HIDDEN`), as SolidWorks' *Bend Lines - Up / Down* line fonts.
- **Bend notes** (`BEND_NOTES`) read direction, angle and inside radius: `UP 90° R.030` in an inch part (the shop format of `Kernel.bendNoteText`; ANSI, no leading zero; SolidWorks writes `UP 90.00° R .06`), `DOWN 90° R0.76` in mm; the Bend Table's columns are TAG / DIRECTION / ANGLE / INNER RADIUS (plain value, no R) and BEND DED. (BEND ALLOW. and K-FACTOR on request) (`Drawing.bendNoteText(bend, units)`); each is centred on its bend line just beside it, upright along it, and follows the part on every rebuild. Drag one to move it (kept as `view.flatAnn[key].off`, key `b0`, `b1` …), Delete hides it (view *Properties ▸ Show hidden bend notes again*). *Bend notes ▸ Bend Table* (view right-click menu, view PropertyManager, Model View or Document Properties) puts tags A, B, C on the lines and a table TAG / DIRECTION / ANGLE / INNER RADIUS (the plain value, `.030`, as SolidWorks' *bend table--standard*) under the view (key `table`); *None* leaves them off.
- **Flat dimensions**: the blank's overall width and height go on every flat view when *Document Properties ▸ Sheet Metal ▸ Overall dimensions* is on (`doc.options.flatDims`, off by default: SolidWorks inserts no dimensions on a flat-pattern view by itself); they follow the part, keep their Dimension PropertyManager settings and do not drive the part. *FLAT SIZE note* (`flatSizeNote`, or per view) writes `FLAT SIZE 6.177 × 2.000` under the view on `NOTES` (key `size`).
- **Inch ANSI dimensions.** In an inch drawing every dimension, tolerance, hole callout and note value reads ANSI decimal inch: three places by default with trailing zeros kept and no zero before the point (`4.000`, `.030`, `Ø.281`, `+.005/-.002`); *Document Properties ▸ Units* sets the decimal places (`doc.options.dimPrec`, also for mm) and, in inches, *Fractions* (`fractions`, finest `fracDenom` 8–64: `2 1/4`, `9/32`). Millimetres keep their leading zero and drop trailing zeros (`0.76`, `2`). `Drawing.fmtDim`, `inchText`, `fracText`, `dimStyle(doc)`.
- **Thread sizes in hole callouts.** A hole feature that names a thread (`size` on a tapped hole, or a name that reads as one: `1/4-20 UNC`, `#10-32 UNF`) and drills the called-out diameter adds its name as a callout line, unless the callout already has it (`Drawing.holeSizeNames(model)`; the diameter from `HoleWizard.spec` when it knows the size, else the feature's `dia` / `diameter` / `drill`).
- **Note** (*Annotation ▸ Note*, *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Note*): click on the sheet, type (Enter starts a new line), ✓ / Ctrl+Enter or a click elsewhere places it, Esc drops it; the tool stays on until Esc. Double-click a note to edit it in place (emptied, it is deleted), drag it to move it, Delete removes it; notes are listed under *Annotations* in the drawing FeatureManager. `doc.notes = [{ id, p: [x, y] (paper, first line's baseline), text, h }]`, 3.5 mm text, lines 1.6 × apart; layer `NOTES` in SVG, DXF (one `TEXT` per line) and PDF.

```js
var doc = Drawing.newDrawing(m, { options: { flatSizeNote: true, bendNotes: 'notes' } });
var v = Drawing.addFlatView(doc, mesh, { model: m, build: b, x: 150, y: 120, bendNotes: 'table' });   // { scale, fit }
var sheet = Drawing.compute(doc, mesh, m, { build: b });   // annotations: bendNote / bendTag / bendTable / flatSize (flat: true)
Drawing.flatAnnotation(doc, v.id, 'b0', { off: [5, 0] });  // move a bend note; { hidden: true } hides it
// a bend note's settings go under its bend's stable key: 'bend:<feature id>:<n>' (Kernel.flatPattern gives every bend
// its id: the flange / Sketched Bend / Jog / Insert Bends feature and the record's place in build.flanges[feature],
// also for a flange on a wall and a bend an open Unfold laid flat) or, for a drawing saved before with no source,
// 'bend@<u>,<v>,<U|D><angle>,R<radius>' (its place, re-found nearest after a change); 'b0' … after the view has been
// computed name the bends in order and become their keys. A dragged note stays with its bend when a rebuild reorders
// or changes the bends. Drawing.bendKey(bend), Drawing.bendKeyMatch(bends, view.flatAnn, diag).
// A Cross-Break's note ('UP 1° R .060', the bend notes' format) sits beside the crossing of its X, level, clear of
// its lines and the bend notes: annotation type crossBreakNote, key 'cb:<feature id>' (drag / hide as a bend note).
Drawing.addNote(doc, { p: [20, 30], text: 'NOTES:\n1. BREAK ALL EDGES' }); Drawing.editNote(doc, id, { text, p }); Drawing.deleteNote(doc, id);
```

- **Note links and leaders** (rfM): a note's `$PRP:"name"` (the drawing's custom properties) and `$PRPSHEET:"name"` (the part's) are filled in on the sheet as in a title block field (the note keeps its raw text: `sheet.notes[i].raw`); `leader: [x, y]` (paper) draws a leader from the nearer side of the first line, with a 4 mm shoulder, to an arrow at that point. Right-click a note: *Add Leader* / *Remove Leader*; drag the arrow tip to move it.
- **Revision Table** (*Annotation ▸ Revision Table*, *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Revision Table*; rfCQ: on the sheet it is inserted on — another sheet shows none unless one is inserted there too, and a drawing saved before keeps its table on the sheet that was active; a sheet without a table still shows the drawing's latest REV, and a table new on a sheet goes on from it): a REVISIONS title row and ZONE / REV / DESCRIPTION / DATE / APPROVED, anchored to the top-right corner of the border (a BOM anchored there goes under it; Standard 3 View keeps the views clear of it). *Add Revision* (right-click the table, the ribbon button once the table is there, *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Add Revision…*) opens a PropertyManager with the next letter — A, B, C … skipping I, O, Q, S, X, Z (ASME Y14.35 / SolidWorks), then AA, AB … — and today's date; a double-click on a row edits it; Delete removes the table. The title block REV shows the latest revision (unless the REV cell was edited), and `$PRP:"Revision"` reads it. Layer `REVISIONS` (DXF colour 7) in SVG, DXF R12 / R2000 and PDF.
- **Surface Finish, Datum Feature, Geometric Tolerance** (*Annotation* tab, *Insert ▸ Annotations*): each opens its PropertyManager; with *Leader* on, click the point the arrow touches, then where the symbol goes (the tool stays on until ✓ / Esc). *Surface Finish*: Basic / Machining Required (bar) / Machining Prohibited (circle), the maximum roughness (Ra, `63`) above the short leg and a production method on the extension bar (ASME Y14.36). *Datum Feature*: a framed letter whose leader ends in a filled datum triangle; letters run A, B, C … skipping I, O, Q. *Geometric Tolerance*: a feature control frame — the characteristic (Position, Flatness, Straightness, Circularity, Cylindricity, Profile of a Line / Surface, Perpendicularity, Parallelism, Angularity, Concentricity, Symmetry, Circular / Total Runout), Ø, the tolerance, Ⓜ / Ⓛ, and up to three datums (`B(M)` puts Ⓜ after B). Every symbol is drawn as lines, circles, arcs, fills and text on the `SYMBOLS` layer, so SVG, DXF and PDF carry it alike. Double-click for its PropertyManager, drag to move (the leader tip stays), drag the arrow tip, Delete removes; they are listed under *Annotations* in the drawing FeatureManager.
- **Sheet format blocks**: left of the title block, COMPANY (`$PRP:"CompanyName"`), DRAWN / DATE (`"DrawnBy"`, `"DrawnDate"`), CHECKED / DATE (`"CheckedBy"`, `"CheckedDate"`) and the general tolerances block (UNLESS OTHERWISE SPECIFIED; `$PRP:"GeneralTolerances"`, by default `DIMENSIONS ARE IN INCHES / TOLERANCES: / .XX ±.01 / .XXX ±.005 / ANGLES ±1°`, in mm `X.X ±0.2 / X.XX ±0.1`). *File ▸ Properties…* in a drawing edits those properties (`doc.properties`); every cell also edits in place by double-click like the title block's. A sheet narrower than the blocks need leaves out the tolerance block first (A4), then the rest.
- **Unilateral tolerances**: a bilateral tolerance with a zero limit writes the zero at the other limit's precision, `+.003` over `-.000` in inches (ASME Y14.5), a single `0` in mm.

```js
Drawing.addRevision(doc, { description: 'INITIAL RELEASE', approved: 'LH' });    // { zone, rev: 'A', description, date, approved }
Drawing.nextRev('H');  // 'J'      Drawing.revBox(doc, units); Drawing.editRevision(doc, i, props); Drawing.deleteRevisionTable(doc);
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'gtol', p: [x, y], leader: [x2, y2], char: 'position', dia: true, tol: '.010', mod: 'M', datums: ['A', 'B', 'C'] });
Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'datum', p, leader });  Drawing.addSymbol(doc, { type: 'surfaceFinish', p, symbol: 'machined', ra: '63', method: 'GRIND' });
Drawing.addNote(doc, { p: [20, 30], text: 'FINISH: $PRPSHEET:"Finish"', leader: [10, 20] });
doc.properties = { DrawnBy: 'LH', CompanyName: 'Acme' };   // the sheet format's $PRP links; Drawing.titleBlock(doc, m).drawnBy
```

In the app (rfM): `anvil.drawing.revisionTable({ anchor })` / `revisionTable(null)`, `addRevision(spec)`, `editRevision(i, props)`, `deleteRevision(i)`, `symbol(spec)`, `editSymbol(id, props)`, `deleteSymbol(id)`, `symbols()`, `properties({ name: value | null })`; `summary()` adds `revisions: { rows, latest, anchor, box }`, `symbols` and per note `raw` and `leader`.
