- **Empty View** (*Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Empty*, click where it goes): a view with no model, to sketch and annotate in; its sketch moves with it, nothing projects from it (`Drawing.addEmptyView(doc, { x, y, scale, name })`, `kind: 'empty'`, layer `EMPTY_VIEW`; `anvil.drawing.emptyView(opts)`).
- **Hide/Show Annotations** (*View ▸ Hide/Show Annotations*): click dimensions, notes, symbols, balloons, centre marks / callouts or flat-view notes to hide them; while the tool runs hidden ones show grey (`#a8a8a8`) and a click shows one again; Esc ends. Hidden ones are left off the sheet and every export. Saved on the records (`hidden: true`; a model item's key in `doc.hiddenAnn`; a flat note's `view.flatAnn[key].hidden`). `Drawing.setAnnotationHidden(doc, ref, hidden)` with ref `{ kind: 'dim', key } | { kind: 'dim', view, index } | { kind: 'annot', id } | { kind: 'flat', view, key } | { kind: 'note' | 'symbol', id } | { kind: 'balloon', view, index }`; `compute(…, { showHidden: true })` draws them grey; `anvil.drawing.hideAnnotation(ref, hidden)`, `anvil.drawing.hideShowAnnotations()` (toggles the tool).

In the app: `anvil.drawing.flatView({ x, y, scale, bendNotes })` (rfJX: at the sheet scale or `scale`, as SolidWorks inserts a flat pattern view; where this sheet has no clear spot at that scale it goes to a new sheet at the same scale, shrinking there only when the blank cannot fit an empty sheet; the engine's `addFlatView(..., { fit: true, newSheet: false })` keeps the old half-scale try on this sheet), `flatViewProps(id, { bendNotes, flatSize })`, `flatAnnotation(viewId, key, { off, hidden })`, `note({ p, text })`, `editNote(id, props)`, `deleteNote(id)`, `notes()`, `options({ flatDims, flatSizeNote, bendNotes, dimPrec, fractions, fracDenom })`; `summary()` adds `notes` and per flat view `flat: { size, bendNotes, bends: [{ id, key, up, angle, R, a, b, note }], circles, arcs, lines, annotations }`.

## Exports

- **STEP AP214**, **3MF** and **OBJ** (+ `.mtl`) of a part or an assembly: *File ▸ Save As* (Save as type) or *File ▸ Export STEP AP214… / 3MF… / OBJ…* (see *Import and export of neutral formats*).
- **STL** (ASCII) of the whole body.
- **DXF** of a sketch (LINE, CIRCLE, ARC, POLYLINE entities), of a selected planar face, or of the sheet-metal flat pattern, with the shop's `OUTLINE` / `HOLES` / `BEND` / `NOTES` layer table and `$INSUNITS` / `$MEASUREMENT` set from the document units (see *Laser-ready DXF* below).
- **Script** (`*.anvil.js`) and **JSON** (`*.anvil.json`) of the model. Both reopen through *File ▸ Open*.
- **PNG** screenshot of the viewport.
- **Drawing** of the part or assembly as SVG, DXF (one layer per view, plus `BOM` and `BALLOONS` for assemblies) or PDF (see *Drawings*).
- **DWG** of anything Anvil writes as DXF (a face, the flat pattern, a sketch, a drawing). (lane DG) Anvil writes DWG itself: `dwg-writer.js` (global `DWG`, plain JavaScript, no server, so the published copy saves DWG too) reads the R2000 DXF and encodes an **AutoCAD 2000 (AC1015)** DWG per the Open Design Alliance specification: header variables (INSUNITS, extents, LTSCALE, the dimension style), the tables (LTYPE with HIDDEN / PHANTOM / CENTER dashes, LAYER colours / line types / weights, STYLE, APPID, DIMSTYLE, VPORT *Active, BLOCK_RECORD), the Model / Layout1 layouts and dictionaries, and LINE, ARC, CIRCLE, POINT, ELLIPSE, SPLINE, LWPOLYLINE (bulges), TEXT, MTEXT, INSERT, SOLID, HATCH and DIMENSION (with its *D block); every CRC and the object map are its own. `DWG.fromDXF(text)` → `Uint8Array`; `node anvil/headless/anvil.js dwg in.dxf out.dwg`; the runner's `--dwg*` options use it too (`converter: 'anvil'`). Checked with LibreDWG: `dwgread` reads the flat, sketch and drawing samples with no error and `dwg2dxf` gives back the same entities, coordinates (exact), layers, line types and units. Only when the writer throws (an entity it does not write) does the DXF go to an external converter on the machine running `serve.ps1` (or the Node runner), never to the internet. The converter order is fixed: the **ODA File Converter** (Open Design Alliance, free, installed by hand from opendesign.com under `C:\Program Files\ODA\ODAFileConverter*\`, or `$env:ANVIL_ODA_CONVERTER`) writes an ACAD2018 DWG that other CAD software opens cleanly; without it, **LibreDWG**'s `dxf2dwg` (`vendor/libredwg/dxf2dwg.exe`, fetched by `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File vendor\get-libredwg.ps1` — the official 0.14 win64 release, SHA-256 checked, ~35 MB, not in git; or `$env:ANVIL_LIBREDWG`) writes an R2000 DWG. Every reply says which one did the work. Trust note: the DXF is the file for CAM and for anything machine-read, and it needs no converter; a DWG from ODA is compliant; LibreDWG's DWG *writer* is experimental (its own `dwgread` reads the result back with every entity, other software may not), so use it for a quick look, not for delivery. The server contract, which the *DXF/DWG Output* PropertyManager uses: `HEAD /api/dxf2dwg` → 200 with `X-Anvil-Converter: oda | libredwg`, or 503 when there is none; `POST /api/dxf2dwg` with the DXF text as the body → 200 `application/acad` with the DWG bytes, the same header and `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="output.dwg"`, 400 JSON `{ error, converter }` when the converter fails (its first stderr lines), 503 JSON `{ error, hint }` without a converter; 30 s limit per conversion, temp files deleted afterwards.

**DXF / DWG Output.** *File ▸ Save As* with type *DXF (\*.dxf)* or *DWG (\*.dwg)* (parts only), or *File ▸ Save As DXF / DWG…*, opens SolidWorks' **DXF/DWG Output** PropertyManager; ✓ downloads the file and the status bar reports its size. *Export*: *Faces / loops / edges* (the selected planar face, greyed with "Select a planar face first" until one is picked; a face clicked while the PropertyManager is open becomes the one exported), *Views* (the standard views at full size, 1:1 whatever the part's size, laid out third angle with no border or title block, one layer per view: check boxes Front, Top, Right, Isometric, default Front + Top + Right, plus *Current view*, the viewport's orientation projected as one more view on the `CURRENT` layer; it takes the pictorial corner, or shares it under the Isometric), or *Sheet metal* (the flat pattern, only on a part with a Base Flange: *Geometry* always, *Bend lines* and *Notes* drop the `BEND` / `NOTES` layers when unchecked), or *Sketch* (a sketch as drawn, full size in its own coordinates: lines as LINE, circles as CIRCLE, arcs as ARC, rectangles / polygons as their LINE edges, slots as two LINEs and two ARCs; *Sketch* picks which one, *Construction geometry* adds centerlines and construction entities on a `CONSTRUCTION` layer, off by default; points and dimensions are not written; layers as the flat pattern's: entities on a closed loop nested inside another closed loop — holes, slots, cutouts, the sketch profile's loops at depth 1 or more — go on `HOLES`, the outermost loops and open geometry on `OUTLINE`). Faces, Views and Sheet metal need a solid. The default is Sketch while a sketch is open or when the part has no solid yet (the sketch being edited, else the one selected in the tree, else the last one), then Sheet metal on a sheet-metal part, else Faces when a face is selected, else Views. File ▸ Save As with type DXF / DWG and the Sketch tab's *Sketch to DXF* open this PropertyManager. *Output format*: DXF, or DWG when the local server has a converter (`HEAD api/dxf2dwg`; greyed otherwise, with the reason; with LibreDWG a note recommends DXF for CAM or the ODA File Converter). *Version*: DXF R12 (AC1009, the default) or R2000 (AC1015) for faces, sketches and the flat pattern (Views are R12), DWG R2000 (the converter is given an R2000 DXF). *Loops as* (faces and sheet metal): *Polylines (one per loop)* or *Lines and arcs*. *File name* defaults to the model name plus `_faceN`, `_views` or `_flat`. The published copy of Anvil has no server, so DWG is greyed there.

**Laser-ready DXF** (faces and the flat pattern; `Kernel.exportDXFFlat(flat, units, opts)`, `Kernel.exportDXFFace(mesh, i, units, opts)`, opts `{ version: 'R12' | 'R2000', style: 'polyline' | 'entities', bendLines, notes, origin }`; the flat also takes SolidWorks' DXF/DWG Output entities, all off by default: `boundaryBox` (a rectangle round the blank, arcs' extents included, on BOUNDARY), `bendLayers: 'split'` (bend lines on BEND_UP / BEND_DOWN by direction instead of one BEND layer), `reliefLayer` (the edges of bend-relief notches on RELIEF; that loop is then written as LINE / ARC entities that still chain into one contour), `hiddenEdges` (each Forming Tool's far-side outline on HIDDEN, line type HIDDEN). A flat's $EXTMIN / $EXTMAX are the blank's extents (what a nesting import reads as the part size); the notes outside it do not count (rfJU). One file per body is not offered: a sheet-metal part has one flat). The loops arrive as tessellated outlines; `Kernel.dxfLoopCurves(loop)` turns each back into what was drawn before it is written: segments shorter than 1e-3 (model units) collapse onto the corner their neighbours meet at (the 1e-3 overlap strips of the unfold leave such slivers), points on a straight line (where the CSG split an edge) go, runs of three or more equal chords turning by the same small angle (≤ 25°: every Anvil circle has ≥ 16 segments, every arc ≥ 4) are fitted with a circle and kept as an arc when every vertex lies on it within 1e-6·(1 + r) — its centre moved onto the perpendicular bisector of its ends so both ends lie on it exactly — and a whole loop of them is a circle; straight runs merge into one line. Round holes are `CIRCLE`s with the exact radius (a Ø.28125 hole writes 0.140625), rounded corners and slot ends are arcs (bulges of the polyline, or `ARC`s), a hexagon stays six lines. *Polylines* writes each loop as one closed polyline with bulges (joined by construction; `POLYLINE` + `VERTEX` in R12, `LWPOLYLINE` in R2000); *Lines and arcs* writes `LINE` / `ARC` entities sharing their end points (within 1e-6). The flat pattern's lower-left corner is at 0,0 (the extents of its arcs counted), coordinates are in the part's units (`$INSUNITS` 1 inch / 4 mm, `$MEASUREMENT` 0 / 1), `$EXTMIN` / `$EXTMAX` and an `*Active` viewport frame it. The sheet note reads `T=0.06 R=0.06 K=0.71  BLANK 7.1612 X 3 IN`; bend labels say `UP 90%%d R2` (°, Ø and ± are written `%%d`, `%%c`, `%%p`: DXF before 2007 is codepage text). R2000 files carry the full structure AutoCAD expects (handles and `$HANDSEED`, the nine symbol tables, `*Model_Space` / `*Paper_Space` blocks with AutoCAD's own handles 1F / 1B, subclass markers, an `OBJECTS` section with the root dictionary); ezdxf reads every output with no audit errors and LibreDWG converts the R2000 file with none.

**STEP units.** An inch part's STEP declares `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT('INCH')` = 25.4 mm with coordinates in inches, from Anvil's writer and from OpenCascade's (whose export sets both `write.step.unit` and the shape's `xstep.cascade.unit` to INCH; before 2026-10-07 an inch part came out 25.4 times too small, or in a fresh engine with millimetre numbers under an INCH label). Sheet-metal bends are exact cylinders in OpenCascade's STEP (`--occt`); in Anvil's own STEP a bend is a cylinder when its ends are square cuts (circles), rulings, or one plane cutting it obliquely (an `ELLIPSE` edge, read back exactly by Anvil and OpenCascade). A bend is written whole, both sides or neither: the outer bends at a 4-edge mitred tray's or a Closed Corner box's corners end on faceted mitre faces, so those bends still go out as facets (`test-reports/2026-11-06-step-audit.md`). Both engines' headers name the part: `FILE_NAME('<part>.step', <local time>, …, 'Anvil', '')`, `PRODUCT('<part>', '<part>', …)` (OpenCascade's own "Open CASCADE Shape Model" header is rewritten by `OcctKernel.stepHeader`).

Downloads are blocked inside some sandboxes; every export dialog also offers *Copy*.

## Import and export of neutral formats

`exchange.js` (global `Exchange`, no DOM; also loaded by `worker.js`) reads and writes the formats other CAD and print software use. Every body is first made into a watertight triangle mesh (`Exchange.bodyMesh`: vertices welded on a 1e-6 grid, the T-junctions a CSG boolean leaves split, so each edge has exactly two triangles), which is what makes the files below closed and manifold.

| Format | Export | Import |
| --- | --- | --- |
| STEP AP214 (`.step`, `.stp`) | `AUTOMOTIVE_DESIGN` schema. One `MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP` per body; curved faces go out on their true surfaces (`CYLINDRICAL_SURFACE`, `CONICAL_SURFACE`, `SPHERICAL_SURFACE`, `TOROIDAL_SURFACE`, one `ADVANCED_FACE` per connected patch) with `CIRCLE`, `ELLIPSE` (a plane cutting a cylinder obliquely) and `LINE` edges and seams in OpenCascade's layout (faces bounded by other curves, such as crossing holes, stay faceted); planar faces: a `CLOSED_SHELL` of `ADVANCED_FACE`s on `PLANE`s bounded by `EDGE_LOOP`s of straight `EDGE_CURVE`s shared by the two faces they join (split points only two faces share are dropped, so edges come out whole). `PRODUCT` named after the part; the part colour as a `STYLED_ITEM`; units mm or `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT('INCH')`. An assembly is a root product with one `NEXT_ASSEMBLY_USAGE_OCCURRENCE` per component (`Pin<2>`), each placed by an `ITEM_DEFINED_TRANSFORMATION` in a `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT_SHAPE_REPRESENTATION`; components with the same source share one part product. Pattern and mirror instances are ordinary components (one occurrence each, at their solved transform); an opposite-hand Mirror Components instance (det R < 0, which no rigid placement can express) exports as its own part `Block (opposite hand)` whose mesh is the part reflected in its local YZ plane, placed by the proper rotation R·diag(−1, 1, 1) (the same in 3MF build items and OBJ). An exploded view does not change exports: they use the collapsed transforms. | Part 21 reader (simple and complex entities, `\X2\` strings). Solids: `MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP`, `FACETED_BREP`, `BREP_WITH_VOIDS`, bare `CLOSED_SHELL`s. Faces: `ADVANCED_FACE` / `FACE_SURFACE` on a `PLANE`, or `FACE` with `POLY_LOOP`s (faceted); edges `LINE` / `POLYLINE` (also wrapped in `SURFACE_CURVE` / `SEAM_CURVE`); outer bounds and holes triangulated by ear clipping with hole bridges. Instances: every path of `REPRESENTATION_RELATIONSHIP_WITH_TRANSFORMATION` / `MAPPED_ITEM` placements up to the root (so each assembly occurrence becomes a solid named `Product<n>`). Units: the length unit of the geometric context (SI prefixes, INCH, FOOT). Curved faces on `CYLINDRICAL_SURFACE`, `CONICAL_SURFACE`, `SPHERICAL_SURFACE` and `TOROIDAL_SURFACE` bounded by circles, straight lines and seams (cylinders also by ellipses) are tessellated with the kernel's surface records, so their mass properties stay exact; B-spline and other curved faces or trimmings are refused with a message naming them. |
| 3MF (`.3mf`) | A stored (uncompressed) zip written in JS: `[Content_Types].xml`, `_rels/.rels`, `3D/3dmodel.model` in `millimeter` (or `inch`), one mesh `object` per body, the part colour as `basematerials`. An assembly: per part a components object over its bodies, one build `item` per component with its `transform` and `partnumber`. | Stored packages (the ones Anvil writes and most writers' uncompressed ones); objects, components and build-item transforms, units. A deflate-compressed package is refused with a message. |
| OBJ (`.obj` + `.mtl`) | `o` / `g` group per body (per component body in an assembly, placed), one `vn` per planar face, `f v//vn`, `mtllib` / `usemtl` when the part has a colour; `Exchange.exportMTL` writes the `.mtl` (`Kd` from the colour). | `v` / `f` (n-gons ear-clipped, negative indices), one solid per `o` (or `g` when there is no `o`), else per connected shell. |
| STL (`.stl`) | `Kernel.exportSTL` (ASCII). | ASCII and binary; one solid per connected shell (an inner, inward shell stays with the solid around it). |

**Materials.** With `materials.js`, each body is exported with its material: the body's own (`model.bodyMaterials`), else the part's (`model.material`), else the part colour. OBJ gets one `usemtl` per body and one `.mtl` entry per distinct material (named after it, `Kd` from its appearance colour); 3MF one `basematerials` base per material (named after it, `displaycolor`) with a `pindex` per body object; STEP a `STYLED_ITEM` colour per solid. STEP product names stay the part names. Assembly components carry their parts' materials.

Imports are scaled to the target model's units (`units: 'mm' | 'in'`, or `'auto'` to keep a STEP / 3MF file's inches); STL and OBJ are unitless (`sourceUnits: 'mm'` default, `'in'`, `'cm'`, `'m'`). Imported meshes get the same weld / T-junction repair and are turned outward; open edges that remain are reported as a warning.

### The Imported feature

*File ▸ Open* of a STEP / STL / OBJ / 3MF file opens a new part (named after the file) whose only feature is **Imported1**; *Insert ▸ Features ▸ Imported…* appends one to the open part. Its solids are ordinary bodies from then on: sketch on their faces, Cut-Extrude through them, Boss-Extrude onto them, pattern, fillet, measure, export again. Double-click the feature for its PropertyManager (file, format, solids, triangles, *Merge result*).

```js
{ "id": 1, "type": "import", "name": "Imported1", "file": "bracket.step", "format": "step", "units": "mm",
  "merge": true,                                   // optional: union each solid into the bodies it touches
  "solids": [{ "name": "Bracket", "vertices": "<base64 Float64 LE x,y,z…>", "triangles": "<base64 Uint32 LE i,j,k…>" }] }
```

The mesh is stored as base64 of little-endian arrays: exact, about half the size of decimal JSON, and cheap for the rebuild's feature keys. `p.importMesh(spec)` is the script form (`spec.solids` may also carry plain `positions` / `triangles` arrays; they are encoded on the way in) and `generateScript` writes it in the feature's place. Each solid becomes its own body unless `merge` is set.

**The kernel hook.** `kernel.js` knows nothing about `import` features (its `buildTool` returns `null` for an unknown type, so a plain rebuild skips them), and it is not edited. On load, `exchange.js` wraps three `Kernel` functions:

- `Kernel.rebuild(model, cache)`: for each `import` feature in order, it rebuilds the prefix of the model up to and including that feature (with the cache), puts the imported solids into the step the kernel recorded for it (`bodies`, `bodyFeatures`, `body`, `tool`) and passes the patched steps on as the cache of the next pass; the last pass rebuilds the whole model from that cache, so every later feature builds against the imported bodies with the kernel's own code. A patched step is flagged (`importApplied`) so a reused cache never adds the solids twice; when every step up to the last import came from the caller's cache, the caller's later steps are reused too. Models without `import` features go straight to the original.
- `Kernel.cacheFromBuild`: flags the import steps of a cache made from a finished (worker) build, whose bodies already hold the solids.
- `Kernel.generateScript`: writes `var fN = p.importMesh({…});` where the feature stands (via a placeholder plane line it then replaces), and `Kernel.Builder.prototype.importMesh` reads it back.

If the kernel ever gets a registry for feature builders, the `import` case is `patchImportStep` in `exchange.js` and can move there unchanged.

### Script / automation API

```js
anvil.exportStep({ fileName }) -> STEP text      anvil.export3mf() -> Uint8Array      anvil.exportObj({ mtlName }) -> OBJ text      anvil.exportMtl()
anvil.importFile(name, textOrBytes, { into: 'new' | 'current', units, sourceUnits, merge }) -> Promise of the summary
anvil.saveAs(type, baseName?, opts?)   // type: json, script, step, 3mf, obj, stl, iges, dxf, dwg; downloads and returns { name, data, mime, extra }
                                       // (dxf / dwg take the anvil.dxfOutput options; dwg returns a Promise, see *Exports*)
Exchange.exportSTEP(model, build?, opts) / export3MF / exportOBJ / exportMTL      // parts and assemblies, build optional
Exchange.importFile(name, data, opts) -> { format, name, file, units, solids: [{ name, positions, triangles }], warnings, stats }
Exchange.importSTEP(text) / importSTL(data) / importOBJ(text) / import3MF(bytes) / parseStep(text)
Exchange.newPartFromImport(result) -> part model with Imported1      Exchange.importFeature(model, result) -> feature
```

Validated outside the browser: the STEP files open in OpenCascade 8 (the kernel under FreeCAD) as valid solids (`BRepCheck_Analyzer`) with the same volume and bounding box, and assemblies read through XCAF as one assembly with the right component names; lib3mf reads the 3MF packages as manifold, oriented meshes with the right units and build items; trimesh reads the OBJ (watertight once merged by position, colour from the `.mtl`). STEP files written by OpenCascade (a planar part and its own layout of an assembly) import with exact volumes; two of them are test fixtures in `tests/fixtures/`.

## Evaluate tools

`evaluate.js` (global `Evaluate`, no DOM) holds the logic and `ui-evaluate.js` the PropertyManagers and viewport overlays of the Evaluate tab. Geometry is read through `Kernel.meshFromBody`, `faceOutline`, `bodyEdges`, `sketchProfile`, the CSG body API and (for Check) `Exchange.bodyMesh`; nothing in `kernel.js` or `csg.js` changes.

| Command | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| **Check** | Validates every body (every component in an assembly) as a welded triangle mesh: **open edges** (one triangle), **non-manifold edges** (three or more triangles, or two that run the edge the same way), **zero-area faces** (area ≤ 1e-14·diag² or ≤ 1e-10·longest edge² — collinear, not merely thin), **self-intersections** (a triangle edge through another triangle's interior, triangles sharing no vertex, x-sweep). The PM lists counts and every problem; each is drawn in its colour, and clicking a row shows that one alone (status bar: its position). |
| **Zebra Stripes** | A `ShaderMaterial` overlay: the view direction reflected about smoothed normals (`Evaluate.smoothNormals`: facets meeting at an edge within 22° are averaged, real edges stay sharp) picks black / white stripes. PM: number of stripes (size), direction (horizontal / vertical / diagonal), sharpness. OK keeps it on (the ribbon button shows pressed), a rebuild redraws it, clicking the button again turns it off. |
| **Section Properties** | Of the selected planar face (`sectionOfFace`: the face's polygons integrated triangle by triangle, holes included), the selected sketch or the sketch being edited (`sectionOfSketch`: the extrude profile, holes by nesting): area, perimeter, centroid, Ixx = ∫(y−ȳ)², Iyy = ∫(x−x̄)², Ixy = ∫(x−x̄)(y−ȳ) about the centroid on the section's x / y, polar J, principal moments I1 ≥ I2 with their world directions and the angle from x, and the moments about the section origin. Exact Green's-theorem integrals of the polygons; the centroid and principal axes are drawn. |
| **Draft Analysis** | Direction of pull (a standard plane's normal, reversed, or a planar face you click), draft angle, *Find straddle faces*. Each face's draft is asin(n·pull): **positive** (≥ angle, green), **requires draft** (between, yellow), **negative** (≤ −angle, red), **straddle** (blue: a curved surface — facets joined by tangent edges — with both positive and negative facets). Counts are per surface; hovering a face shows its angle. |
| **Clearance Verification** | The exact minimum distance between each pair of the selected bodies / components (all pairs when none are selected, one against the rest when one is, or *Selected items and the rest*): triangle–triangle distances over a BVH, 0 with *interferes* when they cross. Results are listed smallest first against the *Minimum acceptable clearance*; the two closest points and the line between them are drawn. Click bodies in the graphics area to change the selection. |
| **Interference Detection** | (assemblies) Every overlapping pair, largest common volume first (red volumes, click a result for that pair alone), with a *Selected Components* box: with components selected (before the command or by clicking them while it is open) only pairs among them are intersected (`CSG.intersect`, same tolerance as `Assembly.interference`); *Selected items and the rest* adds their pairs with every other component; *Entire assembly* clears the selection. |

Interference Detection and Clearance Verification analyse an assembly **collapsed**: pattern and mirror instances are ordinary components with their own world bodies (opposite-hand ones turned outward by `assembly.js`), and in the exploded state the collapsed bodies come from `Assembly.explodedBodies(model, build, view, 0)`; the common volumes and closest points are drawn shifted by each component's explode offset, onto the components as shown, and the PM says the results are collapsed. In Clearance Verification `interfering` means shared volume (`CSG.intersect` above 1e-9 · diag³), which also catches coplanar-sided and nested overlaps (a nested solid reports distance 0).

Also under *Tools ▸ Evaluate ▸*. Script / automation:

```js
Evaluate.check(bodies, names?) -> { ok, counts: { open, nonManifold, zeroArea, selfIntersection }, bodies, items: [{ kind, label, lines, tris, points }] }
Evaluate.checkMesh({ positions, triangles }) / checkBody(csg)      Evaluate.smoothNormals(mesh, angleDeg = 22) -> Float32Array
Evaluate.sectionOfFace(mesh, faceIndex) / sectionOfSketch(sketch, model, planes) / sectionFromLoops(loops, frame?)
  -> { area, perimeter, centroid, centroid2, Ixx, Iyy, Ixy, J, I1, I2, angle, axis1World, axis2World, origin: { Ixx, Iyy, Ixy, J } }
Evaluate.draftAnalysis(mesh, pull, angleDeg) -> { faces: [{ index, angle, cls, surface }], counts (surfaces), faceCounts }
Evaluate.minDistance(a, b) -> { distance, pa, pb, interfering }      Evaluate.clearance(bodies, names, selected, min, { withRest })
Evaluate.interference(bodies, names, selected, { withRest }) -> { pairs: [{ aName, bName, volume, body }], volume, checked }
anvil.check()   anvil.sectionProperties(faceIndex | 'Sketch1')   anvil.draftAnalysis([0, 0, 1], 3)
anvil.clearance(['Body1', 'Body2'] | [], min)   anvil.interferenceOf(['Base<1>', 'Pin<2>'], withRest?)   anvil.zebra(true, { stripes, direction, sharpness })
```

## Selection and Instant3D

`ui-select.js` owns selection in the viewport. It adds small hooks only: `ui-core.js` calls `UI.drawSelectionExtras` at the end of `updateHighlights` and `UI.entitySelected` when colouring sketch entities; `ui-sketch.js` asks `UI.boxSelectHook` on a left press, `UI.pickHover` for the pre-highlight, `UI.selectClick` for a part / assembly click and `UI.sketchSelectClick` for a Select-tool click in a sketch.

- **Multi-selection.** `App.sel` / `App.sel2` stay what every command reads (first and second item); `App.multi` holds the whole list while `App.multi[0] === App.sel`, so code that assigns `App.sel` directly simply starts a new selection. Ctrl or Shift + click toggles an item. `UI.selection()` / `UI.setSelection(list)`; items are `{ kind: 'face' | 'edge' | 'vertex' | 'body' | 'entity' | 'sketchPoint' | 'feature' | 'plane', … }`. Delete with several sketch entities selected removes them all in one undo step.
- **Sketch selection and its PropertyManager** (rfF, as SolidWorks 2024). With the Select tool a click on a line's or an arc's end point, or a circle's / arc's centre (within 9 px, and no farther than the curve under the cursor or within 4 px of the point, so a small hole's edge still selects the circle) selects the **point** (`{ kind: 'sketchPoint', ref: '3.p2' }`) and opens a *Point* PM — rfH: so do the points of entities that stay whole, an ellipse's centre and axis ends (a partial ellipse's ends), an arc slot's centre and arc ends, and the corners (a centre rectangle's / polygon's centre) of a rectangle, polygon, slot or polyline a script or an import made (not a corner rectangle's or a slot's middle; spline points stay with the Spline PM): *Existing Relations*, *Add Relations* for that point, *Parameters* X / Y (a typed value drives the point through the solver). Anywhere else on a curve selects the entity and opens its own PM (Line Properties, Circle, Arc …). Ctrl / Shift + click, or a box, with two or more items selected opens *Properties*: *Selected Entities*, *Existing Relations*, *Add Relations* (the buttons that apply to the selection, `SketchSolver.applicable`) and *For construction*; a relation added there keeps the selection and the PM. Ctrl+clicking back to one item shows that item's PM, to none closes it; ✓ / ✗ / Esc deselect. These PMs never take the keyboard (`noFocus`; an entity PM opened by a click gives the focus back to the graphics area), so **Delete** deletes the selected circle, arc, slot, polygon or ellipse. Delete with only sketch points selected deletes nothing (a point is part of its entity). Points, curves and boxes on a hidden layer or level are not picked. `UI.sketchPickItem(x, y)`, `UI.selectedSketchRefs(list?)` (the selection as sketch refs, for a preselection), `UI.sketchSelectionPM(list)`.
- **Box selection.** A left press on empty space (no face, plane or sketch entity under it) and a drag draws a box; a press on the model still rotates the view, as it always has in Anvil (middle button and Alt + left rotate anywhere). Left to right is a **window** (blue, solid): items entirely inside. Right to left is a **crossing** box (green, dashed): items inside or touching. Plain replaces the selection, **Ctrl** adds, **Shift** (or Ctrl + Shift) toggles. What a box selects: in a part the sharp edges, in an assembly the components, in a sketch the entities (a rectangle or polyline counts as one entity); with the Selection Filter on, the filtered kinds instead (faces: every triangle inside, or any touching; bodies likewise over all their faces; vertices: the point inside; sketch entities in a part: the visible sketches). Hidden bodies are skipped. In the Fillet, Chamfer and flange PropertyManagers a box sends its edges to the selection box.
- **Select hidden entities** (rfFD; *Tools ▸ Options ▸ Display/Selection ▸ Select hidden entities*, off by default as in SolidWorks, saved in `localStorage['anvil.selectHidden']`): off, a box or lasso in a shaded view (Shaded With Edges / Shaded) takes only the model **edges, vertices, faces and bodies that can be seen**: a depth buffer of the shaded part is rasterised once per box (orthographic, at screen resolution) and an edge counts when any of a few points along it is not clearly behind the part (an edge on the outline counts). Hidden Lines Removed, Hidden Lines Visible and Wireframe take hidden ones too; on = through the part. A face or body counts when a pixel of it inside the box or lasso shows (a face seen edge-on: when its middle is not behind the part). `UI.selectHidden(on?)`, `anvil.selectHidden(on?)`.
- **Lasso Selection** (rfFD, SolidWorks'): right-click an empty spot of the graphics area (or empty space in a sketch) ▸ *Lasso Selection*; it stays until *Box Selection* is picked there. A left-drag then draws a free-form loop (grey, closed back to its start on release) and what lies **wholly inside** it is selected (the window rule; a concave loop is honoured), under the Selection Filter, with the Ctrl / Shift rules of the box: part edges (faces / vertices with the filter), sketch entities and points, assembly components. In a part the loop may start on the model (a plain click still selects; middle-drag rotates). `BoxSelect.lasso(points)` is the region (`inLoop`, `crossesLoop`); `UI.selectionMode('box' | 'lasso')`, `UI.applyLasso(points, mods)`, `anvil.selectionMode(mode?)`, `anvil.lassoSelect(points, mods)`.
- **Selection Filter** (**F5** shows / hides the toolbar, also *View ▸ Selection Filter*): Toggle Selection Filters (**F6**, suspends the set without forgetting it), Clear All Filters, Select All Filters, Filter Faces, Edges, Vertices, Solid Bodies, Components, Sketch Entities. With no button pressed everything is selectable; with some pressed, clicks, hover, box selection and every PropertyManager selection box (`App.command.onPick`) only take those kinds; an assembly selection box that maps a face to its component still gets the face when only Bodies / Components are on. Switching a filter on drops selected items it no longer allows. Vertices are picked only with Filter Vertices on (8 px, occluded behind the face under the cursor).
- **FeatureManager sync.** Rows of selected features, bodies and components are marked; the feature that owns a selected face or edge gets a lighter *owner* mark. Ctrl / Shift + click on a row adds it to or removes it from the selection. **Esc** clears the selection in both.
- **Smart Dimension on the model** (lane model-dims, `ui-model-dims.js`). With no sketch open, Smart Dimension (Sketch tab, or **D**) dimensions the part's solid, as SolidWorks lets you dimension model geometry. The dimensions *measure* the part; they never change a feature. One straight edge gives its length (the whole edge, however the booleans split it), a circular edge or a cylinder face Ø (a whole circle or hole) or R (an arc, a fillet); two items give a distance (parallel faces, edge to edge, vertex to vertex / edge / face, face to a parallel edge; a circular edge pairs as its centre, a cylinder as its axis; two points read aligned or along X / Y / Z by where the dimension is placed, as the sketch tool's horizontal / vertical / aligned) or an angle (non-parallel faces or edges, an edge and a face; the quadrant by where it is placed). Hovering pre-highlights what a click picks; the picked items show selected and the dimension follows the cursor; a click on empty space places it, and a length / Ø / R already on the cursor is placed by a click on a face too (a hole sits inside its face), so pick a face first for a face-to-edge distance. Two items at zero distance are refused. The dimension is drawn in 3D with the sketch dimensions' ANSI look (extension lines, slim arrows, horizontal text in a break of the line, constant pixel size), the offset snapped to a model axis or face normal within 30°, in the document's units (`.500`, `Ø.375`, `R.250`, `135°`). Saved in the model JSON as `modelDims: [{ id, kind: 'len' | 'dia' | 'rad' | 'dist' | 'ang', refs, off, mode?, value, text, at }]` (File ▸ Save keeps it; the script form does not); each ref re-finds its face / edge / vertex after every rebuild by the creating feature's id and the face normals, nearest its last place, so a face-to-face dimension follows an extrude depth edited in the feature; items that vanish (a suppressed chamfer) draw the dimension red until they come back. Click a label to select it (drag to move it), Delete removes it, Ctrl+Z undoes, Esc ends the tool, *View ▸ Model Dimensions* hides / shows them all. API: `UI.modelDimTool(on?)`, `UI.modelDims()` -> `[{ id, kind, value, text, dangling, selected }]`, `UI.modelDimsShown(on?)`, `UI.modelDimSelect(id)`, `UI.modelDimDelete(id)`. Smoke `rfHK`.
- **Instant3D** (*Features ▸ Instant3D* toggles it; on by default). Selecting a face of a Boss-Extrude / Cut-Extrude (blind or mid-plane, depth not an equation) or a Revolve (angle below 360°), or the feature in the FeatureManager, shows a blue arrow on the end face (the cap at the revolve angle). Dragging it changes the depth / angle live (only the dragged feature is previewed; once the drag rests 180 ms the dependents follow): it moves freely at display precision and snaps only while the pointer is over the ruler shown along the arrow (1-2-5 steps at least 8 px apart; 1°, 5° or 15° on the revolve arc). The feature's sketch dimensions are drawn in the viewport too, each with a drag dot on its arrow (placed dimensions, plus the driven ones while *View ▸ Sketch Dimensions* is on); dragging one re-solves the sketch (`SketchSolver.setDim`) and rebuilds. The whole drag is **one undo step**, the end face is selected again afterwards, and a value box opens where the arrow ended: type a number or `=expression`, Enter applies (same undo step), Esc closes. Clicking a dimension's value opens the same box. rfFD: a selected face of a **Fillet / Chamfer** (radius / distance: the arrow runs from the old sharp edge across the face it meets), a **Shell** (an inner wall: the wall thickness, the outer face stays) or a **pattern copy** (Linear Pattern spacing, Circular Pattern angle; a copy's faces carry the seed's id, so a face outside the seed's tool and inside the pattern's is the pattern's, judged at the clicked point) shows the arrow the feature's PropertyManager shows; a drag or a typed value edits the feature the same way (one undo step, value box, `ui-commands.js` `UI.instant3DFeatureHandles(f, faceIndex)` gives the handles). In a sketch (Select tool) the same dots sit on the dimension arrows, shown only while the pointer is over one, and hidden while sketch geometry is dragged; dragging a dot drives the dimension like typing it. Handles seen end-on are left out.

```js
anvil.selectionFilter({ faces: true });                 // { faces: true, …, active: true }
anvil.boxSelect([100, 80], [400, 300]);                 // window; [400, 300] → [100, 80] is a crossing box
anvil.boxSelect([100, 80], [400, 300], { ctrl: true }); // add
anvil.selection();                                      // [{ kind: 'face', index: 5 }, …]
anvil.instant3D();                                      // { on, handles: [{ kind: 'depth' | 'angle' | 'sdim', label, value, at, grab, step }] }
```

## Tests

`tests/kernel-tests.html` builds parts through the script API and checks them against known volumes, bounding boxes, face counts, profile nesting, edge extraction, chamfers and fillets, sheet-metal flanges and flat patterns (including the mitred-corner blank and two Material Outside flanges sharing a corner: blank 9692.80 with bend allowance 5.372), export contents, error messages and script round-trips, then builds every entry in `examples/examples.js` and round-trips each one through `generateScript`. The `normal cut` group checks that a cut parallel to the sheet is unchanged, that a 45° sketch removes a straight prism (same volume as the oblique slab, centroid z −29.8069 instead of −29.7827 on the 100×60×2 sheet, every face axis-aligned), that a window through a flange wall removes outline × T from either side and in both directions, that the option is ignored without sheet parameters and warns when the tool crosses no sheet face. The `pack & worker` group round-trips solids and whole builds through `pack`/`unpack`, clones a packed build through a `MessageChannel`, checks `cacheFromBuild` (step shape, full reuse, appended feature, change in the middle) and drives `worker.js` end to end (build, cache reuse, a broken feature). The `csg early-out` group checks `getBounds`, disjoint / touching / overlapping / nested cubes (volumes, the unsplit 12 quads of a disjoint union), a far boss and hole on a plate (analytic volume and centroid), pack/unpack of an early-out result, and pins every Design Library part's volume in `EXPECTED_VOLUMES` (an entry at `null` is only checked to be finite and positive; the page records the numbers in `window.__anvilVolumes` and `window.__anvilVolumesJS()` prints them ready to paste). The `cut direction hint` group covers `Kernel.cutDirectionHint` on a Front sheet and a Top box: top face true, bottom face false, planes above and below the body, a plane whose normal line misses the body, a plane inside the material, face frames from `planeFromFace`, and the null cases (no body, unknown or non-sketch id, no model) without throwing. The `multi-body` group builds two cubes with `merge: false` and checks the body list (`bodies`, `bodyFeatures`, `bodyInfo` names, face and polygon tags), unscoped and scoped cuts (600 + 600, 1000 + 600), the merge rule (a touching boss joins body 0, a bridging boss collapses both into slot 0, a disjoint boss starts a third body, a cut that empties a body removes it, `merge: false` on an overlapping boss, a cylinder, a revolve and a pattern), fillet / chamfer / shell acting on the picked body only (1000 stays exactly 1000; 424 + 1000 for the shell), the *removed no material* warning with and without scope, mirror-body copies apart and touching, the `generateScript` round trip (`merge: false`, `bodies: [1]`), pack / unpack of the bodies and of a legacy single-body build, the rebuild cache with and after the worker, `bodyNearPoint` and the empty model. The `equations` group covers the expression grammar (precedence, right-associative powers, unary minus, functions, degree helpers), every error case, `resolveEquations` order, forward references, duplicate and invalid names, `resolveFeature` on nested and skipped fields, rebuilds driven by `=` fields (T = 10 → 2000, T = 5 → 1000; sketch-plane offset, pattern spacing and count), feature and equation errors that never throw, the `generateScript` round trip, `packBuild` / `unpackBuild`, the rebuild cache (only dependants rebuild; a same-value edit reuses everything) and the *Parametric plate* example at T = 12. The `configurations` group covers the Builder calls (strings stored, `=` stripped, an unknown active name throws, `null` clears), resolving with and without an active configuration, an unknown active name, an override that uses an earlier variable and one that is ignored, `effectiveEquations` / `captureConfiguration`, `applyEquations` in both modes and for dropped names, analytic volumes per configuration, the rebuild cache on a switch (only the dependants rebuild; switching back is bit-identical), the `generateScript` round trip, `packBuild` / `unpackBuild`, and every configuration of each Design Library example that documents them (*Configured spacer*: Small 18000, Medium 29000, Large 32000). The `assembly` group drives `assembly.js` directly: the new model's shape, insert naming (`Plate<1>`, `Plate<2>`, first fixed), `partModel` for every source kind and the unknown-example error, rebuild bodies / names / polygon and face tags, volumes that add regardless of placement, a pin inserted at an arbitrary position and rotation mated concentric into the plate's (10, 10) hole with flush bottoms (origin [10, 0, 10], axis +Y), a rotated block seated face to face on the plate top (bottom at y = 8), plane, origin and axis refs, alignment flips, DOF counts (6 unmated, 2 concentric, 1 with the coincident, 0 fixed), wrong-kind, conflicting and unresolvable mates reported as errors, suppressed mates, removing components and mates, rebuild stability (1e-9), part-build cache reuse, the `generateScript` → `runScript` round trip, `isAssemblyScript`, `refFromPick` (face vs cylinder) and mass properties with two densities. Assembly examples (`kind: "assembly"`) are built with `Assembly.runScript` + `Assembly.rebuild`, checked against their documented volume, pinned in `EXPECTED_VOLUMES`, their `checks` (solved component positions) verified to 1e-6, and round-tripped through `Assembly.generateScript`. The `csg overlap split` group checks union / subtract / intersect of overlapping, nested, disjoint and touching boxes and n-gon prisms against analytic volumes, watertight meshes and no degenerate polygons, faces away from the overlap left unsplit, a half ring, and shell-like slab unions (six walls = 3904 in any order, stacked and staircase slabs). The `drawings` group (drawing.js) checks the view frames, a box (4 lines per ortho view, 9 visible + 3 hidden in the isometric), a blind hole (two hidden walls and the hidden bottom), a post split into visible and hidden pieces by a wall in front of it, the Bushing's silhouettes and hidden bore, the Design Library *Plate with bolt holes* drawing (extents 100×8 / 100×60 / 60×8 and the isometric from its corners, 4 + 4 hidden lines in Front and Right, 4 Ø8 circles in Top, A3 at 2:1, third-angle alignment, dimensions `100, 8, 60, 4X Ø8`), an independent ray cast from points on every drawn piece (visible pieces unblocked, hidden ones blocked) on four parts in all four views, first-angle placement on A2 at 1:1, an inch part on ANSI B, the SVG / DXF / PDF writers (parsed back: dashes, layers, `HIDDEN` linetype, circles, xref offsets, stream length), `moveView` alignment and suppressed dimensions, and every part example's view extents against its bounding box (assembly examples are skipped). The `sketch solver` group (sketch-solver.js) checks DOF counts per entity type, a constrained rectangle solved from four sloppy lines (exact 40 × 20, DOF 0, every entity fully defined), a dimension edit re-solving the whole rectangle, a tangent arc-line with shared end, external circle tangency, equal circles driven by one diameter, redundant and conflicting relations and a refused dimension edit, the other relation types and an angle dimension 30 → 45, distance dimensions, dragging (under-defined follows, fully defined stays), inference, relations following entity deletion, the `.rel()` script round trip, and that solving every Design Library sketch moves nothing. The `materials & properties` group (materials.js) checks the library (classes, properties, aliases, unknown names), mass as volume × density on a 100 × 50 × 10 box (6061-T6 135 g, Brass 425 g, ABS 51 g, Plain Carbon Steel 390 g), the Design Library bolt-hole plate in 6061-T6 / AISI 304 / Nylon 6/6 against its analytic 16-gon volume, an inch part in pounds, a two-body part with a body material (7.80 + 1.02 g, mass-weighted centroid, per-body colours), assembly mass with each component's part and body materials, custom and configuration-specific properties with `"SW-"` links, the `p.material` / `p.property` / `p.bodyMaterial` / `a.property` script round trips (and that no Design Library example gains a line), the title block fields and their text in the SVG / DXF / PDF exports, BOM property columns (titles, widths, hidden columns) and Top-level only vs Parts only on part and assembly drawings. The `exchange` group (exchange.js) checks watertight body meshes of six Design Library parts (0 open edges, same volume), OBJ / 3MF / STEP round trips of those parts (volume and bbox; OBJ groups, `vn` per face and `.mtl` colour; the 3MF zip entries, CRC and units; STEP header, units, solids, planes and every `EDGE_CURVE` used by exactly two `ORIENTED_EDGE`s), the *Pinned plate assembly* in all three formats (4 NAUOs over 3 products, 4 build items, 4 OBJ groups, instance names, solved volume and bbox), inch parts, the two OpenCascade-written fixtures (27875 and 69600, instance names), a faceted cube in centimetres, the curved-face refusal, ASCII and binary STL and OBJ with quads / negative indices / groups, ear clipping with four holes, the Imported feature (cut and boss against it, cache reuse, pack / unpack, `cacheFromBuild`, merge), its script round trip, a worker build of it, and materials in the exports (part and body materials as OBJ `usemtl` / MTL, 3MF basematerials, STEP colours). The `evaluate` group (evaluate.js) checks Section Properties against closed forms — a 40 × 20 rectangle (bh³/12, hb³/12, parallel-axis origin moments, perimeter), an L-section by composite rectangles (centroid, Ixx / Iyy / Ixy, Mohr's-circle principal moments, zero principal product), a ring against the n-gon formulas (n/2 R² sin a, J = nR⁴ sin a (2 + cos a)/12) and within 1 % of π(R⁴ − r⁴)/4, the annular face and a holed face; Draft Analysis of a 5° tapered extrude (sides +5°, positive at 3°, requires draft at 6°, reversed pull) and a cross cylinder (one straddle surface); Clearance between two boxes (10 face to face, √50 corner to corner with the corners as closest points), two 16-gon cylinders (12 side by side, 7 end to end, 0 and interfering when overlapping, touching boxes 0 and not interfering) and assembly components; Interference Detection on selections against `Assembly.interference`; Check on deliberately broken meshes (missing, duplicated, flipped and collinear triangles, interpenetrating vs touching cubes, a zero-area polygon added to a CSG body) and on every Design Library example (all clean except the Funnel's known sliver crack, which is reported); Interference / Clearance on Linear Component Pattern and (opposite-hand) Mirror Components instances, on an exploded assembly's collapsed bodies, and coplanar-sided and nested overlaps; and the zebra normals. The `drawing annotations` group checks auto-inserted centre marks on the bolt-hole plate at their analytic sheet positions (cross and extension lines) and its Front / Right centrelines, linear and circular centre marks (Ø40 bolt circle fitted through six pattern holes), hole callouts read from the solid (THRU, blind, counterbore through and blind, countersink 90°, a blind hole from the far side, a boss that is not a hole, `4X Ø8 THRU` on the plate), ordinate values (0 / 6 / 14 / 86 / 94 / 100 and 0 / 10 / 50, following a width change to 120), baseline stacking and `2 X 45°` chamfer dimensions, tolerance and precision formatting (bilateral, limit, symmetric, `<DIM>` text, inches, stacked texts, kept through a part change), dimension sources and driven rebuilds (width 120, Ø10, depth 6 against the analytic volume; an equation-driven depth refused), dimension line / leader / flip geometry, manual and stacked balloons with QTY, and the CENTER linetype, symbol words and WinAnsi ± in the exports. The `assembly: sub-assemblies and live links` group checks a part two levels down against the composed transforms (centroid, bbox, a path ref and a pick through both levels), Top-level / Indented / Parts only BOM rows and quantities over three levels, mass properties of the Hinged cover against the analytic mass-weighted centroid, Flexible vs Rigid (the Angle mate over-defines the rigid hinge, opens the flexible one to 30°, `flexState` round trip), a part edit in the document store propagating to two assemblies (one through a linked sub-assembly; dirty edits; `saveReferences`), broken references (suppressed, not an error; mates suppressed; restored) and a circular reference, interference inside and across levels and with sub-assemblies as components, STEP NAUO nesting (the NAUO tree, one B-rep per distinct part) with STEP / 3MF / OBJ re-import of every part at its world place and Pack and Go exporting the same file with an empty store, and the script round trip with `{ file }` sources, path refs and flexible components. The `box selection geometry` group checks `BoxSelect` from `ui-select.js` alone: window vs crossing from the drag direction, segments (through, corner graze, just outside a corner, boundary, end-on), triangles (box inside, triangle inside, an edge through, a miss inside the bounding box, slices of a face array), sketch polylines with their closing segment, the replace / add / toggle merge, and the Instant3D ruler steps and snapping. 763 tests at the time of writing (2026-10-03). A test may return a Promise: its row shows *WAIT* until it settles, a pending test fails after 10 s, and the summary and `window.__anvilTests` are written only once every test has settled; the summary shows the run's wall-clock time and the synchronous tests' own sum (`wallMs`, `syncMs`). Run it after touching `csg.js`, `kernel.js` or `worker.js`. Circles are polygonal in the kernel, so tests compare against the polygonal area (`n/2 · r² · sin(2π/n)` with `n = clamp(round(8√r), 16, 72)`) rather than `πr²`. The `true curves: kernel` group checks profile curves, the chord tolerance, `model.tessellation` and its cache keys, exact volumes / areas / centroids of cylinders, cones, frusta, spheres, tori, holes, counterbores, bosses, partial revolves, a drafted cylinder and a fillet, tessellation independence and pack / unpack, records through splits, patterns and mirrors, `curveEdges`, and old models.

The same page runs under Node without a browser: `node anvil/headless/anvil.js test` (or `npm test`) loads the page's script list, runs its inline script (which reads the test group files in `tests/kernel/`) under a small DOM / `XMLHttpRequest` / `DOMParser` / `Worker` shim and prints the pass count and every failure (811 / 811 at the time of writing, the worker tests included); `node anvil/headless/anvil.js example --all` checks every example's volume. See *Headless (Node)*.

`tests/bench.html` times every example and a set of stress parts and reports medians; `tests/BENCHMARKS.md` keeps dated baselines so performance work in Phase 4 has something to compare against.

`tests/occt-tests.html` builds every part example and feature model with both kernels (51 tests at the time of writing): OpenCascade's solid valid, volume / bounding box / body count matching Anvil's, exact volumes where known, STEP / IGES round trips and the rebuild cache. It needs `vendor/occt`.

**Confidence corpus (rfFT, 2026-10-30).** `tests/kernel/rfFT.js` holds 69 shop parts in inches (42 machined / welded solids: plates with Hole Wizard and patterned holes, brackets with rounds and chamfers, revolved bushings and shafts, ribs, drafts, shells, sweeps, lofts, mirrors; 27 sheet-metal parts: flanges, hems, jogs, Miter Flange, Sketched Bend, Break-Corner, Closed Corner, Rip / Insert Bends, Tab, Vent, Normal Cut, Unfold / Fold) and two seeded fuzz generators (`rfFTfuzz('sheet' | 'round', seed)`). Every part must build without errors, be closed, match OpenCascade's volume / bbox / body count (or a closed form) and, for sheet metal, give a closed flat of the expected size; differences are `rfFT TODO:` rows that pass and carry both numbers. `node anvil/tests/fixtures/rfFT/compare.js` prints Anvil's numbers for every part, `--occt --root <main checkout>` OpenCascade's (vendor/occt is not in worktrees), `--fuzz 1000 [--seed S]` runs the fuzz by hand. Findings: `test-reports/2026-10-30-solids-confidence.md`.

**Second confidence corpus (rfGA, 2026-10-30).** `tests/kernel/rfGA.js` holds 48 more inch parts on the features rfFT skipped (revolve + circular pattern, mirror of features / bodies / cuts / fillets touching the plane, shells of boxes, cups and filleted boxes, swept tubes and bent bar, 2- and 3-section lofts, Combine by Intersect (add / subtract / common), rib one side / normal / in a shell, draft on planar faces, patterns whose copies touch or overlap, Hole Wizard into a shaft, Wrap scribe / deboss, chamfer + fillet on revolved parts, and sheet metal: Miter Flange round three edges, Insert Bends, hems on flanges, Rip + Insert Bends, Sketched Bend on a holed strip, Unfold / slot / Fold, each Closed Corner type, Base Flange from an open profile, Lofted Bend) with closed forms where derivable, and a feature-stack fuzz `rfGAfuzz(seed)` (a plate or revolved blank, then 3–8 random bosses, cuts, revolve cuts, patterns, mirrors, shells, fillets / chamfers, ribs, drafts and Intersect combines). `compare.js --table rfGA` runs its table (`--occt`, `--fuzz N` as above). Findings: `test-reports/2026-10-30-solids-confidence-2.md`.

**Refinement rounds.** Since 2026-10-02 each parallel lane adds its tests in a marked block, since 2026-10-13 a group file of its own (*Test group files* below; markers `// --- refine/<lane> ---`, `smooth/<lane>`, `r3/<lane>` … `r8/<lane>`), so branches merge cleanly. They pin what the rounds changed: bit-identical example volumes after the CSG speed-ups, no grid snapping and the inference rules, typed values and the numeric-input option, virtual sharps and offset dimensions through the script, view transitions that leave the logical camera in place, render on demand, worker previews, PropertyManager handles and selection lists, Select Other, assembly drag by pose, SmartMates and the pop-ups, saved transparency and ignored interferences, and drawing linework (free line ends, tangent edges, hatch per material, view fitting). The engine integrity check was verified by hand (occt-tests on the plain and the gzipped engine, a substituted engine refused); no page tests it. Each lane's tests must keep kernel-tests and occt-tests fully green; ui-smoke steps that drag or redraw can fail when the browser pane is hidden or shared (background tabs get about one animation frame per second), so the final run is made with the pane visible and nothing else running, on port 8812.

`tests/ui-smoke.html` is the app-level regression: it loads `index.html` in a frame and walks the main workflows through the same functions the ribbon and PropertyManager call (`UI.cmdSketch`, `UI.pmOK`, `UI.deleteFeature`, …), asserting volumes after each step and failing on any uncaught error or `console.error` inside the app. Every step awaits the rebuild it triggers (`await anvil.run(...)`, `await UI.pmOK()`, `await UI.whenIdle()`). Later steps cover the Cut-Extrude *Normal cut* checkbox (on by default on a sheet part, absent on a plain part, `normalCut: true` in the committed feature), the Cut-Extrude *Reverse direction* default (checked for a sketch on the top face of a sheet, clear for one on the bottom face, and the cut removes 10 × 10 × 2 either way), the icon set (every ribbon, tree and panel icon id renders as an `<svg` string that `DOMParser` accepts, and the rendered ribbon SVGs re-parse), *View ▸ Theme* (`UI.setTheme`, `anvil.theme`, `localStorage`, `UI.viewportTheme`, the menu check marks), and background rebuilds: worker mode on by default, synchronous mode when off, coalescing of three rapid runs, the *Rebuild in background* menu item, and the main-thread cache after a worker build (`UI.buildCacheInfo()`, a following `UI.rebuild({ sync: true })` reuses every feature). The multi-body steps build two cubes with `merge: false` and check the *Solid Bodies(2)* folder and its rows, `anvil.bodies()` / `anvil.selectBody()` / `UI.selectBody()` / a click on a body row, `UI.setBodyHidden` (viewport only: the mesh and mass properties keep both bodies), the Boss-Extrude *Merge result* checkbox (checked by default; unchecked commits `merge: false` and a second body, re-checked merges again), the Cut-Extrude *Feature Scope* group (*Selected bodies* ▸ *Boss-Extrude2* commits `bodies: [1]` and cuts body 1 only, and the edit PM reopens on it; absent on a single-body part) and the per-body volumes in *Mass Properties*. Run it after touching any `ui-*.js` file or `worker.js`. Results are also exposed as `window.__anvilSmoke`. The equations steps cover `anvil.equations()` get / set / undo, the *Equations* folder and rows, *Tools ▸ Equations…* (live re-evaluation, OK rebuilds, a red row refuses OK, add / reorder / delete, Cancel), a Boss-Extrude depth of `=W+1` (hint, committed text, follows T, a broken equation disables OK), *Equation W: …* in the status bar, `UI.evalInput` and the sketch Modify box (`UI.editDim(0, "=T*3")`, an unknown name keeps the box open), and `UI.bodyBounds` / `UI.zoomToBody`. The configurations steps cover `anvil.configurations()` get / set / activate with volumes and `summary().configuration(s)`, the *ConfigurationManager* tab (`#config-panel`, `.cfg-row` active and selected, the part row *Name (Config)*), the first *Add* creating *Default* plus the new one, Rename / Activate / Delete through the panel buttons and the name prompt, `UI.configurations` and `anvil.configurations.add / rename / remove / activate`, one undo step per operation, the Equations dialog editing only the active configuration, and every configuration of the *Configured spacer* example. The assembly steps open an assembly with `anvil.newAssembly`, insert a plate and a pin (`anvil.components()`, tree rows `(f) Plate<1>` / `(-) Pin<1>`), mate the pin into a hole (it moves to [10, 0, 10], *Mates* folder and rows, 1 DOF, `UI.selectComponent`), undo / redo a mate, round-trip `anvil.script()` through `anvil.run` and `anvil.model()` through `anvil.load`, check `anvil.mass()`, fix / float / remove through `anvil`, open the *Pinned plate assembly* from the Design Library, and return to a part with `anvil.newPart`. The drawing steps open *File ▸ Make Drawing from Part* on the bolt-hole plate (View Layout tab only, drawing tree rows, layers, extents and hidden counts, dimensions), add a *Smart Dimension* by clicking the Front view's bottom edge and undo it, drag the Top view (x stays on Front's), place a Bottom view through the *Model View* PropertyManager and a click, switch to first angle in *Sheet Properties*, open the three export dialogs, check that part shortcuts are swallowed and that a depth change redraws the views, go back to the part and reopen the drawing from the *Window* menu, and, on the *Pinned plate assembly* example, check that *Make Drawing from Part* is disabled and refused, that *Window* lists the assembly, and that *File ▸ New ▸ Assembly* from a drawing leaves it. The sketch-relations steps draw a rectangle with the Line tool through real mouse events (inferred Horizontal / Vertical / Coincident, yellow inference icon, Under Defined and blue), place two Smart Dimensions (Fully Defined, black) and edit one (the whole rectangle follows), add a redundant Parallel through the Add Relations PM (yellow, Over Defined) and delete it from Display/Delete Relations, delete a relation by its icon, drag an under-defined side with the mouse, place a distance dimension between two lines and drive it to 70, round-trip the script, load an old model without relations unchanged, and use the ribbon, Tools and View menu entries. The materials steps apply a material from the tree row's Edit Material dialog and the favourites menu (density, viewport colour, mass, undo), give a second body its own material from its row's menu (vertex colours per body, Mass Properties per body, Use Part Material, undo), use the task pane list and a custom density, fill *File ▸ Properties* (default rows, evaluated links, a Configuration Specific row, OK, script, undo / redo), read the properties in a part drawing's title block and a Parts only BOM built through its PropertyManager (Add Column, hidden column, a title block link edited in Sheet Properties, exports), and check an assembly's component colours, tooltips, Mass Properties, BOM columns and title block. The exchange steps open an OpenCascade STEP with `anvil.importFile` (Imported1 row and tooltip) and cut it, open its PropertyManager, check *File ▸ Save As* (type list, *Export STEP AP214…* preselecting STEP, Save), open a 3MF through the *File ▸ Open* file input (with the *Files of type* filter) and a pasted ASCII STL, import into the open part through *Insert ▸ Features ▸ Imported* and undo it, and export the *Pinned plate assembly* to STEP / 3MF / OBJ. The Evaluate steps check the ribbon and *Tools ▸ Evaluate* entries, Check on a clean box and on a body with a face removed (4 open edges, drawn, click-to-highlight), Section Properties of a selected face then of *Sketch1* (the PM follows the selection), Zebra Stripes (shader uniforms from the PM, on after OK and a rebuild, toggled off), Draft Analysis of the tapered extrude (legend counts, green side faces, a picked face as the pull), Clearance Verification (row, closest points, below minimum, selection by clicking) and Interference Detection limited to selected components on the *Interference check* assembly, and both on an exploded assembly with a pattern and an opposite-hand mirror (collapsed results, drawn on the exploded components). The annotation steps check the auto-inserted centre marks and centrelines of a new plate drawing, the Annotation tab, Document Properties (centre marks off for a new view), the Center Mark PM (Linear), the Hole Callout tool and dragging it, the Centerline tool, Delete / Undo of a mark, a vertical Ordinate Dimension (0, 10, 50), the Chamfer Dimension tool (`2 X 45°`), dragging a dimension, the Dimension PM (bilateral tolerance, then Value 120 driving the part, Undo), the Balloon tool on a part drawing (drag, Balloon PM quantity) and the Stacked Balloon tool on the assembly (1 | 2 | 3, Delete, Undo). The sub-assembly steps open the *Hinged cover*, expand Hinge<2> in the FeatureManager, read the BOM dialog in all three types, switch Hinge<2> to Rigid and back from its menu (one undo step), turn a pick inside Hinge<1> into a path ref, Edit Part an embedded part and undo it, and mate a block to a face inside Hinge<1> through the Mate PM; the live-link steps save a part, insert it as a link from Insert Components, Open Part and Edit Part it (banner, Return), save the assembly with its edited part, update a second assembly, break and restore the link, and check *File ▸ Pack and Go…*. The selection steps use real synthetic mouse drags: on a 40 × 20 × 10 block in the Front view a window over the left half selects the 4 edges at x = −20 and the crossing box 8 (box colour and line style checked while dragging), Ctrl adds and Shift toggles the right half's 4, a drag started on the block still rotates; with Filter Faces the window takes the one face seen edge-on and the crossing box 5, Filter Vertices 4, Filter Solid Bodies 1 / none; the F5 toolbar and its buttons, clicks under each filter (Ctrl toggles vertices, switching filters drops what is no longer allowed), the Fillet selection box refusing an edge under Filter Faces, F6; Instant3D drags a Boss-Extrude depth 10 → 30 (24000, one undo step, value box types 25 → 20000, Undo → 8000), a Cut-Extrude depth 4 → 6, a Revolve 90° → 135° (volume × 1.5, typed 180° × 2, one undo), the Instant3D ribbon toggle, a sketch width dimension 40 → 50 in the part (10000) and a height dot 20 → 25 inside the sketch; box selection in a sketch (window 2 of 3, crossing 2, Ctrl adds, Delete all three in one undo step) and in the *Pinned plate assembly* (a crossing box selects Pin<2>), the component rows marked, Ctrl-click on a row, Esc, and the owner-feature mark for a selected face. 234 steps at the time of writing (2026-10-03).

### Test group files

Since 2026-10-13 the two big pages hold only their harness: `tests/kernel-tests.html` (about 440 lines) and `tests/ui-smoke.html` (about 400 lines) keep the shared helpers (`test`, `group`, `assert`, `near`, `build`, … / `step`, `assert`, `near`, `idle`, `wait`, `W`, `D`, `K`, `UI`, `$`, `pmField`, …), a `GROUPS` list and the runner. Every test group lives in its own small file: `tests/kernel/*.js` (141 files) and `tests/smoke/*.js` (143 files), one per group marker of the old pages, named after it (`rfAZ.js`, `r3-sketch3.js`, `refine-assembly.js`, `sheet-metal.js`, …), each file the old block byte for byte.

- **How they run.** The page reads the files of its `GROUPS` list in order with a synchronous `XMLHttpRequest` (so the pages must be served over HTTP, as before; under Node, `headless/anvil.js`'s `XMLHttpRequest` shim reads them from disk), joins them and runs them with one direct `eval` inside the page script. The groups therefore see every helper of the page, and each other's top-level helpers (`selClick`, `sheetMouse`, `exByName`, … declared in one smoke file and used in later ones), exactly as when they were inline; `?only=<text>` on ui-smoke still filters steps by name. A file that cannot be read or does not parse is left out and shows as one FAIL row; stack traces name `kernel-tests.groups.js:<line>`, which the kernel page maps back to `tests/kernel/<file>:<line>` in a failure's detail.
- **Adding a group.** Write `tests/kernel/rfXX.js` or `tests/smoke/rfXX.js` (one file per lane and round: `rfXX.js`, the same marker comments inside as before) and add one line for it to the page's `GROUPS` list, in the place it should run (normally the end). Run `node anvil/tests/split-tests.js --check`: it fails when a listed file is missing or does not parse, when a `.js` file in the folder is not listed, or when the page's own helpers / runner would need a name only a group file declares. A helper several groups share belongs above `GROUPS` in the page; a helper only your group uses stays in your file (wrap the group in `(function () { … })();` as the newer groups do, so its names do not collide with another file's).
- **For agents.** Open only the helper part of a page (from the top to `// ---- test groups`) and your own group file; do not read the other group files or the whole `GROUPS` list unless you need them. Parallel lanes each add their own file plus one `GROUPS` line, so merges only meet on that list.

## Known limitations

- Sheet metal versus SolidWorks (test-reports/2026-10-12-sheetmetal-fidelity.md): Edge Flange defaults to Bend Outside (SolidWorks: Material Inside); no Auto Relief default on the sheet. Lane DC (2026-10-26): Jog from a sketch line, Flatten as a state, Link to thickness and the Sheet-Metal / Flat-Pattern tree nodes are built (see *Flatten and the FeatureManager* below); lane DK (2026-10-27) added the Jog's *Fix projected length*, the Flat-Pattern's fixed face / grain direction and Closed Corner's closed bend region.
- No rib or wrap yet (see `ROADMAP.md`). Draft is exact on planar faces in Anvil's own kernel; a cylindrical or conical face is drafted by the OpenCascade kernel only (Anvil refuses it with a message). In Anvil's own kernel, chamfers and fillets work on straight and polygonal edges between planar faces; a fillet between planar faces is an exact cylinder, along a chain of facets of a curved face it is polygonal (the OpenCascade kernel does curved and tangent edge chains exactly). Sweeps use mitred polyline paths (arcs are polygonal), lofts are the convex hull of convex parallel sections and ruled surfaces between resampled outlines otherwise, and Shell is exact for planar faces; on polygonal round faces the cavity is the polygonal offset.
- Anvil's own STEP writer and reader handle cylinders, cones, spheres and tori bounded by circles, lines and seams exactly; faces bounded by other curves (crossing holes, the hex nut's chamfer cone) are exported faceted, and B-spline faces are refused on import. Re-importing a part with crossing holes leaves 3 open edges (ear clipping of one notched facet). With the OpenCascade kernel on, STEP and IGES of any surfaces go through OpenCascade instead (see *OpenCascade kernel*). No Parasolid. 3MF import reads stored (uncompressed) packages only.
- Sub-assemblies: Flexible applies one level (a flexible sub-assembly's own sub-assemblies and pattern instances move with it rigidly); no configurations of sub-assemblies; Edit Part opens the part on its own (no transparent assembly around it, no in-context references to assembly geometry). The document store lives in the browser (no files on disk).
- Assemblies have no Collision Detection while dragging, and SmartMates offers Coincident / Parallel / Perpendicular, Concentric / Tangent only (no Width or Distance by dragging).
- Drawings: remaining visible-line end gaps of 0.5–1.9 mm (Tray top corners, Handlebar mitres) look like tangent fade-outs and are unchecked on OpenCascade meshes; one 0.002 mm end on the Hex nut. A sketched (non-convex) detail profile clips solid area fills to its convex hull (lines and hatch lines are clipped to the profile itself).
- Evaluate: a planar face in Section Properties is every coplanar polygon of the body in that plane (two separate coplanar faces count together); straddle faces exist only as faceted curved surfaces; self-intersection checking stops after 2 million triangle pairs or 200 findings; Sensors, Undercut and Thickness Analysis are still placeholders.
- CSG is BSP based: a few hundred features is fine, thousands of polygons per feature starts to take hundreds of milliseconds per rebuild.
- Tools that only touch the body along a face can leave hairline slivers.
- Mitres join flanges of one feature only, at corners where both fold the same way; a flange meeting another flange at a concave (inside) corner is not closed or relieved automatically. Reliefs are straight slots normal to the edge; there is no tear or bend-line relief.
